First slice where two separate WASM binaries in separate user Workers with distinct linear memories actually exchange bytes through a PMos IPC socket — the natural architectural successor to M1's init-spawns-multiple-children substrate and the first concrete demonstration that "separate processes" in PMos means more than "parallel compute" (they can cooperate over IPC). The round-trip is protocol-ordered: display-client-demo prints "sent pixels" only after `fd_write(PIXELS)` succeeds; display-server prints "fb blit ok" only after `fd_read` unblocks + the 16 bytes hit `/dev/fb0`. `proc_wait` (T075) still deferred; `ipc_accept` blocking semantics deliberately NOT changed (the poll-loop variant wraps existing `-EAGAIN` return). Known follow-up tech-debt: socket cleanup on `proc_exit` — today the orphaned `/run/display` binding survives display-server's exit, which is why display-client-demo's `display_connect` succeeds-but-gets-stuck under sequential vitest runAllSpawns rather than returning ECONNREFUSED; kernel-side fix is a small targeted slice.

- `fa3391b` — display-server std binary over M1 substrate (first `std` binary to do IPC through a real user Worker + real SAB + real Atomics wake protocol). `crates/display-server/src/main.rs` replaces the 28-line `Server::new()` + `println!` stub with the display-server-lite flow (bind + connect + accept + fd_write + fd_read + path_open(/dev/fb0) + fd_write) promoted from a no_std cdylib into a std bin-target: `fn main()` + `println!("display-server starting")` at entry + `std::process::exit(N)` on every intermediate failure (codes 10..16 matching display-server-lite's numbering) + trailing `println!("display-server fb blit ok")` after the final `fd_write(fb_fd)` that only runs on exit 0 (so its presence on the page console implicitly certifies every step succeeded). Extern block mirrors display-server-lite exactly — `wasi_snapshot_preview1.{path_open, fd_write, fd_read}` + `pmos_ext.{display_bind, display_connect, ipc_accept}` + Ciovec/Iovec repr(C) structs + `PIXELS: [u8; 16]` red/green/blue/white constant; the framebuffer write reads from `recv_buf` (populated by step 5's `fd_read`) not `PIXELS` so the IPC round-trip is load-bearing (a regression that broke the socket but left everything else intact surfaces as wrong framebuffer bytes rather than a green test). `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] fn main()` native stub keeps `cargo test --workspace` + `cargo build --workspace` linking the bin target; the library crate's `display_server::Server` is no longer consumed by the binary (the library itself keeps its existing callers + tests — this is a main.rs-only change). `web/src/bootstrap.ts` `main()`'s `bootBinary` expression gains a `hash.includes("display-server")` → `/bin/display-server` branch positioned before the existing `hash.includes("input-echo")` → `/bin/hello_input_echo` branch (default `/bin/init` for bare URL / `#real-kernel` unchanged); comment block rewritten to document both demo-binary hashes explicitly. `USERLAND_BINS` in `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs:113` already carried `("display-server", "display-server")` from the original scaffolding slice (T009), so no xtask change — cargo's bin-target output `display-server.wasm` lands at `dist/assets/bin/display-server.wasm` and the path key `/bin/display-server` matches. New Playwright test `"display-server: std binary binds, accepts a client, relays pixels to /dev/fb0"` in `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts`: navigates to `/index.html#display-server`, polls `body[data-pmos-peak-live-workers] >= 1` (user Worker alive; same signal the input-echo test uses), polls captured page console for `"[real-kernel] display-server fb blit ok"`, asserts `"[real-kernel] display-server starting"` appeared first (ordered indices in both the page console array and `#pmos-real-console.innerText`), asserts no `real kernel panic`. First slice where a real Rust std binary does IPC (display_bind + ipc_accept + fd_write/fd_read on an IPC socket + path_open(/dev/fb0)) end-to-end through the multi-process substrate — what M1 made possible in principle + what the input-echo slice proved on the no_std side now has its std-binary counterpart. The binary still plays server + client + fb-writer in one process (same self-connect shape as display-server-lite's composition test); the next slice — a persistent accept loop + a second process as client — is blocked on `ipc_accept` blocking semantics (today returns EAGAIN on empty backlog) + `proc_wait` T075 for init-side supervision of multiple concurrent children.
- `e0ba2ba` — input round-trip in real-kernel mode (first slice after M1; first browser-keypress end-to-end to a user Worker). `web/src/bootstrap.ts` `runRealKernelMode(bootBinary: string)` takes the path as a parameter; `main()` picks it from the URL hash — `#input-echo` → `/bin/hello_input_echo`, anything else (bare URL / `#real-kernel`) → `/bin/init` (existing default unchanged). `runRealKernelMode` installs `document.addEventListener("keydown", ...)` that gates on `!event.ctrlKey && !event.metaKey && !event.altKey`, runs the existing module-scope `keyToBytes` (printable ASCII → UTF-8, Enter → 0x0a, Backspace → 0x7f; returns `null` for non-printables + modifier chords), `event.preventDefault()`s the browser default on a hit, and posts `{kind:"input:kbd", bytes}` on the kernel Worker. ConsoleHost's bootConfig flips `enableInput: false → true` so `bootKernelWorker` registers the `InputDriver` in real-kernel mode — the driver's `onHostMessage` path was already wired (`input:kbd` → `pushInputToKernel(Devnum.InputKbd, bytes)` → `kernel.injectInput`), but with `enableInput: false` the driver wasn't registered and the `input:kbd` case in `scaffold.handleMainMessage` silently dropped every message. `KernelWasmHost.injectInput(devnum, bytes)` switches its devnum discriminator from `DEV.CONSOLE`/`DEV.INPUT_KBD`/`DEV.INPUT_MOUSE` (DriverId values: 5 / 1 / 2) to `Devnum.Console`/`Devnum.InputKbd`/`Devnum.InputMouse` (devfs values: 4 / 20 / 21) — aligns with `MockKernel.injectInput`'s convention and with the `DriverHost.pushInputToKernel` contract that `InputDriver` + `ConsoleDriver` both call (they pass `Devnum` values; the previous mismatch was a pre-existing bug that only surfaced once the scaffold backed a real kernel via the new `enableInput: true` path). Three vitest test files migrate to `Devnum.*`: `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` (6 uses — `DEV.CONSOLE`/`DEV.INPUT_KBD`/`DEV.INPUT_MOUSE` → `Devnum.*`; the "rejects unrouted devnums" test flips its negative example from `DEV.BLOCK` → `Devnum.Fb0` because block is no longer distinguishable without a Devnum-side block value), `sab-backend.test.ts` (2 `DEV.CONSOLE` → `Devnum.Console` for the seed-injection half of the byte-equivalence harness), `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` (1 `DEV.INPUT_KBD` in the `hello-input-echo` composition test). `crates/hello-input-echo/src/lib.rs` `_start` swaps the single-shot `fd_read` + `nread == 0` → `proc_exit(12)` for a `loop { fd_read; if rc == 0 && nread > 0 break; if rc == 0 || rc == EAGAIN continue; proc_exit(11); }` + module doc updated; the EAGAIN-poll survives the Playwright race where the user Worker spawns and runs `_start` before the first `page.keyboard.press` injection reaches the kbd ring (existing vitest test pre-injects bytes BEFORE spawning, so the first `fd_read` finds the ring non-empty and the loop is a no-op there). `web/src/kernel-worker-entry.ts` `bootRealKernel` grows a sixth parameter `onScaffoldReady: (scaffold, host) => void` the caller invokes AFTER `bootKernelWorker` returns but BEFORE `runBootBinary` enters the long-running dispatch loop — critical because previously `installWorkerEntry`'s `.then()` callback only set the `scaffold` + `realKernel` variables after `bootRealKernel`'s promise resolved (= after every spawned pid exited + the dispatch loop halted), so any driver message (`input:kbd`, `console:input`, etc.) arriving during execution hit the `scaffold === undefined` pre-boot panic branch and emitted `"kernel-worker: input:kbd received before boot"` to main. The new callback publishes the scaffold to `installWorkerEntry`'s closure immediately; the kernel wake-slot post + `runBootBinary` call continue in the async tail. New Playwright test `"input round-trip: keydown in real-kernel mode echoes to console"` in `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts`: navigates to `/index.html#input-echo`, polls `body[data-pmos-peak-live-workers] >= 1` (user Worker alive), `page.keyboard.press("x")` + `page.keyboard.press("Enter")`, polls the captured page console for the exact `"[real-kernel] x"` line, reads `#pmos-real-console.innerText` and asserts `toContain("x")`, asserts no `real kernel panic`. tasks.md T082 + T083 deviation notes progressed to describe the bootstrap-side keydown capture (T082: "a secondary keyboard-capture path on `document` in `web/src/bootstrap.ts`'s `runRealKernelMode()`") + the Playwright-level coverage (T083: "Playwright coverage for the real-kernel keyboard path landed as the 'input round-trip' block in `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts`"). Rebuilds `dist/assets/bootstrap.js` (27.7 → 28.5 KiB, +hash-gate + keydown listener) and `kernel-worker.js` (56.0 → 56.3 KiB, +onScaffoldReady plumbing); `user-worker.js` unchanged; manifest entry count unchanged at 27. Files: `crates/hello-input-echo/src/lib.rs` (EAGAIN loop), `web/src/bootstrap.ts` (bootBinary param + hash gate + enableInput flip + keydown listener), `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts` (Devnum switch + import update + doc comment), `web/src/kernel-worker-entry.ts` (bootRealKernel onScaffoldReady), `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts` (+1 test), `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` + `sab-backend.test.ts` + `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` (DEV → Devnum migrations), `specs/001-browser-os-v1/tasks.md` (T082 + T083 notes), `dist/assets/bootstrap.js` + `kernel-worker.js` (rebuilt). The keydown path lives beside the preview-slice InputDriver, not inside it — the driver still owns pointer routing (`pointermove`/`pointerdown`/`pointerup` in `runTerminalMode` for `#mock-kernel`), and the real-kernel keyboard half lands on `document` via a separate listener at the bootstrap scope. T079's cleanup of the input driver (migrating BOTH halves into `drivers/input.ts` under a common API) remains deferred; today the scaffold's `InputDriver` only handles receipt-side `input:kbd` / `input:mouse` messages — the event-capture side lives in whichever caller (bootstrap, future compositor) wants to populate those messages.

The default `onSpawnProcess` collapses from three branches to two: explicit `options.onSpawnProcess` wins if provided, else if `binaryRegistry + kernelWorkerChannel` are BOTH set install the posting variant, else no default handler (accept-no-op). Plan correction in `multi-process-plan.md` §13: the M1.6 plan said `SpawnHistoryEntry` would "stay" post-drain-delete, but with no caller (the production drain was the only writer, and the only readers were composition tests that now read `runAllSpawns`'s direct return value) keeping an unpopulated type + getter would have been a feature without a caller — the plan-text expectation of "stays" is overridden by the cleaner "delete" that T235 actually landed; also §11 M1.6's "tests in `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` and `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`" was wrong — kernel-wasm-host.test.ts never touched the drain, the tests needing migration were in `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` + `kernel-worker-entry.test.ts`. Composition tests in `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` migrated onto a test-local `runAllSpawns(kernel, captures: CapturedSpawn[])` helper + `captureSpawn(registry, captures)` factory that returns an `onSpawnProcess` callback — the helper mirrors the old drain's semantics (sequential; reentrant for children spawned mid-parent-run; returns per-child `{pid, path, exitCode}[]`) without depending on deleted production types. 10 composition tests rewritten in place using the new helper: PROC_SPAWN+child basic, two-level reentrancy (spawner mid-run → hello), missing-binary -EIO, ipc-self-test, hello-framebuffer, display-server-lite, hello-wasi-bootstrap, hello-fb-blit, hello-input-echo, hello-std, init+hello-std-ordering — each swaps `binaryRegistry: ...` for `onSpawnProcess: captureSpawn(binaryRegistry, captures)`, swaps `await kernel.drainPendingSpawns()` for `const history = await runAllSpawns(kernel, captures)`, swaps `kernel.spawnHistoryEntries` for the helper's return. Ordering-sensitive test (init + hello-std) keeps its console-line assertions intact — `runAllSpawns` is sequential so the parent-before-child ordering is preserved. Three pre-T233 tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-worker-entry.test.ts` (fetcher-fallback, manifest-populate, auto-spawn) that relied on the legacy `!useMultiProcess → drainPendingSpawns` fallback migrated to the T233 message-driven pattern: await `proc:spawn` on `msg.posted`, pre-seed SAB with a FD_WRITE of the expected payload via `seedFdWriteOnce(sab, "hello from std\n", 1)`, post `proc:sab(pid, sab)` + await `console:write`, post `proc:exited(pid, 0)`, await `whenReady`. The pattern matches the T233 tests already in the file (lines 666+); `seedFdWriteOnce` + `waitFor` + SAB-layout imports hoist correctly from the later describe block. Total 1139 passing, 0 failing across all layers. **The multi-process substrate (M1) is now complete end-to-end: every user pid runs in a dedicated Web Worker with a distinct WASM linear memory, parked on real `Atomics.wait` + woken via real `Atomics.notify` on per-pid `SharedArrayBuffer`s, orchestrated by `startDispatchLoop` with no legacy fallback.** The runway's next slice is pulled from the Beyond M1 candidates below.
- `f6db0de` — first real user Workers in the browser + production Atomics wake protocol (M1.5 landing). `bootstrap.ts`'s `runRealKernelMode()` now constructs `createSpawnRouter({kernelWorker: worker, workerFactory: () => new Worker("/assets/user-worker.js", {type:"module"}), allocSab: () => new SharedArrayBuffer(SAB_SIZE) (with ArrayBuffer fallback), getKernelWakeSlot: () => kernelWakeSlotBuffer})` alongside the kernel Worker, installs a second `worker.addEventListener("message", ...)` (ConsoleHost keeps its own — events fan out to every listener) that skims `kernel:wake-slot` into the wake-slot closure + routes `proc:spawn` into the router + tracks `router.liveWorkers.size` after every handled message into `<body data-pmos-peak-live-workers>` for Playwright to read. `BootConfig` gains `enableMultiProcessSpawn?: boolean` (new in `shared/worker-proto.ts`) that production sets true; `kernel-worker-entry.ts`'s `bootRealKernel` reads it alongside the existing `WorkerEntryOptions.enableMultiProcessSpawn` (OR-gated so T233 tests that drive installWorkerEntry directly still work). `KernelToMain` gains a `{kind: "kernel:wake-slot", sab: ArrayBufferLike}` variant that `bootRealKernel` posts exactly once AFTER `KernelWasmHost.create` and BEFORE `runBootBinary` — so main has the 32-byte wake-slot buffer in hand before the first `proc:spawn` arrives, ensuring every spawned user Worker gets the wake slot in its boot message. `MainToUser.boot` gains an optional `kernelWakeSlot?: ArrayBufferLike` field the router stamps onto every spawn when `getKernelWakeSlot()` returns non-null; `user-worker-entry.ts`'s `runOnce` builds an `Int32Array` view and passes it as `SabBackendOptions.kernelWakeSlot`. `SabBackend.dispatch`'s production wake protocol runs when `kernelWakeSlot` is set AND no `serviceHook` (which always wins when set, keeping T231 byte-equivalence tests untouched): step 1 `Atomics.store(header, OFF_USER_WAIT_SLOT/4, STATUS_REQUESTED)` BEFORE the push (so a kernel that pops + services + writes `STATUS_READY` before we reach `Atomics.wait` makes the wait return "not-equal" immediately — the double-check pattern applied to the wait sentinel), step 2 producer-push (existing), step 3 `Atomics.add(kernelWakeSlot, 0, 1)` + `Atomics.notify(kernelWakeSlot, 0)` (notify gated on `instanceof SharedArrayBuffer`), step 4 `Atomics.wait(header, OFF_USER_WAIT_SLOT/4, STATUS_REQUESTED)` (also gated on SAB; sync wait is legal in user-Worker scope — Workers can park, only the browser main thread can't), step 5 consumer-pop (existing). On a plain `ArrayBuffer` (vitest under node without COI) both notify + wait are gated off, so the dispatch falls through to pop and throws "ring empty" if the caller forgot to pre-service — intentional so tests without a real kernel surface the missing-servicing bug. `KernelWasmHost.startDispatchLoop` adds the kernel-side half: after each successful `serviceSab` inside the inner per-pid budget loop, store `STATUS_READY` on `OFF_USER_WAIT_SLOT` + `Atomics.notify` (gated on sab-is-shared) so a user Worker parked on `Atomics.wait(..., STATUS_REQUESTED)` wakes (the notify wakes it) OR returns "not-equal" immediately (if it reaches the wait AFTER the store). Extends `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts` with two new T234 assertions on top of the existing four-line ordered-output check: `data-pmos-peak-live-workers >= 2` proves init and hello-std actually ran in DIFFERENT user Workers concurrently (the bootstrap pid stays in-process inside the kernel Worker, but every later pid is a real user Worker managed by `createSpawnRouter`; peak reaches 2 during the window where init is still printing + hello-std is already spawned but not yet reaped), and `data-pmos-wake-slot-ready === "1"` proves the kernel posted `kernel:wake-slot` before the first `proc:spawn` arrived (bootstrap's listener stamps the attribute synchronously on receipt). +2 vitest tests in `web/tests/unit/dispatch-loop.test.ts` (startDispatchLoop transitions `user_wait_slot` from `STATUS_REQUESTED` → `STATUS_READY` after serviceSab, verified for single-request + multi-request passes). +2 in `web/tests/unit/sab-backend.test.ts` (serviceHook wins over kernelWakeSlot when both are set — wake slot stays at 0; with only kernelWakeSlot, dispatch pre-stages `STATUS_REQUESTED` + bumps wake slot + pops pre-canned response). +1 in `web/tests/unit/user-worker-entry.test.ts` (boot.kernelWakeSlot threads into SabBackend, verified via a wake-slot increment on hello-std's first syscall before the dispatch crashes on an empty response ring — the crash is expected in vitest because no kernel services; the increment proves SabBackend received the wake slot from the boot message). +2 in `web/tests/unit/bootstrap-spawn.test.ts` (createSpawnRouter with `getKernelWakeSlot` returning a buffer → boot message carries `kernelWakeSlot`; returning null → field omitted). This is the first slice where two distinct WebAssembly instances (init + hello-std) actually run in separate Workers with separate linear memories, parked on real `Atomics.wait` + woken via real `Atomics.notify` on a `SharedArrayBuffer` — the single biggest Principle V delta in the project's history. `drainPendingSpawns` + the legacy `pendingSpawns` queue are still alive for the composition tests that still use them; T235 retires both.
- `e312f19` — kernel-Worker dispatch loop + proc:spawn-routing default (M1.4 landing). `KernelWasmHost.startDispatchLoop({pidSource, halted, parkFn?, budget?}): Promise<void>` in `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts` implements the round-robin service loop: each pass walks every live pid in `pidSource()` and calls the existing `serviceSab(pid, view)` primitive up to `budget` (default 8) times per pid; breaks the inner loop on the first empty-ring response; parks on `parkFn()` when a pass completed with nothing serviced anywhere; exits when `halted()` returns true (checked at the start AND end of every pass so a pid that arrives + exits during servicing doesn't waste a park). A dedicated 32-byte wake SAB is allocated inside `KernelWasmHost.create` (tries `new SharedArrayBuffer(32)`, falls back to `new ArrayBuffer(32)` in environments without COI) and exposed via the new `wakeSlot` getter — T234 wires `SabBackend` + main-thread `injectInput` to `Atomics.add(wakeSlot, 0, 1) + Atomics.notify` so the kernel wakes from park without waiting for the 50 ms timeout. Default `parkFn` uses `Atomics.waitAsync` on the wake slot with a 50 ms timeout when available; falls back to `setTimeout(0)` (a microtask yield) when either `SharedArrayBuffer` or `Atomics.waitAsync` is unavailable — vitest under node can park with `setTimeout` safely, production Worker scope gets the real async wait. New `KernelWasmHostOptions.kernelWorkerChannel?: {postMessage(msg: KernelToMain): void}`: when set alongside `binaryRegistry`, the default `onSpawnProcess` swaps from the legacy queue-into-`pendingSpawns` drain to posting `{kind:"proc:spawn", pid, path, wasmBytes}` on the channel + returning `{ ok: true }` synchronously. The legacy path stays intact when `kernelWorkerChannel` is absent so existing callers (every existing test + `real-kernel.spec.ts` Playwright) keep their current behavior; T235 removes the legacy path along with `drainPendingSpawns`. New `WorkerEntryOptions.enableMultiProcessSpawn?: boolean` in `kernel-worker-entry.ts` opt-in-gates the new path at the entry level: when true, `bootRealKernel` sets `kernelWorkerChannel` on the host + `runBootBinary` collapses to "PROC_SPAWN bootstrap-pid dispatch + `startDispatchLoop(pidMap, () => hasEverSpawned && pidMap.size === 0)`"; when false the entry keeps calling `drainPendingSpawns` (the pre-M1 in-process path) so every existing test that just sends `boot {useRealKernel: true, bootBinary: "/bin/hello-std"}` and awaits `whenReady` continues to auto-drain. The entry gains `proc:sab` + `proc:exited` handlers on the `MainToKernel` channel that maintain a `Map<pid, ArrayBufferLike>` the dispatch loop iterates via `pidSource`; a `lifecycle.hasEverSpawned` flag flips on the first `proc:sab` so the halt predicate closes a race where a pid arrives and exits between halt probes (the parker's 50 ms timeout is longer than a trivial child's lifetime under vitest, so tracking the flip at message-receipt time is mandatory). +5 vitest tests in `web/tests/unit/dispatch-loop.test.ts` (new file): round-robin drains every live pid's ring; 8-request-per-pid budget proven by seeding pid A with 16 requests + pid B with 1 request and halting after exactly one pass (A services 8 / B services 1 / parks NOT reached); immediate halt when `halted()` is true from the start (park count 0); parks exactly once on empty rings when the halt only fires after the first park; terminates cleanly when the pidMap empties between passes (proves the `halted()`-after-pass check works). +2 vitest tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-worker-entry.test.ts` under a new `installWorkerEntry with useRealKernel + proc:spawn routing` describe: boot with `enableMultiProcessSpawn: true` + `bootBinary: "/bin/hello-std"` → `proc:spawn` lands on the messaging channel → test pre-seeds a SAB with a FD_WRITE request + sends `proc:sab(pid, sab)` → dispatch loop services the ring + lands a response in the SAB's slot 0 + fires `console:write` with the expected bytes → test sends `proc:exited(pid, 0)` → loop halts → `whenReady` resolves; spurious `proc:exited` for an unknown pid is ignored, real `proc:sab` + `proc:exited` still drives the loop to completion (no-op delete for unknown pid, real lifecycle proceeds). - `46859d0` — `SabBackend` over per-pid SAB ring (M1.2 landing). New file `web/src/sab-backend.ts` exporting `class SabBackend implements KernelBackend` from `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`. Constructor: `{sab: Uint8Array, pid: number, serviceHook?: () => void}`. `dispatch(request, heapIn?)` stages heap input at the user-chosen `request.heapPtr` offset inside the SAB heap-scratch region (rejecting overflow against `HEAP_SCRATCH_BYTES = 32 KiB` with the same `/capacity/` shape `KernelWasmHost.dispatch` uses), producer-pushes the encoded request slot via `Atomics.load/store` on the SAB header (mirrors `ring::Sab::try_push_request` from `crates/ring/src/lib.rs`), invokes `serviceHook?.()`, consumer-pops the response slot, decodes via `decodeResponse`, advances `OFF_RES_TAIL`, then reads heap output (if any) from the same SAB heap-scratch window the input was staged in. The `serviceHook` is the T231 stand-in for the production wake protocol; T233+ replaces it with `Atomics.store(userWaitSlot, REQUESTED) → Atomics.notify(kernelWakeSlot) → Atomics.wait(userWaitSlot, REQUESTED)`. Wake slots themselves (`OFF_USER_WAIT_SLOT`, `OFF_KERNEL_WAIT_SLOT`) are intentionally untouched by `SabBackend` — that's T233's concern, same deferral T230's `serviceSab` honours. New file `web/tests/unit/sab-backend.test.ts` with 5 byte-equivalence tests against `KernelWasmHostBackend`: each runs the same `SyscallRequest` through both backends against fresh `KernelWasmHost` instances and asserts the `DispatchResult` is byte-identical (response slot fields + heap-out bytes), plus any kernel-side observable effect (e.g. `onConsoleWrite`) matches. The five opcodes covered: `FD_WRITE → /dev/console` (both hosts' `onConsoleWrite` fire with identical bytes), `FD_READ` after pre-injected console input (heap-out matches the seed), `PATH_OPEN("/does/not/exist")` (`-ENOENT` byte-equivalent), `PROC_EXIT(0)` (OK response byte-equivalent), heap-overflow rejection (a 40 KiB payload exceeds both backends' scratch caps; both throw `/capacity/`). The test harness's `serviceHook` synchronously calls `host.serviceSab(pid, sab)` between the SabBackend's request push and response pop. Vitest runs under node without cross-origin-isolation, so a plain `ArrayBuffer` stand-in for `SharedArrayBuffer` is used — `Int32Array.{load,store}` over plain buffers work identically; the only path that needs a real SAB is `Atomics.wait`, which T231 doesn't exercise. - `f1e416b` — SAB-ring servicing primitive (M1.1 landing). `KernelWasmHost.serviceSab(pid, sab: Uint8Array): 0 | 1` in `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts` pops one request out of the SAB's request ring, decodes via `decodeRequest` (new helper in `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`), calls the existing synchronous `dispatch`, and pushes the encoded response back via `encodeResponse` (new). Wake slots deliberately untouched — those are T233's concern. **Plan correction**: the original plan called for a new kernel-side `kernel_service_sab` export wrapping `Dispatcher::service_one` over a `ring::Sab::from_raw` view of the SAB. That design doesn't work — a WASM module's linear memory is a distinct address space from a `SharedArrayBuffer`, so the kernel cannot construct a `Sab` over SAB bytes without memcpy-each-way through its own scratch, and once the memcpy is on the JS side there's nothing the kernel does that `kernel_dispatch` isn't already doing. The entire orchestration lives in TS; no Rust changes landed. A new "Plan corrections" §13 in `multi-process-plan.md` records the correction + its downstream impact on T233's loop pseudocode (`host.serviceSab(pid, sabView)` replaces the planned `kernel_service_sab` call). +3 vitest tests: FD_WRITE through the SAB ring fires `onConsoleWrite` + lands a Response at slot 0 + advances req_tail and res_head as expected; empty ring returns `1` without touching any atomics; PROC_EXIT serviced cleanly with status=0 + value=0 in the response slot. Test bytes are seeded into a plain `ArrayBuffer` stand-in for `SharedArrayBuffer` (vitest runs under node with no cross-origin-isolated context), but every atomic op and ring offset is byte-identical to what a real SAB would drive. - `ef1c5cb` — Multi-process substrate (M1) plan + task rows. Docs-only slice (zero test delta). Docs-only slice: new [`multi-process-plan.md`](specs/001-browser-os-v1/multi-process-plan.md) alongside the main v1 plan (~850 lines) covers Worker lifecycle (main-thread-mediated spawn — kernel Worker never calls `new Worker()`; rationale vs. nested Workers), per-pid SAB layout (reused verbatim from `abi::ring`), syscall marshaling (wasm's sync view preserved via `Atomics.wait` inside the user Worker), kernel-side dispatch loop (round-robin across a pidMap with 50 ms wake-slot timeout), driver routing (unchanged — main↔kernel channels already exist; main→user Worker would break Principle V and is not added), scheduler replacement (`drainPendingSpawns` → `startDispatchLoop`), error handling (Worker `error` + `proc_exit` both route through `proc:exited` → `Kernel::proc_exit` → reap → `worker.terminate()`), testability (vitest covers everything except the first real browser Worker spawn; Playwright only where it must), and a 6-sub-slice breakdown T230–T235 each sized for one session. Constitution check: Principles II (strict layering), V (process isolation — the *first real gain* on V; today it's conventional, after M1 it's physical WASM-linear-memory-distinct), and X (testability at every layer) all PASS with reasoning captured in §9 of the plan. No new opcodes, no new deviations; `user-worker.js` added to the ship manifest. Task rows appended to `tasks.md` as a new "Multi-process substrate (M1) sub-slices" block after T219–T222; T091 and T074 deviation notes updated in-place to cross-reference the new T230–T235 rows. Zero test-count delta (0 new tests, 0 code touched). The next code-landing slice is T230 (kernel-side `kernel_service_sab` export + `serviceSab` TS wrapper + 3 vitest tests).
- `61a7f15` — Surface real-kernel console output in a `<pre id="pmos-real-console">` DOM element so the browser page actually shows the boot. Hides the unused `#pmos-fb` canvas in real-kernel mode. Panics flush to both page console and DOM. Playwright test now reads `#pmos-real-console.innerText` alongside the page-console capture and asserts the full four-line sequence AND their ordering. Zero new tests; strengthened assertions. Pure UX slice — before this commit `/index.html#real-kernel` produced a blank page.
- `667c79a` — A real Rust std init binary. `crates/init/` is no longer a stub; it `println!`s a startup line, calls `pmos_ext.proc_spawn(b"/bin/hello-std", u64::MAX)`, `println!`s the result, exits. `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`-gated extern block + native stub so `cargo build --workspace` still links. bootstrap.ts's `runRealKernelMode()` flips boot binary from `/bin/hello-std` to `/bin/init`; the Playwright test now asserts four ordered lines (`init starting` → `init spawned hello-std pid=N` → `init exiting` → `hello from std`). +1 TS-unit composition test; +0 Playwright count, but strengthened assertions. First proof that a real Rust std binary can call a PMos extension syscall end-to-end in a real browser.
- `038f1aa` — Real-kernel mode in bootstrap.ts (`#real-kernel` URL hash) + first Playwright integration test. `runRealKernelMode()` spawns the worker with `useRealKernel: true` + `bootBinary: "/bin/hello-std"`, forwards `/dev/console` lines to `console.log` with a `[real-kernel]` prefix. Playwright config + `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts` + `web/vitest.config.ts` (restricts unit discovery to `tests/unit/`). +0 unit tests; +1 Playwright test.
- `171c10a` — kernel-worker.js into dist (Justfile esbuild step + xtask copy) + Worker-scope fetch fallback for `/assets/kernel.wasm` and binary registry from `/manifest.json`. +2 TS tests (fetcher fallback + manifest-driven registry build). Tests inject a fetcher mock; production hits the network from Worker scope. Manifest entry count 25 → 26.
- `8ab39da` — KernelWasmHost wired into kernel-worker-entry behind `useRealKernel` + boot-binary auto-spawn through real wasm. `BootConfig` gains `useRealKernel` + `bootBinary`; `installWorkerEntry` accepts a second `WorkerEntryOptions` arg with `kernelWasmBytes` + `binaryRegistry`. Default flag-off path keeps every existing test green; flag-on path async-constructs `KernelWasmHost`, wires `onConsoleWrite` → `console:write` postMessage, registers init pid + dispatches `PROC_SPAWN(bootBinary)` + drains. +4 TS tests; the capstone test loads the real `kernel.wasm` + `hello-std.wasm` from disk and asserts `"hello from std\n"` reaches the messaging channel.
- `09709a5` — A real Rust std binary (println! + fn main) runs end-to-end through PMos. New `crates/hello-std/` bin target; the WASI startup path completes through the kernel's already-shimmed opcodes (fd_write + proc_exit + environ_get + environ_sizes_get). +1 TS composition test.
- `1f86630` — Input path end-to-end: injected `/dev/input_kbd` bytes echo to `/dev/console` through real user wasm. New `crates/hello-input-echo/`; `KernelWasmHost.injectInput` gains a routing map for INPUT_KBD + INPUT_MOUSE.

Older slices (capstone-era and before) are summarised in the long block at the bottom of this file under "Append-only slice log".

## Where the runway points next

With the `1a4f5f2` POSIX-perfect Vfs::resolve_nofollow landed on top of `a26d0ca` AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW in path_open's lookup_flags and `51a095d` PROC_CAPS_GET ext opcode and `e9caf5c` PROC_KILL ext opcode and `98a3341` PROC_WAIT ext opcode and `6b89d84` path_open oflags wiring and `83de656` symlink-aware path resolution in Vfs::resolve and `1b6e5f8` IPC_PIPE ext opcode on top of `fbddb91` fd_read/fd_write-on-pipe-fds + `ca5bb8a` PipeBroken → EPIPE + `de672d8` SOCK_SHUTDOWN half-close upgrade + the `8d7c3e1` FD_PRESTAT_DIR_NAME + `83129ba` PATH_READLINK + `b6449b2` PATH_SYMLINK + `657b07f` PATH_LINK + `e22dd5e` SOCK_SHUTDOWN (original) + the `5186479` SOCK_ACCEPT + `03794fc` path_open WASI fdflags fix + `f46a2d9` SOCK_SEND + SOCK_RECV + `c1a3643` FD_PREAD + FD_PWRITE + `1d34dce` FD_FILESTAT_SET_SIZE + `df2dd88` FD_FDSTAT_SET_FLAGS + `a3773a4` PATH_CREATE_DIRECTORY + PATH_REMOVE_DIRECTORY + `56be64e` FD_READDIR + `d5bfca3` PATH_UNLINK_FILE + PATH_RENAME + `4f353a2` FD_RENUMBER + `96cd6f3` FD_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES + `4b46f84` POLL_ONEOFF + `0437093` PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES + `98765ee` real-vnode-timestamps + `17e5985` pending-Connecting-client-cleanup + `a98ce64` fd-state-opcodes bundle + `6d429d9` FD_TELL + `b486b87` FD_SEEK chain, the WASI socket surface is feature-complete for v1: sock_send + sock_recv + sock_accept + sock_shutdown all wired. The ENOSYS probe now points at FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS (0x0026) — a more stable long-term probe target since WASI's rights system is deliberately un-v1-relevant. The libc preopen-discovery loop now terminates cleanly: both fd_prestat_get and fd_prestat_dir_name return EBADF for every fd (v1 has no preopens). The Filesystem trait and the VFS gained three link-family methods: `link` (default `ReadOnly → EROFS`), `symlink` (default `NotSupported → ENOTSUP`), and `readlink` (default `NotSupported → ENOTSUP`). The tmpfs `TmpNode` enum gained a `SymLink(String)` variant; NodeType::SymLink already existed in the NodeType enum pre-batch. The IpcTable gained two new per-Socket fields (shutdown_read + shutdown_write) + a new shutdown_socket method, with send/recv updated to honour them — SOCK_SHUTDOWN now honours SHUT_RD / SHUT_WR individually per POSIX. IpcError::PipeBroken now maps to a dedicated KernelError::PipeBroken variant → EPIPE (rather than the pre-slice NotSupportedOnFd → EINVAL conflation), so userland sees the POSIX-correct errno for broken-pipe writes on sockets. fd_read/fd_write on PipeRead/PipeWrite FdObjects wire through to ipc::Pipe::try_read/try_write and userland can now create pipe pairs via the new IPC_PIPE ext opcode (0x1007) — POSIX pipe(2) surface is complete end-to-end through kernel.wasm. The sock_accept opcode is also in place — userland can atomically accept + set NONBLOCK on the accepted connection fd in a single syscall (matching glibc accept4 semantics). The path_open WASI fdflags decode bug is fixed and both call sites that accept WASI fdflags share a single translation helper. The socket-alias bundle is also in place — userland can call sock_send / sock_recv on Socket fds with identical errno behaviour to fd_write / fd_read (the same IpcTable path underneath; the extra si_flags / ri_flags u16 word is accepted and discarded). The positional I/O bundle is also in place — userland can pread / pwrite at an explicit offset without disturbing the fd's seekable position (non-Vnode fds reject with EINVAL, unopened fds EBADF). The ENOSYS probe that tracks "the next still-unhandled WASI opcode" now points at SOCK_SHUTDOWN (0x0073); FD_PREAD is no longer the probe target. The ftruncate opcode is also in place — userland can truncate / zero-extend seekable tmpfs files via fd (non-Vnode fds reject with EINVAL, directory targets reject with EISDIR, read-only filesystems reject with EROFS). The F_SETFL opcode is also in place — userland can mutate an fd's NONBLOCK / APPEND bits (sync-family bits accepted + ignored since v1's tmpfs is already synchronous), with CLOEXEC preserved across the call per POSIX F_SETFL/F_SETFD split. The directory-mutation pair is also in place — userland can create and remove directories in tmpfs through the WASI surface (devfs + procfs still reject with EROFS, as they do for every mutation opcode). No new Vfs methods or Filesystem trait additions: the slice is purely wire layout + dispatcher guards over the existing Vfs::mkdir + Vfs::rmdir public API. ERRNO.EEXIST + ERRNO.ENOTEMPTY mirrored for TS (matching the ERRNO.EISDIR + ERRNO.ENOTDIR pattern from FD_READDIR). The ENOSYS probe that tracks "the next still-unhandled WASI opcode" continues to point at FD_PREAD (0x002A); PATH_CREATE_DIRECTORY + PATH_REMOVE_DIRECTORY were never the probe target (their opcodes 0x0040 + 0x0046 already had scaffolding in the abi crate). The **eighteen-slice WASI-surface cadence** CLOCK_TIME_GET + CLOCK_RES_GET + FD_FILESTAT_GET + PATH_FILESTAT_GET + FD_SEEK + FD_TELL + {fd_advise,fd_allocate,fd_sync,fd_datasync} + PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES + POLL_ONEOFF + FD_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES + FD_RENUMBER + {PATH_UNLINK_FILE, PATH_RENAME} + FD_READDIR + {PATH_CREATE_DIRECTORY, PATH_REMOVE_DIRECTORY} + FD_FDSTAT_SET_FLAGS + FD_FILESTAT_SET_SIZE + {FD_PREAD, FD_PWRITE} + {SOCK_SEND, SOCK_RECV} now covers the full seekable-file + metadata + clock + fd-state + metadata-mutation + readiness + fd-management + filesystem-mutation + directory-traversal + directory-mutation + fd-flags-mutation + file-size-mutation + positional-I/O + socket-alias surface area std binaries + libc-style C callers reach for — the WASI surface is effectively complete for v1's scope. The IPC state machine continues to distinguish listener-drop (ECONNREFUSED) from accept-race (EINVAL retry) cleanly. The four-pid default-boot substrate still runs end-to-end under Playwright (init + hello-std + display-server + display-client-demo concurrent Workers) and exits cleanly under sequential vitest `runAllSpawns`. Long-running init supervision stays blocked on `proc_wait` T075; `ipc_accept` still returns `-EAGAIN` on an empty backlog (not blocking). Pick one for the next session:

- **Stretch the WASI surface further** (the five-slice link-family / filesystem-expansion batch + PipeBroken → EPIPE cleanup + fd_read/fd_write on pipe fds + IPC_PIPE ext opcode all landed). Remaining candidates: the seven-slice path-resolution / process-management batch is now complete (symlink-aware-resolve → path_open oflags → PROC_WAIT → PROC_KILL → PROC_CAPS_GET → AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW → POSIX-perfect resolve_nofollow). The full filesystem + process-management surface for v1 is functionally WASI-complete AND POSIX-semantically accurate on lstat/O_NOFOLLOW. Post-batch runway: (a) **Long-running display-server accept loop** — the architectural slice to let the display server serve N clients over time. PROC_WAIT is wired so the init-supervised respawn half is no longer blocked; still needs `ipc_accept` blocking semantics (today returns -EAGAIN on empty backlog). 2+ sessions. (b) **MockKernel replacement for live-terminal** — 3+ sessions, blocked on the long-running-accept slice. (c) **term::Session → Canvas migration** (Known Deviation #4) — 3+ sessions, blocked on toolkit Canvas being ready + display server being the target surface. All remaining items are multi-session architectural slices that don't fit the single-slice cadence this batch has been tuned for.
- **Long-running accept loop (display-server serves multiple clients)** (larger architectural slice, still blocked on `proc_wait` T075). Today's display-server accepts ONE client then exits. A real display-server serves N clients over time. Three coupled kernel changes: (1) `ipc_accept` grows blocking semantics (today returns `-EAGAIN`); (2) init-side supervision via `proc_wait` so a supervisor can restart a crashed display-server; (3) the now-landed `proc_exit` cleanup already makes the fd-table side correct, but the server's inner `accept + serve` loop needs a way to tear down per-client fds without exiting. 2+ session slice.
- **Replace MockKernel as the default for live-terminal**: `useRealKernel: true` is already the default (`5adbd35`); the remaining work is replacing MockKernel for the live-terminal experience. Requires a real terminal-emulator app that talks to display-server + a kernel-side tty. 3+ sessions. Blocked on the long-running-accept slice (real term is a display-protocol client with a persistent connection).
- **`term::Session` → `Canvas` migration**: the big post-`preferred_size` one (Known Deviation #4 in `plan.md`). `crates/term/src/session.rs` currently renders to a bespoke `rasterize_snapshot` flow; reconciliation plan migrates onto `toolkit::draw::Canvas`. Blocked on toolkit's canvas being ready + display server being the target surface. 3+ sessions.

Don't bundle these into one slice. Each is a fresh-session-sized chunk. **Recommended next target** for a fresh session: the **long-running display-server accept loop** — a 2+ session architectural slice now unblocked by PROC_WAIT. Needs (1) `ipc_accept` blocking semantics (today returns -EAGAIN on empty backlog); (2) init-side supervision via proc_wait so a supervisor can restart a crashed display-server; (3) a way to tear down per-client fds on the server side without exiting. There's no remaining small-slice follow-up on the path-resolution / process-management surface — the seven-slice batch closed out the WASI-aligned surface cleanly.

## The big one after preferred_size: `term::Session` migration onto `Canvas`

Deserves its own focused session. Complex and has test-suite / integration-test / cross-crate touch points. Do not try to combine with anything else.

**Motivation**: closes the BGRA rasterizer Known Deviation, fixes the byte-order pin the `two_term_windows` integration test carries, and moves term onto the same rendering path as every other toolkit widget.

**Scope outline — 8 numbered items sketched during the Layout slice**:

1. Delete `crates/term/src/rasterizer.rs` (253 lines) entirely, along with its BGRA byte-order bug (`fill_bg` / `set_pixel` currently write `[b, g, r, a]`).
2. Replace it with something like `term::session::render(&TerminalSnapshot, &mut Canvas, bounds: Rect)` that walks the snapshot and calls `Canvas::draw_text` + `Canvas::fill_rect` for the cursor block.
3. Update `crates/term/src/lib.rs` to stop re-exporting `rasterize_snapshot`, `rasterize_snapshot_with_palette`, `Palette`, `colors`, `PADDING`, `BYTES_PER_PIXEL`.
4. Hunt down every caller of those re-exports. Known touch points: `crates/integration-tests/tests/two_term_windows.rs`, possibly `crates/integration-tests/tests/term_over_display_server.rs`, and any TS counterparts under `web/src/shared/rasterizer.ts`. Grep before starting — I may have missed some.
5. Fix `two_term_windows.rs::TERM_BG_BGRA` → RGBA order. Drop the "byte-order mismatch" comment block. The test should pin clean RGBA behaviour now.
6. Update the **Known Deviations** block in `specs/001-browser-os-v1/tasks.md` to close the BGRA rasterizer entry (item 4 currently: "term crate + TS preview slice"). Note the term crate is now partially reconciled — the rasterizer half is done, the Session-over-IPC half still waits on T110. Preserve the half that's still a deviation.
7. Verify `crates/term/src/session.rs` (497 lines, the protocol-level term client) doesn't need changes. Likely it doesn't — it's the wire-protocol side, not the paint side — but grep for `rasterize_snapshot` there before assuming.
8. Regression check: every existing term test (`crates/term/tests/rasterizer.rs`, `crates/term/tests/terminal.rs`) must either continue to pass or be rewritten in place. `rasterizer.rs` test will almost certainly need deletion or rewrite against the new `render` entry point.

**Open design question for the migration slice** (not yet decided): whether `term` should grow a widget-like entry point (a `toolkit::widget::Term` struct, or a `term::Session::draw(&mut Canvas, Rect)` method) so it composes with `WindowFrame::content_rect()` more naturally than a freestanding `render()` function + manual caller-side plumbing. Decide during the slice based on what the callers actually want.

**Performance note** (Principle IX): term rendering is on the cold-boot + input-latency critical path. The migration shouldn't regress either budget. The new Canvas path should be at least as fast as the old rasterizer (same inner loop, same font lookups, no extra allocations per frame). Spot-check with a microbenchmark if the structure ends up meaningfully different.
