This slice lifts the REPL loop into the library at `crates/sh/src/run.rs` so the binary's behaviour is testable in-process against `Cursor<Vec<u8>>` stdin + `Vec<u8>` stdout/stderr buffers. Public API: `pub fn run<R: BufRead, W: Write, E: Write>(stdin, stdout, stderr) -> ExitStatus` with `ExitStatus::{Eof, Exit(i32), IoError}` (and `ExitStatus::code() -> i32` translating the outcome into the process-level exit code the userland binary hands to `__wasi_proc_exit`). The loop prints `"$ "`, flushes, reads one newline-terminated line via `BufRead::read_line`, strips a trailing `\n`/`\r`, splits on whitespace (`str::split_whitespace` — no quoting, no escaping, no variable expansion; those land in T142), and dispatches the first token against the minimal four-builtin set in `dispatch_builtin`: `echo` joins remaining args with single spaces + newline to stdout; `exit [code]` returns `ExitStatus::Exit(code)` (default 0), with a strict `i32::from_str` parse that writes `sh: exit: <arg>: numeric argument required` to stderr and returns `Exit(2)` on non-numeric input (matches bash/dash's behaviour, deliberately stricter than the existing `Shell::eval` path which defaults garbage to 0 — the new surface is the userland-binary one, the library's `Shell::eval` semantic stays unchanged so `integration-tests` + `term` + the kernel's T077 gate keep their assertions green); `cd [path]` defaults to `/` when no arg, joins relative paths against a local `PathBuf` cwd tracked inside `run`, normalises `.`/`..`/repeated `/` via `std::path::Component`, and makes a best-effort `std::env::set_current_dir` call (wasip1 likely no-ops, so the local `PathBuf` is the source of truth for `pwd`); `pwd` writes the local cwd + newline to stdout. Unknown commands return `BuiltinOutcome::NotBuiltin` and the REPL writes `sh: command not found: <token>` to stderr and continues. EOF on stdin → `ExitStatus::Eof` (exit code 0); any stdio I/O error → `ExitStatus::IoError` (exit code 1). Blank lines are a no-op that just re-prints the prompt. `crates/sh/src/main.rs` collapses to a thin wrapper: locks `io::{stdin,stdout,stderr}`, wraps stdin in `BufReader`, calls `sh::run`, translates the returned `ExitStatus::code()` (i32) into a `process::ExitCode` (u8 via `rem_euclid(256)` clamp since the process-level exit surface is one byte). A default-on `std` feature in `crates/sh/Cargo.toml` gates the `run` module + the `ExitStatus` re-export; the existing `no_std + alloc` embedding path (the tokenizer + `Shell::eval` state machine consumed by `term` and `integration-tests`) stays available by setting `default-features = false` on the `sh` dep. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. Test coverage at `crates/sh/tests/shell.rs` (+11 new `run`-facing tests on top of the existing 26 `Shell::eval` tests — shell-tests binary 26 → 37, crate total 39 → 50, workspace total 1242 → 1253): `run_eof_exits_clean` (empty stdin; `run` returns `ExitStatus::Eof`; stdout is just the single prompt the loop emits before `read_line` hits EOF; stderr empty), `run_echo_writes_args_with_spaces_and_newline` (stdin `"echo hello world\n"`; `Eof` after the echo line; stdout contains `"hello world\n"`), `run_echo_with_no_args_emits_just_newline` (stdin `"echo\n"`; stdout is the exact sequence `"$ \n$ "` — prompt + bare newline + second prompt before EOF), `run_exit_zero_returns_exit_status` (stdin `"exit\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(0)`), `run_exit_with_code_returns_that_code` (stdin `"exit 42\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(42)`), `run_exit_with_invalid_code_returns_exit_two` (stdin `"exit notanumber\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(2)`, stderr contains `"numeric argument required"` + `"notanumber"`), `run_unknown_command_writes_to_stderr_and_continues` (stdin `"foo\nexit\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(0)`; stderr contains `"sh: command not found: foo"` — shell survived the unknown command and ran the next line), `run_pwd_writes_current_dir` (stdin `"pwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; strip the leading prompt and assert the first line-terminated chunk is an absolute path starting with `/`), `run_cd_changes_current_dir_and_affects_pwd` (stdin `"cd /tmp\npwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout contains `/tmp\n` — note: uses `contains("/tmp\n")` rather than a line-splitting assertion because the prompt never emits a newline so `stdout.lines()` yields prompt+output concatenations rather than clean lines), `run_blank_line_reprints_prompt` (stdin `"\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout is exactly `"$ $ "` — two prompts before the exit, no prompt after exit returns), `run_cd_with_no_args_returns_to_root_and_pwd_reflects_it` (stdin `"cd /tmp\ncd\npwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout contains `"/\n"` — pwd-after-reset shows root). Each test drives `run(BufReader::new(Cursor::new(input)), &mut stdout_vec, &mut stderr_vec)` and asserts against the exact `"$ "`-prompt-interleaved byte stream. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the `run` module consumes only `std::io::{BufRead, Write}` + `std::path::PathBuf` + the crate's own `shell` / `tokenize` modules and could be swapped for an alternative REPL shape (e.g. a line-editor with history) without touching either the library state machine or the binary wrapper; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — the shell uses `std::env::set_current_dir` + `std::path` which route through WASI preview 1's `fd_prestat_get`/`path_*` syscalls if wasip1 supports them, or no-op on the local cwd otherwise; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the REPL loop is now isolation-testable in a strictly stronger way than the existing `Shell::eval` tests or the T077 kernel gate (those can't exercise the I/O-error path or the strict `exit` arg parsing, both of which are `run`-layer behaviour). Deviation introduced: strict `exit` arg parsing diverges from `Shell::eval`'s silent-default-to-0 path — the new behaviour matches POSIX shells and is exposed only via the `run` surface; the `Shell::eval` surface stays unchanged so `integration-tests/shell_over_kernel.rs` + `term::Terminal` keep their existing assertions green. Deviation closed: T123's "not started" flag — the minimal `/usr/bin/sh` now lands with echo/exit/cd/pwd + the REPL loop in library shape. `dist/assets/bin/sh.wasm` rebuilds to 49 KB from `just build`. **Architectural milestone**: first userland binary with its REPL loop lifted into the library layer, so behaviour is exercisable in-process against a byte-stream mock rather than through the kernel's fd surface. The pattern (library `run()` entry point + thin `main.rs` wrapper + `ExitStatus` outcome enum) is directly applicable to every bundled app in Phase 3+ that has a binary-level event loop — `/usr/bin/term` (T124) will call `run_terminal(app, spawn_child_fn)` from its own `lib.rs`, `/usr/bin/files` + `/usr/bin/edit` + `/usr/bin/settings` + `/usr/bin/sysmon` will follow. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T124 minimal `/usr/bin/term` — toolkit window + scrollback area + input line + `proc_spawn(/usr/bin/sh)` child + pipe its stdin/stdout through the Terminal's input line + scrollback (blocked on `proc_spawn` from userland, which needs an `abi::ext::proc_spawn` pmos_ext syscall in the WASI shim — see the init crate's existing `extern "C" { fn proc_spawn(...) }` import pattern); (b) T142 pipes/redirection in the sh binary — promote `run` to handle `|`, `<`, `>`, `>>` tokens, thread a `Pipeline` builder through the dispatcher, route each stage's fd 0/1/2 through an `ipc_pipe` syscall pair; (c) T146 coreutils bridge — wire `proc_spawn` into the `NotBuiltin` arm of `dispatch_builtin` so unknown-command tokens spawn a child from `/bin/<token>` or `/usr/bin/<token>` via path resolution; (d) T122 launcher content source (desktop-shell) — parse `/usr/share/applications/*.desktop` into an in-memory `Vec<LauncherEntry>` at startup + re-poll every 5 s; (e) T130 taskbar surface in the desktop shell — a second `Window` anchored to the bottom edge of the display (blocked on a display-proto surface-anchoring extension).

After the **T106 keymap loader (PMKM binary format) slice**: workspace **1791 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1275 → 1292 +17, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 5513c11. First display-server slice in Phase 3; adds a self-contained keymap data model + parser with no changes to the main loop, wire dispatch, or client-facing protocol. New module `crates/display-server/src/protocol/keymap.rs` (250 lines) + asset `crates/display-server/assets/keymaps/us-qwerty.bin` (613 bytes, 55 entries). **Binary format** (PMKM v1): 4-byte magic `b"PMKM"`, 2-byte version LE (=1), 2-byte entry-count LE, then N×11-byte entries — 1-byte scancode + 4-byte unshifted UTF-32 LE + 4-byte shifted UTF-32 LE + 2-byte modifier-mask LE (v1: always 0). **Scancode enum** mirrors USB HID ids: alphabet 0x04–0x1D, digits 0x1E–0x27, Enter/Backspace/Tab/Space 0x28–0x2C, 11 punctuation pairs 0x2D–0x38, four modifier keys 0xE0–0xE5. **Public API**: `Scancode` (55 variants, `TryFrom<u8>`), `KeymapEntry` (`codepoint_unshifted`, `codepoint_shifted`, `modifier_mask`), `Keymap` (`BTreeMap<Scancode,KeymapEntry>`; `get`, `len`, `is_empty`), `KeymapError` (WrongMagic/UnsupportedVersion/Truncated/UnknownScancode(u8)), `parse(&[u8]) -> Result<Keymap,KeymapError>`, `load_default() -> Keymap` (panics only if the embedded asset is corrupt — caught by CI tests). Module wired at `display_server::protocol::keymap` via new `src/protocol/mod.rs` + `pub mod protocol` in `src/lib.rs`. No other file in the display-server crate was touched. **Test coverage** (17 tests in `crates/display-server/tests/keymap.rs`): 9 happy-path (parses_default_keymap_without_error; letter a on KeyA; shifted A; digit 1 on Digit1; shifted 1 → '!'; space; Enter → '\r'; modifier-keys-have-zero-codepoints; zero-modifier-mask-in-default; roundtrip-manually-constructed-keymap) + 8 error-path (WrongMagic; empty-input; truncated-at-magic; truncated-after-count; UnsupportedVersion; UnknownScancode(0xFF); zero-entries-is-ok). Asset produced by a one-shot standalone Rust helper (not a build.rs) and committed as a static file — no build-time code generation in the crate. **Explicitly deferred**: `pmd_keymap_manager` protocol global + `list_keymaps`/`set_keymap` request handlers (T189); broadcast of `pmd_keyboard.keymap(fd, size)` to bound clients (T189); additional keymap layouts UK/Dvorak (T188); join with the input driver's scancode→PMos mapping (happens outside this crate). Constitution check PASS I–X; Principle IX neutral — O(N) parse at startup only, no per-event cost. Next slice candidates: T189 `pmd_keymap_manager` global (can now be built on top of this loader); T122 launcher content source; T096 init.conf (if not yet landed).

After the **T117 flex batch layout slice** (Option A batch `row_with_grow` / `col_with_grow`): workspace **1808 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1292 → 1309 +17, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit c1fc76f. Toolkit layout module gains a batch API on top of the existing 178-line `Row`/`Column` cursor structs: new `pub enum Item { Fixed(u32), Grow(u32) }` + `pub fn row_with_grow(parent, padding, spacing, items: &[Item], height) -> Vec<Rect>` + `pub fn col_with_grow(parent, padding, spacing, items: &[Item], width) -> Vec<Rect>`. Grow items split the leftover interior (after Fixed sizes + N-1 gaps) proportional to weight; weight 0 treated as 1; saturating u32 arithmetic; no unsafe, no panics, no allocations beyond the returned Vec. No shrink / min-max in v1. Cursor API (`Row::new`/`Row::next`/`Column::new`/`Column::next`) is untouched. 17 new isolation tests at `crates/toolkit/tests/layout.rs` (total 34) mirror Row + Column coverage of: single-Grow claims all leftover, two equal Grow split evenly, mixed Fixed+Grow, unequal weights (1:2), zero leftover → zero-size Grow, spacing deducted before split, cross-axis centring preserved, empty items returns empty vec, weight-0 treated as 1. Also corrects the stale "1-line stub" note in `tasks.md` T117 line to describe the actual pre-slice state + the post-slice addition. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel, KernelError, or Cap changes; no new errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed.

After the **T183 preferences crate slice** (parallel dispatch agent A): workspace **1825 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1309 → 1319 +10, TS-unit 506 → 506, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 0686316. Ships `crates/preferences/` — a shared `#![no_std] + alloc` library with zero external crate deps, consumed ahead of the US9 settings app (T184) and init's preference read-at-spawn env-var whitelist (T190). `Preferences` struct holds six `Option<String>` fields (`theme_name`, `theme_fit`, `wallpaper_name`, `keyboard_layout`, `timezone_iana`, `terminal_font`); `Preferences::empty()` is `const` so init can stash it in a `static` on the fallback path; `Preferences::parse(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Preferences, PreferencesError>` is the only constructor. Schema mirrors `/etc/preferences.toml` in `contracts/init-conf.md §3.6`: flat `[theme]` / `[wallpaper]` / `[keyboard]` / `[timezone]` / `[terminal]` sections with a single `name` / `fit` / `layout` / `iana` / `font` key each. Parser is hand-rolled line-oriented — skip blank lines + `#` comments, parse `[section]` headers (unknown sections enter a silent "skip this section" mode, matching the forward-compat convention `crates/shell/src/launcher.rs` uses for `.desktop` files), then `key = "value"` with unknown keys inside known sections silently dropped. `PreferencesError` variants: `InvalidUtf8`, `MalformedLine(u32)` (1-based line number), `MalformedSection(u32)` — three so section-header errors surface distinctly from generic line errors. Deliberate no-TOML-crate trade-off — the schema is flat string-valued, and `toml` / `toml_edit` / serde would drag `indexmap` + `serde` into the kernel's `no_std` universe; the 150-line hand-rolled parser pays its way by keeping the dependency tree flat. Test coverage at `crates/preferences/tests/preferences.rs` (+10 tests): empty-bytes → all-None; full-config → all six fields; unknown-key inside known section silently ignored; unknown-section silently ignored; partial-config → only present fields; malformed-line errors with line number; plus bonus cases for comments, blank lines, invalid UTF-8, malformed section header, key-outside-any-section. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / toolkit / shell changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — preferences is a pure byte-in data-out library with no display-protocol dependency and will live happily behind whatever settings-app or init consumer lands next; Principle VIII (bottom-up) strengthens — the library lands before its T184 settings-app + T190 init-env-whitelist consumers, so those slices can depend on a stable `Preferences::parse` signature on landing day. **Architectural milestone**: first shared utility library in the workspace with an explicitly no_std-first dependency discipline — the trade-off against a mature TOML crate is the precedent future `no_std` shared crates will follow. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T184 settings app main UI — consume `Preferences::parse` in the `crates/settings` crate, compose the six-tab UI on top of the T114–T119 toolkit stack; (b) T190 timezone-picker wiring — add an `fs_open` + `fd_read` pipeline in init to read `/etc/preferences.toml` at spawn time and apply `TZ` to the child's envp per `contracts/init-conf.md §3.6` (blocked on T071's `fd_read`/`path_open` handler arms if init is the first no-std consumer); (c) T187 theme watcher — a `toolkit::watch_theme(callback)` helper that calls `fs_watch("/etc/preferences.toml", FS_WATCH_COALESCE_MODIFY)` + re-parses on each change event (blocked on T072's `fs_watch` opcode 0x1402); (d) T183 follow-up — a `Preferences::to_toml(&self) -> String` serialiser for the settings-app's apply path, so the app has a single serialisation path rather than hand-formatting.

After the **T079 driver common module slice** (parallel dispatch agent B): workspace **1839 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1319 → 1319, TS-unit 506 → 518 +12, Playwright 2 → 2). After the **T220 input-latency perf harness slice** (parallel dispatch agent D, round 2): workspace **1843 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1319 → 1323 +4, TS-unit 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 1432f1b. Replaces the 2a15925 placeholder binary at `crates/integration-tests/src/bin/input-latency.rs` with a real native-Rust measurement harness, wiring the SC-003 / Principle IX input-to-pixel p95 budget into a per-commit executable gate. **Methodology**: each iteration pushes an `InputEvent { kind, code, ts_us }` into an in-process `VecDeque`, pops it, derives a 64×64 ARGB8888 tile (4 KiB) from the event fields, writes the tile to `io::sink()`; `Instant::now()` brackets the whole cycle so allocation + write-call cost stay in the measurement. This is a **deliberately preliminary proxy** for the real input-to-pixel path — the top-of-file doc comment enumerates every cross-process / browser / worker hop the native binary omits, so future readers know the scope honestly. The percentile logic factors out as `pub fn percentile_us(samples: &[u64], pct: u32) -> u64` in `crates/integration-tests/src/lib.rs` (uses the sorted-index formula `samples[(N * pct) / 100]`, consistent with the task text's explicit formula). **CLI**: `--iterations N` (default 1000; non-numeric arg → exit 2 with usage error). **Output**: four JSON-per-line metric records on stdout (`input_to_pixel_p50_us`, `input_to_pixel_p95_us`, `input_to_pixel_p99_us`, `input_to_pixel_mean_us`), each with `{ value, budget: 100000, iterations: N, pass: true|false }`; exits 0 if p95 ≤ 100,000 µs, else exit 1. Smoke-measured p95: 2 µs at `--iterations 1000` (release), 4 µs at `--iterations 100` (debug) — both orders of magnitude under the 100 ms budget, as expected since the native proxy omits every full-stack hop. Test delta: +4 tests. `percentile_us_matches_documented_formula` (lib unit) covers the formula in isolation; `percentile_computation_is_correct` (in `crates/integration-tests/tests/input_latency.rs`) asserts the formula output for `1..=100` — the documented expected values (p50=50, p95=95, p99=99) and the formula output (51, 96, 100) disagreed by ±1; the test follows the formula (authoritative for the binary's pass/fail contract) with a comment block explaining the reconciliation; `binary_runs_and_exits_zero_under_budget` drives `Command::new(CARGO_BIN_EXE_input-latency).args(["--iterations", "100"])` and asserts exit 0 + the four JSON metric lines on stdout; `binary_rejects_bad_iterations_arg` asserts bad arg exits 2. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle IX **strengthens materially** — the workspace now has an executable input-to-pixel p95 gate for the first time, even if the proxy is preliminary. Deferred: promoting to a full-stack Playwright-based measurement (boot system, open 6 apps, inject 300 events, measure to next `present_complete`) — blocked on T126/T127 (boot-to-desktop) + the bundled apps; the full promotion consumes the same `percentile_us` helper from lib.rs so the Playwright slice is additive, not replacement. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `just test-perf` target in the Justfile — runs the native harness + reports metrics, wires into `just test`; (b) `integration-tests/src/proxy.rs` — extract the synthetic cycle into a reusable helper a later Playwright slice can call from Node; (c) once T126 lands, a narrow Playwright slice that injects one real input event and measures to first-pixel via `Performance.now()`, threaded through the same JSON output format.

After the **T146 cat coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent C, round 2): workspace **1848 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1323 → 1328 +5, TS-unit 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 2655551. First userland coreutil lands — new `crates/coreutils` workspace member with a single `[[bin]] name = "cat" path = "src/bin/cat.rs"` binary (46 lines, std-only, zero external crate deps). `src/lib.rs` is empty-ish, reserved for helpers that later coreutils (`ls`, `grep`, `mkdir`, `rm`, `cp`, `mv`) will share when they land as separate future slices on top of this crate's scaffolding. **POSIX cat behaviour choices**: no-arg invocation streams stdin to stdout via `io::stdin().lock()` → `io::stdout().lock()`; each path arg is read with `std::fs::read(path)` and written to stdout in order; a failed open/read writes `cat: <path>: <err>\n` to stderr, sets a had-error flag, and **does NOT abort the remaining files** — matching the GNU/BSD cat contract users rely on for `cat a missing b` style invocations. Exit 0 on full success and 1 if any file failed to open/read. Test coverage at `crates/coreutils/tests/cat.rs` (+5, exceeding the brief's +4): `cat_single_file_writes_content_to_stdout`, `cat_two_files_concatenates`, `cat_stdin_with_no_args_echoes`, `cat_missing_file_exits_one_and_stderr_has_path`, plus a bonus `cat_partial_success_continues_after_missing_file_middle` that locks down the POSIX "keep going after bad file" behaviour end-to-end. Tests drive the binary via `std::process::Command` + `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_cat")`; temp dirs use `std::env::temp_dir()` + a per-test counter so parallel-run collisions don't fire. **Xtask integration**: one new `("coreutils", "cat")` tuple in `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs`'s `USERLAND_BINS` array so `just build` copies `target/wasm32-wasip1/release/cat.wasm` → `dist/assets/bin/cat.wasm`. The bin's dash-free name means cargo outputs the file as `cat.wasm` directly, matching the `hello-std` pattern in the same list — no name-mangling special-case needed. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — cat is a pure userland WASI preview 1 consumer with no toolkit or display-protocol dependency; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) strengthens — cat exercises the full `fd_read` / `fd_write` / `path_open` handler arms that T071's partial landing wired, giving the first end-to-end userland test of that syscall triad driven from a shipped bundled binary; Principle VIII (bottom-up construction) strengthens — the `crates/coreutils` crate scaffold is now in place so the six deferred coreutils each become one-slice-per-binary additions on top. **Architectural milestone**: (a) first bundled coreutil in the workspace (cat.wasm now ships in `dist/assets/bin/`); (b) round 2 validates the Round 1 "disjoint-directory parallel dispatch" heuristic a second time — T146 (new Rust crate) + T220 (existing Rust crate extension) landed concurrently on the same main without conflict, with the slight wrinkle that agent D's worktree committed directly onto main while agent C's went through a cherry-pick (net effect: same final history, resolved transparently). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T146 `ls` coreutil — same crate, new `[[bin]] name = "ls"` (blocked on `fd_readdir` opcode in T071); (b) T146 `grep` coreutil — no readdir needed, just `path_open` + line-oriented scan of each file arg; (c) T146 `mkdir` / `rm` / `cp` / `mv` — each needs its respective WASI opcode arm (`path_create_directory`, `path_unlink_file`, `path_open` + `fd_write`, `path_rename`) to be wired in T071's residual scope; (d) `just test-perf` Justfile target that runs the T220 native harness + reports metrics (completes T220's "wire into CI gate" scope without waiting on Playwright).

After the **T209 deploy-github-pages.md slice** (parallel dispatch agent E, round 3): workspace **1848 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1328 → 1328, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 9fa2d4e. Ships a new 225-line deployment recipe at `docs/deploy-github-pages.md` covering GitHub Pages as a PMos host via a Cloudflare Worker COOP/COEP pre-pend pattern — the first of the four scheduled deployment docs (T209 GH Pages, T210 S3+CloudFront, T211 apps tutorial, T212 debugging guide). The doc is a step-by-step: push `dist/` to `gh-pages` branch, CNAME the zone to `<user>.github.io`, deploy a Worker that re-fetches each response and force-sets `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` + `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp` (preserving upstream headers + streaming body), add the route, verify `window.crossOriginIsolated === true`. The Worker ships as a complete ES-module-syntax snippet (`export default { async fetch(request, env, ctx) { ... } }`) rather than the deprecated `addEventListener("fetch", ...)` form — modern Workers compatibility_date ≥ 2023-10-01 requires the module form, so that's the version that ages well. Troubleshooting covers the three most likely failure modes: `crossOriginIsolated === false` (almost always a third-party sub-resource lacking `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin` or an embedder opt-in), `gh-pages` 404s on deep links (missing `.nojekyll` or absent `index.html`), and Worker timeout/error (stale `compatibility_date` in `wrangler.toml`). Cross-links `.specify/memory/constitution.md` Principle IV (offline-first + persistent) as the upstream constraint driving the COOP/COEP requirement — PMos's kernel syscall transport depends on `SharedArrayBuffer` + `Atomics.wait`, both of which only unlock when `crossOriginIsolated === true`. No kernel / abi / syscall / wire-format / crate additions; docs-only; Constitution check PASS on all ten with Principle IV strengthening (the deployment path for offline-first hosting is now runnably documented rather than tribal knowledge). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T210 — `docs/deploy-s3-cloudfront.md` with the response-header policy JSON (similar shape, different host); (b) T211 — `docs/apps.md` "Developing apps for PMos" tutorial cross-referencing `quickstart.md §5`/`§6`; (c) T212 — `docs/debugging.md` covering kernel-panic recovery + `/proc` + `/dev/console` + Playwright harness use; (d) T215 non-goal accessibility doc (touches CLAUDE.md, deferred).

After the **T146 partial — grep coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent G, round 3): workspace **1854 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1328 → 1334 +6, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit f667018. Second binary in `crates/coreutils` (scaffold landed by T146-cat in 2655551). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/grep.rs` (82 lines, std-only) implements a minimal POSIX-ish fixed-string `grep <pattern> [file ...]`: no regex, no flags (`-E` / `-F` / `-i` / `-n` / `-v` all deferred to later slices — v1 is strictly fixed-string); reads stdin when no file args; prefixes matches with `<path>:` when multiple files are passed (standard POSIX multi-file disambiguation); per-file open errors go to stderr + continue with remaining files (same partial-success convention T146-cat uses). **Exit codes** match POSIX grep: 0 if at least one match AND no open errors; 1 if zero matches AND no open errors; 2 on open error / missing pattern arg / usage error. Streams input via `BufRead::split(b'\n')` — no full-file buffering, so grep over a large file stays O(1) in memory regardless of file size. Test coverage at `crates/coreutils/tests/grep.rs` (+6): `matches_single_line_in_single_file`, `no_match_exits_one`, `stdin_mode_when_no_files`, `multiple_files_prefix_filename`, `missing_file_continues_and_exits_two`, `missing_pattern_arg_exits_two`. Tests drive the binary via `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_grep"))`; temp files use `std::env::temp_dir()` + per-test counter for parallel-run safety, matching T146-cat's idiom. **Xtask wiring**: `("coreutils", "grep")` tuple added to `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` adjacent to cat's entry — `just build` now ships `dist/assets/bin/grep.wasm` alongside `cat.wasm`. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) strengthens — grep exercises `path_open` + `fd_read` + `fd_write` from a shipped userland binary, giving a second end-to-end proof point of the T071 partial handler arms (cat was the first); Principle VIII (bottom-up construction) strengthens — second coreutil lands on top of T146-cat's crate scaffold with zero scaffolding additions, validating the "one bin per slice" scaling heuristic the partial-cat slice predicted. **Architectural milestone**: second coreutil in the workspace (grep.wasm now in `dist/assets/bin/`). The cat → grep progression is the intended template for the remaining five coreutils: each future slice (ls / mkdir / rm / cp / mv) adds one `[[bin]]` entry + one `src/bin/*.rs` + tests + an xtask tuple, with ls blocked on `fd_readdir` (T071 residual) and mkdir / rm / cp / mv each waiting on their own T071 opcode arm. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T146 `mkdir` — needs `path_create_directory` opcode wired in T071's residual scope; (b) T146 `rm` — needs `path_unlink_file`; (c) T146 `cp` — composes `path_open` + `fd_read` + `path_open` + `fd_write`, so unblocked today (similar shape to cat + grep); (d) T146 `mv` — `path_rename`, blocked on T071; (e) continue docs runway: T210 `deploy-s3-cloudfront.md`, T211 `apps.md`, T212 `debugging.md`.

After the **T184 partial — settings CLI slice** (parallel dispatch agent F, round 3): workspace **1858 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1334 → 1338 +4, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 1b22510. First slice against the US9 settings-app block and the first userland consumer of the `preferences` crate landed by T183 in `0686316`. `crates/settings/src/main.rs` collapses from its stub into a 47-line CLI binary: takes an optional single positional arg (path to preferences.toml; default `/etc/preferences.toml`), reads via `std::fs::read`, parses via `preferences::Preferences::parse`, and prints six `key = "value"` (or `(unset)`) lines with `{:<15}` padding so the `=` column aligns. Present fields render as `"<quoted>"`; absent fields as `(unset)` (no quotes). **Error shaping**: IO failures write `settings: failed to open <path>: <error>` to stderr + exit 1; parse failures write `settings: failed to parse <path>: <debug-error>` + exit 1. **Empty/comment-only configs parse cleanly** (all six fields `(unset)`, exit 0) — this is the preferences crate's forward-compat contract surfacing through settings: a blank file is a valid empty config, not an error state. This slice exists to (a) prove the preferences crate is consumable from a shipped userland binary, (b) give the project a debugging tool for inspecting the live `/etc/preferences.toml` without a graphical tab-based UI, and (c) establish the CLI-first-then-GUI partial convention already used by T121 / T123 / T146-cat / T146-grep. **Dependency wiring**: `crates/settings/Cargo.toml` adds `preferences = { path = "../preferences" }` to `[dependencies]`; `abi` + `toolkit` deps stay in place so the follow-up GUI slice doesn't have to re-add them. Test coverage at `crates/settings/tests/cli.rs` (+4 tests): `reads_full_config_and_prints_all_six_fields` (temp file with all six keys → stdout contains each quoted value verbatim, exit 0), `empty_config_prints_six_unset_lines` (temp file with just `# comment only\n` → stdout has exactly six newline-terminated `(unset)` lines, exit 0), `missing_file_exits_one_and_stderr_has_path` (nonexistent path → exit 1, stderr contains the path), `malformed_toml_exits_one_and_stderr_has_error_class` (temp file with `[unterminated\n...` → exit 1, stderr contains `failed to parse` + the `MalformedSection` variant name). Tests drive the compiled binary via `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_settings"))`; temp files use `env::temp_dir()` + per-test atomic counter + pid suffix so parallel cargo test runs don't collide. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / toolkit changes. After the **T210 deploy-s3-cloudfront.md slice** (parallel dispatch agent I, round 4): workspace **1858 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1338 → 1338, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 9cba289. Second of the four scheduled deployment docs (T209 GH Pages ✓, T210 S3+CloudFront ✓, T211 apps tutorial, T212 debugging guide). Mirrors T209's shape for AWS: 269-line recipe at `docs/deploy-s3-cloudfront.md` walking through S3-as-origin + CloudFront-as-HTTPS-edge + COOP/COEP header injection via a CloudFront Response Headers Policy. The policy-over-Lambda@Edge choice is deliberate — `SecurityHeadersConfig`'s first-class `CrossOriginOpenerPolicy` + `CrossOriginEmbedderPolicy` blocks with `Override: true` are the managed path AWS introduced specifically for this COOP/COEP use case, and it requires no code to maintain (Lambda@Edge would need JS, a deployment pipeline, and per-request cold-start cost). The doc's 7-step deploy flow uses placeholder shell vars (`BUCKET_NAME` / `DISTRIBUTION_ID` / `POLICY_ID`) declared at the top of each code block so a reader can `export BUCKET_NAME=mypmos` once and paste the rest. Step 4's `coop-coep-policy.json` is a full, valid AWS policy (validated with `python3 -m json.tool`); step 5's `distribution-config.json` is a minimal skeleton with the `ResponseHeadersPolicyId` slot threaded through from step 4. Verification: `curl -sI https://<dist>.cloudfront.net/ | grep -E '^cross-origin-'` + DevTools `window.crossOriginIsolated`. Troubleshooting covers three AWS-specific gotchas: (a) `crossOriginIsolated === false` on deploy (same third-party CORP opt-in issue as T209); (b) CloudFront serves stale headers after a policy change (fix: `aws cloudfront create-invalidation --paths "/*"` + wait ~60 s); (c) S3 403 when using Origin Access Control (expected — direct bucket access is blocked by design; check the bucket policy allows the OAC principal). Intentional divergences from T209: added the `distribution-config.json` skeleton in step 5 since CloudFront needs more structure than a Worker; added the OAC troubleshooting item which has no Cloudflare-side analogue. Cross-links `.specify/memory/constitution.md` Principle IV. No kernel / crate changes; docs-only. Constitution check PASS on all ten with Principle IV strengthening — a second deployment path for offline-first hosting is now runnably documented, which reduces the operational risk if pricing / terms shift in a way that makes T209's path less attractive for a given deployer. After the **T146 partial — cp coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent H, round 4): workspace **1862 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1338 → 1342 +4, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 56904e9. Third binary in `crates/coreutils` (cat in 2655551, grep in f667018). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/cp.rs` (36 lines, std-only) implements minimal POSIX-ish single-file `cp <src> <dst>`: no `-r` / `-R` (directory copy deferred), no `-n` / `-i` / `-v` / `-p`; one-shot `std::fs::read(src)` → `std::fs::write(dst, bytes)`; POSIX overwrite default (dst is clobbered). **Error shape matches the cat/grep house style**: `cp: usage: cp <src> <dst>\n` on arg errors → exit 1; `cp: <path>: <error>\n` on per-file issues → exit 1. Memory is O(file-size) via one-shot read — acceptable for v1 since PMos files fit in memory until proven otherwise; a streaming version behind a feature flag would be the path if that changes. Test coverage at `crates/coreutils/tests/cp.rs` (+4): `copies_file_content_verbatim` (src has a null byte + a non-ASCII byte so the test catches a naive `read_to_string` regression; dst byte-equal), `overwrites_existing_dst` (dst pre-exists with different content; cp; dst now holds src content — POSIX default pinned), `missing_src_exits_one_and_stderr_has_path` (nonexistent src → exit 1, stderr contains `cp:` and the src path), `wrong_arg_count_exits_one_with_usage` (0-arg + 1-arg cases both → exit 1, stderr contains `usage` and `cp <src> <dst>`). Tests drive the binary via `Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_cp"))`; temp files use `env::temp_dir()` + per-test counter — same idiom cat and grep already use. **Xtask wiring**: `("coreutils", "cp")` tuple added to `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` adjacent to cat + grep — `just build` now ships `dist/assets/bin/cp.wasm` alongside `cat.wasm` + `grep.wasm`. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) strengthens — cp exercises `path_open` + `fd_read` + `fd_write` from a shipped userland binary, the **fourth** such end-to-end proof point this session (cat, grep, cp, and settings CLI via T184 partial); Principle VIII (bottom-up construction) strengthens — third coreutil lands on top of the T146-cat crate scaffold with zero scaffolding additions, validating that the "one-bin-per-slice" scaling heuristic holds beyond two iterations. **Architectural milestone**: three coreutils now in `dist/assets/bin/` (`cat.wasm`, `grep.wasm`, `cp.wasm`). The T146 partial strategy (one binary per slice, shared crate scaffolding, per-binary test file, per-binary xtask tuple) has now been validated three times and can reasonably be considered the standard template for any subsequent coreutil slice. Remaining T146 slices: **`ls`** — blocked on `fd_readdir` T071 opcode arm; **`mkdir`** — blocked on `path_create_directory`; **`rm`** — blocked on `path_unlink_file`; **`mv`** — blocked on `path_rename`. All four are waiting on T071 residual work. After the **T222 non-goal compliance audit slice** (parallel dispatch agent L, round 5): workspace **1862 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1342 → 1342, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit d95a2ae. Closes the `/speckit.analyze` U5 finding — the non-goal surface (FR-040 multi-user, FR-041 accounts, FR-043 GPU 3D, FR-044 raw TCP/IP, plus cloud-URL patterns) now has a deterministic, re-runnable audit gate. `scripts/non-goal-audit.sh` (92 lines, bash + `set -euo pipefail`, cwd-independent) greps the repo (excluding `target/`, `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `.git/`) for five pattern categories with one header per category: cloud-URL (`s3://` / `gs://` / `azure` / `supabase` / `firebase`), authentication keywords (`login` / `signup` / `oauth` / `jwt` / `session_token`), WebGL/WebGPU (`webgl` / `webgpu` / `GPUDevice`), raw TCP/IP (`net::TcpStream` / `net::TcpListener` / `net::UdpSocket`), multi-user APIs (`uid` / `gid` / `getpwnam` / `/etc/passwd` / `/etc/shadow`). Matches are case-insensitive, sorted for determinism (verified by double-run diff), and always printed with file:line:content so a reviewer can judge. Exit code is always 0 — the spec explicitly scopes this as a non-blocking v1 audit. **Baseline `docs/non-goal-compliance.md`** records every current match with a one-line justification: today the repo has **32 matches across 5 categories** — 4 cloud-URL + 8 auth-keyword + 7 WebGL/WebGPU + 3 TCP/IP + 10 multi-user. **Zero are real violations.** Each match is one of: (a) a T222 self-reference — this task's own description at `tasks.md:464` enumerates all forbidden patterns verbatim, producing a hit per pattern (expected, documented in the false-positive column); (b) a POSIX-compat stat field reserved at 1000 — `data-model.md`'s uid/gid placeholder for a future multi-user amendment; (c) spec-kit template boilerplate (`spec-template.md`, `speckit.git.feature.md`) — unavoidable upstream artefact, scope-excluded from PMos proper; (d) a legitimate spec affirmation of the non-goal — e.g. `CLAUDE.md:53`, `research.md:410/427`, `spec.md:808` all *state* that login/signup/etc. are non-goals, which the grep picks up as pattern hits. The markdown file has a summary table at the bottom tallying matches × false-positive × legitimate × TODO per category — TODO count is **0 across every category**, which is the Principle III / Principle I contract holding at audit-time. **Justfile wiring**: new `test-non-goal-audit` recipe runs the script + prints `[just] non-goal audit: <N> lines across <M> categories` as a one-line summary; wired into `just test` as a **non-blocking** dependency (runs but doesn't fail `just test` on any diff, per the task spec's v1-gate-shape). How to keep `docs/non-goal-compliance.md` current: run `bash scripts/non-goal-audit.sh > tmp.txt`, diff against the markdown's grep-output blocks, add a justification line for any new match. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle III (browser-only, zero backend) **strengthens materially** — the project now has an automated way to catch accidental cloud-URL / auth-keyword drift before it ships, which closes the "only humans review for this" risk the U5 finding named; Principle X (testability) strengthens — adds a re-runnable, deterministic gate to the CI surface that's independent of the cargo/vitest/Playwright test layers. **Architectural milestone**: last of the `/speckit.analyze`-derived follow-up tasks (U2 → T219 pending, U3 → T220 partial, U4 → T221 pending, U5 → T222 ✓ **this slice**) now has its audit shipped. With T222 landed, the only analyze-derived gap remaining is U1 (text lost; documented in `specs/001-browser-os-v1/analyze-findings.md`) + the three pending T-IDs (T219 / T220 full-stack / T221) — none of which block v1 docs or the non-goal contract. After the **T168 partial — /proc/<pid>/status slice** (parallel dispatch agent N, round 6): workspace **1873 passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1343 → 1353 +10, TS 518 → 518, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 876d855. First real content in `/proc/<pid>/` — closes the "procfs is framework-only" gap the existing module-level docs acknowledged. The slice turned out larger than a single-field landing because the existing procfs **had no per-pid subtree plumbing at all** (just top-level files like `/proc/version`); the agent had to build the per-pid lookup / readdir / read / stat dispatch path from scratch before the `status` field could hang off it. That scaffolding is the load-bearing addition; every future `/proc/<pid>/*` file (fd/, maps, cmdline, environ, cgroup, cwd) will compose against the same shape. **Public surface additions to `ProcFsSource`**: two defaulted methods `pid_status(pid: Pid) -> Option<ProcStatusSnapshot>` + `live_pids() -> Vec<Pid>`, plus companion types `ProcStatusSnapshot { name, state, pid, ppid }` + `ProcStatusState { Running, Sleeping, Zombie, Stopped }`. Defaulted so existing `ProcFsSource` impls (there's at least one in `tests/` + one in the live Kernel) compile unchanged; overriders opt in when they care. **Per-pid inode region**: `PID_DIR_BASE = 1_000_000 + pid * PID_STRIDE` with `PID_STRIDE = 16` — keeps top-level inos (root dir, `version`, `storage`, etc.) under 1M and gives each pid 15 stride slots for future files (currently only `status` at offset 1). Over-provisioned on purpose; 15 slots is more than the Linux `/proc/<pid>/` surface at entry density and the range is cheap. **Status bytes**: one `alloc::format!("Name:\t{name}\nState:\t{letter} ({human})\nPid:\t{pid}\nPPid:\t{ppid}\n")` — no per-field heap alloc, bounded time, matches Linux `/proc/<pid>/status` format so future downstream code (sysmon in T170, `ps`-like coreutils later) reads via standard line-oriented parse. `ProcState` → letter projection: `Running → R`, all `Starting` / `Ready` / `Blocked*` → `S` (a kernel internal-state distinction that userland doesn't care about), `Zombie` / `Dead` → `Z`, `Stopped` reserved as `T` for when job control lands. `StaticProcFsSource` (the test-only source) gained a `BTreeMap<Pid, ProcStatusSnapshot>` so isolation tests can seed canned process entries without spinning up a full Kernel + proc-table composition — this is the test-harness shape that unblocks T170 sysmon from needing a live kernel in its unit tests. **Test coverage** at `crates/kernel/tests/procfs.rs` (new file, +10 tests): 4 required (status_contains_name_for_spawned_process, status_reports_state_running_for_running_process, status_reports_pid_and_ppid_correctly, status_reports_state_zombie_after_proc_exit) + 6 bonus (lookup_returns_dir_for_live_pid, readdir_lists_status_under_pid_dir, lookup_rejects_nonexistent_pid, readdir_proc_lists_live_pids_as_dirs, inode_region_doesn_t_collide_with_top_level, stat_on_status_reports_regular_file_and_mode). No kernel-wide API signature changes (the new trait methods are defaulted); no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed from this slice; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle I (real OS, not a simulation) **strengthens** — `/proc/<pid>/` is textbook OS structure, and the inode region layout (fixed base + stride) is the Linux-family convention; Principle X (testability) strengthens materially — the `StaticProcFsSource + BTreeMap<Pid, ProcStatusSnapshot>` shape lets any procfs consumer (sysmon, coreutils) unit-test without a full kernel, which is the isolation-test-before-integration-test gate the constitution requires. Deferred remaining T168 scope: `VmSize` / `VmPeak` (need process-table memory tracking that isn't wired), `Threads` / `Uid` / `Gid` / signal masks (mostly not-relevant-for-v1 multi-user gaps), and the `/proc/<pid>/fd/` symlink directory (separate slice on top of this slice's per-pid subtree plumbing — the stride-16 ino region was sized precisely for this follow-up). **Architectural milestone**: first `/proc/<pid>/*` file in the workspace, unblocking the T170 sysmon app's data source. With T168 partial + T169 `/proc/storage` pending, sysmon's data feed has one of its two legs ready; the second leg (storage quota counters) is a parallel-safe follow-up slice. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T168 `/proc/<pid>/fd/` follow-up — add fd-symlink readdir under the same per-pid subtree; (b) T169 `/proc/storage` read — plugs into the block-driver's quota + used counters (T084 partial); (c) T170 sysmon app itself — can now consume `/proc/<pid>/status` via `StaticProcFsSource` in tests + real procfs in the boot path; (d) a "procfs roundtrip" integration scenario where a coreutil (e.g. a partial `ps` — future T146 slice) reads `/proc/<pid>/status` and formats one row per pid, demonstrating end-to-end userland consumption of the new procfs surface.

After the **T168 partial — /proc/<pid>/fd/ fd symlinks slice** (parallel dispatch agent V, round 9): workspace **1378 Rust passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1371 → 1378 +7). Commit 643c279 (direct to main). Closes the `/proc/<pid>/fd/` scope of T168; the remaining residuals are `VmSize` / `VmPeak` / `Threads` / `Uid` / `Gid` which all need process-table accounting that isn't wired. Extends `ProcFsSource` with a defaulted `pid_fds(pid) -> Vec<ProcFdSnapshot>` method and a new `ProcFdSnapshot { fd: u32, target: String }` companion type — same trait-seam + static-source pattern T168 main and T169 storage-info used. `StaticProcFsSource` gains a `pid_fds: BTreeMap<Pid, Vec<ProcFdSnapshot>>` field with a `set_pid_fds` chainable helper (matching `set_pid_status` shape). **VFS plumbing is the load-bearing bit**: `ProcFs::lookup` / `readdir` / `read` / `stat` / `readlink` all extended to resolve `/proc/<pid>/fd` (a directory at per-pid stride-offset 2, reserving offsets 3..15 for future `cmdline` / `maps` / `environ` / `cwd` / `cgroup`) and `/proc/<pid>/fd/<n>` (a symlink whose byte content is the target path). **New disjoint inode region** for fd symlinks: `FD_INO_BASE = 100_000_000`, `FD_PID_STRIDE = 1024`, `fd_symlink_ino(pid, fd) = FD_INO_BASE + pid*1024 + fd` — separate from the `PID_DIR_BASE = 1_000_000 + pid * 16` region because stride-16 can't fit arbitrary fd counts. `decode_pid_ino` caps at `FD_INO_BASE` so the two regions can never alias. Stat on fd symlinks reports `NodeType::SymLink` + mode `0o777` (Linux-family convention); read returns the raw target bytes; readlink works as the primary path. **One deviation**: the enum variant in the existing kernel code is `NodeType::SymLink` (camel case split), not `Symlink` — the brief said the latter; agent used the actual variant name. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs-top-level / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. After the **T146 partial — mkdir coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent X, round 9): workspace **1377 Rust passing, 0 failing** in X's worktree, integrated as 1399→1405 after cherry-pick. Fourth coreutil in `crates/coreutils/src/bin/mkdir.rs` (35 lines, std-only, zero deps) following the cat/grep/cp pattern: POSIX-ish single-path and multi-path mkdir with partial-success continuation. `mkdir <dir> [<dir> ...]` calls `std::fs::create_dir(path)` per path (which errors on missing parent or existing target — no `-p`, no `-i`, no `-m`, no `-v`); failures write `mkdir: <path>: <error>` to stderr, set a had-error flag, and continue with remaining paths; zero args writes `mkdir: usage: mkdir <dir>...` to stderr and exits 1. Exit 0 on full success, 1 if any path failed. After the **T146 partial — rm + mv coreutils + T169 KernelProcFsSource bridge slices** (parallel dispatch round 10, agents Y / Z / AA): workspace **1422 Rust passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1405 → 1422 +17, matching exactly Y=+6 rm, Z=+6 mv, AA=+5 bridge). Commits db55782 (rm, direct to main), 1b71859 (mv, cherry-pick with conflict resolution), 1f50924 (bridge). **T146 is now one slice away from complete** — only `ls` remains, blocked on T071 WASI `fd_readdir` residual. **Round-10 postmortem — two notable process events**: (1) the parallel rm + mv dispatch hit the expected Cargo.toml + assemble_dist.rs conflict on cherry-pick; integrator resolved by unioning both agents' additions in order (rm before mv in the `[[bin]]` list). (2) Mid-integration Bash cwd drifted into agent AA's worktree during a `git log main..worktree-...` probe, causing the next cherry-pick to land on AA's branch instead of main; integrator recovered by `cd /opt/webos` explicitly, which un-shadowed the main branch; the accidental commit (75e0f0d) lives on AA's orphan branch and dies with the worktree cleanup. A second wrinkle: the conflict-resolved cherry-pick initially committed with conflict markers still in `assemble_dist.rs` because the Edit tool required a Read-first handshake that hadn't happened yet; amended the commit (1b71859 replacing 4c6e8e1) once the Read+Edit completed. Lesson: always explicit-Read before editing a file the current session's chain of thought hasn't loaded, even when the conflict resolution is trivial. **T146 rm** (db55782, agent Y): `crates/coreutils/src/bin/rm.rs` (37 lines) calls `std::fs::remove_file` per path; partial-success on multi-path; directories are refused implicitly (remove_file errors on dir) rather than via a pre-metadata check — same exit/stderr shape as missing-file, consistent with mkdir/cp style. 6 integration tests cover single-file, multi-file, missing-file exit 1, directory refusal, partial-success, and zero-args usage. No `-r` / `-R` / `-f` / `-i` / `-v` — recursive removal raises an open design question (reuse `std::fs::remove_dir_all` vs. walking the tree for per-entry error reporting) that's deferred to a follow-up slice. **T146 mv** (1b71859, agent Z): `crates/coreutils/src/bin/mv.rs` (56 lines) calls `std::fs::rename` with a fallback arm on `ErrorKind::CrossesDevices` that does `fs::copy` + `fs::remove_file` manually; rolls forward on partial failure (partial move preferred to data loss, documented). Error shape: `mv: <src> -> <dst>: <error>`. 6 integration tests cover same-dir move, cross-dir move, dst overwrite, missing-src, missing-dst-parent, and wrong arg count (0/1/3 args). The cross-device fallback arm is hard to exercise on a single-mount tmpdir — caveat documented. No `-i` / `-f` / `-n` / `-v`. **T169 KernelProcFsSource** (1f50924, agent AA): `crates/kernel/src/fs/procfs.rs` gains `KernelProcFsSource<'a>` holding a `&'a ProcessTable` reference; impls `ProcFsSource` reading `pid_status` + `live_pids` from the live process table via its already-public API (`ProcessTable::get`, `::live_pids`, `::transition`, `::exit`, `::allocate_pid`, `::insert` — no API additions needed). `version` hardcodes `"PMos 0.1.0-alpha (native-test)\n"` (distinguishable from `StaticProcFsSource::default()`'s `"PMos 0.1.0 (native-test)\n"` so a test can tell the two sources apart); `uptime` / `meminfo` / `loadavg` stay as placeholder strings because they need clock + meminfo + scheduler hooks that aren't wired yet. `pid_fds` inherits the trait default (empty Vec) because the fd tables live inside `Kernel.fds` (private `BTreeMap<Pid, FdTable>`), not `ProcessTable` — threading them through would widen the bridge to `&Kernel` or add a getter, out of scope per the brief. `storage_info` stays `None`; the default `storage()` falls back to `"0 0 0\n"`. Tests at `crates/kernel/tests/procfs.rs` (+5): `kernel_procfs_source_pid_status_returns_spawned_process` (pid 42 with name "edit" is readable via bridge), `kernel_procfs_source_live_pids_lists_spawned` (ascending order), `kernel_procfs_source_pid_status_returns_none_for_missing_pid`, `kernel_procfs_source_state_reflects_process_state` (Running → Zombie transition visible), plus a bonus `kernel_procfs_source_version_uptime_meminfo_loadavg_are_placeholders` that pins every placeholder so a regression is visible. **Boot-path wiring is NOT in this slice** — the bridge exists and is unit-tested, but the actual `ProcFs::new(Box::new(KernelProcFsSource::new(&kernel.procs)))` wire-up at boot stays with StaticProcFsSource. That's the follow-up slice; the unit tests prove the bridge is correct; the integration wiring is a separate landing. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten for all three slices; Principle I (real OS) strengthens — `rm` / `mv` are textbook POSIX, and `KernelProcFsSource` makes `/proc` reflect live kernel state instead of canned data; Principle VIII (bottom-up) strengthens — the procfs data source layer is now demonstrably swappable between canned + live sources, unblocking the sysmon-GUI consumers; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the trait-seam pattern is now exercised by both StaticProcFsSource (canned) and KernelProcFsSource (live), proving the seam's design goal. One intermittent flake surfaced in agent AA's first workspace run (`proc_spawn_rolls_back_when_platform_refuses` in `tests/syscall.rs`) but passed in rerun and is unrelated to procfs; flag for future runs as a known intermittent. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T169 KernelProcFsSource boot-path wiring — replace `ProcFs::with_static()` at boot with the bridge (small slice, but touches the boot path); (b) T071 WASI `fd_readdir` — unblocks the last coreutil `ls`, closes T146 entirely; (c) T184 tabbed GUI — T116 primitives are complete now, the settings GUI can land a single-tab slice as a proof of concept; (d) T221 external-dev container validation (analyze-finding U4, still pending); (e) T170 sysmon-GUI — T116 Container + List are ready, needs the frame-callback runtime.

After the **round-11 triple slice — T071 fd_readdir seam + T184 set-theme CLI + T168 cmdline** (parallel dispatch agents AB / AC / AD): workspace **1442 Rust passing, 0 failing** (Rust 1422 → 1442 +20 = AB+4 + AC+6 + AD+10 exactly). Commits 08cab5b (AB), dabfedb (AC), 92a2d4a (AD). All three cherry-picked cleanly — no Cargo.toml or xtask collisions this round because each slice touched different crates (kernel/syscall, settings, kernel/fs). **AB — T071 partial (08cab5b)**: agent discovered `FD_READDIR` was already implemented end-to-end (opcode 0x002F in `abi::wasi`, the 24-byte `dirent` header contract in `abi::wasi::dirent`, `handle_fd_readdir` in `wasi.rs` with full cookie pagination and buf-cap truncation, 8 wire-layout tests in `tests/syscall.rs` all passing) but that `handle_fd_readdir` was reaching directly into `kernel.fds(pid)` + `kernel.vfs.readdir_ino(...)` — a layering inversion relative to the module-doc contract ("The `Kernel` method is always the semantic truth — these handlers are adapters, not logic"). Every other fd/path opcode routes through a `Kernel::*` semantic method; fd_readdir was the outlier. Agent added the missing `Kernel::fd_readdir(&mut self, pid, fd) -> Result<Vec<DirEntry>>` semantic seam in `sys.rs` with resolve-fd + directory-guard; refactored `handle_fd_readdir` in `wasi.rs` to call the seam and keep the wire-format serialisation where it already lived. 4 new semantic-layer tests in `tests/sys.rs` (happy path + BadFd + NotSupportedOnFd + Fs(NotADirectory)). **Scope deviation**: method signature is `(pid, fd)` not `(fd, cookie, buf_cap)` as the brief suggested — agent's reasoning (in commit body): cookie pagination and buffer-cap truncation are WASI wire concerns owned by the adapter, not the filesystem-agnostic kernel method. Solid architectural call. **AC — T184 partial (dabfedb)**: `crates/settings/src/main.rs` gains a third subcommand `set-theme <name> [--config <path>]` that reads, updates `theme.name`, and writes back the preferences TOML. Preferences-crate write-API choice: hand-serialise in the settings binary (option b in the brief). Rationale documented in commit: the `preferences` crate is `#![no_std]` and parse-only to keep init slim; adding a serde-style API would widen scope into init's `no_std + alloc` universe for no benefit since `settings` is the only writer in v1. The emitter is the inverse of the parser (canonical section order: theme / wallpaper / keyboard / timezone / terminal; empty sections skipped; plain-string quoting). Round-trip is test-validated: `set_theme_preserves_other_fields` writes then re-parses via `preferences::Preferences::parse` and asserts every non-theme field byte-for-byte equal. 6 integration tests (fresh-config write, other-field preservation, overwrite exactly-one occurrence, empty name, missing arg with `usage:`, garbage TOML failure). **Valid-theme allow-list deferred** to the eventual GUI; CLI accepts any non-empty string with quotes and newlines rejected so the TOML emitter cannot escape. **File mode 0o644 deferred** — host `std::fs::write` cannot portably set Unix permissions; kernel VFS defaults ignore host bits. **AD — T168 partial (92a2d4a)**: adds `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` at per-pid stride-offset 3 (slot 1 = status, 2 = fd, 3 = cmdline; 12 slots remain). `ProcFsSource::pid_cmdline(&self, pid) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` defaulted to `None`. `StaticProcFsSource.pid_cmdlines: BTreeMap<Pid, Vec<u8>>` with `set_pid_cmdline` chainable helper. Bytes are the Linux convention: `argv[0]\0argv[1]\0...\0argv[n]\0` (NUL-separated, trailing NUL, no newline). Mode 0o444 (read-only). `None` = FsError::NotFound on lookup; `Some(vec![])` = live pid with empty argv (file exists, reads as 0 bytes). **KernelProcFsSource wiring: WIRED, not deferred** — agent found `Process` already owns `pub argv: Vec<String>` from `Process::new_starting`, so the bridge projection needed only ~7 lines via a new `format_argv_cmdline` helper (well below the ~15-line budget). No widening of `Process` / `ProcessTable` needed. `pid_status_ino` promoted private → `pub const fn` for symmetry with the other slot helpers (needed by the collision test). 6 required + 4 bonus tests: lookup returns regular file, read returns NUL-separated bytes, stat mode 0o444, lookup rejects pid without cmdline, readdir lists cmdline alongside status/fd, ino disjointness, format-contract, empty-argv live-pid, kernel-bridge projection, kernel-bridge missing-pid. **Round-11 postmortem**: all three agents self-rebased their stale worktrees cleanly (base `2a15925` → local main); no Cargo.toml / xtask conflicts this round because each slice hit a different crate. No direct-to-main commits this round. Clean three-way cherry-pick. **Cumulative session delta since post-compact resume** (commits cbbebf6 through 92a2d4a): 14 feature commits + 5 docs commits integrated; Rust tests 1355 → 1442 (+87 net). T146 is at 6/7 with only `ls` remaining (now buildable on the AB fd_readdir adapter that already existed in wire form; the remaining blocker was exposing it through the coreutils crate, not the kernel). T116 widget primitives complete. T168 per-pid procfs has status + fd + cmdline in place (slots 1/2/3 of 16). T169 has the seam, the StaticSource, and the KernelBridge — only `/proc/storage` and `/proc/<pid>/fd` integration with the block driver's live counters remain. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T146 `ls` — now genuinely unblocked by the fd_readdir adapter; (b) T071 `clock_time_get` + `random_get` + `environ_get` + `args_get` — the remaining non-fd WASI ops; (c) T168 `/proc/<pid>/maps` + `/proc/<pid>/environ` at slots 4 and 5; (d) T184 additional setters (`set-wallpaper`, `set-keyboard`, `set-timezone`) mirroring set-theme; (e) T221 external-dev container validation (still pending U4); (f) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T144 export + env builtins slice**: workspace +6 in the sh-tests crate (shell-tests crate 50 → 56). Commit 4694a1d. First partial against the Phase 6 US4 T144 builtin block — `cd / pwd / echo / exit` already landed in T123 (f6bff57); this slice adds `export` + `env` to the same REPL `dispatch_builtin` and threads a caller-owned `BTreeMap<String, String>` env state through the loop the same way `cwd: &mut PathBuf` is. `BTreeMap` (not `HashMap`) is deliberate so `env` / `export` no-arg output is deterministic-sorted alphabetically by key — both for human readability and for byte-exact stdout assertions in tests. `env` with no args writes every `KEY=VALUE` entry on its own line to stdout in sort order; `env <anything>` writes `sh: env: too many arguments\n` to stderr and returns `Continue` (REPL stays alive — a future slice can extend with the full POSIX `env [-i] [NAME=VALUE]... [command]` shape without breaking the no-arg contract). `export NAME=VALUE` splits the arg at the first `=` and inserts `NAME → VALUE` (VALUE may be empty if the arg is `NAME=`); `export NAME` without `=` seeds `NAME → ""` if absent and is a no-op when present (POSIX-style "set the exported bit" doesn't apply — the v1 minimal shell has no exported/unexported distinction, so post-call `env` will list `NAME=` either way); `export` with zero args prints every entry as `export NAME=VALUE` lines sorted to stdout (bash convention); `export =foo` (or any zero-length name before `=`) writes `sh: export: <arg>: not a valid identifier\n` to stderr and continues with the remaining args (no full POSIX identifier-charset validation — empty-name is the only rejected shape). Multiple args (`export A=1 B=2`) apply each in order. New public API: `pub fn run_with_env<R: BufRead, W: Write, E: Write>(stdin, stdout, stderr, env: &mut BTreeMap<String, String>) -> ExitStatus` re-exported as `sh::run_with_env` — the test entry point that lets a caller pre-seed env entries and observe mutations after the loop returns. `run` stays the binary-facing thin wrapper that constructs an empty map and delegates to `run_with_env`, so the userland `sh` binary's surface is unchanged. The `dispatch_builtin` signature gains an `env: &mut BTreeMap<String, String>` parameter between `cwd` and `stdout` — `pub(crate)` visibility so no external caller's signature breaks. Test coverage at `crates/sh/tests/env.rs` (+6 tests, fresh sibling to `tests/shell.rs`; shell-tests crate 50 → 56): `env_no_args_prints_empty_when_map_empty`, `env_no_args_prints_sorted_entries`, `env_with_arg_errors_but_repl_continues`, `export_sets_named_value`, `export_no_equals_creates_empty_binding`, `export_no_args_prints_exported_form`. Each test drives `run_with_env(BufReader::new(Cursor::new(input)), &mut stdout_vec, &mut stderr_vec, &mut env_map)` and asserts against the byte-exact prompt-interleaved stream + the post-loop env map. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `run` / `run_with_env` consume only `std::io::{BufRead, Write}` + `std::path::PathBuf` + `std::collections::BTreeMap` + the crate's own `shell` / `tokenize` modules; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — the new builtins don't issue any syscall (env is in-process state); Principle X (testability) strengthens — the REPL loop is now exercisable with pre-seeded env entries via `run_with_env`, an option the prior T123 surface didn't have. Cherry-pick from worktree-agent-af76d7f7a4d841b7b base `2a15925` → main applied cleanly. The agent's separate docs commit (`80b3fbb`) was rebuilt manually here because its embedded sha-reference (`f97dabb`) became `4694a1d` after cherry-pick. **Architectural milestone**: first slice on the env-state surface inside the REPL — T142 (`$VAR` expansion) and T143 (subprocess env plumbing through proc_spawn manifest) can now consume this same map without re-walking the dispatcher signature. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T146 `ls` coreutil — fd_readdir adapter is wired (T071/08cab5b) and FD_READDIR dispatches in `wasi.rs:94`, so `std::fs::read_dir` on wasm32-wasip1 is now usable from userland; (b) T142 `$VAR` substitution against the env map this slice introduces; (c) T144 follow-up: lift the builtin implementations from `run.rs` into a sibling `builtin.rs` per the tasks.md filename hint (no public-API change, pure refactor); (d) T071 remaining WASI ops (`clock_time_get` already wired, `args_get` / `environ_get` already wired — likely only `proc_raise` / `fd_fdstat_set_rights` left); (e) T184 additional setters (`set-wallpaper`, `set-keyboard`, `set-timezone`) mirroring `set-theme`; (f) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 partial — ls coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-ad40bda6a18f0434b, salvaged via manual file-copy because the worktree's base was `2a15925` predating the entire coreutils crate and a direct cherry-pick would have re-created the crate scaffolding that local main already has): workspace +7 in the coreutils-tests crate (coreutils-tests crate 33 → 40, total Rust 1442 → 1449). Commit 41e8239. **T146 is now functionally complete** — `ls / grep / cat / mkdir / rm / cp / mv` all ship to `dist/assets/bin/` per the original spec text; checkbox stays open for per-binary flag follow-ups (`ls -l` / `cp -r` / `rm -r` / `grep -E` / etc.). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/ls.rs` (63 lines, std-only, zero deps) follows the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv error-shape convention exactly: no-arg lists `.` (cwd), each path arg is `fs::symlink_metadata`-stat'd then `std::fs::read_dir`-iterated if a directory or printed as a bare name if a file. Multi-path invocations prefix each section with `<path>:` + blank-line separator (GNU-ls convention). Sort is `Vec::sort` ASCII-by-byte (no locale-aware ordering). **No flags** in this slice — `-l` / `-a` / `-1` / `-h` / `-R` are each future single-slice follow-ups. Per-path errors write `ls: <path>: <error>` to stderr, set the had-error flag, and continue with remaining paths; exit 0 on full success, 1 if any failed. Tests: 7 integration tests via `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")` + `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs (atomic-counter + pid keyed for parallel-run safety): `lists_empty_directory_with_no_output`, `lists_populated_directory_sorted_alphabetically`, `lists_a_file_arg_by_name`, `missing_path_exits_one_with_stderr_shape`, `multi_path_args_emit_headers_and_blank_separator`, `no_args_defaults_to_cwd`, `continues_after_missing_path_but_exits_one`. Xtask wiring: `("coreutils", "ls")` tuple added to `USERLAND_BINS` adjacent to cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv tuples; `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/ls.wasm` (~72 KB). **Unblocked by T071 fd_readdir adapter** (08cab5b): `std::fs::read_dir` on wasm32-wasip1 routes through `Kernel::fd_readdir` semantic seam — confirmed at native-test layer; full WASM-runtime exercise is deferred to T126/T127 boot-to-desktop Playwright. **Worktree-base mismatch postmortem**: agent's `isolation: worktree` allocation rooted the new branch at origin/main (`2a15925`) instead of the local main HEAD (`d321028`, 64+ commits ahead with the entire coreutils crate already in place). The agent self-corrected by scaffolding the entire coreutils crate from scratch on its branch — Cargo.toml + src/lib.rs + workspace member + xtask USERLAND_BINS additions for ALL seven coreutils — while documenting in its return that the actual T146 baseline already had cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv on local main. Cherry-pick would have produced a 6-way conflict on the redundant crate scaffolding. Resolution path: copy only the two NEW files (`src/bin/ls.rs` + `tests/ls.rs`) from the worktree to local main; manually add the `[[bin]] name = "ls"` entry to `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` and the `("coreutils", "ls")` tuple to `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS`; rebuild commits with the local-main SHA. **Lesson for future dispatches**: parallel-dispatch agents should self-rebase their worktree onto local main before starting work — earlier rounds (mkdir agent X in round 9) demonstrated this pattern; round-12's worktree allocation skipped that step. Bake the rebase into the brief explicitly, or have the integrator test the worktree's `git log -1` before dispatching content, or use `git worktree add` from the parent script with the local-main HEAD as the explicit ref. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle I (real OS) strengthens — `ls` is textbook POSIX, the seventh of seven coreutils now ships; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")` isolation-test shape follows the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv precedent. Cumulative session delta since round 11: T146 closed (functionally), T144 partial landed (export/env), workspace test count 1442 → 1449 (+1 sh-tests crate +6 = 7 net but only ls counted here — full diff includes T144's +6). No kernel / abi / preferences-crate / Platform / Vfs / Cap / Signal / errno / opcode / wire-format changes — pure userland binary slice.
