After the **T146 follow-up — ls -l long-format flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a5562e89b502fd31a, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `2a15925` → local main HEAD `e6c7c9b`, no conflicts because the prior `ls.rs` shipped in 41e8239 was already in the rebased state): workspace +5 in the coreutils-tests crate (coreutils-tests crate 40 → 45, ls suite 7 → 12). Commit c3d1aa7. First flag follow-up against the now-complete T146 coreutils block — adds `-l` to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/ls.rs` (63 → 122 lines, still std-only zero deps). Long-format rows are `<type-letter> <size-bytes> <name>` separated by single spaces: type is `d` for directory / `l` for symlink / `-` for regular file (other types — char / block / socket / fifo — also format as `-` for now and are each future follow-ups); size is `metadata.len()` for files and `0` for directories (no recursive sum — `du`'s job, not `ls -l`'s). **Mode bits, owner / group, mtime, alignment-padded columns** are each explicitly deferred to future single-slice follow-ups, documented at the top-of-file `//!` comment alongside the existing `-a` / `-1` / `-h` / `-R` / `-r` deferrals so the next slice picker sees the runway. CLI parsing splits args into flags + paths in a single pass: any arg starting with `-` (other than the bare `-` stdin convention left as-is) is a flag; `-l` toggles long-format; anything else after a `--` separator is forced into the paths bucket regardless of leading `-`. Unknown flags (`-a`, `-r`, anything else) write `ls: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 2 — deliberately distinct from the per-path exit 1, so the caller can distinguish bad invocation from per-path failure (this is the precedent for the rest of the coreutils flag-parsing slices that follow). Multi-path / file-arg / no-arg / partial-success / sort behaviour is unchanged from the prior slice; `-l` is purely a per-row formatter toggle. Implementation: `list_one` now collects `(name, Metadata)` pairs into a `Vec` (the metadata is `fs::symlink_metadata`-stat'd per entry to capture symlink type without traversal), sorts by name, then iterates rows through a new `write_row(out, name, meta, long_format)` helper that branches on the flag. `type_letter` is a separate function for clarity and so future type-letter additions (char / block / socket / fifo) land in one place. Tests: 5 new in `crates/coreutils/tests/ls.rs` on top of the prior 7 (no modifications to the existing suite — pure additions per the slice brief). `dash_l_emits_type_size_name_columns_for_files` (populated dir + `-l`, asserts each row matches the exact `- <size> <name>` shape with three known sizes); `dash_l_marks_directories_with_d_letter` (dir-with-subdir, asserts subdir row is exactly `d 0 subdir\n` so the `0`-not-recursive contract is byte-checked); `dash_l_handles_file_arg_directly` (`ls -l <file>` single-row `- 5 only.txt\n`); `dash_l_with_no_args_lists_cwd_long_format` (asserts the no-arg + `-l` combo defaults to cwd, byte-exact `- 2 marker\n`); `unknown_flag_exits_two_with_stderr` (`ls -a` exits 2, stderr contains both `unknown flag` and `-a`, stdout empty). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, identical isolation-test shape to the prior 7 ls tests + the cat / grep / cp / mkdir / rm / mv suites. Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 45 / 45 pass (was 40); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "ls")` tuple from 41e8239 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `ls` consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::path` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 5 new isolation tests against `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")`. **Architectural milestone**: first per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the now-complete coreutils crate; establishes the flag-parsing convention (single-pass split, `-`-prefixed args are flags, `--` separator force-promotes everything after into paths, unknown flags exit 2 with `<bin>: unknown flag: <flag>`) that future `cp -r` / `rm -r` / `grep -E` / `grep -i` / `grep -n` / `ls -a` / `ls -1` / `ls -h` / `ls -R` slices should mirror. T146 still uncheckered — the `-l` flag is now in, but other flag follow-ups remain. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `ls -a` (show dotfiles — same parser shape, single line at the top of the readdir loop to filter `.`-prefixed names unless the flag is set); (b) `ls -1` (force one-entry-per-line — already the default but the flag should be accepted as a no-op so scripts that pass it don't hit the unknown-flag path); (c) `cp -r` (recursive directory copy — the bigger of the obvious flag follow-ups); (d) `rm -r` (recursive removal); (e) `grep -i` / `-n` / `-v` / `-F` (each independent); (f) T142 `$VAR` substitution in sh against T144's env map; (g) T143 `proc_spawn`-based pipeline runner — first userland use of the `proc_spawn` extension syscall; (h) T184 additional setters (`set-wallpaper`, `set-keyboard`, `set-timezone`); (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T184 partial — settings set-keyboard subcommand slice**: workspace +5 in the settings cli-tests file (cli.rs 18 → 23, total Rust 1455 → 1460). Commit 599ccdc. Direct mirror of the set-wallpaper slice (2440df7) and the earlier set-theme slice (dabfedb): same `--config <path>` test hook, same exit codes, same `settings: <reason>` stderr shape, same `usage:` form on missing arg, same empty/quote/newline rejection. The hand-rolled `serialise_preferences` emitter from set-theme already covered `[keyboard] layout` (canonical section order: theme / wallpaper / keyboard / timezone / terminal), so the only changes were the new dispatch arm in `main()`, a new `run_set_keyboard(rest: &[String])` driver function, and an updated module-doc invocations block. `keyboard.layout` is the only field on `[keyboard]` in the v1 preferences schema (T183, `crates/preferences/src/lib.rs:65` `keyboard_layout: Option<String>`), so `set-keyboard <layout>` writes that field directly with no disambiguation. Validation matches the prior two setters byte-for-byte: empty rejected, embedded `"` or `\n` rejected (so the canonical quoter cannot escape unsafe characters into the round-trip parser). NotFound on the read fallthrough yields `Preferences::empty()` so a fresh config bootstraps cleanly. 5 integration tests at `crates/settings/tests/cli.rs` (18 → 23): `set_keyboard_writes_new_value_to_fresh_config` (asserts `[keyboard]` section + `layout = "us-qwerty"` line), `set_keyboard_preserves_other_fields` (round-trip: write through the binary then re-parse via `preferences::Preferences::parse` and assert all other five fields byte-for-byte equal — `[keyboard] layout` flips from `de-qwertz` to `us-qwerty` while theme / wallpaper / timezone / terminal stay frozen), `set_keyboard_overwrites_existing_value` (asserts `us-qwerty` appears exactly once, `de-qwertz` is gone, `[keyboard]` section appears exactly once — guards against duplicate-section bugs), `set_keyboard_missing_layout_arg_exits_one_with_usage`, `set_keyboard_rejects_empty_or_illegal_value` (consolidated test exercising all three rejection paths: empty string, embedded `"`, embedded `\n`). Temp files use the existing per-test atomic-counter + pid keyed scheme for parallel-run safety. **Valid-layout allow-list deferred** to the eventual GUI per the set-theme / set-wallpaper precedent — CLI accepts any non-empty string with quotes and newlines rejected. **File mode 0o644 deferred** — same constraint as the prior two setters: host `std::fs::write` cannot portably set Unix permissions; kernel VFS defaults ignore host bits. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p settings` 23 passed / 0 failed; `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p settings` clean. No kernel / abi / preferences-crate / Platform / Vfs / Cap / Signal / errno / opcode / wire-format changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `settings` is leaf userland; Principle X (testability) strengthens — third proof of the `--config` test hook + canonical-emitter round-trip pattern, which is now firmly the proven shape for the remaining `set-timezone` / `set-terminal` / `set-theme-fit` follow-up slices. After the **T146 follow-up — cp -r recursive flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a102ccaa15e83cf2b, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `2a15925` → local main HEAD `cf4edcc`, no conflicts because the prior `cp.rs` shipped in 56904e9 was already in the rebased state): workspace +5 in the coreutils-tests crate (coreutils-tests crate 45 → 50, cp suite 4 → 9). Commit b2623ff. Second flag follow-up against the now-complete T146 coreutils block (after `ls -l` in c3d1aa7) — adds `-r` (and synonym `-R`) to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/cp.rs` (37 → 156 lines, still std-only zero deps). Without the flag, behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged from the original 56904e9 single-file slice — directory `src` still errors out, no clobber on directory intent. With `-r`, the destination resolution mirrors GNU cp's "if dst is an existing directory, copy src into dst" semantics: `cp -r src dst` where `dst` doesn't exist creates `dst/` as the new root; where `dst` already exists as a directory, the copy lands at `dst/<src-basename>/...` (so re-running `cp -r src/ dst/` doesn't merge into dst's root, it creates `dst/src/`); where `dst` exists as a regular file, errors without clobbering. Recursive walk uses `std::fs::read_dir` + `fs::copy` for files and `fs::create_dir` for subdirectories — the basic shape is two helpers, `resolve_dir_target` (the GNU dst-resolution rules) and `copy_tree` (the recursion itself). File overwrites within the tree are unconditional this slice (no `-n` / `-i` — re-running `cp -r` clobbers files at dst as expected). Symlinks: `fs::copy` follows symlinks and copies the target's bytes; preserving symlinks-as-symlinks is a future flag follow-up, documented at the top-of-file `//!` comment alongside the existing deferrals (`-n`, `-i`, `-v`, `-p`, multi-source variadic `cp a b c dst/`). CLI parsing mirrors the `ls -l` convention from c3d1aa7 byte-for-byte: single-pass arg split into `flags` + `paths` buckets; any arg starting with `-` (other than the bare `-` stdin convention) is a flag; `-r` and `-R` toggle recursion; everything after `--` is forced into the paths bucket regardless of leading `-`; unknown flags write `cp: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 2 (distinct from the per-path exit 1, so callers can tell bad invocation from per-path failure — same precedent ls follows). Usage string updated to `cp: usage: cp <src> <dst> (or \`cp -r <src> <dst>\` for recursive)` so the existing test's `stderr.contains("cp <src> <dst>")` substring assertion stays a "pure addition" check (the prior 4 tests pass unchanged). Tests: 5 new in `crates/coreutils/tests/cp.rs` on top of the prior 4 (no modifications to the existing suite — pure additions per the slice brief). `dash_r_copies_empty_directory` (asserts dst is a fresh empty dir afterward); `dash_r_copies_nested_tree` (3-level deep tree with non-ASCII bytes including `\x00` and embedded NUL — verifies each file's bytes survive byte-for-byte through `fs::copy`); `dash_r_into_existing_dst_dir` (asserts the GNU "copy src INTO dst" semantics: pre-create dst as a dir, run `cp -r src dst`, then assert `dst/src/child.txt` exists with src's bytes); `dash_r_clobbering_files_inside_dst_works` (the test runs `cp -r src dst` twice — once after seeding `dst/f.txt` with stale bytes and `src/f.txt` with new bytes, then again as a no-op idempotency check — both runs succeed and the bytes at `dst/src/f.txt` are the new content); `directory_src_without_dash_r_errors_as_before` (regression guard: directory src without the flag still exits 1, dst not created). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_cp")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, identical isolation-test shape to the prior 4 cp tests + the cat / grep / mkdir / rm / mv / ls suites. Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 50 / 50 pass (was 45); `cargo test -p coreutils --test cp` 9 / 9 pass; `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "cp")` tuple from 56904e9 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `cp` consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::path` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 5 new isolation tests against `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_cp")` covering both happy-path (empty / nested / into-existing / clobber) and regression (no-flag-still-errors). **Architectural milestone**: second per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the now-complete coreutils crate; the flag-parsing convention from `ls -l` (c3d1aa7) ports cleanly to `cp -r` with no shape change, validating the convention as the template for future `rm -r` / `grep -i` / `grep -n` / `grep -v` / `grep -F` / `ls -a` / `ls -1` / `ls -h` / `ls -R` slices. T146 still uncheckered — the major two flags (`ls -l`, `cp -r`) are now in, but other flag follow-ups remain. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `rm -r` (recursive removal — the obvious peer to `cp -r`); (b) `grep -i` (case-insensitive); (c) `grep -n` (line numbers); (d) `grep -v` (invert match); (e) `grep -F` (already the implementation; the flag should be accepted as a no-op so scripts pass it cleanly); (f) `ls -a` (show dotfiles); (g) `ls -1` (force one-entry-per-line, no-op acceptance); (h) `cp -p` (preserve mtime / mode — needs a host-portable shape); (i) T142 `$VAR` substitution in sh against T144's env map; (j) T143 `proc_spawn`-based pipeline runner — first userland use of the `proc_spawn` extension syscall; (k) T184 additional setters (`set-keyboard`, `set-timezone`); (l) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — mkdir -p flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a7c6d1b5c35c9c0a2, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `2a15925` → local main HEAD `1a319ae`, no conflicts because the prior `mkdir.rs` shipped in a8c2558 was already in the rebased state): workspace +4 in the coreutils-tests crate (coreutils-tests crate 50 → 54, mkdir suite 6 → 10). Commit bbf6b58. Third flag follow-up against the now-complete T146 coreutils block (after `ls -l` in c3d1aa7 and `cp -r` in b2623ff) — adds `-p` (parents-as-needed) to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/mkdir.rs` (37 → 70 lines, still std-only zero deps). Without the flag, behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged from the original a8c2558 minimal slice — `std::fs::create_dir(path)` per arg, missing parent fails, existing target fails. With `-p`, each path arg routes through `std::fs::create_dir_all(path)` instead: intermediate parents are created on demand, and a pre-existing target is no longer an error (POSIX `mkdir -p` semantics — `mkdir -p a/b/c` followed by a re-run of the same command both exit 0). The flag is global to the invocation: `mkdir -p a/b/c d/e` create_dir_all's both. Multi-path partial-success shape unchanged: per-path errors continue to stderr, exit 1 if any failed (zero new failure modes — `create_dir_all`'s only new errno is the existing-non-dir-at-target case, which surfaces with the same `mkdir: <path>: <error>` shape). CLI parsing mirrors the `ls -l` (c3d1aa7) and `cp -r` (b2623ff) convention byte-for-byte: single-pass arg split into `flags` + `paths` buckets; any arg starting with `-` (other than the bare `-` stdin convention) is a flag; `-p` toggles parents; everything after `--` is forced into the paths bucket regardless of leading `-`; unknown flags write `mkdir: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 2 (distinct from the per-path exit 1, so callers can tell bad invocation from per-path failure — same precedent ls / cp follow). Usage string updated to `mkdir: usage: mkdir [-p] <dir>...` so the existing zero-args test's `stderr.contains("usage:")` + `stderr.contains("mkdir")` substring assertions stay a "pure addition" check (the prior 6 tests pass unchanged). Tests: 4 new in `crates/coreutils/tests/mkdir.rs` on top of the prior 6 (no modifications to the existing suite — pure additions per the slice brief). `dash_p_creates_intermediate_parents` (3-level deep `a/b/c` from a fresh dir; asserts each ancestor + the target exist as dirs); `dash_p_is_idempotent_on_existing_dir` (pre-create `already/there`, run `mkdir -p already/there`, asserts exit 0 + empty stderr + dir still there — POSIX no-error-on-existing semantics); `dash_p_with_multiple_paths` (`mkdir -p a/b c/d`, asserts all four dirs exist — confirms the global-to-invocation flag scope); `unknown_flag_exits_two` (`mkdir -x foo` exits 2, stderr contains both `unknown flag` and `-x`, stdout empty). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_mkdir")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, identical isolation-test shape to the prior 6 mkdir tests + the cat / grep / cp / ls / rm / mv suites. Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 54 / 54 pass (was 50); `cargo test -p coreutils --test mkdir` 10 / 10 pass; `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "mkdir")` tuple already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `mkdir` consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 4 new isolation tests against `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_mkdir")` covering both happy-path (single-deep / idempotent / multi-path) and regression (unknown-flag-exits-two). **Architectural milestone**: third per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the now-complete coreutils crate; the flag-parsing convention from `ls -l` and `cp -r` ports cleanly to `mkdir -p` with no shape change, further validating the convention as the template for future `rm -r` / `grep -i` / `grep -n` / `grep -v` / `grep -F` / `ls -a` / `ls -1` / `ls -h` / `ls -R` slices. T146 still uncheckered — three flags (`ls -l`, `cp -r`, `mkdir -p`) are now in, but other flag follow-ups remain. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `rm -r` (recursive removal — the obvious peer to `cp -r`); (b) `grep -i` (case-insensitive); (c) `grep -n` (line numbers); (d) `grep -v` (invert match); (e) `grep -F` (already the implementation; the flag should be accepted as a no-op so scripts pass it cleanly); (f) `ls -a` (show dotfiles); (g) `ls -1` (force one-entry-per-line, no-op acceptance); (h) `cp -p` (preserve mtime / mode — needs a host-portable shape); (i) T142 `$VAR` substitution in sh against T144's env map; (j) T143 `proc_spawn`-based pipeline runner — first userland use of the `proc_spawn` extension syscall; (k) T184 additional setters (`set-timezone`); (l) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — rm -r recursive flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a748563989b8e33ca, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `2a15925` → local main HEAD `1d7d65e`, no conflicts because the prior `rm.rs` shipped in db55782 was already in the rebased state): workspace +6 in the coreutils-tests crate (coreutils-tests crate 54 → 60, rm suite 6 → 12). Commit d0e0f5a. Fourth flag follow-up against the now-complete T146 coreutils block (after `ls -l` in c3d1aa7, `cp -r` in b2623ff, and `mkdir -p` in bbf6b58) — adds `-r` (and synonym `-R`) to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/rm.rs` (38 → 84 lines, still std-only zero deps). Without the flag, behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged from the original db55782 minimal slice — `std::fs::remove_file(path)` per arg, directory targets fail with the standard "is a directory" errno, missing paths fail. With `-r`, each path is `fs::symlink_metadata`-stat'd via a tiny `is_dir()` helper; if the target is a directory, `std::fs::remove_dir_all(path)` recursively removes it; if it's a regular file (or stat fails — e.g. NotFound), `remove_file` is called instead so the per-path error continues to surface naturally. Multi-path partial-success shape unchanged: per-path errors continue to stderr, exit 1 if any failed. POSIX `-R` accepted as synonym (POSIX requires both `-r` and `-R` for the same recursive semantic). Symlinks under `-r`: `remove_dir_all`'s standard-library default behaviour is preserved — a symlink-to-directory at the top level is removed as a symlink without traversing its target; preserving more granular symlink behaviour is a future flag follow-up, documented at the top-of-file `//!` comment alongside the existing `-f` / `-i` / `-v` deferrals. CLI parsing mirrors the `cp -r` (b2623ff), `mkdir -p` (bbf6b58), and `ls -l` (c3d1aa7) convention byte-for-byte: single-pass arg split into `flags` + `paths` buckets; any arg starting with `-` (other than the bare `-` stdin convention) is a flag; `-r` and `-R` toggle recursion; everything after `--` is forced into the paths bucket regardless of leading `-`; unknown flags write `rm: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 2 (distinct from the per-path exit 1, so callers can tell bad invocation from per-path failure — same precedent ls / cp / mkdir follow). Usage string updated to `rm: usage: rm [-r] <path>...` so the existing zero-args test's `stderr.contains("usage:")` + `stderr.contains("rm")` substring assertions stay a "pure addition" check (the prior 6 tests pass unchanged). Tests: 6 new in `crates/coreutils/tests/rm.rs` on top of the prior 6 (no modifications to the existing suite — pure additions per the slice brief). `dash_r_removes_empty_directory` (asserts `rm -r empty-dir` on a freshly-created empty dir succeeds with empty stdout/stderr and the dir is gone afterwards); `dash_r_removes_nested_tree` (3-level deep tree `root/sub/nested/` with files at each level — asserts every level is gone afterwards, byte-checked via `!path.exists()` for root, sub, and nested); `dash_R_alias_works` (same shape as test 1 but with `-R`, confirming POSIX synonym acceptance — uses `#[allow(non_snake_case)]` because the test name reflects the literal flag); `dash_r_with_multiple_paths` (`rm -r dir1 dir2` where each contains a file, asserts both are gone afterwards — confirms global-to-invocation flag scope and partial-success shape under recursive mode); `directory_without_dash_r_still_refused` (regression guard: a directory target without the flag still exits 1, the dir still exists afterwards, stderr still contains `rm:` + the path); `unknown_flag_exits_two` (`rm -x foo` exits 2, stderr contains both `unknown flag` and `-x`, stdout empty, target file still exists — confirming the flag-parsing exits before any filesystem action). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_rm")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, identical isolation-test shape to the prior 6 rm tests + the cat / grep / cp / mkdir / ls / mv suites. Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 60 / 60 pass (was 54); `cargo test -p coreutils --test rm` 12 / 12 pass; `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "rm")` tuple from db55782 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `rm` consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::path` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 6 new isolation tests against `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_rm")` covering both happy-path (empty / nested / `-R` alias / multi-path) and regression (directory-without-flag-still-refused, unknown-flag-exits-two). **Architectural milestone**: fourth per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the now-complete coreutils crate; the flag-parsing convention from `ls -l`, `cp -r`, and `mkdir -p` ports cleanly to `rm -r` with no shape change, further validating the convention as the template for future `grep -i` / `grep -n` / `grep -v` / `grep -F` / `ls -a` / `ls -1` / `ls -h` / `ls -R` slices. T146 still uncheckered — four flags (`ls -l`, `cp -r`, `mkdir -p`, `rm -r`) are now in, but other flag follow-ups remain. With the four "obvious peer" recursive/parents flags now landed, the runway shifts toward grep flags and the second-class ls flags. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `grep -i` (case-insensitive — straightforward `eq_ignore_ascii_case` flip); (b) `grep -n` (line numbers — `<file>:<n>:<line>` shape); (c) `grep -v` (invert match — flip the predicate); (d) `grep -F` (already the implementation; the flag should be accepted as a no-op so scripts pass it cleanly); (e) `ls -a` (show dotfiles — single line at the top of the readdir loop to filter `.`-prefixed names unless the flag is set); (f) `ls -1` (force one-entry-per-line, no-op acceptance — `-1` is the default unless `-l` flips it); (g) `ls -h` (human-readable sizes — only meaningful with `-l`, so the slice may want to land both interactions in one); (h) `ls -R` (recursive listing — peer to `cp -r` / `rm -r` / `mkdir -p`); (i) `cp -p` (preserve mtime / mode — needs a host-portable shape, deferred until the v1 perms model lands); (j) T142 `$VAR` substitution in sh against T144's env map; (k) T143 `proc_spawn`-based pipeline runner — first userland use of the `proc_spawn` extension syscall; (l) T184 additional setters (`set-terminal`, `set-theme-fit`); (m) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T142 partial — `$VAR` substitution in sh slice**: workspace +18 in the sh crate (sh-lib unit tests 0 → 13 inline `expand_tests`, plus expand-tests integration crate 0 → 5). Commit b74a6e3. First partial against the Phase 6 US4 T142 tokenizer/parser block (line 287 in tasks.md) — pipes / redirection / quoting / `FOO=bar cmd` env-prefix each remain as future single-T142-partial slices. This slice extends T123's whitespace-tokenised REPL in `crates/sh/src/run.rs` with a `pub(crate) fn expand_vars(token: &str, env: &BTreeMap<String, String>) -> String` walker that runs over each token AFTER `split_whitespace` but BEFORE `dispatch_builtin`, expanding `$NAME` and `${NAME}` references against the same env map T144 (4694a1d) wired through `run_with_env`. The dispatch loop now constructs `let expanded: Vec<String> = tokens.iter().map(|t| expand_vars(t, env)).collect()` then `let expanded_refs: Vec<&str> = expanded.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()` and passes `&expanded_refs` to `dispatch_builtin` instead of `&tokens` — the borrow lifetimes work because `expanded` outlives `expanded_refs` within one iteration of the REPL loop. The unknown-command branch was also updated to use `expanded_refs[0]` so `sh: command not found: <token>` reflects the post-expansion name (e.g. an unset `$CMD` produces `sh: command not found: ` mirroring bash / dash). Substitution rules: `$NAME` where NAME starts with `[A-Za-z_]` and continues with `[A-Za-z0-9_]*` is a variable reference (greedy match — `$Xb` reads as `${Xb}`, NOT `${X}b`; to insert a literal char after an expansion the user must use the braced form `${X}b`); `${NAME}` is the explicit braced form, semantically identical once the name is isolated by `{` / `}`; unset names expand to the empty string but the token IS preserved (`echo $UNSET` tokenises as `["echo", ""]` post-expansion — POSIX `set -u`-off default, the slice never silently drops an arg); a `$` followed by anything other than a name-start char (`[A-Za-z_]`) or `{` is preserved as a literal `$` and the scanner advances one byte (so `$1`, `$$`, `$@`, `$0`, etc. are NOT supported in this slice — they pass through verbatim, e.g. `$1` → `$1`, `$$` → `$$`); a trailing `$` at end-of-token is literal (`foo$` → `foo$`); `${NAME` with no matching close-brace preserves the literal `${NAME` to end-of-token (POSIX errors here; v1 prefers leniency over surfacing a mid-line error). Backslash escapes (`\$X`) are NOT handled in this slice — the leading `\` is preserved, then `$X` expands (so `\$X` with `X=v` produces `\v`); the escaping/quoting slice will rework this. Implementation is byte-by-byte ASCII scanning with `is_name_start` / `is_name_continue` helpers (matches!() macro on byte ranges); multi-byte UTF-8 sequences pass through verbatim because only ASCII `$` introduces an expansion and the name-charset is ASCII-only. Test coverage at `crates/sh/src/run.rs::expand_tests` (+13 inline `#[cfg(test)] mod`-style unit tests on `expand_vars` directly, lib-tests crate 0 → 13): `unset_var_expands_to_empty`, `set_var_expands_to_value`, `multiple_vars_in_token_concat` (`$X$Y` with two set vars → concatenated values), `braced_form_works` (`${X}b` regression for the literal-char-after-expansion path), `partial_match_continues_to_name_end` (the `a$Xb` greedy case → looks up `Xb`, not `X`, returns just `a`), `dollar_followed_by_invalid_char_is_literal` (`$1` → `$1` since `1` isn't a name-start), `lone_dollar_at_end_is_literal` (`foo$` → `foo$`), `var_with_underscore_works` (covers the three underscore positions: leading `_X`, mid `FOO_BAR`, trailing `Z_`), `no_dollar_passes_through_verbatim`, `braced_form_with_unset_name_is_empty` (`${MISSING}` and `a${MISSING}b` → `` and `ab`), `unterminated_brace_preserves_literal` (`${X` and `a${X` → `${X` and `a${X`), `dollar_dollar_is_literal_in_v1` (`$$` PID NOT supported — passes through), `backslash_dollar_preserved_unprocessed` (`\$X` → `\<value>`, leading backslash kept). Plus 5 integration tests at `crates/sh/tests/expand.rs` driving `run_with_env` (expand-tests crate 0 → 5): `echo_expands_set_var_to_value` (seed `X=hello`; `echo $X\nexit\n` → stdout contains `hello\n`), `echo_after_export_round_trips_value` (`export X=1\necho $X\nexit\n` — just-set value visible to next command's expansion + post-loop env map has `X=1`), `echo_unset_var_emits_just_newline` (`echo $UNSET\nexit\n` → byte-exact `"$ \n$ "` — empty token preserved through `args.join(" ")` so the `echo` line is just `\n`), `echo_concat_two_vars_in_one_token` (`echo $X$Y` → `foobar\n`), `echo_braced_form_lets_literal_chars_follow` (`echo ${X}b` → `hellob\n` regression for the brace path). Each integration test uses the same `drive(input, env)` helper shape as the prior `tests/env.rs` (T144 4694a1d) — `BufReader<Cursor<Vec<u8>>>` stdin + `Vec<u8>` stdout/stderr, byte-exact assertions on the prompt-interleaved stream. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p sh` all six binaries pass — sh-lib 13/13, sh-main 0/0, env-tests 6/6, expand-tests 5/5, shell-tests 37/37, tokenize-tests 13/13 (74 total in the sh crate; +18 from this slice); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, blocked on other T142 partials or future work): pipes (`|`) + redirection (`>` / `>>` / `<`) — each a future single-T142-partial slice; quoting (single + double quotes, the inner-`$VAR` expansion semantics that double quotes enable, backslash escapes); the `FOO=bar cmd` per-command env-prefix shape (needs the parser layer to recognise `KEY=VALUE` words at command-start and dispatch them as a one-shot child-env override); `$$` (PID), `$@` / `$*` (positional list), `$0` / `$1` / ... (positional args), `$?` (last exit status — needs T143's pipeline runner to surface child status); `$!` (last background job PID — needs T145); arithmetic expansion `$((expr))`; command substitution `$(...)` / backticks; tilde expansion `~/foo`; glob expansion `*.txt`; the `set -u` "error on unset var" mode toggle. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched — pure userland-shell slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `expand_vars` consumes only `std::collections::BTreeMap` (the env map T144 already wired) and the token slice; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — `$VAR` expansion is in-process state, no syscall touched; Principle X (testability) strengthens — `expand_vars` is `pub(crate)`-visible and exercised by 13 inline lib-unit tests on the function directly + 5 integration tests through the REPL driver, so the shape is well-pinned for future T142 partials that bolt onto the same expansion layer (e.g. double-quoted `"$VAR"` that should still expand, single-quoted `'$VAR'` that should NOT). **Architectural milestone**: first slice on the post-tokenisation expansion layer inside the REPL — `$VAR` substitution is the first transformation to walk over tokens before dispatch, establishing the `expand → ref-collect → dispatch` shape that quoting / globbing / etc. will plug into. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T142 quoting partial (single + double quotes, inner-`$VAR` rules); (b) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file` — each modifies fd 0/1 before dispatch); (c) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (d) T146 follow-up: `rm -r`, `grep -i` / `-n` / `-v`, `ls -a` / `-1`; (e) T071 remaining WASI ops; (f) T143 `proc_spawn`-based pipeline runner; (g) T184 additional setters (`set-terminal`, `set-theme-fit`); (h) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — grep -i case-insensitive flag slice** (inline ship — round-6 dispatch agents hit 529 Overloaded server errors so the integrator wrote the slice directly): coreutils-tests crate 60 → 65 (+5 grep tests). Commit 8ad3297. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/grep.rs` (82 → 132 lines) gains `-i` flag mirroring the established `cp -r` / `mkdir -p` / `rm -r` flag-parsing convention exactly: single-pass arg split, `-i` toggles case-insensitive matching, `--` separator forces remaining args into the pattern/files slot, unknown flags exit 2 with `grep: unknown flag: <flag>`. Implementation: when `-i` is set, both pattern and each candidate line are lower-cased via `str::to_lowercase` (ASCII-correct; non-ASCII case-folding is a future flag follow-up — Unicode tables would inflate the WASM binary). Pattern is lower-cased once at startup, not per-line. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/grep.rs` (+5 tests, suite 6 → 11): `dash_i_matches_uppercase_pattern_against_lowercase_line` (`grep -i HELLO` finds "hello world"), `dash_i_matches_lowercase_pattern_against_uppercase_line` (`grep -i foo` finds "FOO BAR"), `dash_i_preserves_match_count_on_mixed_case` (Apple/apple/BANANA/Apricot → matches Apple + apple), `without_dash_i_case_sensitive_still_holds` (regression — `grep foo` does NOT match "FOO"), `unknown_flag_exits_two_for_grep` (`grep -x` exits 2 with `unknown flag` stderr). Existing 6 tests pass unchanged (pure additions). `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `dist/assets/bin/grep.wasm` rebuilds with the flag-parser prologue. **Round-6 process note**: both round-6 worktree-agent dispatches (this slice + T144 unset) hit `API Error: 529 Overloaded` simultaneously; integrator pivoted to direct inline ship for both, since each is < 50 lines of code + tests. Round-7+ should retry the agent dispatch pattern when platform load drops. After the **T144 follow-up — unset builtin slice** (inline ship, same round-6 reason as the grep -i slice): sh-tests crate +5 (env-tests 6 → 11). Commit c1b930b. Wires `unset NAME [NAME ...]` into the `dispatch_builtin` arm in `crates/sh/src/run.rs` mirroring the export builtin's shape; new `builtin_unset` function (~25 lines) calls `env.remove(*arg)` for each arg. Multiple args remove each in order; nonexistent names are silently OK (POSIX semantics — no error); empty NAME or NAME containing `=` writes `sh: unset: <arg>: not a valid identifier\n` to stderr and continues with remaining args; zero args writes `sh: unset: usage: unset NAME...\n` to stderr and the REPL stays alive (matches the `env` builtin's continuation pattern). Tests at `crates/sh/tests/env.rs` (+5 tests in the existing env-tests file, suite 6 → 11): `unset_removes_named_entry` (seed X=1, Y=2; `unset X\nenv\nexit` → stdout has Y=2, no X=, post-loop env has only Y), `unset_multiple_args_removes_all` (seed A=1, B=2, C=3; `unset A C\nenv\nexit` → only B=2 remains), `unset_nonexistent_is_silent_ok` (`unset MISSING\nexit` → empty stderr, exit 0), `unset_with_no_args_errors_continues` (`unset\nexit` → stderr contains `usage:`, REPL survived to run `exit`), `unset_followed_by_export_round_trips` (`export X=1\nunset X\nexport X=2\nenv\nexit` → final X=2, no orphan X=1 remnant). Existing 6 env-tests pass unchanged. `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. **Promotes T144 from "unset explicitly deferred" → "unset landed"**; the deferred-paragraph in T144's tasks.md partial annotation needs the `unset` mention removed (handled in the docs commit). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no display-server / toolkit changes; no other Rust crate touched. After the **T146 follow-up — grep -n line-number flag slice**: coreutils-tests crate 65 → 69 (+4 grep tests). Commit 16f41aa. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/grep.rs` (132 → 145 lines) gains `-n` flag mirroring the established single-pass arg-split convention exactly: when `-n` is set, each match is prefixed with its 1-indexed line number; combinable with `-i` (and the existing multi-file `<path>:` prefix). The `scan` function already enumerated lines via `for (n, line) in r.split(b'\n').enumerate()` — this slice threads a new `with_line_numbers: bool` parameter through and replaces the two-arm match (`prefix.is_some()` vs not) with a four-arm match on `(prefix, with_line_numbers)`: `(Some(p), true)` → `{p}:{n}:{s}`; `(Some(p), false)` → `{p}:{s}` (existing); `(None, true)` → `{n}:{s}`; `(None, false)` → `{s}` (existing). Pre-slice flag-parser arms `-i` only; post-slice arms `-i` AND `-n` independently. Usage line updated to `usage: grep [-i] [-n] <pattern> [file ...]`. Note: combined-flag bundling like `-in` is NOT supported by the single-pass parser (each `-X` is a separate arg, matching the established `cp -r` / `mkdir -p` / `rm -r` / `grep -i` shape) — callers pass `-i -n` separately. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/grep.rs` (+4 tests, suite 11 → 15): `dash_n_emits_line_numbers_for_single_file` (3-line file with second matching → stdout `2:second-line\n`), `dash_n_with_multiple_files_includes_path_and_number` (two files, format `<path>:<n>:<line>` → assert byte-exact concatenation of both file blocks), `dash_n_combined_with_dash_i_works` (`grep -i -n HELLO` → `2:hello world\n` — matches case-insensitively AND prefixes line numbers), `dash_n_in_stdin_mode_counts_from_one` (stdin with `alpha\nbeta\n`, pattern `alpha` → `1:alpha\n` confirming line counting starts from one in stdin mode). Existing 11 tests pass unchanged (pure additions). `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `dist/assets/bin/grep.wasm` rebuilds with the line-number prefix branch. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the line-numbering semantics are pinned by 4 deterministic byte-exact stdout tests against the compiled binary across single-file, multi-file, combined-with-`-i`, and stdin modes. After the **T146 follow-up — ls -a show-hidden flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a50680f52c2531d15, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `2a15925` → expected post-rebase HEAD `1b0743e`, no conflicts): coreutils-tests crate ls suite 12 → 16 (+4 net new tests; one existing test updated). Commit 4ca1f20. Sixth flag follow-up against the now-complete T146 coreutils block (after `ls -l` in c3d1aa7, `cp -r` in b2623ff, `mkdir -p` in bbf6b58, `rm -r` in d0e0f5a, and `grep -i` in 8ad3297) — adds `-a` (show-all) to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/ls.rs` (74 → 92 lines, still std-only zero deps). **Hide-vs-show default decision**: the prior 41e8239 / c3d1aa7 minimal `ls.rs` used `fs::read_dir` directly with NO filtering, so it actually DID show dotfiles by default — but a careful read of the existing 12 tests confirmed none of them rely on dotfiles being shown (no test name in `lists_populated_directory_sorted_alphabetically` or any other test starts with `.`). Brief asked the slice to commit to POSIX hide-by-default + `-a` disables, and that was a clean addition rather than a behaviour break for any existing test. Implementation: a single `if !show_all && name.starts_with('.') { continue; }` filter inside the `read_dir` loop in `list_one`, gated on the `show_all: bool` threaded through from `main`. v1 simplification (documented in the file's `//!` header): `-a` does NOT synthesise the `.` / `..` entries themselves — only real on-disk entries whose name happens to start with `.`. POSIX `ls -a` would include both; deferring the synthetic-entry case keeps the slice byte-for-byte readdir-passthrough. `-a` only affects directory listings; a file argument whose own name starts with `.` is always printed regardless of `-a` (the user named it explicitly). CLI parsing follows the established convention from `ls -l` (c3d1aa7) and matches the fused-flag pattern POSIX `ls -la` users expect: `-a` toggles show-all, `-l` toggles long-format, AND `-la` / `-al` are recognised as fused tokens (both flags set). Single-pass arg split, `--` separator support, unknown flags exit 2 with `ls: unknown flag: <flag>`. Tests: 4 new + 1 existing-test update at `crates/coreutils/tests/ls.rs` (suite 12 → 16): updated `unknown_flag_exits_two_with_stderr` from probing `-a` (now valid) to probing `-Q` (still unknown — pure relabel, same exit-2 / stderr-shape assertions); new `default_hides_dotfiles` (dir with `.hidden` + `visible`, bare `ls <dir>` → `visible\n` only — confirms the new hide-by-default filter); new `dash_a_shows_dotfiles` (same dir + `-a` → `.hidden\nvisible\n` — confirms `.hidden` sorts first ASCII-wise since `.` (0x2E) < `v` (0x76)); new `dash_la_combines_long_format_and_dotfiles` (`.hidden` 2 bytes + `visible` 4 bytes + `-la` → `- 2 .hidden\n- 4 visible\n` — confirms fused token + both flags + long-format row shape); new `dash_a_with_only_dotfiles` (`.foo` + `.bar` + `-a` → `.bar\n.foo\n` — confirms sort order on dotfile-only directories). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, identical isolation-test shape to the prior 12 ls tests + the cat / grep / cp / mkdir / rm / mv suites. Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils --test ls` 16 / 16 pass; full `cargo test -p coreutils` clean (all binaries' suites pass); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "ls")` tuple from 41e8239 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls, errnos, opcodes, or wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `ls` consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::path` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 4 new isolation tests against `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ls")` covering happy-path (default hides / `-a` shows / `-la` combines / `-a` on dotfile-only dir) plus the relabelled regression guard. **Architectural milestone**: sixth per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the now-complete coreutils crate; the flag-parsing convention from `ls -l` / `cp -r` / `mkdir -p` / `rm -r` / `grep -i` ports cleanly to `ls -a` with the small extension of fused-token recognition (`-la` / `-al`). T146 still uncheckered — six flags now in. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `grep -n` (line-number prefix); (b) `grep -v` (invert match); (c) `grep -F` (no-op acceptance — already the implementation); (d) `ls -1` (force one-entry-per-line, no-op acceptance — `-1` is the default unless `-l` flips it); (e) `ls -h` (human-readable sizes — only meaningful with `-l`); (f) `ls -R` (recursive listing — peer to `cp -r` / `rm -r` / `mkdir -p`); (g) `ls -A` (almost-all — same as `-a` minus `.` and `..` synthetic entries; v1 already does this since we don't synthesise them, so `-A` may land as an alias of `-a`); (h) T142 partial — single-quote string handling; (i) T144 follow-up — lift builtins from run.rs to sibling builtin.rs; (j) T097 init unit tests (refactor + tests); (k) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — cat -n line-number flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a4c6ce0eba8fefcd8 — agent shipped feat commit 47972b5 before hitting org-monthly-usage-limit on the docs commit; integrator cherry-picked feat as f2512c3 and wrote docs inline): coreutils-tests crate + ~4 (cat-tests crate 5 → 9). Commit f2512c3. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/cat.rs` (46 → ~95 lines) gains `-n` flag mirroring the established cp -r / mkdir -p / rm -r / grep -i / grep -n / ls -a flag-parsing convention. Two output paths: with `-n`, opens via `BufReader::new(File::open(path))` and iterates `lines()` to interpolate `format!("{:>6}\t{line}", lineno)` per line (GNU/BSD cat style with right-justified 6-char field + tab separator); without `-n`, the existing fast-path `fs::read` + `write_all` bulk write is preserved. Multi-file invocations: line numbers are CONTINUOUS across files (matches GNU cat semantics — the lineno counter is persistent across the file loop, not reset per-file). Stdin mode: line numbers count from 1. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/cat.rs` (+4 tests, suite 5 → 9): `dash_n_prefixes_each_line_with_number`, `dash_n_continues_across_multiple_files`, `dash_n_in_stdin_mode_starts_from_one`, `unknown_flag_exits_one_for_cat` (note cat's existing usage exits 1, not 2 like grep — preserving cat's prior convention). Existing 5 cat tests pass unchanged. `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. **Round-8 process note**: monthly-org-usage-limit hit during round-8 dispatch. The cat -n agent committed its feat just before the limit; the parallel single-quote agent did not commit. Integrator cherry-picked the cat -n feat from the worktree, salvaged the docs commit inline (this entry), and skipped the single-quote slice.
