After the **T142 partial — single-quote string handling slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a1bce051329ab5991, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `1bc6129`, no rebase movement because main was already current): sh-tests crate +6 (new `tests/quoting.rs` 0 → 6, sh total 79 → 85). Commit 2a9f731. Second partial against the Phase 6 US4 T142 tokenizer/parser block (line 287 in tasks.md) — `$VAR` substitution landed in b74a6e3 and now single-quote handling lands here; pipes / redirection / double-quote / `FOO=bar cmd` env-prefix each remain as future single-T142-partial slices. Replaces `crates/sh/src/run.rs`'s `trimmed.split_whitespace()` with a new `pub(crate) fn tokenise_with_single_quotes(line: &str) -> Result<Vec<Vec<TokenPart>>, ()>` that produces per-token segment lists, where `TokenPart` is `Literal(String)` (from inside `'...'`, no expansion) or `Unquoted(String)` (everything else, expansion applies). Adjacent segments concat into one token: `a'bc'd` → `[Unquoted("a"), Literal("bc"), Unquoted("d")]` (three parts, one word); `'a''b'` → `[Literal("a"), Literal("b")]` (two parts, one word). Whitespace inside `'...'` is preserved literally; outside the quotes it splits tokens as before. Unterminated `'` returns `Err(())`; the REPL writes `sh: unterminated single quote\n` to stderr, skips dispatch for that line, and stays alive for the next. The dispatch loop now constructs `let expanded: Vec<String> = parts.iter().map(|t| assemble_token(t, env)).collect()`, where the new `pub(crate) fn assemble_token(parts: &[TokenPart], env: &BTreeMap<String, String>) -> String` walks each token's parts and runs `expand_vars` on `Unquoted` segments while passing `Literal` segments through verbatim — so `$VAR` inside `'...'` does NOT expand. **Design choice**: brief offered approach (a) `Vec<Vec<TokenPart>>` with per-segment expand-or-not OR approximation (b) parallel "any single-quote seen" mask. Picked (a) — clean per-segment semantics with no approximations, so the `a'literal'$X` mixed case (rare but real) already works correctly: `a` and `$X` segments are `Unquoted` and DO expand; `literal` is `Literal` and does NOT. The `TokenPart` enum is `pub(crate)` and structured for future double-quote handling, which the deferred-paragraph in tasks.md documents will plug a third `Quoted` variant onto where `$VAR` still expands but whitespace is preserved. Tests at `crates/sh/tests/quoting.rs` (+6 tests, NEW test file): `single_quoted_preserves_whitespace` (`echo 'hello world'` → stdout contains `hello world\n`), `single_quoted_preserves_dollar_var_literal` (seed `X=1`; `echo '$X'` → stdout contains `$X\n`, NOT `1\n`), `single_quoted_concat_with_unquoted_text` (`echo a'bc'd` → stdout contains `abcd\n`), `two_adjacent_single_quotes_concat_into_one_token` (`echo 'a''b'` → stdout contains `ab\n`), `unterminated_single_quote_errors_to_stderr` (input `echo 'unclosed\nexit\n` → stderr contains `unterminated single quote`, REPL stayed alive for the `exit`), `unquoted_text_outside_quotes_still_expands_dollar_var` (seed `X=hi`; `echo $X 'literal'` → stdout contains `hi literal\n` — confirms expand and literal coexist on one line). Each integration test uses the same `drive(input, env)` helper shape as the prior `tests/env.rs` (T144 4694a1d) and `tests/expand.rs` (T142 b74a6e3) — `BufReader<Cursor<Vec<u8>>>` stdin + `Vec<u8>` stdout/stderr. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p sh` all passing — sh-lib (expand_tests) 13/13, sh-main 0/0, env-tests 11/11, expand-tests 5/5, quoting-tests 6/6, shell-tests 37/37, tokenize-tests 13/13 (85 total in the sh crate; +6 from this slice); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, blocked on other T142 partials or future work): double-quote handling (`"..."` with inner-`$VAR` expansion + backslash escapes — will plug into the same `TokenPart` shape via a third `Quoted` variant); pipes (`|`) + redirection (`>` / `>>` / `<`) — each a future single-T142-partial slice; the `FOO=bar cmd` per-command env-prefix shape; `$$` / `$@` / `$*` / `$0` / `$1` / `$?` / `$!`; arithmetic expansion `$((expr))`; command substitution `$(...)` / backticks; tilde expansion `~/foo`; glob expansion `*.txt`; the `set -u` "error on unset var" mode toggle. **Note on `crates/sh/src/tokenize.rs`**: the existing `tokenize` function (used by `Shell::eval` in shell.rs) already handled single + double quotes for the embedding consumer path, but it returns `Vec<String>` with no per-segment metadata, so it can't distinguish quoted-vs-unquoted bytes for the expand decision. The REPL path (`run_with_env`) needed the richer `Vec<Vec<TokenPart>>` shape to keep the "expand only Unquoted" invariant — so this slice introduces a new tokeniser dedicated to the REPL rather than retrofitting `tokenize.rs`. A future refactor slice could merge them (have `tokenize` return parts and let `Shell::eval` consume them), but the two callers have different needs today. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched — pure userland-shell slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `tokenise_with_single_quotes` and `assemble_token` consume only `BTreeMap` (the env map T144 already wired) and the input line; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — single-quote handling is in-process state, no syscall touched; Principle X (testability) strengthens — both new functions are `pub(crate)`-visible and exercised by 6 integration tests through the REPL driver, so the shape is well-pinned for the deferred double-quote slice that bolts onto the same `TokenPart` enum. **Architectural milestone**: second slice on the post-tokenisation expansion layer (after `$VAR` in b74a6e3) — single-quote handling establishes the `TokenPart` shape that double-quote handling will extend with a `Quoted` variant. The `expand → ref-collect → dispatch` shape from the `$VAR` slice gains a per-segment expand-or-not gate, so the tokenizer + expansion layer is now factored cleanly enough to absorb the remaining quoting / redirection / pipe slices without further refactors. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T142 double-quote handling partial (`"..."` with inner-`$VAR` expansion — plugs into the existing `TokenPart` shape via a `Quoted` variant); (b) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file` — each modifies fd 0/1 before dispatch); (c) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (d) T146 `grep -v` invert-match flag; (e) T146 `ls -1` one-per-line flag; (f) T146 `mv -i` interactive prompt; (g) T097 init unit tests (refactor + tests); (h) T071 final WASI op closing slice (FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS); (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — grep -v invert match flag slice** (inline ship; base `622eb12` after the T142 single-quote docs commit, no rebase movement needed): coreutils-tests crate grep suite 15 → 20 (+5 grep tests). Commit 6f682af. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/grep.rs` (145 → ~155 lines) gains `-v` invert-match flag — when `-v` is set, the emit predicate flips so lines whose substring test is FALSE are emitted instead. The `scan` function gains a new `invert: bool` parameter and the existing emit branch `if hit { … }` becomes `if hit != invert { … }` — the XOR shape keeps the four-arm prefix/line-number `match` block intact and unchanged. Without `-v`, behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged from the prior 16f41aa state. With `-v`, a fully-matching file emits nothing → exit 1 (since "any matched output line" still applies — under `-v` we re-define "matched" to "emitted", so a no-output run is a 1 just like the no-`-v` no-match case). **Architectural shift in flag-parsing**: this slice promotes grep's flag parser from per-arg equality (`if arg == "-i"` / `else if arg == "-n"`) to POSIX-style short-flag clustering — `for ch in arg[1..].chars()` walking each character after the leading `-` and matching `'i'` / `'n'` / `'v'` to the respective bool. This is more idiomatic for grep (POSIX users expect `grep -inv`) and was needed to support the brief's literal `grep -inv FOO` and `grep -nv` and `grep -iv` test invocations without enumerating every permutation as a separate token (the `ls -la` / `ls -al` enumeration approach from c3d1aa7 + 4ca1f20 only handles 2 fused chars and would need 6 enumerations for a 3-flag set). The unknown-char branch reports the FULL fused arg (e.g. `grep -iz` errors with `grep: unknown flag: -iz` — actionable), not just the offending char. Fused-flag clustering only applies to grep's flag char-set today; ls and the others can adopt the same shape in a future refactor or stay on the per-flag enumeration where their flag-set is small. Usage line updated to `usage: grep [-i] [-n] [-v] <pattern> [file ...]`. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/grep.rs` (+5 tests, suite 15 → 20): `dash_v_emits_non_matching_lines` (file `foo\nbar\nfoo\n`, `grep -v foo` → byte-exact `bar\n`); `dash_v_no_match_means_all_lines_pass` (file `aaa\nbbb\n`, `grep -v zzz` → all lines `aaa\nbbb\n` pass through, exit 0); `dash_v_with_dash_i_inverts_case_insensitive` (file `FOO\nbar\nfoo\nbaz\nFoo\n`, `grep -iv foo` → `bar\nbaz\n` — confirms the case-fold AND invert layers compose); `dash_v_combines_with_dash_n` (file `foo\nbar\nfoo\n`, `grep -nv foo` → `2:bar\n` — line numbering still uses the original 1-indexed file position, NOT the post-invert ordinal); `dash_v_no_lines_pass_exits_one` (file `always\nalways\nalways\n`, `grep -v always` → empty stdout, exit 1 — pinning the "no emitted line still means exit 1 under invert" branch). Existing 15 grep tests pass unchanged (pure additions per the slice brief). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils --test grep` 20 / 20 pass; full `cargo test -p coreutils` 82 / 82 pass across all 7 binaries (cat 9, cp 9, grep 20, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `dist/assets/bin/grep.wasm` rebuilds with the invert branch + cluster parser. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "grep")` tuple from f667018 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — grep consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 5 new isolation tests pin the invert semantics across stand-alone (`-v`), case-fold composition (`-iv`), line-number composition (`-nv`), pure-pass-through (no match → all lines), and pure-fail (full match → exit 1) shapes. **Architectural milestone**: seventh per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate; first slice to introduce POSIX-style short-flag clustering inside one binary's parser, leaving the other binaries' simpler per-arg shapes intact. The XOR (`hit != invert`) emit predicate is the canonical pattern for "flag flips a boolean predicate" and ports cleanly to any future binary that grows an inversion flag (`tail -f` / `find ! -name` / etc., none currently in scope). T146 still uncheckered — seven flags now in (`ls -l`, `cp -r`, `mkdir -p`, `rm -r`, `grep -i`, `grep -n`, `ls -a`, `cat -n`, `grep -v`). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T142 double-quote handling partial (`"..."` with inner-`$VAR` expansion — plugs into the existing `TokenPart` shape via a `Quoted` variant); (b) T142 redirection partial; (c) T146 `ls -1` one-per-line flag (no-op acceptance — `-1` is the default unless `-l` flips it); (d) T146 `ls -R` recursive listing; (e) T146 `mv -i` interactive prompt (needs a stdin-reading interaction harness); (f) T146 `grep -F` no-op acceptance (already the implementation); (g) T097 init unit tests (refactor + tests); (h) T071 final WASI op closing slice (FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS); (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T142 partial — double-quote string handling slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-ab5667050bd35ccb5, self-rebased cleanly onto local main per the round-12 postmortem lesson — base `1bc6129` → post-rebase HEAD `622eb12`, picking up the single-quote slice 2a9f731 as a base): sh-tests crate +6 (`tests/quoting.rs` 6 → 12, sh total 85 → 91). Commit afbf05b. Third partial against the Phase 6 US4 T142 tokenizer/parser block (line 287 in tasks.md) — `$VAR` substitution landed in b74a6e3, single-quote handling in 2a9f731, and now double-quote handling lands here; pipes / redirection / `FOO=bar cmd` env-prefix / backslash-escapes-inside-`"..."` each remain as future single-T142-partial slices. Renames `pub(crate) fn tokenise_with_single_quotes(line: &str) -> Result<Vec<Vec<TokenPart>>, ()>` to `pub(crate) fn tokenise_with_quotes(line: &str) -> Result<Vec<Vec<TokenPart>>, QuoteError>`. The error type changes from `()` to a new `pub(crate) enum QuoteError { UnterminatedSingle, UnterminatedDouble }` so the REPL caller can surface a kind-aware "sh: unterminated single quote\n" / "sh: unterminated double quote\n" message — the exact same recoverable-error shape as the single-quote slice (write to stderr, skip dispatch for that line, REPL stays alive). The state-machine inside the tokeniser swaps the prior `bool in_single` flag for a new private `enum QuoteState { Outside, InSingle, InDouble }` and a `match state { ... }` per character. Inside `'...'` (`InSingle`) bytes accumulate into a `TokenPart::Literal` segment — UNCHANGED from the prior slice. Inside `"..."` (`InDouble`) bytes accumulate into a `TokenPart::Unquoted` segment — KEY DESIGN DECISION: rather than introducing a new third `Quoted` variant on `TokenPart` (which the prior slice's deferred-paragraph had previewed), this slice routes double-quoted bytes through the existing `Unquoted` variant. The reasoning: `assemble_token` already runs `expand_vars` over `Unquoted` segments, which is exactly what double quotes need (`$VAR` / `${VAR}` DO expand inside `"..."`); the only behaviour `"..."` differs from bare-Unquoted on is whitespace-splitting suppression, which is handled UPSTREAM at the tokeniser level (the `InDouble` branch never closes the current token on whitespace), not in the `assemble` step. So the `TokenPart` enum stays at two variants — simpler than the brief preview, no API churn for downstream callers — and the "preserves whitespace, still expands" semantics emerge from how the tokeniser opens and closes the segment. The Outside arm of the state machine adds a fourth case for `'"'` parallel to the `'\''` case: flush any pending unquoted bytes into the current token, then transition to `InDouble`. Adjacency with bare text (`a"$X"b`) concatenates into one word with three Unquoted parts, the same way single-quoted adjacency works (`a'bc'd` → three parts, one word). Mixed-quote concat (`"$X"'$Y'`) yields one word with two parts: an Unquoted `$X` (expands) + a Literal `$Y` (stays literal); since adjacency without intervening whitespace already concatenates segments into the SAME word, this works without any special-cased mixed-quote handling — the existing state-machine + `assemble_token` shape carries it for free. Tests at `crates/sh/tests/quoting.rs` (+6 tests, suite 6 → 12; the prior 6 single-quote tests are unmodified per the brief): `double_quoted_preserves_whitespace` (`echo "hello world"` → stdout contains `hello world\n`), `double_quoted_expands_dollar_var` (seed `X=1`; `echo "$X"` → stdout contains `1\n` AND NOT `$X\n` — guards both directions of the expand-inside-double-quotes invariant), `double_quoted_with_braced_var_expansion` (seed `X=hi`; `echo "${X}there"` → stdout contains `hithere\n` — confirms the braced form works inside double quotes via the existing Unquoted-path expand_vars walker), `double_and_single_quote_concat_in_one_token` (seed `X=1`; `echo "$X"'$Y'` → stdout contains `1$Y\n` — Y is unset so the `$Y` literal is byte-exact proof that single quotes blocked expansion while double quotes allowed it on the same line), `unterminated_double_quote_errors_to_stderr` (`echo "unclosed\nexit\n` → stderr contains `unterminated double quote`, REPL stayed alive for the `exit`), `double_quoted_inside_concat_with_unquoted_text` (seed `X=mid`; `echo a"$X"b` → stdout contains `amidb\n` — direct mirror of the single-quote `single_quoted_concat_with_unquoted_text` test, but the middle segment EXPANDS instead of staying literal). The module-doc at the top of `tests/quoting.rs` is rewritten to describe both quote kinds + the per-segment expand/no-expand semantics. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p sh` all passing — sh-lib (expand_tests) 13/13, sh-main 0/0, env-tests 11/11, expand-tests 5/5, quoting-tests 12/12, shell-tests 37/37, tokenize-tests 13/13 (91 total in the sh crate; +6 from this slice); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, blocked on other T142 partials or future work): backslash escapes inside `"..."` (`"\$X"` should NOT expand `$X`, `"\""` should embed a literal `"` inside the quotes, `"\\"` should yield a single `\` — none are handled in afbf05b, so the leading `\` is preserved verbatim by the tokeniser and `expand_vars` then processes the rest, meaning `"\$X"` currently yields the value of X with a leading backslash; future micro-slice will add a backslash-escape pass inside `"..."`); pipes (`|`) + redirection (`>` / `>>` / `<`) — each a future single-T142-partial slice; the `FOO=bar cmd` per-command env-prefix shape; `$$` / `$@` / `$*` / `$0` / `$1` / `$?` / `$!`; arithmetic expansion `$((expr))`; command substitution `$(...)` / backticks; tilde expansion `~/foo`; glob expansion `*.txt`; the `set -u` "error on unset var" mode toggle. No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched — pure userland-shell slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `tokenise_with_quotes` and `assemble_token` consume only `BTreeMap` (the env map T144 already wired) and the input line; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — quote handling is in-process state, no syscall touched; Principle X (testability) strengthens — `QuoteError` and the renamed `tokenise_with_quotes` are `pub(crate)`-visible and exercised by 12 integration tests through the REPL driver. **Architectural milestone**: third slice on the post-tokenisation expansion layer (after `$VAR` in b74a6e3 and single-quote in 2a9f731). The previewed `Quoted` variant on `TokenPart` was NOT needed — routing double-quoted bytes through the existing `Unquoted` variant + handling the whitespace-suppression at tokeniser scope is strictly simpler and produces identical semantics for the in-scope cases (free-form expansion of `$VAR` / `${VAR}` inside the quotes; literal whitespace preservation). The `TokenPart` enum stays two variants, making the future backslash-escape slice a pure upstream tokeniser-loop change with no downstream callers affected. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T142 backslash-escape-inside-double-quotes micro-slice (`"\$X"` literal `$X`, `"\""` literal `"`, `"\\"` literal `\` — pure tokeniser-state change at the `InDouble` branch); (b) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (c) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (d) T146 `grep -v` invert-match flag; (e) T146 `ls -1` one-per-line flag; (f) T146 `mv -i` interactive prompt; (g) T097 init unit tests; (h) T071 final WASI op closing slice (FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS); (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — grep -c count-only flag slice** (inline ship; base `0a4bdc8` after the T142 double-quote docs commit, no rebase movement needed): coreutils-tests crate grep suite 20 → 25 (+5 grep tests). Commit 34671c9. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/grep.rs` (~155 → ~180 lines) gains `-c` count-only flag — when `-c` is set, the `scan` function suppresses the per-line `writeln!` emit and accumulates a `count: u64` counter; after the read loop it emits a single `<count>\n` line (or `<path>:<count>\n` when `prefix.is_some()` — i.e., multi-file invocations). The `scan` signature gains a new `count_only: bool` parameter that fans out to (a) the inside-match branch where `if count_only { count += 1 } else { /* existing four-arm match block */ }` and (b) the function-end emit step + the early-return-on-read-error path (so partial counts still get a well-formed line). The `any` aggregator continues to flip `true` on any predicate-passing line — under `-c`, that means `any == (count > 0)` per the brief, so the existing exit-code aggregator at main scope works unchanged: 0 if any file reports count > 0, 1 if all report 0; a file with count=0 still emits `0\n`. Stdin mode (no file args) emits `<count>\n` with no path prefix. Combinable with `-i`, `-n`, `-v`: under `-c`, the `-n` line-number prefix is suppressed (line numbers don't make sense for a single count line — handled implicitly because the count emit path doesn't consult `with_line_numbers`); `-cv` counts the NON-matching lines (the existing `hit != invert` predicate inside `scan` already covers this — count_only only changes WHAT is emitted, not WHICH lines pass the predicate). The parser (promoted to short-flag clustering in 6f682af) gains `'c'` to the recognised char-set in the `match ch` arm; `grep -cv` / `grep -ic` / `grep -cnv` / etc. fused forms work out of the box. Usage line updated: `usage: grep [-i] [-n] [-v] [-c] <pattern> [file ...]`. New private helper `fn emit_count<W: Write>(out: &mut W, prefix: Option<&str>, count: u64)` factors the path-vs-no-path emit shape so both the loop-end and read-error-path callsites stay one-line. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/grep.rs` (+5 tests, suite 20 → 25): `dash_c_emits_match_count_for_single_file` (file `foo\nbar\nfoo\n`, `grep -c foo` → byte-exact `2\n`); `dash_c_emits_zero_for_no_matches` (file `alpha\nbeta\ngamma\n`, `grep -c xxx` → byte-exact `0\n`, exit 1 — pinning the "count=0 still emits `0\n`, exit-1" branch); `dash_c_with_multi_file_prefixes_path` (two files `foo\nbar\n` and `foo\nfoo\nbaz\n`, `grep -c foo a b` → `<a>:1\n<b>:2\n` — confirms multi-file path prefix shape mirrors the existing `-n` multi-file shape from 16f41aa); `dash_c_combines_with_dash_v_for_non_matching_count` (file `foo\nbar\nfoo\nbaz\n`, `grep -cv foo` → byte-exact `2\n` — confirms invert + count compose at the predicate-vs-emit boundary); `dash_c_in_stdin_mode_emits_count` (pipe `foo\nbar\nfoo\n` to `grep -c foo` → byte-exact `2\n` — confirms stdin path uses no-prefix shape). Existing 20 grep tests pass unchanged (pure additions per the slice brief). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils --test grep` 25 / 25 pass; full `cargo test -p coreutils` 87 / 87 pass across all 7 binaries (cat 9, cp 9, grep 25, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "grep")` tuple from f667018 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — grep consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 5 new isolation tests pin the count-only semantics across single-file (`-c`), zero-match (`-c`, exit 1, count=0 line still emitted), multi-file prefix (`-c <a> <b>`), invert composition (`-cv`), and stdin no-prefix shape. **Architectural milestone**: eighth per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate; second slice on grep's POSIX-style short-flag clustering parser (`-cv` / `-ic` / `-cnv` all work for free thanks to the cluster shape from 6f682af). The `count_only` shape — predicate unchanged, emit path branches between per-line and end-of-loop summary — is the canonical pattern for "flag changes WHAT to emit, not WHICH lines pass" and ports cleanly to any future grep flag in the same family (`-l` filenames-only, `-q` quiet mode). T146 still uncheckered — eight grep flag combinations now in (`-i`, `-n`, `-v`, `-c`, plus all the cluster forms) alongside `ls -l` / `ls -a` / `cp -r` / `mkdir -p` / `rm -r` / `cat -n`. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T142 backslash-escape-inside-double-quotes micro-slice (`"\$X"` literal `$X`, `"\""` literal `"`, `"\\"` literal `\` — pure tokeniser-state change at the `InDouble` branch); (b) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (c) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (d) T146 `ls -1` one-per-line flag; (e) T146 `mv -i` interactive prompt; (f) T146 `grep -l` filenames-only flag (mirrors `-c` shape — predicate unchanged, emit path swaps to "first match → emit path + return"); (g) T146 `grep -q` quiet mode; (h) T097 init unit tests; (i) T071 final WASI op closing slice (FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS); (j) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T071 closing — FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS stub handler slice**: kernel-tests crate +2 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 329 → 331 net; one `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe was rotated to a synthetic unallocated WASI-range opcode, two new `fd_fdstat_set_rights_*` tests were added). Commit fe198de. Final landing slice on the T071 WASI preview 1 handler block (line 137 in tasks.md) — every named WASI preview 1 opcode in `abi::wasi::op` now has a dispatch arm in `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs::dispatch_wasi`, so the wasi.rs surface is officially closed. T071 checkbox flipped from `[ ]` to `[x]`. The last-unhandled opcode `FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS` (0x0026) routes to a new `handle_fd_fdstat_set_rights(req: &Request) -> Response` that returns `ENOTSUP` rather than falling through the catch-all `_ => Response::err(req.request_id, ENOSYS)` arm. **Design choice — ENOTSUP over ESUCCESS**: PMos uses its own capability system (the extension `cap_*` syscalls at 0x1300..0x1302 — `cap_check`, `cap_list`, future `cap_grant`) gating sensitive operations against `Process.caps`, not WASI's per-fd rights vectors. Rights narrowing therefore has no semantic meaning in PMos's model — there IS no per-fd rights vector to narrow. Returning `ESUCCESS` would be a lie that lets userland assume rights were narrowed when they weren't (a security-relevant lie if WASI userland leans on the result for capability enforcement); `ENOTSUP` is the honest answer. The handler doesn't bother resolving the fd before returning because `ENOTSUP` takes precedence over `EBADF` — the operation itself is unsupported regardless of fd validity, so checking the fd first would just add noise. The deviation note in `contracts/syscalls.md §2` ("`fd_fdstat_set_rights` is honoured: if the caller tries to widen a right, `ENOTCAPABLE` is returned") is left alone for the v2 amendment slice if PMos ever decides to track WASI rights vectors per-fd; for v1 the spec's deviation column will need an addendum noting that PMos doesn't track WASI rights at all and returns `ENOTSUP` for the entire operation, but that copy edit is out of scope for this closing slice (touches `contracts/syscalls.md` which is a documentation-layer concern + the slice prompt explicitly says "stub" — fully wiring the rights-tracking semantic would be a different, bigger task). **Tests** at `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: `fd_fdstat_set_rights_returns_enotsup` (real fd 10 against a tmpfs file, expects `resp.status == -ENOTSUP` AND `resp.request_id == 930`) + `fd_fdstat_set_rights_with_invalid_fd_still_returns_enotsup` (fd=999, no `install_fd`, expects `resp.status == -ENOTSUP` not `-EBADF` — pins the precedence invariant). The pre-existing `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` test had been probing `FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS` itself ("This probe was `FD_PRESTAT_DIR_NAME` before that handler landed, then `PATH_READLINK`, `PATH_SYMLINK`, `PATH_LINK`, `SOCK_SHUTDOWN`, `FD_PREAD`, `FD_READDIR`. When `FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS` grows a real handler — if ever — swap this probe to whatever's still unhandled at that point, or delete the test once every WASI opcode has real coverage." — its own comment predicted today's rotation); kept the test alive by swapping the probe to a synthetic unallocated WASI-range opcode at `0x0005` (which sits in the gap between `ENVIRON_SIZES_GET` 0x0004 and `CLOCK_RES_GET` 0x0010 in `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` — `is_wasi(0x0005)` returns true since the WASI range is `0x0001..0x0080`, so the dispatcher routes it to `dispatch_wasi`, the `_ =>` arm fires, and `ENOSYS` echoes back). This keeps the regression guard for any future opcode allocation that lands without a handler. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p kernel` 723 / 723 pass (50 + 19 + 25 + 18 + 36 + 36 + 20 + 36 + 97 + 331 + 55 across the 11 test binaries; syscall.rs 329 → 331 net +2); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p kernel --no-default-features` clean (kernel cdylib release build that the bootstrap loads — critical because kernel work can break the cdylib that JS imports). No abi opcode constant added (`FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS = 0x0026` was already present at `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs:40` since the original WASI opcode-constant block); no new errno (using existing `ENOTSUP = 58`); no new platform hook; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed beyond the verification step; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched — pure kernel-internal slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the handler consumes only `Request` + `Response` from `abi::ring`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) STRENGTHENS — every WASI preview 1 opcode now has a dispatch arm so userland WASI binaries (even ones that erroneously call `fd_fdstat_set_rights` via stale stdlib) get a proper `ENOTSUP` rather than a misleading catch-all `ENOSYS`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 2 new isolation tests pin both the happy path AND the precedence invariant. **Architectural milestone**: T071 is the eighteenth top-level task in the kernel-syscall block to flip from `[ ]` to `[x]`, joining the original Phase 2 Foundational tasks. The "every opcode" framing in the task text is now literal: a `grep -c "op::" crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs` shows 47 match arms (the 46 named WASI opcodes + the `_ =>` catch-all), which lines up with the 46 named opcodes in `abi::wasi::op`. Extension opcodes (T072 — `ipc_*`, `proc_spawn`/`wait`/`kill`/`self`/`parent`/`caps_get`, `display_connect`, `cap_*`, `mount`/`umount`, `fs_watch`, `host_file_recv`) live on a separate dispatch path (`crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs::dispatch_ext`) and stay `[ ] partial` — out of scope for the wasi.rs surface T071 owns. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings (`ipc_*` / remaining `proc_*` / `display_connect` / `cap_grant` / `mount`/`umount` / `fs_watch` / `host_file_recv` — each is a separate per-opcode slice on `ext.rs`); (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for the previously-unhandled WASI opcodes that landed semantic coverage but lack syscall.rs syscall-router coverage (`FD_TELL`, `FD_ADVISE`, `FD_ALLOCATE`, `FD_SYNC`, `FD_DATASYNC`, `CLOCK_*`, `RANDOM_GET`, `SCHED_YIELD`, `ARGS_*`, `ENVIRON_*`, `FD_FILESTAT_GET`, `PATH_FILESTAT_GET`, `PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES`, `FD_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES`, `FD_RENUMBER`, `PATH_UNLINK_FILE`, `PATH_RENAME`, `PATH_LINK`, `PATH_SYMLINK`, `PATH_READLINK`, `PATH_CREATE_DIRECTORY`, `PATH_REMOVE_DIRECTORY`, `FD_FDSTAT_SET_FLAGS`, `FD_FILESTAT_SET_SIZE`, `FD_PREAD`, `FD_PWRITE`, `SOCK_*`, `FD_READDIR`, `POLL_ONEOFF`, `FD_PRESTAT_GET`, `FD_PRESTAT_DIR_NAME`); (c) T142 backslash-escape-inside-double-quotes micro-slice; (d) T142 redirection partial; (e) T146 `ls -1` / `ls -R` / `mv -i` / `grep -F`; (f) T097 init unit tests; (g) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — cat -E show line-end markers slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-acf625bfe4e9c1d92, base `948b1d3` — clean, no rebase movement needed): coreutils-tests crate cat suite 9 → 13 (+4 cat tests). Commit b983ff6. Ninth per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate (after `ls -l` c3d1aa7, `cp -r` b2623ff, `mkdir -p` bbf6b58, `rm -r` 26f50ad, `grep -i` 8ad3297, `grep -n` 16f41aa, `ls -a` 4ca1f20, `cat -n` f2512c3, `grep -v` 6f682af, `grep -c` 34671c9). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/cat.rs` (~131 → ~157 lines) gains `-E` show-line-ends flag — when `-E` is set, cat appends a `$` to the end of each line immediately before the `\n`. The flag composes with `-n` (`cat -nE` → `     1\thello$\n     2\tworld$\n`) and works in stdin mode + multi-file mode. Implementation: the existing `-n` line-by-line path is the natural home for `-E` since both flags need per-line formatting; `fs::read` + bulk-`write_all` can NOT be used with `-E` (we need to inject `$` per line). The slice promotes the parser to **POSIX-style short-flag clustering**, mirroring grep's pattern from 6f682af (and which `grep -c` 34671c9 fully exercised across `-cv` / `-ic` / `-cnv` cluster forms): the prior single-flag-at-a-time `if arg == "-n"` branch becomes a `for ch in arg[1..].chars() { match ch { 'n' => ..., 'E' => ..., _ => unknown-flag } }` per-character loop. So `-nE` and `-En` both parse as both flags, and the existing `-n` cluster forms keep working unchanged. Function signature rename: `number_reader` → `format_reader` since the line-handling path now formats in two dimensions (lineno prefix + `$` suffix), not just one. The new signature is `format_reader<R: BufRead, W: Write>(r, lineno, out, label, number_lines: bool, show_ends: bool) -> io::Result<()>`. Inside the loop, the format uses a small `let suffix = if show_ends { "$" } else { "" }` then branches on `number_lines` to pick between `writeln!(out, "{:>6}\t{s}{suffix}", *lineno)` and `writeln!(out, "{s}{suffix}")` — neither bulk-allocates, suffix is a `&'static str`. Two-path mode selection moved one level up: `let line_mode = number_lines || show_ends;` decides between the bulk-read fast path (when neither flag is set) and the line-by-line path (when either flag is set). The `BufReader::lines()` semantics from f2512c3 carry over verbatim — strips trailing `\n` (we re-emit one via `writeln!`) and treats missing-final-newline as a single trailing line, matching GNU `cat -E`. `lineno` continues to count CONTINUOUSLY across multiple file args under `-n` (whether or not `-E` is also set); under `-E` alone there's no visible counter, just `$` markers per line. Multi-file `cat -E a b` emits `$` markers on every line with no separator between files (mirrors plain multi-file cat's no-separator shape). Stdin mode `cat -E` (no path args) uses the same line-by-line path with label `"stdin"` for any I/O error diagnostic. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/cat.rs` (+4 tests, suite 9 → 13): `dash_E_appends_dollar_to_line_ends` (file `hello\nworld\n`, `cat -E` → byte-exact `hello$\nworld$\n`); `dash_nE_combines_line_numbers_with_dollar_markers` (file `hello\nworld\n`, `cat -nE` → byte-exact `     1\thello$\n     2\tworld$\n` — pins both the cluster-parse AND the format-order invariant: line number first, then `$` at end); `dash_E_in_stdin_mode` (pipe `foo\nbar\n` to `cat -E` → byte-exact `foo$\nbar$\n`); `dash_E_multi_file_continues` (two files `alpha\nbravo\n` and `charlie\ndelta\n`, `cat -E a b` → byte-exact `alpha$\nbravo$\ncharlie$\ndelta$\n` — confirms no inter-file separator). Each test marked `#[allow(non_snake_case)]` to keep the brief-specified test names verbatim while satisfying the `non_snake_case_functions` lint (the upper-case `E` in `dash_E_*` would otherwise warn — same shape future `cat -T` / `cat -A` slices can mirror). Existing 9 cat tests pass unchanged (pure additions per the slice brief). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils --test cat` 13 / 13 pass; full `cargo test -p coreutils` 91 / 91 pass across all 7 binaries (cat 13, cp 9, grep 25, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "cat")` tuple from 2655551 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — cat consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 4 new isolation tests pin the cat -E semantics across single-file (`-E`), cluster-parse (`-nE`), stdin no-prefix shape, and multi-file continuation. **Architectural milestone**: ninth per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate; first cat slice to use the cluster-parse pattern (the original `cat -n` f2512c3 had a single-flag parser since it shipped before the cluster pattern was on the table). The `format_reader` shape — one helper, two boolean flags, a per-line format branch — is the canonical pattern for "line-by-line cat output with optional decorations" and ports cleanly to any future cat decoration flag (`-T` show-tabs, `-A` show-all = `-vET`, `-b` number-non-blank-only). T146 still uncheckered — flag combinations now in (cat: `-n`, `-E`, plus cluster forms; grep: `-i`, `-n`, `-v`, `-c`, plus cluster forms; ls: `-l`, `-a`; cp: `-r`; mkdir: `-p`; rm: `-r`). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 backslash-escape-inside-double-quotes micro-slice; (d) T142 redirection partial; (e) T146 `ls -1` / `ls -R` / `mv -i` / `grep -F` / `cat -T` / `cat -A` / `cat -b`; (f) T097 init unit tests; (g) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T144 follow-up — lift builtins to crates/sh/src/builtin.rs slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a6895e9a718f56e64; base `948b1d3`, no rebase movement needed): pure refactor — zero behaviour change — file moves only. Commit 0f2e5b9. T144's tasks.md spec line 289 specifies builtins live in `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs`; the T123 implementation kept them inside `run.rs` and the existing T144 partial annotation explicitly listed "relocating the builtin implementations from `crates/sh/src/run.rs` to `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` per T144's tasks.md filename hint" as deferred. This slice closes that deferral. **Moved from `crates/sh/src/run.rs` to a new `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` (263 lines)**: `BuiltinOutcome` enum (`pub(crate)` — Continue / Exit(i32) / IoError / NotBuiltin); `dispatch_builtin` function (`pub(crate)` — same signature, dispatches `tokens[0]` against the seven-name builtin set); each `builtin_*` function (private — `builtin_echo`, `builtin_exit`, `builtin_cd`, `builtin_pwd`, `builtin_env`, `builtin_export`, `builtin_unset`); the private `normalise(path: &Path) -> PathBuf` helper that only `builtin_cd` consumed (collapses `.` / `..` / repeated `/` segments). **Stayed in `run.rs` (tokeniser concerns, not builtins)**: `expand_vars` (the `$NAME` / `${NAME}` walker — `pub(crate)`), `tokenise_with_quotes` (`pub(crate)` — quote-aware tokeniser), `assemble_token` (`pub(crate)` — runs `expand_vars` over `Unquoted` segments + passes `Literal` through), `is_name_start` / `is_name_continue` (private byte-range helpers), `TokenPart` enum (`pub(crate)` — Literal / Unquoted), `QuoteError` enum (`pub(crate)` — UnterminatedSingle / UnterminatedDouble), `QuoteState` private internal-state enum, plus the `expand_tests` inline `#[cfg(test)] mod` (13 tests on `expand_vars` directly). `crates/sh/src/lib.rs` declares `mod builtin;` (private to the crate, `#[cfg(feature = "std")]`-gated like `run` — `run.rs` is the sole consumer; matches the project's "minimum visibility" pattern rather than the public `pub mod shell` / `pub mod tokenize` exposed for external embedders). `run.rs` consumes via `use crate::builtin::{dispatch_builtin, BuiltinOutcome}`; the `pub(crate)` visibility on the moved items still works after the move because both files live in the same crate. **No test imports needed updating** — every existing test in the sh crate goes through the public `run` / `run_with_env` API, so no `crates/sh/tests/*.rs` file was touched. Test counts unchanged: 91 passing tests across 8 binaries (`sh::expand_tests` lib inline 13, `tests/env.rs` 11, `tests/expand.rs` 5, `tests/quoting.rs` 12, `tests/shell.rs` 37, `tests/tokenize.rs` 13, plus two zero-test artefact lines: lib unit-tests crate 0 and doctests 0 — same shape as the pre-refactor baseline). **Verification**: `cargo test -p sh` → 91 passing identical to baseline (each of the 6 non-zero test binaries pinned at the same count: 13 / 11 / 5 / 12 / 37 / 13); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. Files: `crates/sh/src/run.rs` 805 → 563 lines (-242); `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` 0 → 263 lines (+263); `crates/sh/src/lib.rs` 46 → 48 lines (+2 — added `#[cfg(feature = "std")] mod builtin;` declaration). Net diff: 3 files changed, 268 insertions, 245 deletions — most of the new lines are file headers + the relocated module-level `//!` doc-comment explaining the layout. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS / display-server / toolkit / coreutils changes — pure intra-sh-crate refactor. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — refactor is internal to the `sh` crate, no public-API surface touched (`run` / `run_with_env` / `ExitStatus` re-exports unchanged); Principle X (testability) NEUTRAL — same test count, same coverage shape, files now live where the spec said they should from the start. **Architectural milestone**: closes the last deferred item from T144's "Explicitly deferred" list besides the `export -p` / full POSIX `env` / identifier-charset items that are scope-bound to future feature slices, not refactors. Updated tasks.md line 289: appended `; builtins lifted to builtin.rs in 0f2e5b9` to the partial-annotation prefix and dropped the "relocating the builtin implementations from..." sentence from the deferred-paragraph. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 backslash-escape-inside-double-quotes micro-slice; (d) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (e) T146 `ls -1` / `ls -R` / `mv -i` / `grep -F` / `grep -l` / `grep -q`; (f) T097 init unit tests; (g) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — cp -n no-clobber flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a80ef0607d7c42124; self-rebased onto local main HEAD `07c1270`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate cp suite 9 → 12 (+3 cp tests). Commit bb49d13. Eleventh per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate (after `ls -l` c3d1aa7, `cp -r` b2623ff, `mkdir -p` bbf6b58, `rm -r` 26f50ad, `grep -i` 8ad3297, `grep -n` 16f41aa, `ls -a` 4ca1f20, `cat -n` f2512c3, `grep -v` 6f682af, `grep -c` 34671c9, `cat -E` b983ff6). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/cp.rs` (~159 → ~175 lines) gains `-n` (no-clobber) flag — when `-n` is set, an existing dst is silently skipped instead of overwritten, matching GNU `cp -n` behaviour. Without the flag, behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged from the prior `cp -r` slice (POSIX clobber default). The flag is global to the invocation: `cp -n a b` skips if `b` exists; `cp -rn srcdir dstdir` walks the tree and skips per-file inside `copy_tree` for any file whose dst already exists. CLI parsing follows the established pattern from b2623ff (cp -r): single-pass arg split into `flags` + `paths` buckets via the existing for-loop; `-n` toggles `no_clobber = true` in a new branch alongside the `-r` / `-R` branch; everything else is unchanged. Module doc comment updated to describe the new `-n` paragraph and to drop `-n` from the **Explicitly deferred** list (the deferred item just landed); `-i` / `-v` / `-p` / preserve-symlinks / multi-source variadic are still deferred. **Single-file mode**: before `fs::write(dst_path, &bytes)`, a new `if no_clobber && fs::symlink_metadata(dst_path).is_ok() { return ExitCode::from(0); }` short-circuit fires when dst exists. The probe uses `symlink_metadata` (not `Path::exists`) deliberately — `Path::exists` follows symlinks and would say "doesn't exist" for a broken symlink at dst; GNU `cp -n` treats a broken symlink as "exists, skip". Symlink-aware probe matches that and is the same convention `copy_tree` already uses for the dst-tree-already-exists check. Exit 0 is the GNU `cp -n` shape — no stderr, no stdout, no diagnostic; the skip is silent (a future `-v` slice can add `cp: '<dst>' not overwritten` chatter). **`-r` mode**: the `copy_tree` function gains a third `no_clobber: bool` parameter (recursive, threads through every recursion level). Inside the `for entry in fs::read_dir(...)` loop's else-branch (the file copy path, not the subdirectory descent), a new pre-check fires before `fs::copy(entry.path(), &entry_dst)`: `if no_clobber && fs::symlink_metadata(&entry_dst).is_ok() { continue; }`. So existing files inside the dst tree are skipped, missing files are copied. Subdirectories are still descended unconditionally — so a partially-populated tree fills in only its missing leaves while preserving the existing ones. The recursive call `copy_tree(&entry.path(), &entry_dst, no_clobber)` propagates the flag down so nested dst trees behave identically. The dst-root pre-existence check in `copy_tree` (the `m.is_dir() => {}` arm that accepts an existing dst directory as the landing target) is not affected — `-n` only governs leaf-file overwrites, not whether the recursive walk can use an existing parent dir as the root. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/cp.rs` (+3 tests, suite 9 → 12): `dash_n_skips_existing_dst_file` (pre-create `dst.txt` with bytes `"old"`, `cp -n src dst` → exit 0, no stderr, dst still byte-equal `"old"`); `dash_n_writes_to_nonexistent_dst` (no pre-existing dst, `cp -n src dst` → exit 0, no stderr, dst byte-equal src content `"fresh content"`); `dash_n_with_dash_r_skips_existing_files_in_tree` (src has `kept.txt` + `fresh.txt`; pre-create `dst/src/` with `kept.txt` containing `"existing"`; `cp -r -n src dst` → exit 0, no stderr, `dst/src/kept.txt` still byte-equal `"existing"` (preserved), `dst/src/fresh.txt` byte-equal `"src fresh"` (newly copied) — pins both halves of the partial-tree merge invariant in one test). Existing 9 cp tests pass unchanged (the `overwrites_existing_dst` test runs without `-n` so the POSIX clobber default is unaffected; the `wrong_arg_count_exits_one_with_usage` substring matcher continues to find `cp <src> <dst>` in the usage line; the `dash_r_clobbering_files_inside_dst_works` test runs with `-r` only — no `-n` — so the unconditional file-overwrite path inside `copy_tree` keeps clobbering as before). **Verification**: `cargo test -p coreutils` 94 / 94 pass across all 7 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No xtask change needed (the `("coreutils", "cp")` tuple from 56904e9 already ships the binary). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — cp consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::path` / `std::process`; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 3 new isolation tests pin the `cp -n` semantics across single-file existing-dst skip, single-file fresh-dst write, and `-rn` partial-tree merge. **Architectural milestone**: eleventh per-binary flag-follow-up slice on the coreutils crate; cp's flag set is now `-r` + `-n` (mirrors GNU cp's two most-used non-preservation flags). The `no_clobber: bool` thread-through pattern in `copy_tree` is the canonical shape for any future per-leaf gating flag in cp (`-i` interactive prompt, `-u` update-only-if-newer — both will follow the same "extra bool param threaded down the recursion" extension). T146 still uncheckered — flag combinations now in (cat: `-n`, `-E`, plus cluster forms; grep: `-i`, `-n`, `-v`, `-c`, plus cluster forms; ls: `-l`, `-a`; cp: `-r`, `-n`; mkdir: `-p`; rm: `-r`). After the **T142 partial — backslash escapes inside double quotes slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a430cafb3ea7ae2fe; base `07c1270`, no rebase movement needed): sh-tests crate quoting suite 12 → 18 (+6 tests, sh total 91 → 97). Commit 5612b8f. Fourth partial against the Phase 6 US4 T142 tokenizer/parser block (line 287 in tasks.md) — `$VAR` substitution landed in b74a6e3, single-quote handling in 2a9f731, double-quote handling in afbf05b, and now backslash-escapes-inside-`"..."` lands here; pipes / redirection / `FOO=bar cmd` env-prefix each remain as future single-T142-partial slices. Inside `"..."`, recognise three backslash escapes — `\$` → literal `$` (suppresses `$VAR` expansion at that position), `\"` → literal `"` (does NOT close the double quote), `\\` → literal `\`. Any other `\<char>` is preserved as the two-byte sequence `\<char>` (so `\n` inside `"..."` stays the two bytes `\n`, NOT a newline) — `\` is not consumed in the unrecognised case, matching POSIX/bash's "preserve the backslash unless followed by a special char" semantics inside double quotes. Outside `"..."` (in unquoted text or inside `'...'`) backslash is preserved as a literal byte — existing behaviour unchanged, the slice does NOT touch the Outside or InSingle branches of the state machine. **KEY DESIGN DECISION — Literal-segment routing for expansion suppression**: `\$` inside `"..."` must suppress `$VAR` expansion at that one position while preserving expansion of any other `$VAR` in the same quoted span. The cleanest path uses the existing `TokenPart` enum (Literal / Unquoted) — when `\$` is recognised, flush the in-progress `Unquoted` buffer (if non-empty), then push a fresh `TokenPart::Literal("$".to_string())` segment, then resume accumulating into a new `Unquoted` buffer. `assemble_token` already runs `expand_vars` only over `Unquoted` segments + passes `Literal` through verbatim, so the `$` byte inside a `Literal` segment is invisible to `expand_vars` and the surrounding `Unquoted` segments still get their `$VAR` references expanded. Same shape applies to `\"` (literal `"` segment) and `\\` (literal `\` segment) for consistency, even though `"` and `\` aren't expansion-relevant — the segmentation cost is one Vec push per escape, nothing else changes. The `TokenPart` enum stays at two variants (Literal / Unquoted) — no new `Escaped` variant needed; the existing two-variant shape carries the slice for free. **Iterator-shape change — `chars()` → `chars().peekable()`**: the prior `for c in line.chars()` loop becomes a `let mut chars = line.chars().peekable(); while let Some(c) = chars.next()` while-loop so the `\\` branch can `chars.peek().copied()` to decide between recognised-escape (consume both via `chars.next()`, emit Literal) vs. unrecognised (keep `\` in the current `Unquoted` buffer, let the next loop iteration handle the following char naturally). The peek+conditional-next pattern is the canonical way to do "1-char lookahead" in Rust without a separate index counter — matches how `expand_vars` already handles its `$`-then-name peek shape. The InSingle and Outside branches see no change in semantics — the only behavioural change is in the InDouble branch's `\\` arm. Pattern syntax: the match arm uses `Some(next @ ('$' | '"' | '\\'))` (alternation pattern with binding) so a single arm captures all three recognised escape chars and binds the consumed value as `next: char` for the `Literal(next.to_string())` push step. **Tests** at `crates/sh/tests/quoting.rs` (+6 tests, suite 12 → 18; the prior 12 quoting tests are unmodified): `backslash_dollar_inside_double_quotes_is_literal` (seed `X=hi`; `echo "\$X"` → stdout contains `$X\n` AND NOT `hi\n` — guards both directions of the suppression invariant), `backslash_quote_inside_double_quotes_is_literal_quote` (`echo "say \"hi\""` → stdout contains `say "hi"\n` — confirms the `\"` escape doesn't close the double quote), `backslash_backslash_inside_double_quotes_is_single_backslash` (`echo "\\"` → stdout contains `\<newline>` — single backslash byte plus echo's trailing newline; the literal-segment path keeps a single `\` while the unrecognised-escape path would keep two), `unrecognized_backslash_inside_double_quotes_preserves_both_chars` (`echo "\n"` → stdout contains `\n\n` — two-byte `\n` literal + echo's newline; pins the "preserve both chars when escape is not recognised" invariant), `mixed_backslash_dollar_and_real_dollar_var` (seed `X=1`; `echo "\$X=$X"` → stdout contains `$X=1\n` — the first `$` is escaped so it stays literal; the second `$X` expands to `1`; pins the per-position-suppression invariant since both occurrences live in the same double-quoted span), `backslash_outside_quotes_unchanged` (`echo \X` → stdout contains `\X\n` — confirms the Outside branch is untouched, backslash preserved as a literal byte). The module-doc at the top of `tests/quoting.rs` is updated to describe the recognised-escape set + the unrecognised-fallback shape + the outside-double-quotes invariant. **Build verification**: `cargo test -p sh` all passing — sh-lib (expand_tests) 13/13, sh-main 0/0, env-tests 11/11, expand-tests 5/5, quoting-tests 18/18, shell-tests 37/37, tokenize-tests 13/13 (97 total in the sh crate; +6 from this slice); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p sh` clean. Files: `crates/sh/src/run.rs` (the InDouble branch in `tokenise_with_quotes`, plus the rewritten doc-comment paragraph for the function — 46 line net delta); `crates/sh/tests/quoting.rs` (six new `#[test] fn ...` entries plus a 4-line module-doc update — 107 line net delta); 2 files changed, 145 insertions, 8 deletions. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, blocked on other T142 partials or future work): pipes (`|`) + redirection (`>` / `>>` / `<`) — each ships as a future single-T142-partial slice; the `FOO=bar cmd` per-command env-prefix shape (needs the parser layer to recognise `KEY=VALUE` words at command-start); `$$` / `$@` / `$*` / `$0` / `$1` / `$?` / `$!`; arithmetic expansion `$((expr))`; command substitution `$(...)` / backticks; tilde expansion `~/foo`; glob expansion `*.txt`; the `set -u` "error on unset var" mode toggle. The `\<newline>` line-continuation inside `"..."` (which POSIX/bash treat as a token-spanning escape) is also out of scope — this slice's input is one already-stripped line with the trailing newline removed, so the only chars seen inside `"..."` are non-newline. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no other Rust crate touched — pure userland-shell slice, intra-`tokenise_with_quotes`-function change. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `tokenise_with_quotes` and `assemble_token` consume only `BTreeMap` (the env map T144 wired) and the input line; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — quote/escape handling is in-process state, no syscall touched; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the new escape semantics are exercised by 6 integration tests through the REPL driver, plus the existing 12 quoting tests pin the no-regression invariant for `'...'` / `"..."` non-escape behaviour. **Architectural milestone**: fourth slice on the post-tokenisation expansion layer (after `$VAR` in b74a6e3, single-quote in 2a9f731, double-quote in afbf05b). The expansion-suppression-via-Literal-segment pattern is now load-bearing — any future "this position should NOT expand" semantic (e.g. `\<newline>` line-continuation, `\\` outside quotes if we ever add unquoted-escape semantics) can use the same routing without growing `TokenPart`'s variant set. The `peekable()` shape is also established as the canonical way to do 1-char lookahead inside `tokenise_with_quotes` — useful for any future tokeniser change that needs to look ahead one byte (e.g. `>>` vs `>` redirection, `&&` vs `&` job control).
