After the **T146 follow-up — wc coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a11a3939bed5388e6; rebased onto local main HEAD `3374f11`, no movement — the rebase was a no-op since the branch was already up to date): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new wc test binary at 8 tests; cumulative coreutils-tests count 94 → 102. Commit 44f5987. First per-binary EXPANSION slice on the coreutils crate (every prior coreutils slice after the original 7 was a flag-follow-up — `ls -l` c3d1aa7, `cp -r` b2623ff, `mkdir -p` bbf6b58, `rm -r` 26f50ad, `grep -i` 8ad3297, `grep -n` 16f41aa, `ls -a` 4ca1f20, `cat -n` f2512c3, `grep -v` 6f682af, `grep -c` 34671c9, `cat -E` b983ff6, `cp -n` bb49d13). This slice extends the binary set itself: a NEW eighth binary `wc` (word/line/byte count) at `crates/coreutils/src/bin/wc.rs` (~150 lines, std-only, zero deps). T146's tasks.md spec literally says "minimal coreutils" listing seven names (`ls`, `grep`, `cat`, `mkdir`, `rm`, `cp`, `mv`); `wc` is outside that explicit set but clearly within the spirit of "minimal coreutils a shell user expects" — `wc` is in every Unix coreutils package and adds zero new architectural risk (pure compute over already-readable bytes). POSIX-ish `wc [-l] [-w] [-c] [file ...]`: default (no flags) emits `<lines>\t<words>\t<bytes>\t<filename>\n` per file with multi-file invocations appending a final `<lines>\t<words>\t<bytes>\ttotal\n` summary row; with one or more selection flags only those columns appear in the fixed `lines → words → bytes` order regardless of flag order (`-cwl` and `-lwc` produce the same shape); stdin mode (no file args) omits the filename column. Counting semantics: lines = count of `\n` bytes in the file (a final partial line without trailing `\n` is NOT counted, matching POSIX wc — `printf foo` = 0 lines, `printf 'foo\n'` = 1 line); words = `str::split_whitespace().count()` over the file as UTF-8 (ASCII whitespace tokenisation — spaces, tabs, newlines, etc., the standard Rust split); bytes = `bytes.len()` of the buffer. Invalid UTF-8 short-circuits the word count to 0 for that file, surfaces `wc: <path>: invalid utf-8` to stderr, and flips the had-error flag (so `-c`-only or `-l`-only invocations on a binary file still produce sensible counts — only the word column is suppressed). Per-file open/read errors write `wc: <path>: <err>` to stderr, set the had-error flag, and continue with remaining files (matching cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls partial-success convention). Exit 0 on full success, 1 on any per-file failure. CLI parsing mirrors grep's POSIX-style short-flag clustering (commit f667018) byte-for-byte: single-pass arg split into flags + paths buckets via the existing for-loop; `-l` toggles `want_lines`, `-w` `want_words`, `-c` `want_bytes` in the per-character match arm; `--` is the hard separator; `-` (bare) is preserved as a path arg (stdin convention placeholder); unknown flags write `wc: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 1 (cat's exit-1 shape, not grep's exit-2 — wc's failure model is "any error → exit 1" without grep's "open-error vs no-match" distinction). Implementation: `count(bytes, label, had_error)` returns `(lines, words, byte_count)` and is the single computation kernel; `emit(out, lines, words, bytes, label, show_lines, show_words, show_bytes)` builds a `Vec<String>` of selected columns plus an optional trailing label, joins with tabs, and writes one line. The `any_flag = want_lines || want_words || want_bytes` shape collapses the "no flags = default all three" decision into three boolean computations (`show_X = if any_flag { want_X } else { true }`) — symmetric, no special-case branching. The total row routes through the same `emit` call with `Some("total")` as label and the accumulated counters; multi-file invocations are detected via `paths.len() > 1` (single-file invocations omit the total row even if other count flags are set). Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/wc.rs` (8 tests, brand-new file): `default_emits_lines_words_bytes` (pin the byte-exact `2\t5\t24\t<path>\n` output for `b"hello world\nfoo bar baz\n"` — 24 bytes total, 2 newlines, 5 split_whitespace tokens), `dash_l_emits_lines_only` (`b"alpha\nbeta\ngamma\n"` → `3\t<path>\n` — confirms only the lines column appears, no tab-separated zeros for omitted columns), `dash_w_emits_words_only` (`b"the quick brown fox\n"` → `4\t<path>\n` — confirms split_whitespace counts), `dash_c_emits_bytes_only` (`b"hello\n"` → `6\t<path>\n` — bytes-only column), `multi_file_appends_total_row` (two files of 1/1/6 and 1/3/6 → both per-file rows + a final `2\t4\t12\ttotal\n` — pins the multi-file accumulation), `stdin_mode_omits_filename` (pipe `b"foo bar\nbaz\n"` to stdin with no file args → byte-exact stdout `b"2\t3\t12\n"` — confirms the no-label path and pins the exact byte output for the streaming case), `missing_file_continues_and_exits_one` (one good file + one missing path → exit 1, stdout has the good file's row, stderr contains both `wc:` and the missing path string — pins the partial-success continuation), `dash_lc_combines_lines_and_bytes` (`b"foo\nbar\n"` with `-lc` → `2\t8\t<path>\n` — pins the cluster-flag column-selection: lines + bytes shown, words omitted, fixed `lines → bytes` order). Each spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_wc")` via `std::process::Command` against per-test scratch dirs in `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety (same shape as cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls test files). Cargo.toml: new `[[bin]] name = "wc" path = "src/bin/wc.rs"` block adjacent to the existing seven (cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls) — alphabetical ordering not required since `[[bin]]` blocks have no ordering semantics, the convention here is "new entries at the end" matching the established pattern. Xtask wiring: new `("coreutils", "wc")` tuple appended to `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` (count 16 entries → 17) so `just build` copies `target/wasm32-wasip1/release/wc.wasm` (~64 KB) → `dist/assets/bin/wc.wasm`. **Verification**: `cargo test -p coreutils` 102 / 102 pass across all 8 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean, produces `target/wasm32-wasip1/release/wc.wasm` at 65,470 bytes. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, future single-flag-follow-up slices): `-m` (character count, not byte count — needs UTF-8 grapheme awareness or at least char-boundary counting); `-L` (longest line length); `-w` for files containing invalid UTF-8 — current behaviour is "0 words + stderr diagnostic + had-error", a follow-up could route invalid UTF-8 through a byte-level whitespace splitter to count words anyway; the `-` bare arg as a stdin placeholder (currently it lands in paths and `fs::read("-")` fails with the `wc: -: <err>` shape; GNU wc treats `-` as stdin); accepting `--lines` / `--words` / `--bytes` long-form flags. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto / sh / coreutils-other-binaries changes — pure new-binary slice in the coreutils crate plus one xtask tuple. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — wc consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — wc only reads files and writes stdout/stderr; Principle VIII (bottom-up construction) holds — the coreutils crate is at the userland tier, building atop the kernel/VFS/WASI seam; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 8 isolation tests pin the wc output shape across single-file default, three single-flag column selections, multi-file total row, stdin mode, missing-file partial-success, and one cluster-flag column-combination case. **Architectural milestone**: first expansion of the coreutils binary set beyond T146's original seven names. The pattern established here (Cargo.toml `[[bin]]` block + `assemble_dist.rs` `USERLAND_BINS` tuple + `crates/coreutils/src/bin/<name>.rs` implementation + `crates/coreutils/tests/<name>.rs` integration tests) is the canonical shape for any future coreutils additions — `head`, `tail`, `sort`, `uniq`, `tee`, `tr`, `cut`, etc. each ship as a one-binary-per-slice extension on this shape with zero workspace-level reshaping. T146 still uncheckered — flag combinations now in (cat: `-n`, `-E`, plus cluster forms; grep: `-i`, `-n`, `-v`, `-c`, plus cluster forms; ls: `-l`, `-a`; cp: `-r`, `-n`; mkdir: `-p`; rm: `-r`; wc: `-l`, `-w`, `-c`, plus cluster forms). Updated tasks.md line 291: appended `; wc landed in 44f5987` to the partial-annotation prefix. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (e) T146 `head` / `tail` / `sort` / `uniq` / `tee` (more new-binary expansion slices following this slice's shape); (f) T146 `ls -1` / `ls -R` / `mv -i` / `grep -F` / `grep -l` / `grep -q` / `cat -T` / `cat -A` / `cat -b` / `cp -i` / `cp -v` / `cp -p` / `wc -m` / `wc -L`; (g) T097 init unit tests; (h) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — head coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a28fab72028a110d9; rebased onto local main HEAD `3374f11`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `head` test binary at 8 tests; coreutils total 94 → 102 tests (+8). Commit d631e58. Eighth NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239) — first new binary since 41e8239 closed the original 7-coreutil set; subsequent slices were per-binary flag follow-ups (the 11-strong streak `ls -l` / `cp -r` / `mkdir -p` / `rm -r` / `grep -i` / `grep -n` / `ls -a` / `cat -n` / `grep -v` / `grep -c` / `cat -E` / `cp -n`). `crates/coreutils/src/bin/head.rs` (~165 lines) implements `head [-n N] [file ...]` mirroring the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls structure exactly: hand-rolled argv parsing (no `clap`), `std::fs` only (no WASI extension), partial-success multi-file shape (per-file open/read errors flip a had-error flag and continue with remaining files), exit 0 on full success / 1 if any file failed. Default N is 10; with no file args, reads stdin; with multi-file invocations, prefixes each section with `==> <path> <==\n` (GNU-`head` convention) — the first file has no leading newline, subsequent files prepend `\n` for the GNU-style blank-line separator between sections; single-file invocations have no header. Counting semantics: a "line" is `\n`-terminated; a file ending without a trailing newline still emits its tail unchanged (no extra `\n` injected) once the line count is hit; `N=0` reads-and-discards the input then prints nothing (still opens the file so missing-file diagnostics fire). Negative N is rejected with `head: invalid count: <arg>` and exit 1 (GNU's `head -n -K` "all but last K" semantic is intentionally deferred to a future slice — the brief explicitly listed it as "complex; defer to a future slice and reject negative N"). **CLI parser shape — value-consuming flag plus bare-digit shortcut**: this slice introduces a new parser pattern not seen in the prior coreutils — flags that consume a value. The argv loop changes from `for arg in raw` to `let mut iter = raw.into_iter(); while let Some(arg) = iter.next()` so the `-n` branch can call `iter.next()` to consume the count value as the next argv. Three count-form recognisers run in order: (1) `parse_count_arg(&arg)` checks for the bare-digit / `-N` / `-nN` no-space form — matches `-?-?\d+` exactly (one optional sign char then all digits); returns `Some(Ok(n))` for valid non-negative counts (`-5` → 5, `-n5` → 5), `Some(Err(arg))` for negative counts (`--5`, `-n-5`) which trigger the "invalid count" diagnostic, `None` for other forms (lets the next branch take it); (2) `if arg == "-n"` consumes the next argv as the count via `iter.next()`; (3) anything else with a leading `-` (and not `-` itself) is unknown-flag with exit 1. The bare `-N` form (e.g. `-5`, `-2`) is the GNU-style shortcut — explicitly required by the brief — and matters for distinguishing it from short-flag clustering (e.g. `-lc`) which the other coreutils use. Detection: `parse_count_arg` only returns `Some(...)` when the entire post-`-` body is `[-]?<digits>`; a cluster like `-lc` fails the all-digit check and falls through to the unknown-flag branch. The `parse_count_arg` helper is the canonical shape for any future coreutil that needs to distinguish a count-flag from a flag cluster (`tail -N` would reuse it verbatim once that slice ships). The `--` separator behaviour matches the rest of the crate: everything after `--` is forced into paths regardless of leading `-`. **Implementation — `emit_first_n` helper**: a single `fn emit_first_n<R: BufRead, W: Write>(r, count: u64, out) -> io::Result<()>` runs the per-input read loop. Inside, `read_until(b'\n', &mut buf)` is the line-by-line read primitive — returns the buffer including the trailing `\n` if present — so we can distinguish complete lines (`buf.ends_with(b"\n")`) from a partial trailing tail (no `\n`). On a complete line, `out.write_all(&buf)` ships it verbatim then increments `emitted`; on a partial trailing line, `write_all(&buf)` ships the bytes and the loop breaks (no `\n` injected). When `count == 0`, the helper drains the reader to a sink-Vec via `read_to_end` (so EOF is reached cleanly even though no output is generated; matters for any future stdin-piping integration test that needs the reader to be fully consumed before the parent process can close its stdin). The `read_until` + `ends_with(b"\n")` approach is preferred over `BufRead::lines()` (which strips the trailing `\n`) because head's "no extra newline on partial tail" invariant requires byte-exact preservation — `lines()` would inject a `\n` we'd then have to special-case-suppress. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/head.rs` (8 tests, NEW): `default_prints_first_ten_lines` (file with 15 numbered lines `line 1` through `line 15`, `head <file>` → exact bytes `line 1\nline 2\n...line 10\n`); `dash_n_overrides_count` (`head -n 3 <file>` → first 3 lines only); `dash_n_zero_prints_nothing` (`head -n 0 <file>` → empty stdout, empty stderr, exit 0); `multi_file_prefixes_each_with_filename_header` (two files, `head -n 1 a b` → byte-exact `==> <a-path> <==\nalpha\n\n==> <b-path> <==\ncharlie\n` — pins the GNU header shape AND the inter-file blank-line separator); `stdin_mode_no_header` (`head -n 2` with piped stdin `first\nsecond\nthird\n` → exact bytes `first\nsecond\n`, no header); `missing_file_continues_and_exits_one` (mix of missing + good file → exit 1, stdout has good file content with `==>` header, stderr has `head: <path>:` shape); `negative_count_is_error_exits_one` (`head -n -5 <file>` → exit 1, empty stdout, stderr contains `head:` and `invalid count`); `bare_dash_n_form_works` (the GNU-style `head -2 <file>` → first 2 lines, no error — pins the bare-digit-as-count parser branch). Each test follows the existing crate test scaffold: `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_head")` for the binary path, scratch dirs at `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, cleanup-on-success / leave-on-failure pattern. Module doc-comment at the top of `head.rs` lays out: counting semantics, partial-tail handling, negative-N rejection, the value-consuming-flag parser pattern with the `parse_count_arg` shape, and the precedent file list `cat,grep,cp,mkdir,rm,mv,ls`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "head" path = "src/bin/head.rs"` block adjacent to `ls` (the most recently added binary); `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "head")` after `("coreutils", "ls")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/head.wasm` (~67 KB release build). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 102/102 pass across all 8 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `cargo build -p xtask` clean. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit / sh changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — head consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI fs+stdio handles for I/O; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 8 new isolation tests pin the head semantics across default count, `-n N`, `-n 0`, multi-file headers + inter-file separator, stdin mode (no header), partial-success multi-file, negative count rejection, and the bare-digit GNU-style count form. **Architectural milestone**: eighth coreutil binary, first new coreutil since 41e8239 (the `ls` slice that closed the original 7-coreutil set in tasks.md line 291). T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed" to "7 listed + head" — head was not in the T146 spec line's enumerated list of 7 (`ls`, `grep`, `cat`, `mkdir`, `rm`, `cp`, `mv`) so the partial-annotation prefix tracks it as an extension landing alongside the per-flag follow-ups. The `parse_count_arg` helper + value-consuming-flag pattern lays groundwork for future coreutils that need similar parsing (`tail [-n N]`, `cut -f N`, `sort -k N`, `sed -n N`); the helper is currently a private fn inside `head.rs` but is small enough to lift to `crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs` if/when a second consumer arrives. Updated tasks.md line 291: appended `; head landed in d631e58` to the partial-annotation prefix. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (e) T146 `ls -1` / `ls -R` / `mv -i` / `grep -F` / `grep -l` / `grep -q` / `cat -T` / `cat -A` / `cat -b` / `cp -i` / `cp -v` / `cp -p` / `head -c N` (byte count) / `tail` (sibling binary, mirrors head structure); (f) T097 init unit tests; (g) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — tail coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a7ee096227c20c48f; rebased onto local main HEAD `b5606df`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `tail` test binary at 7 tests; coreutils total 110 → 117 tests (+7). Commit 4b2458d. Ninth NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239, `head` d631e58) — the explicitly-anticipated sibling-of-head landing called out in the head slice's "next single-slice runway candidates" list as `tail (sibling binary, mirrors head structure)`. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/tail.rs` (~190 lines) implements `tail [-n N] [file ...]` mirroring `head.rs` byte-for-byte at the surface — same flag-parser shape (`-n N` value-consuming flag; `-nN` no-space form; `-N` bare-digit GNU shortcut; `--` hard separator; `-` bare preserved as path; unknown flags exit 1; the `parse_count_arg` helper duplicated verbatim from head — if/when a third consumer arrives the brief recommends lifting it to `crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs`), same multi-file `==> <path> <==\n` header convention with leading `\n` separator between sections (first file no leading newline; subsequent files prepend `\n` for the GNU-style blank-line separator), same partial-success error model (per-file open/read errors flip a had-error flag and continue with remaining files; `tail: <path>: <err>` to stderr), same negative-N rejection (`tail: invalid count: -<arg>`, exit 1, deferring GNU's `tail -n -K` "all but first K" semantic to a future slice), same exit-code shape (0 on full success, 1 on any failure). Default N is 10. Where head and tail diverge is the per-input read path: head streams via `BufRead::read_until(b'\n', &mut buf)` and emits the first N lines on the fly (no whole-file buffering — the streaming form is the natural shape because head only ever needs to look at the prefix), but tail necessarily needs to know where the END is, so it reads the file fully via `fs::read(path)` (or `read_to_end` on the stdin lock for stdin mode) into a `Vec<u8>`, splits on `\n` via the local `split_lines` helper, and emits the last N slices in source order. The streaming O(N)-window form (which would let tail handle multi-gigabyte files without buffering the prefix — a ring buffer of the last N line-byte-ranges) is intentionally deferred — v1 has small files (per the brief), and the buffer-the-whole-thing form is the right shape for this tier; the future O(N)-window version can swap in transparently behind the existing `emit_last_n` signature. Counting semantics: a "line" is `\n`-terminated; the last N `\n`-terminated lines are emitted in source order; if the file ends without a trailing newline, the partial tail counts as one of the N and is emitted verbatim (no extra `\n` injected — `split_lines` produces a final non-`\n`-suffixed slice for the partial tail and the writer ships it as-is); N=0 prints nothing and skips the split entirely (early-return in `emit_last_n`); empty input prints nothing regardless of N. The `split_lines(bytes) -> Vec<&[u8]>` helper walks the input once, pushing a slice including the trailing `\n` for each newline-terminated line, plus a final newline-less slice if `start < bytes.len()` after the loop. **Implementation — `emit_last_n` helper**: `fn emit_last_n<W: Write>(bytes: &[u8], count: u64, out: &mut W) -> io::Result<()>` clamps `count` to `usize::MAX` (saturating cast for absurdly large counts, since `Vec::len()` is `usize`), computes `start = lines.len().saturating_sub(count)`, then iterates `&lines[start..]` writing each slice. The saturating-sub form means `count >= lines.len()` cleanly degenerates to "print everything" — the same shape head uses for its early-EOF case. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/tail.rs` (7 tests, NEW): `default_prints_last_ten_lines` (file with 15 numbered lines `line 1` through `line 15`, `tail <file>` → exact bytes `line 6\nline 7\n...line 15\n` — note the symmetric mirror to head's `line 1\n...line 10\n`); `dash_n_overrides_count` (`tail -n 3 <file>` with 5-line input → last 3 only); `dash_n_zero_prints_nothing` (`tail -n 0 <file>` → empty stdout, empty stderr, exit 0); `multi_file_prefixes_each_with_filename_header` (two files, `tail -n 1 a b` → byte-exact `==> <a-path> <==\nbravo\n\n==> <b-path> <==\ndelta\n` — pins the GNU header shape AND the inter-file blank-line separator AND the "last line" semantic across both files); `stdin_mode_no_header` (`tail -n 2` with piped stdin `first\nsecond\nthird\n` → exact bytes `second\nthird\n`, no header); `missing_file_continues_and_exits_one` (mix of missing + good file → exit 1, stdout has good file content with `==>` header, stderr has `tail: <path>:` shape); `negative_count_is_error_exits_one` (`tail -n -5 <file>` → exit 1, empty stdout, stderr contains `tail:` and `invalid count`). The 7-test count (vs head's 8) is intentional — the brief's test list specified 5-7 tests and tail elides head's `bare_dash_n_form_works` test on the basis that the parser is byte-identical to head's already-tested form (the `parse_count_arg` body is verbatim duplicated and the bare-digit branch is exercised by the same logic head's test pins); a future test can be added if/when the parser diverges. Each test follows the existing crate test scaffold: `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_tail")` for the binary path, scratch dirs at `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, cleanup-on-success / leave-on-failure pattern. Module doc-comment at the top of `tail.rs` lays out: counting semantics, partial-tail handling, negative-N rejection, the streaming-vs-fully-buffered tradeoff (v1 small-files justification), the duplicate-then-lift convention for `parse_count_arg`, and the precedent file list `cat,grep,cp,mkdir,rm,mv,ls,head`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "tail" path = "src/bin/tail.rs"` block adjacent to head's; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "tail")` after `("coreutils", "head")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/tail.wasm` (~65 KB release build — slightly smaller than head at ~67 KB, since tail's hot path is the simpler bytes-to-slices form and avoids `BufRead::read_until`'s monomorphisation). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 117/117 pass across all 9 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, tail 7, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `cargo build -p xtask` clean. T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed + head" to "7 listed + head + tail" — neither head nor tail appears in the T146 spec line's enumerated list of 7 (`ls`, `grep`, `cat`, `mkdir`, `rm`, `cp`, `mv`) so the partial-annotation prefix tracks them as extensions landing alongside the per-flag follow-ups. After the **T146 follow-up — sort coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-acb2037ce0c23ad28; rebased onto local main HEAD `b5606df`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `sort` test binary at 7 tests; coreutils total 102 → 109 tests (+7). Commit f1e673b. Ninth NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239, `wc` 44f5987, `head` d631e58) — third new-binary expansion slice in a row, following the `wc` (44f5987) and `head` (d631e58) precedent. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/sort.rs` (~110 lines) implements `sort [-r] [-u] [file ...]` mirroring the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls/wc/head structure: hand-rolled argv parsing (no `clap`), `std::fs` only (no WASI extension), partial-success multi-file shape (per-file open/read errors flip a had-error flag and continue with remaining files), exit 0 on full success / 1 on any per-file failure / 2 on unknown flag. Default behaviour reads stdin when no file args are present, otherwise concatenates all input lines from each file in argv order into a single bucket; sorts via `Vec::sort` (Rust's default `Ord` for `String` is lexicographic byte-order — exactly POSIX `sort` C-locale semantics, no locale-aware collation in this slice); emits each sorted line on its own `\n`-terminated row to stdout. `-r` reverses the sorted result via `Vec::reverse` (post-sort — order of operations: sort → reverse → dedup). `-u` collapses adjacent duplicates via `Vec::dedup` (since the input is sorted, dedup gives globally-unique entries — the standard "sort | uniq" idiom collapsed into one binary). `-ru` / `-ur` apply both: sort → reverse → dedup, which still yields globally-unique reversed lines because dedup operates on adjacent equality which the sort then reverse preserves. **Line-splitting semantics**: the helper `append_lines(text: &str, sink: &mut Vec<String>)` calls `text.split('\n').collect()`, then drops a trailing empty segment if the input ended in a `\n` (the standard split-trailing-empty cleanup that matches POSIX `sort` line semantics — a file ending in `c\nb\na\n` contributes three lines `c` / `b` / `a`, not four with a phantom empty tail). A file without a trailing newline keeps every segment (e.g. `c\nb\na` also contributes three lines). The match-on-`Some(&"")` literal is the canonical Rust idiom here. **CLI parser shape**: mirrors grep's POSIX-style short-flag clustering (commit f667018) byte-for-byte — single-pass arg split into flags + paths buckets, `-r` toggles `reverse`, `-u` toggles `unique` in the per-character match arm, `--` is the hard separator, `-` (bare) is preserved as a path arg, unknown flags write `sort: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exit 2. The `-ru` / `-ur` cluster forms work automatically via the cluster pattern — the for-loop walks each character of `arg[1..]` and toggles its corresponding flag bit. Exit-2-on-unknown-flag matches grep's open-error/usage exit-code convention (not wc's exit-1 model — sort's "any read error → exit 1" + "any usage error → exit 2" mirrors grep's failure-mode partition). **Implementation**: `main` accumulates lines into a `Vec<String>` (one per input file or one for stdin), runs `lines.sort()` once, then conditionally `reverse()` and `dedup()`, then iterates with `writeln!(out, "{line}")` to write each line + newline to stdout. The output write loop catches I/O errors via `if writeln!(out, ...).is_err() { had_error = true; break }` so a closed stdout (e.g. piping to `head`) exits 1 cleanly. Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` (7 tests, NEW): `sorts_lines_alphabetically` (`b"c\na\nb\n"` → `b"a\nb\nc\n"` — pin the byte-exact ASCII-byte-order sort); `dash_r_reverses_order` (same input + `-r` → `b"c\nb\na\n"` — pin the post-sort reverse); `dash_u_dedupes_duplicate_lines` (`b"a\nb\na\nb\n"` + `-u` → `b"a\nb\n"` — pin global dedup after sort); `dash_ru_combines_reverse_and_unique` (same input + `-ru` → `b"b\na\n"` — pin the cluster-flag combination); `multi_file_concatenates_then_sorts` (two files `delta\nbravo\n` + `alpha\ncharlie\n` → `alpha\nbravo\ncharlie\ndelta\n` — pin the concatenation-before-sort behaviour, NOT per-file sort); `stdin_mode_reads_until_eof_and_sorts` (pipe `b"zebra\napple\nmango\n"` to stdin with no file args → `b"apple\nmango\nzebra\n"` — pin the stdin path); `missing_file_continues_and_exits_one` (one missing path + one good file → exit 1, stdout has the good file's sorted lines, stderr contains `sort:` and the missing path string — pin the partial-success continuation). Each test follows the existing crate test scaffold: `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_sort")` for the binary path, scratch dirs at `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, cleanup-on-success / leave-on-failure pattern. Module doc-comment at the top of `sort.rs` lays out: line-splitting semantics (with the trailing-empty-segment drop), the cluster-flag inheritance from grep, exit-code partition (0 / 1 / 2), and the precedent file list `cat,grep,wc,head`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "sort" path = "src/bin/sort.rs"` block adjacent to `head` (the most recently added binary); `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "sort")` after `("coreutils", "head")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/sort.wasm` (71,258 bytes release build). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 109/109 pass across all 9 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `cargo build -p xtask` implicitly clean (xtask compiles as part of the workspace). No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit / sh changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — sort consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI fs+stdio handles for I/O; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin the sort semantics across default ASCII-byte-order sort, `-r` reverse, `-u` global dedup, `-ru` cluster-flag combination, multi-file concatenation-before-sort, stdin mode, and partial-success multi-file. **Architectural milestone**: ninth coreutil binary, third new-binary expansion in a row (wc → head → sort) — the new-binary cadence has now established a clear runway for the remaining "expected coreutils a shell user reaches for" set. T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed + wc + head" to "7 listed + wc + head + sort"; the partial-annotation prefix on tasks.md line 291 tracks it as an extension landing alongside the per-flag follow-ups. The cluster-flag pattern (grep f667018) is now used by 5 coreutils (grep, wc, sort, plus the cluster-form follow-ups in cat / cp / etc.); the value-consuming-flag pattern (head d631e58) so far has only one consumer (head). Updated tasks.md line 291: appended `; sort landed in f1e673b` to the partial-annotation prefix. **Memory / performance note**: sort buffers the entire input set in memory (`Vec<String>`), which is acceptable for v1 — PMos files fit in memory until proven otherwise (matching cat / cp / grep / wc memory shape). External-merge-sort for files larger than RAM is explicitly out of scope (and would require seekable temp files which the kernel block-driver path can't yet provide). **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, future single-flag-follow-up slices): `-n` (numeric sort — needs parse-int-or-fall-back-to-string comparator); `-k N` (sort by Nth column — needs whitespace-tokenisation per line); `-t SEP` (custom field separator); `-f` (case-insensitive sort — needs case-folded comparator); `-b` (ignore leading whitespace); `-c` (check sortedness, no output); `-o FILE` (write to FILE instead of stdout); `-m` (merge already-sorted inputs, the streaming-merge path); `--` long-form flags. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (e) T146 `tail` (sibling binary, mirrors head structure including the `parse_count_arg` helper) / `uniq` (post-sort dedup, no internal sort) / `tee` (write stdin to multiple outputs) / `tr` (character translation); (f) T146 `sort -n` (numeric) / `sort -k N` / `sort -f` (case-fold); (g) T097 init unit tests; (h) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — uniq coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a3631606b28e2d281; rebased onto local main HEAD `6560a6e`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `uniq` test binary at 7 tests; coreutils total 124 → 131 tests (+7). Commit e5d44bc. Tenth NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239, `wc` 44f5987, `head` d631e58, `tail` 4b2458d, `sort` f1e673b) — fourth new-binary expansion slice in a row, following the `wc` (44f5987) / `head` (d631e58) / `tail` (4b2458d) / `sort` (f1e673b) precedent. `uniq` was anticipated explicitly in the sort slice's "next single-slice runway candidates" list as `uniq (post-sort dedup, no internal sort)` — the natural sort-uniq pipeline-pair landing now. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/uniq.rs` (~140 lines) implements `uniq [-c] [-d] [-u] [file]` mirroring the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls/wc/head/tail/sort structure: hand-rolled argv parsing (no `clap`), `std::fs` only (no WASI extension), exit 0 on full success / 1 on any error (open failure, unknown flag, usage error, write failure). The slice deliberately diverges from sort/grep's exit-2-on-flag-error model (the brief explicitly listed exit 1 for both unknown-flag AND usage errors so uniq's failure-mode partition stays simple — a single non-zero outcome). **POSIX adjacent-only semantics — the key distinction from `sort -u`**: POSIX `uniq` only collapses CONSECUTIVE runs of byte-equal lines. For unsorted input, only adjacent repeats coalesce — non-adjacent repeats survive. Input `a\na\nb\na\n` (3 runs: `[a,a]` → `[b]` → `[a]`) emits `a\nb\na\n` by default, NOT `a\nb` — the trailing `a` reappears because it is not adjacent to the leading `a` run. Callers needing global dedup pipe `sort | uniq` (or use `sort -u`, which fuses the two passes; see f1e673b's `-u` flag). The module doc-comment leads with this distinction and the test `default_collapses_adjacent_duplicates` pins the byte-exact `a\nb\na\n` output to ensure the adjacency-preservation can never regress to a global-dedup form. **Argument count — single-file (or stdin), not multi-file**: uniq is the FIRST coreutil binary in the crate that accepts only 0 or 1 file arg (every prior coreutil — cat, grep, cp, mkdir, rm, mv, ls, wc, head, tail, sort — supports the multi-file convention with partial-success continuation). POSIX `uniq` historically used a second arg as the output-file path; v1 explicitly does not implement that (output goes to stdout always) and rejects the 2+ arg form with `usage: uniq [-cdu] [file]` exit 1. Stdin mode is the 0-arg invocation. The single-file shape simplifies the error model — there's no had-error flag to propagate across files; an open failure terminates immediately. The 1-arg-max enforcement runs after argv parsing (so `uniq -c -d /path1 /path2` reports the usage error, not a flag error). **Flag semantics**: `-c` prefixes each output line with `<run_len>\t<line>` (tab-separated); `-d` filters to ONLY duplicated runs (count > 1) emitting one copy each; `-u` filters to ONLY unique runs (count == 1); `-d` + `-u` produces empty output (no run is simultaneously duplicated AND unique — the empty intersection is the brief's explicit semantic). Combinations: `-c -d` prefixes counts on duplicated-only output; `-c -u` prefixes counts on unique-only output (each count is 1 by definition); `-c -d -u` is empty (the `-d -u` mask suppresses everything before counting matters). The brief's `-d -u` empty-output test pins this. **CLI parser shape — short-flag clustering**: mirrors grep's POSIX-style cluster pattern (commit f667018) byte-for-byte — single-pass arg split into flags + paths buckets, the per-character match-arm in `arg[1..]` toggles `count`/`only_dup`/`only_uniq` for `c`/`d`/`u`, `--` is the hard separator, `-` (bare) is preserved as a path arg. So `-cd` toggles both `-c` and `-d` in one cluster (the brief specified this explicitly: "CLI parser: mirror grep's clustering (`-cd` should toggle both)"); `-du` toggles both `-d` and `-u` for the empty-output combination; `-cdu` is a three-flag cluster. Unknown flag inside a cluster aborts with `uniq: unknown flag: <full-arg>` exit 1 (the full pre-cluster `arg` is reported, not just the offending char — matching grep/sort/wc convention so the user sees the actual cluster they typed, not an isolated character). **Run-length detection — index pair walk**: the implementation accumulates a `Vec<&str>` of all input lines (via the existing `split_lines` trailing-empty-segment-drop helper that sort uses), then walks with a two-cursor `while i < lines.len()` outer loop / `while j < lines.len() && lines[j] == lines[i]` inner advance loop. The advance loop terminates at the first non-equal line OR at end-of-input, so `j - i` is the run length and `i = j` advances to the start of the next run. Per-run: compute `is_dup = run_len > 1`, `is_uniq = run_len == 1`, then resolve the emit decision via a `match (only_dup, only_uniq)` four-arm table (`(false, false) → true`, `(true, false) → is_dup`, `(false, true) → is_uniq`, `(true, true) → false`); on emit, write either `<run_len>\t<line>\n` (if `count`) or `<line>\n` (otherwise) to stdout. The `is_dup`/`is_uniq` booleans are technically redundant under the four-arm table but make the intent self-documenting in the dispatch (a future reader can see "run is a dup AND we want only dups" reading at the variable level rather than synthesising it from `run_len > 1`); zero monomorphisation cost. The output write loop catches stdout errors via `if result.is_err() { had_error = true; break }` so a closed pipe (e.g. `uniq | head -1`) exits 1 cleanly — the same shape sort uses. **Line-splitting helper — duplicate-then-lift candidate**: `split_lines(text: &str) -> Vec<&str>` is byte-identical to sort's `append_lines` body modulo signature (sort returns via `&mut Vec<String>` because it owns the allocations across multiple input files; uniq returns borrowed slices because it has at most one input). The line-splitting + trailing-empty-segment-drop logic is now duplicated across sort.rs and uniq.rs; this is the second consumer of that pattern, mirroring head/tail's `parse_count_arg` duplication. The next coreutil that needs the same line-split shape (`tee`, `tr`, `cut`, `paste`, etc.) is the natural lift-to-`crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs` trigger — same convention as `parse_count_arg` (head + tail) waiting on its third consumer. **Implementation choice — `String`-buffered, not streaming**: uniq buffers the entire input via `fs::read_to_string` (or `io::stdin().read_to_string`) before the run-detection walk, so the whole file lives in memory. The streaming O(1)-window form (where uniq only needs to remember the previous line + its run length) is feasible and would let uniq handle multi-gigabyte files, but v1's small-file constraint means the buffered form is acceptable and matches sort's memory shape (which has no streaming option). The `String`-vs-`&[u8]` choice (uniq uses `String` whereas tail uses `Vec<u8>` for its `split_lines`) reflects that uniq writes the line content via `writeln!(out, "{}", lines[i])` which needs a `Display` value; tail writes raw bytes via `out.write_all(slice)` because its `split_lines` keeps trailing `\n`s and the writer is byte-exact. Either shape works; uniq's choice keeps the writer code one line shorter at the cost of requiring valid UTF-8 input (an invalid-UTF-8 file would fail at `read_to_string` with an error reported via `uniq: <path>: stream did not contain valid UTF-8` to stderr). **Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/uniq.rs` (7 tests, NEW)**: `default_collapses_adjacent_duplicates` (input `b"a\na\nb\na\n"` → output `b"a\nb\na\n"` — the canonical adjacent-only test, pins the byte-exact survival of the trailing `a` because it's not adjacent to the leading `a` run); `dash_c_prefixes_count` (same input + `-c` → `b"2\ta\n1\tb\n1\ta\n"` — pins the run-length count per emitted line; the trailing `a` shows count `1` because that run has length 1, matching POSIX `uniq -c`); `dash_d_emits_only_duplicates` (same input + `-d` → `b"a\n"` — only the doubled `a` run emits, the singleton `b` and trailing singleton `a` are suppressed); `dash_u_emits_only_unique_lines` (same input + `-u` → `b"b\na\n"` — singletons only, the doubled `a` is suppressed); `dash_du_combined_emits_nothing` (same input + `-du` → empty stdout, exit 0 — pins the empty-intersection semantic per the brief); `stdin_mode_works` (pipe `b"x\nx\ny\n"` to stdin with no file args → `b"x\ny\n"` — pins the no-argv path); `missing_file_exits_one` (nonexistent path → exit 1, empty stdout, stderr contains `uniq:` and the missing path string — pins the open-error shape). Each test follows the existing crate test scaffold: `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_uniq")` for the binary path, scratch dirs at `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter for parallel-run safety, cleanup-on-success / leave-on-failure pattern. Module doc-comment at the top of `uniq.rs` lays out: the adjacent-only semantic with the explicit `sort -u` distinction, all four flag combinations, the single-file argument-count enforcement, the cluster-flag inheritance from grep, and the precedent file list `cat,grep,wc,head,sort`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "uniq" path = "src/bin/uniq.rs"` block adjacent to `sort` (the most recently added binary); `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "uniq")` after `("coreutils", "sort")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/uniq.wasm` (65,175 bytes release build — slightly smaller than sort at 71,258 bytes since uniq has no sort/dedup/reverse codegen, just the run-length walk). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 131/131 pass across all 12 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, tail 7, uniq 7, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit / sh changes — pure userland binary slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — uniq consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI fs+stdio handles for I/O; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin the uniq semantics across default adjacent-only collapse, `-c` count prefix, `-d` duplicates-only filter, `-u` unique-only filter, `-du` empty-intersection, stdin mode, and missing-file exit 1. **Architectural milestone**: tenth coreutil binary, fourth new-binary expansion in a row (wc → head → tail → sort → uniq, but in commit order it's wc → head → tail → sort → uniq making this five from the original 7 — the post-7 set is now 5 strong: wc + head + tail + sort + uniq). T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort" to "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq". The cluster-flag pattern (grep f667018) is now used by 6 coreutils (grep, wc, sort, uniq, plus the cluster-form follow-ups in cat / cp / etc.); the value-consuming-flag pattern (head d631e58) so far has only one consumer (head); the trailing-empty-segment-drop line-split pattern (sort f1e673b) now has its second consumer (uniq) and is queued for the lift-to-lib.rs trigger. Updated tasks.md line 291: appended `; uniq landed in e5d44bc` to the partial-annotation prefix. **Sort/uniq pipeline pair complete**: with sort (f1e673b) and uniq (e5d44bc) both in, the canonical Unix `sort | uniq` pipeline can now run end-to-end inside PMos once T143's pipeline runner ships. The `sort -u` form is also already supported (sort's `-u` flag globally dedupes), so users have both the pipe-composition and the fused-flag forms. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, future single-flag-follow-up slices): `-i` (case-insensitive line comparison — needs case-folded equality predicate); `-f N` (skip first N fields per line before comparing — needs whitespace tokenisation); `-s N` (skip first N chars per line before comparing); `-w N` (compare at most N chars per line); the `--` long-form flags; the historical 2-arg input-file/output-file form (intentionally rejected in v1 per the brief). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (e) T146 `tee` (write stdin to multiple outputs) / `tr` (character translation, in-flight in worktree-agent-a8b... per task #59) / `cut` (field/byte extraction) / `paste` (parallel line merge) — more new-binary expansion slices following this slice's shape; (f) T146 `uniq -i` (case-insensitive); (g) lift line-split helper to `crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs` once the third consumer arrives; (h) T097 init unit tests; (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — tr coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-af9f9145c8c637099; rebased onto local main HEAD `6560a6e`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `tr` test binary at 7 tests; coreutils total 124 → 131 tests (+7) across all 12 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, tail 7, tr 7, wc 8). Commit 2718c78. Tenth NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239, `wc` 44f5987, `head` d631e58, `tail` 4b2458d, `sort` f1e673b) — fourth new-binary expansion slice in a row, following the `wc` (44f5987), `head` (d631e58), `tail` (4b2458d), and `sort` (f1e673b) precedent. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/tr.rs` (~125 lines, std-only, zero deps beyond the std library `BTreeMap` / `BTreeSet`) implements `tr [-d] SET1 [SET2]` — POSIX-ish character translate / delete reading stdin only (no file args, mirroring POSIX `tr` which is always stdin-only by spec). Two modes: default `tr SET1 SET2` translates each char in SET1 to the corresponding char in SET2 (1-to-1 char-by-char mapping in source order; if SET2 is shorter than SET1 the last char of SET2 pads the remaining SET1 entries — POSIX-required behaviour); `-d SET1` deletes every char in SET1 (no SET2 with `-d`; POSIX forbids it and this implementation rejects `tr -d SET1 SET2` as a usage error). Chars outside SET1 pass through unchanged. SETs are LITERAL char sequences in v1 — range syntax (`a-z` → expanded to `abc...xyz`) and backslash escapes (`\n`, `\t`, `\\`, `\r`, `\NNN` octal) are explicitly DEFERRED to future slices, called out at module-doc level so future readers know the v1 limit. Errors: wrong arg count writes `tr: usage: tr SET1 SET2  or  tr -d SET1` to stderr and exits 1; unknown flag writes `tr: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr and exits 1; SET2 with `-d` writes the same usage line and exits 1 (POSIX: "extra operand"). Exit-1-on-any-error matches cat / wc / sort exit-1-on-any-failure shape (not grep's exit-2-on-usage shape — tr's failure model is "any usage problem → exit 1" without grep's open-error/no-match partition). **CLI parser shape**: mirrors grep's POSIX-style short-flag clustering (commit f667018) — single-pass arg split into flags + sets buckets, `-d` toggles `delete` in the per-character match arm, `--` is the hard separator, `-` (bare) is preserved as a non-flag arg (would land in `sets` and fail the count check, matching the convention). Future slice could add `-c` (complement SET1), `-s` (squeeze repeats) using the same cluster pattern. **Implementation**: `main` first parses flags, then validates arg count by mode (`-d` requires exactly 1 SET; default requires exactly 2), then reads stdin via `io::stdin().lock().read_to_string(&mut input)` (UTF-8 decoded — non-UTF-8 input would surface as a stdin read error and exit 1; future slice could route through `read_until` byte-level for invalid-UTF-8 robustness). For delete mode, `BTreeSet<char>::from_iter(set1.chars())` is the lookup table; the input walk is `input.chars().filter(|c| !drop.contains(c)).collect::<String>()` — the BTreeSet is fine for small SETs (typical `tr -d` usage has < 10 chars; a HashSet would also work but BTreeSet has zero allocation overhead for tiny sets and avoids the hasher dependency). For translate mode, `build_translation_map(set1, set2) -> BTreeMap<char, char>` walks SET1 char-by-char, picks `s2[i]` if `i < s2.len()` else `s2.last()` (the pad char) else continues; returns the full map. The input walk is `for c in input.chars() { buf.push(*map.get(&c).unwrap_or(&c)); }` — `unwrap_or(&c)` is the pass-through-on-miss idiom. Both modes write the result via a single `out.write_all(...)` call to the locked stdout (no per-char write — the entire output is buffered in a String first, which is acceptable for v1 since input is bounded by stdin-buffer-size). Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/tr.rs` (7 tests, NEW): `translates_set1_to_set2_one_to_one` (`abc` + `xyz` SETs, stdin `abc\n` → stdout `xyz\n` — pin the byte-exact 1-to-1 mapping); `set2_shorter_pads_with_last_char` (`abc` + `xy` SETs, stdin `abc\n` → stdout `xyy\n` — pin the POSIX padding behaviour: a→x, b→y, c→y since SET2's last char is y); `dash_d_deletes_chars_in_set1` (`-d lo` SET, stdin `hello world\n` → stdout `he wrd\n` — pin the deletion behaviour and the multi-char SET semantics); `chars_outside_set1_pass_through` (`abc` + `xyz` SETs, stdin `abXcd\n` → stdout `xyXzd\n` — pin the pass-through-on-miss: a→x, b→y, X→X (untouched, not in SET1), c→z, d→d (untouched), \n→\n; **note**: the brief's example output `xyXyz\n` was a typo — the actual semantically-correct output for SET1=abc / SET2=xyz mapping `a→x b→y c→z` applied to input `abXcd` is `xyXzd`, not `xyXyz`); `wrong_arg_count_exits_one` (no args, no stdin → exit 1, stderr contains `usage:`); `unknown_flag_exits_one` (`-x abc xyz` → exit 1, stderr contains `unknown flag`); `dash_d_with_set2_arg_is_error` (`-d abc xyz` → exit 1, stderr contains `usage:` — POSIX forbids SET2 with `-d`, this implementation flags it as a usage error). The 7-test count matches the brief's "5-7 tests" specification. Each test routes through a shared `run_with_stdin(args, stdin_bytes) -> (Option<i32>, Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>)` helper that spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_tr")` via `std::process::Command` with `Stdio::piped()` for stdin/stdout/stderr, writes the input, drops stdin, and waits for completion — the test scaffold is simpler than cat/sort/wc because tr has no file args (no scratch-dir helper, no atomic counter). Module doc-comment at the top of `tr.rs` lays out: the two modes (default + `-d`), the literal-SET (no range / escape) v1 semantic with explicit deferral notes for ranges and escapes, the error shape (`tr: usage: ...` and `tr: unknown flag: ...`), and the precedent file list `cat,grep,sort,head`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "tr" path = "src/bin/tr.rs"` block adjacent to `sort` (the most recently added binary, prior to this slice); `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "tr")` after `("coreutils", "sort")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/tr.wasm` (67,736 bytes release build — slightly larger than sort's 71 KB and head's 67 KB because of the BTreeMap / BTreeSet monomorphisations). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 131/131 pass across all 12 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, tail 7, tr 7, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `cargo build -p xtask` clean. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit / sh changes — pure userland binary slice in the coreutils crate plus one xtask tuple. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — tr consumes only `std::env` / `std::io` / `std::process` / `std::collections`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI stdio handles for I/O (no fs_*, only fd_read on stdin and fd_write on stdout); Principle X (testability) strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin the tr semantics across 1-to-1 translation, SET2 padding (POSIX corner case), `-d` deletion, pass-through-on-miss, missing-args usage error, unknown-flag rejection, and `-d`-with-SET2 rejection. **Architectural milestone**: tenth coreutil binary, fourth new-binary expansion in a row (wc → head → sort → tr) — the new-binary cadence remains the dominant runway. T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort" to "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + tr"; the partial-annotation prefix on tasks.md line 291 tracks it as an extension landing alongside the per-flag follow-ups. The cluster-flag pattern (grep f667018) is now used by 6 coreutils (grep, wc, sort, tr, plus the cluster-form follow-ups in cat / cp / etc.); the value-consuming-flag pattern (head d631e58) so far has only two consumers (head, tail). The literal-SET pattern is new to tr — future slices that add range expansion (`a-z`) and escape parsing (`\n`, `\t`) would slot directly into the SET-parsing helper without touching the translate / delete paths. **Memory / performance note**: tr buffers the entire stdin input in a `String` (`read_to_string`), which is acceptable for v1 — PMos stdin payloads fit in memory until proven otherwise (matching cat / sort memory shape). A streaming form (read-line / write-line) would let tr handle multi-gigabyte stdin without buffering, but is out of scope for v1; the buffered form is the right shape for the current tier. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, future single-feature-follow-up slices): range syntax `a-z` (expand to `abc...xyz` — needs a SET preprocessor that walks the source string and detects `<char>-<char>` sequences); backslash escapes `\n`, `\t`, `\\`, `\r`, `\NNN` (octal) (needs a similar SET preprocessor that walks the source and translates `\\` + next char or `\\NNN` triplets); `-c` (complement SET1 — match every char NOT in SET1); `-s` (squeeze repeats — collapse runs of the same char in SET1); `-t` (truncate SET1 to SET2's length, suppressing the pad behaviour); character class names `[:alpha:]` / `[:digit:]` / `[:upper:]` / `[:lower:]` / `[:space:]` (the POSIX class-name syntax); `tr -cs SET1 SET2` cluster of complement + squeeze. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file`); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first); (e) T146 `uniq` (post-sort dedup, no internal sort) / `tee` (write stdin to multiple outputs) / `cut` (column extraction) / `paste` / `nl` (numbered lines) / `expand` / `unexpand` (more new-binary expansion slices); (f) T146 `tr a-z A-Z` (range syntax follow-up) / `tr` escape parsing follow-up / `sort -n` (numeric) / `sort -k N` / `sort -f` (case-fold) / `head -c N` / `tail -c N` / `wc -m` / `wc -L` (per-binary flag follow-ups); (g) lift `parse_count_arg` to `crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs` once a third value-consuming-flag consumer arrives; (h) T097 init unit tests; (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration.

After the **T146 follow-up — tee coreutil slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-agent-a8cb7c78a9dd6f496; rebased onto local main HEAD `d321028`, no movement — branch was already up to date so the rebase was a no-op): coreutils-tests crate gains a brand-new `tee` test binary at 7 tests; coreutils total 138 → 145 tests (+7) across all 14 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, tail 7, tee 7, tr 7, uniq 7, wc 8). Commit d06c78e. Eleventh NEW coreutil binary on the coreutils crate (after `cat` 2655551, `grep` f667018, `cp` 56904e9, `mkdir` a8c2558, `rm` db55782, `mv` 1b71859, `ls` 41e8239, `wc` 44f5987, `head` d631e58, `tail` 4b2458d, `sort` f1e673b, `uniq` e5d44bc, `tr` 2718c78) — fifth new-binary expansion slice in a row, following the `wc` (44f5987) / `head` (d631e58) / `tail` (4b2458d) / `sort` (f1e673b) / `uniq` (e5d44bc) / `tr` (2718c78) precedent. `tee` was anticipated explicitly in the uniq slice's "next single-slice runway candidates" list as `tee (write stdin to multiple outputs)` and again in the tr slice's runway list — the natural fan-out coreutil landing now. `crates/coreutils/src/bin/tee.rs` (~95 lines, std-only, zero deps) implements `tee [-a] [file ...]` mirroring the cat/grep/cp/mkdir/rm/mv/ls/wc/head/tail/sort/uniq/tr structure: hand-rolled argv parsing (no `clap`), `std::fs::OpenOptions` for the truncate-vs-append distinction, partial-success multi-file shape (per-file open / write errors flip a had-error flag and continue with the remaining files; stdin/stdout still receives the bytes regardless), exit 0 on full success / 1 on any error (per-file failure, unknown flag, stdin read failure, stdout write failure). **Zero file args is valid** — tee with no path args degrades to a "stdin → stdout only" pass-through (acts like `cat` with no args). This is THE distinguishing feature from cat's no-arg behaviour: tee's primary purpose is the fan-out (one input → many outputs), but the zero-fan-out shape is still a legitimate use of the binary, and the brief explicitly called this out as required behaviour. **Default truncate vs `-a` append**: the default invocation opens each file with `OpenOptions::new().write(true).create(true).truncate(true)` so an existing file is fully overwritten with the new bytes (POSIX `tee` default); `-a` swaps `truncate(true)` for `append(true)` so the new bytes are concatenated to the end of any existing content (a pre-created file with `old\n` + tee `-a` of `new\n` yields `old\nnew\n` — the canonical log-append pattern). The `OpenOptions` builder is used rather than `File::create` (truncate) / `File::options().append(true).create(true).open()` (append) because the same builder handles both cases by toggling one boolean — the parser sets `append = true` for `-a` and the open path conditionally calls `truncate(true)` or `append(true)` on the otherwise-shared `OpenOptions`. **CLI parser shape — short-flag clustering**: mirrors grep's POSIX-style cluster pattern (commit f667018) byte-for-byte — single-pass arg split into flags + paths buckets, the per-character match-arm in `arg[1..]` toggles `append` for `a`, `--` is the hard separator, `-` (bare) is preserved as a path arg (would attempt to open a file literally named `-`, which is the v1 simplification — POSIX historically maps `-` to stdin/stdout depending on context, but tee is purely "open files" so the `-`-as-stdout convention isn't applicable here). Unknown flag (e.g. `-x` or `-ax` cluster) writes `tee: unknown flag: <full-arg>` to stderr and exits 1 (the full pre-cluster `arg` is reported, not just the offending char — matching grep/sort/wc/uniq/tr convention). Exit-1-on-any-error matches cat / wc / sort / tr exit-1-on-any-failure shape (not grep's exit-2-on-usage — tee's failure model is "any problem → exit 1" without the open-error/no-match partition). **Implementation — buffered fan-out**: `main` reads stdin via `io::stdin().lock().read_to_end(&mut input)` into a `Vec<u8>` (raw bytes, NOT `String`/UTF-8 decoded — tee is meant to be byte-faithful so binary data passes through unchanged, the same way cat does), then iterates over the parsed `paths`: per-path opens via the `OpenOptions` builder, on success writes the buffered bytes via `f.write_all(&input)` (per-path failure flips `had_error` and continues), on open-failure logs `tee: <path>: <err>` and continues with the next path. After the file fan-out, the same buffered bytes are written to stdout via the locked stdout writer; a stdout write failure also flips `had_error`. The order of operations (files first, then stdout) is deliberate: even if stdout is closed (e.g. piping `tee out.txt | head -1`), the file writes still complete cleanly, mirroring GNU tee's "broken pipe doesn't lose file output" guarantee. (POSIX historically wrote to both file and stdout per-byte interleaved, but for the buffered v1 implementation the order doesn't change semantics — every output sink sees the same complete byte stream.) The buffered form means tee's memory shape is O(input-size); a streaming form (read-chunk → write-to-each-output) would let tee handle multi-gigabyte stdin without buffering, but the buffered form is the right shape for v1 and matches cat / sort / tr / uniq memory shape. **Tests at `crates/coreutils/tests/tee.rs` (7 tests, NEW)**: `writes_stdin_to_stdout_when_no_files` (zero file args, pipe `b"hello\n"` → exit 0, stdout `b"hello\n"`, stderr empty — pin the cat-like pass-through path); `writes_stdin_to_stdout_and_one_file` (one file arg, pipe `b"hello\n"` → exit 0, stdout `b"hello\n"`, file contents `b"hello\n"` — pin the canonical fan-out behaviour); `writes_stdin_to_multiple_files` (two file args, pipe `b"shared bytes\n"` → exit 0, stdout has the bytes, both files have the bytes — pin the multi-file fan-out); `dash_a_appends_to_existing_file` (pre-create file with `b"old\n"`, `-a` flag, pipe `b"new\n"` → exit 0, stdout `b"new\n"`, file contains `b"old\nnew\n"` — pin the POSIX-`-a` append semantic); `default_overwrites_existing_file` (pre-create file with `b"old"`, default mode, pipe `b"new"` → exit 0, stdout `b"new"`, file contains `b"new"` — pin the POSIX truncate-on-open default); `bad_path_continues_writing_others` (`/nonexistent/foo` (unwritable parent dir) + a good path, pipe `b"payload\n"` → exit 1, stdout `b"payload\n"`, good file has `b"payload\n"`, stderr contains `tee:` — pin the partial-success continuation: even though one path failed, stdin/stdout is unaffected and the good file got the bytes); `unknown_flag_exits_one` (`-x`, no stdin → exit 1, stdout empty, stderr contains `unknown flag` — pin the unknown-flag rejection). The 7-test count matches the brief's "5-7 tests" specification and follows uniq / tr / sort precedent in choosing 7. Each test routes through a shared `run_with_stdin(args, stdin_bytes) -> (Option<i32>, Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>)` helper that spawns `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_tee")` via `std::process::Command` with `Stdio::piped()` for stdin/stdout/stderr. The args are passed as `&[&OsStr]` rather than `&[&str]` because the `PathBuf::as_os_str()` paths returned by `scratch_dir(tag).join(...)` are `OsStr`-natured and converting them back through `to_str()` would impose a UTF-8 constraint that the rest of the test scaffold doesn't need. The scratch-dir helper (per-test directory at `std::env::temp_dir()` keyed by tag + pid + atomic counter) follows the sort / uniq pattern byte-for-byte; cleanup-on-success / leave-on-failure pattern. Module doc-comment at the top of `tee.rs` lays out: the read-stdin-then-fan-out semantic, the truncate-vs-append distinction with explicit OpenOptions invocation, the partial-success multi-file shape, the cluster-flag inheritance from grep, and the precedent file list `cat,grep,sort,tr`. **Cargo.toml + xtask wiring**: `crates/coreutils/Cargo.toml` gains a new `[[bin]] name = "tee" path = "src/bin/tee.rs"` block adjacent to `tr` (the most recently added binary, prior to this slice); `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs::USERLAND_BINS` gains `("coreutils", "tee")` after `("coreutils", "tr")` so `just build` ships `dist/assets/bin/tee.wasm` (60,515 bytes release build — slightly smaller than tr's 67 KB and sort's 71 KB since tee has no BTreeMap / BTreeSet codegen, just an OpenOptions chain and a Vec<u8> buffer). Verification: `cargo test -p coreutils` 145/145 pass across all 14 binaries (cat 13, cp 12, grep 25, head 8, ls 16, mkdir 10, mv 6, rm 12, sort 7, tail 7, tee 7, tr 7, uniq 7, wc 8); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p coreutils` clean; `cargo build -p xtask` clean. No abi / kernel / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new syscalls / errnos / opcodes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS / display-server / toolkit / sh changes — pure userland binary slice in the coreutils crate plus one xtask tuple. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — tee consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs::OpenOptions` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI fs+stdio handles for I/O (path_open via OpenOptions, fd_write via write_all, fd_read via read_to_end on stdin); Principle X (testability) strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin the tee semantics across no-files cat-like pass-through, single-file fan-out, multi-file fan-out, `-a` append-mode, default truncate-mode, partial-success continuation, and unknown-flag rejection. **Architectural milestone**: eleventh coreutil binary (or fourteenth if you count the per-binary slot — cat / cp / grep / head / ls / mkdir / mv / rm / sort / tail / tee / tr / uniq / wc), fifth new-binary expansion in a row (wc → head → sort → uniq → tr → tee, which is six total post-7-listed). T146's binary inventory grows from "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr" to "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee"; the partial-annotation prefix on tasks.md line 291 tracks it as an extension landing alongside the per-flag follow-ups. The cluster-flag pattern (grep f667018) is now used by 7 coreutils (grep, wc, sort, uniq, tr, tee, plus the cluster-form follow-ups in cat / cp / etc.); the value-consuming-flag pattern (head d631e58) so far has only two consumers (head, tail); the OpenOptions-with-toggleable-flag pattern is new to tee — future slices that need an explicit "create-or-append" semantic (e.g. a hypothetical `cp -a` archive mode, or future `>>` redirection-target handling in T142's redirection slice) could mirror this shape. The byte-faithful (Vec<u8>, not String) stdin-buffer pattern is also new to tee within coreutils — uniq / tr / sort all decode as UTF-8 because they operate on character-level semantics; cat uses `fs::read` (byte-faithful) per-file but its no-arg stdin path uses `io::copy` rather than buffer-then-fan-out. **Memory / performance note**: tee buffers the entire stdin input in a `Vec<u8>`, which is acceptable for v1 — PMos stdin payloads fit in memory until proven otherwise (matching cat / sort / tr / uniq memory shape). A streaming form (read-chunk-write-to-each-sink loop) would let tee handle multi-gigabyte stdin without buffering, but is out of scope for v1; the buffered form is the right shape for the current tier. **Explicitly deferred** (out of slice scope, future single-feature-follow-up slices): `-i` (ignore SIGINT during write — would let tee continue logging through user-initiated interrupts, useful for shell logging idioms); `-p` (operate in pipe-only mode, diagnose write errors but don't exit on EPIPE — the GNU extension); `--output-error MODE` (the GNU long-form for the four-way error-handling switch: `warn` / `warn-nopipe` / `exit` / `exit-nopipe`); `-` as a path-arg alias for stdout (the POSIX convention that maps the bare `-` to stdout — currently lands in `paths` as a literal file named `-`); the streaming write path that splits stdin into chunks instead of buffering whole; the SIGPIPE / EPIPE handling that lets tee's stdout close while file outputs continue (tee technically already has this since stdout-write happens after the file writes, but the `had_error` flag still flips on stdout failure, which means a closed pipe is reported as exit 1 even though the file outputs succeeded — a future slice could partition the error model so file-write success + stdout EPIPE = exit 0). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests for previously-unhandled WASI opcodes; (c) T142 redirection partial (`> file`, `>> file`, `< file` — tee's OpenOptions pattern is the natural reference); (d) T142 pipe partial (`|` — needs T143's `proc_spawn` runner first; with sort / uniq / tee / tr all in, the canonical Unix `cmd | tee log.txt | grep pattern` pipeline is fully runnable once pipes ship); (e) T146 `cut` (column extraction) / `paste` (parallel line merge) / `nl` (numbered lines) / `expand` / `unexpand` (more new-binary expansion slices); (f) T146 `tee -i` (signal-suppressed) / `tr a-z A-Z` (range syntax follow-up) / `tr` escape parsing follow-up / `sort -n` (numeric) / `sort -k N` / `sort -f` (case-fold) / `head -c N` / `tail -c N` / `wc -m` / `wc -L` (per-binary flag follow-ups); (g) lift line-split helper to `crates/coreutils/src/lib.rs` once the third consumer arrives (sort + uniq are the current two consumers); (h) T097 init unit tests; (i) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration. **Sort / uniq / tee / tr quartet complete**: with sort (f1e673b), uniq (e5d44bc), tee (d06c78e), and tr (2718c78) all in, the canonical Unix logging idiom `cmd | sort | uniq -c | tee summary.txt` can now run end-to-end inside PMos once T143's pipeline runner ships.

After the **T192 follow-up — settings about reads /proc/storage slice**: settings cli-tests file +3 (cli.rs 28 → 31), settings binary +~100 lines (`run_about` + new `read_proc_storage` helper), no kernel/abi/TS movement. Commit 29ea53e. T192's partial (cbbebf6) shipped `settings about` emitting version + ABI + LICENSE/CREDITS; the `/proc/storage` clause was deferred pending T169's `KernelProcFsSource` bridge wiring live block-driver counters into procfs. With T169 (1f50924 / 92a2d4a) now landed, this slice extends `about` to read `/proc/storage` via plain `std::fs::read_to_string` and emit a Storage section after License/Credits. The file format is the one T169's `format_storage_snapshot` in `crates/kernel/src/fs/procfs.rs` produces: `<quota> <used> <files>\n` — three space-separated u64 values on a single line. The reader splits on ASCII whitespace via `body.split_ascii_whitespace()`, takes the first three tokens, parses each as `u64`, and on success emits `Storage:` + `  quota: <u64>` + `  used:  <u64>` + `  files: <u64>` (note the two-space alignment under `used:` to match `quota:`/`files:` on the colon column — eyeballed alignment, not column-pinned formatting). **Failure modes**: FileNotFound silently elides the section (returns `None` from `read_proc_storage` → the `if let Some(...)` block in `run_about` skips the print) — many test environments do not mount procfs and the existing `about_prints_version_abi_license_credits` + `about_missing_doc_root_exits_one` tests must keep passing in environments without the file; the default `/proc/storage` path is never present in `cargo test`. Other I/O errors (permission denied, etc.) write `settings: about: failed to read <path>: <err>` to stderr and also return `None`. Parse failure (fewer than three tokens, or any token not parseable as u64) writes `settings: failed to parse <path>: <reason>` to stderr and returns `None` — about is informational, not a hard procfs probe, so a malformed body shouldn't fail the command. The exact stderr shapes match the brief: `failed to parse` is the brief-pinned substring (covered by `about_warns_on_malformed_proc_storage`); per-token parse failures emit `<path>: quota: <err>` or `<path>: used: <err>` or `<path>: files: <err>` so the offending field is identifiable in stderr. **CLI surface**: new `--proc-storage <path>` flag mirrors the existing `--doc-root <dir>` flag's pattern byte-for-byte — same arg-walking loop in `run_about`, same `--proc-storage requires a path argument` error on missing value, same exit 1 on missing arg. Default path is `/proc/storage` (new `DEFAULT_PROC_STORAGE` constant adjacent to `DEFAULT_CONFIG` and `DEFAULT_DOC_ROOT`). Tests use the flag to point at temp fixture files. **Output formatting**: bytes printed raw (decimal `12345`), not human-readable (KB/MB/GB) — human format is a future flag, called out in the module-doc deferral note. **3 new integration tests in `crates/settings/tests/cli.rs` (28 → 31)**: `about_includes_storage_section_when_proc_storage_present` (write a fixture file with `1024 256 5\n`, run `about --doc-root <dir> --proc-storage <fixture-storage>` → exit 0, stdout contains `Storage:`, `quota: 1024`, `used:  256` (two spaces), `files: 5`); `about_skips_storage_section_when_proc_storage_missing` (pass nonexistent `--proc-storage` path → exit 0, stdout has version + ABI + license but NO `Storage:`, no stderr noise); `about_warns_on_malformed_proc_storage` (fixture with `not numbers\n` → exit 0, stderr contains `failed to parse`, stdout has the rest, no Storage section). **Existing tests untouched**: the existing 28 cli.rs tests do NOT pass `--proc-storage` (they predate the flag), so the default `/proc/storage` path is consulted; in the test environment that path doesn't exist; `read_proc_storage` returns None on FileNotFound and the section is silently elided — none of the existing about / set-* tests see new output. **Module doc-comment updates**: the invocations block grows a new line `settings about --proc-storage <path>` mirroring the `--doc-root` line; the deferred-T192 paragraph is rewritten — `/proc/storage` is no longer in the deferred list, replaced with a "now reads via T169 bridge" paragraph plus the human-readable byte formatting deferral note. **No schema / wire changes**: no new opcode, no abi addition, no kernel rebuild, no display-server change, no toolkit change — pure userland slice in `crates/settings/`. **Verification**: `cargo test -p settings` 31/31 pass (28 existing + 3 new); `cargo build --release --target wasm32-wasip1 -p settings` clean. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — settings consumes only `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::env` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral — userland binary, no new capability surface; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — uses standard WASI fs handles (path_open + fd_read on `/proc/storage`); Principle X (testability) strengthens — 3 new isolation tests pin the storage-section behaviour across the present / missing / malformed axes. **Architectural note**: the read-procfs-and-elide-on-missing pattern is a useful template for future about-pane extensions that consume procfs files (e.g. `/proc/version` once it becomes a stable source — currently emits a placeholder; or `/proc/uptime`, `/proc/meminfo`, `/proc/loadavg` if any of those land in About in future v1.x slices). The `--<flag> <path>` test-hook convention now has three settings consumers: `--doc-root` for LICENSE/CREDITS, `--proc-storage` for /proc/storage, `--config` for /etc/preferences.toml. T192's deferred list shrinks: `/proc/storage` is now landed; remaining deferred items are the tabbed graphical About pane (T116 / T110 blocked) and `/proc/version` as a live source (procfs placeholder), plus the new human-readable-byte-format deferral. Workspace Rust 1465 → 1468 (+3) integration tests; TS 506 → 506 (no movement).

After the **T192 follow-up — settings about --human flag for Storage bytes slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T192-storage-human-bytes; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `1f6ea8d`): settings cli-tests crate +3 (`crates/settings/tests/cli.rs` 31 → 34); workspace +3 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1622 → 1625), summed to +15 across the parallel batch on main (1622 → 1637 after T192 + T144 + T146 cherry-picks together). Commit c65c931. Third T192 follow-up after cbbebf6 (CLI `about` subcommand) and 29ea53e (`/proc/storage` section). Lands deferred item (c) from the `/proc/storage` slice: human-readable byte formatting (KB/MB/GB) for the Storage section. New `--human` boolean flag on the `about` subcommand sits alongside the existing `--doc-root <dir>` and `--proc-storage <path>` value-flag arms in the same argv walker — no parser restructuring required, the third arm slotted in cleanly. `format_bytes_human(n: u64) -> String` is a small free function placed near `read_proc_storage`'s printer: thresholds are base-1024 (>= 1 GiB → `<x.x> GB`, >= 1 MiB → `<x.x> MB`, >= 1 KiB → `<x.x> KB`, else `<n> B`), one decimal place above the B branch via `format!("{:.1} {unit}", n as f64 / divisor as f64)`, plain integer at the B branch via `format!("{n} B")`. The `files` count stays a raw integer in either mode (it's a count, not a size).
