The default (no `--human`) path is byte-for-byte unchanged so the existing storage tests (`about_includes_storage_section_when_proc_storage_present` etc.) keep passing — the slice is purely additive to the existing argv parser. Three new integration tests in `crates/settings/tests/cli.rs`: `about_human_formats_kb_mb_gb` (fixture `2147483648 1572864 5\n` spans GB and MB thresholds via 2 GiB and 1.5 MiB; asserts presence of `GB` / `MB` substrings in stdout AND absence of those substrings under default mode — the negation locks the regression in both directions); `about_without_human_still_prints_raw_bytes` (regression test on the unchanged default path, asserts raw decimal `1024` etc. appear in default but not in human mode); `about_human_with_zero_or_small_values_uses_b` (sub-KB case `512 0 0\n` → plain `B` suffix, exercises both `0 B` and `512 B`). T192's deferred list shrinks: (c) human-byte formatting now landed; remaining deferred items are (a) the tabbed graphical About pane (blocked on toolkit `Container` widget T116 + display-server protocol dispatch T110) and (b) `/proc/version` as a live source (procfs emits a placeholder today — the version string stays hardcoded until a real release ships). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/settings/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — settings consumes only `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::env` / `std::process`; Principle V (process isolation) neutral; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral; Principle X (testability) strengthens — 3 new isolation tests pin the `--human` formatter across the GB/MB threshold, the regression-on-default path, and the sub-KB plain-B branch.

After the **T144 follow-up — `:` null-command builtin slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-colon-builtin; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `1f6ea8d`): sh-tests crate +5 (sh tests 97 → 102 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run); workspace +5 of the +15 in the parallel batch on main. Commit bfab621. Eighth landed builtin in the sh dispatch table after cd / pwd / echo / exit (T123 f6bff57) and export / env (4694a1d) and unset (c1b930b) and the lift-to-builtin.rs refactor (0f2e5b9). Adds the POSIX null command — `:` does nothing, ignores all arguments, exits with status 0. Used in idiomatic shell patterns: `while :; do ...` (infinite loop body), `: ${VAR:=default}` (default-assignment shorthand evaluated for side effect), `if cmd; then : ; fi` (no-op then-arm placeholder). Implementation is a single inline arm in `dispatch_builtin` returning `BuiltinOutcome::Continue` — no helper function (the arm is one expression so a `builtin_colon` wrapper would just add a name without behaviour, breaking the slice's "smallest possible additive change" intent). Module docstring's builtin list updated to include `:` so a future reader scanning the file's top-of-doc enumeration sees it. `dispatch_builtin`'s signature is unchanged — colon needs no new state threading (no env, no cwd, no stdout / stderr); the dispatch table picks up the new arm automatically. No edit to `run.rs` was needed; no edit to `Cargo.toml`. Five new tests in NEW file `crates/sh/tests/colon.rs`: `colon_alone_is_no_op_with_exit_zero` (single-line `:` invocation → REPL re-prints prompt, no other stdout, exit 0 on subsequent `exit`); `colon_with_extra_args_is_still_no_op` (`: foo bar baz` → ignored args, no error, no stdout); `colon_with_unset_var_does_not_error` (`: $UNSET_VAR` → variable expansion to empty token, then no-op; key for the `: ${VAR:=default}` idiom which v1 doesn't yet expand fully but will once the assignment expansion shape lands); `colon_in_sequence_with_other_commands` (multi-line script with `:` between two `echo` commands → both echoes execute cleanly, REPL stays alive); `colon_returns_continue_outcome_not_exit` (exercises the dispatch return value indirectly by checking the REPL doesn't terminate after `:`). T144's deferred list still includes `true`, `false`, `[`/`test`, `:` POSIX siblings (each ships as a separate single-builtin slice — same scope-per-slice cadence the export / env / unset landings used). One transient flake observed during the agent's `cargo test --workspace` run (4 kernel syscall tests `proc_spawn_*` / `random_get_*` failed once, passed on rerun) — investigated and confirmed unrelated to the colon change (the colon arm only touches `dispatch_builtin`, which kernel tests don't exercise); pre-existing parallel-test ordering effect, no action taken on this slice. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — colon arm consumes only the existing `BuiltinOutcome` enum; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 5 new isolation tests pin the colon semantics across alone-no-op, with-args-no-op, with-unset-var-no-op, in-sequence, and continue-outcome.

After the **T146 follow-up — `tr -s` squeeze flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-tr-squeeze; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `1f6ea8d`): coreutils tr-tests crate +7 (`crates/coreutils/tests/tr.rs` 7 → 14); workspace +7 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1622 → 1629), summed to +15 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 5595c8c. First flag follow-up on `tr` after the original binary landed in 2718c78. Adds the standalone POSIX `-s SET1` form: reads stdin and replaces each input run of a repeated character that is in SET1 with a single occurrence of that character; non-SET1 chars pass through unchanged. The slice scope was deliberately narrow — `-s` standalone only; combinations like `tr -ds SET1 SET2`, `tr -s SET1 SET2` (post-translate squeeze), and `tr SET1 SET2` with post-translate squeeze interaction are all DEFERRED to future single-feature slices. Mutual exclusion with `-d` is enforced before the per-flag arg-count branch, so the diagnostic shape is identical regardless of which path tripped it; passing a SET2 with `-s` is also rejected as a usage error. The four `usage:` writelns were collapsed to share a single `usage` const — small refactor that was natural at the parser layer touch and didn't restructure anything outside the slice's stated scope. CLI parser shape unchanged: short-flag clustering (the for-loop walks `arg[1..]` toggling flag bits), `--` hard separator, unknown-flag exit 1 with `tr: unknown flag: <flag>` to stderr — exactly matches the existing `-d` arm. Implementation: the squeeze loop walks `input.chars()` with a `prev: Option<char>` cursor and a `BTreeSet<char>` membership-test for SET1, skipping any char that is both in SET1 and equal to `prev`. `prev` updates on each emit so distinct SET1 chars adjacent to each other (e.g. `ab`) survive intact — only same-char runs collapse, matching POSIX semantics. Empty SET1 short-circuits to a no-op pass-through (the `BTreeSet::contains` returns false for every char). Seven new tests in `crates/coreutils/tests/tr.rs`: `dash_s_squeezes_repeated_runs_in_set1`, `dash_s_squeezes_multiple_chars_in_set1`, `dash_s_passes_non_set1_chars_through_unchanged`, `dash_s_with_empty_set1_is_no_op_pass_through`, `dash_s_single_char_input_is_no_op`, `dash_s_with_set2_arg_is_error`, `dash_ds_combination_is_rejected_as_usage_error`. Tee was the previous T146 expansion landing (d06c78e); this slice begins the post-binary FLAG-follow-up runway on `tr`. T146's binary inventory remains "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee" — no new binary, just the first per-binary flag extension on `tr`. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — tr consumes only `std::env` / `std::io` / `std::process` / `std::collections::BTreeSet` (already a dep for `-d`); Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin the squeeze semantics across single-char SET1, multi-char SET1, non-SET pass-through, empty SET1, single-char input, SET2-with-`-s` rejection, and `-ds` mutual-exclusion. Next single-slice runway candidates remain: (a) T072 extension opcode landings; (b) T078 opcode-level isolation tests; (c) T142 redirection partial; (d) T142 pipe partial; (e) more T146 `tr -c` (complement) / `tr a-z A-Z` (range syntax) / `tr` escape parsing / `head -c N` / `tail -c N` / `sort -n` / `wc -m` (per-binary flag follow-ups); (f) T097 init unit tests; (g) T126/T127 boot-to-desktop integration. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (first multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently (T192 c65c931 + T144 bfab621 + T146 5595c8c) via three isolated agent worktrees (`.worktrees/T146-tr-squeeze`, `.worktrees/T144-colon-builtin`, `.worktrees/T192-storage-human-bytes`), each with `web/node_modules` symlinked from main to avoid the per-worktree `npm install` disk exhaustion called out in prior memory. Coordinator integration was sequential cherry-pick onto main with each feat commit's tasks.md change deferred to its companion docs commit (matching the established two-commit-per-slice cadence: feat for code, docs for tasks.md + SESSION-NOTES). Workspace baseline 1622 → 1637 (+15) post all three cherry-picks, all green.

After the **T146 follow-up — `head -c N` byte-count flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-head-bytes; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `e5b55f8`): coreutils head-tests crate +6 (`crates/coreutils/tests/head.rs` 8 → 14); workspace +6 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1637 → 1643), summed to +20 across the parallel batch on main (1637 → 1657 after T146 + T144 + T142 cherry-picks together). Commit 25b4166. Second flag follow-up on `head` after the original binary landed in d631e58 (the first per-binary expansion was the `-n` value-consuming flag built into the original landing; this slice adds `-c` as the byte-count selector). The slice is purely additive — a new `Mode { Lines, Bytes }` enum drives the emit dispatch, and the existing `-n N` / `-nN` / `-N` (bare-digit) paths each set `Mode::Lines` so the latest count-selection flag wins (matches GNU behavior — `head -n 2 -c 3 file` prints the first 3 bytes, `head -c 3 -n 2 file` prints the first 2 lines). The `-cN` no-space form is also accepted via `strip_prefix("-c")`. The original `parse_count_arg` regex-shaped detector for `-?-?\d+` (which handles bare-digit `-5` and `-nN` clusters) is unchanged. Implementation reuses `BufRead::take(count)` + `io::copy` for the byte-mode emit (streaming, no full-file buffering — matches the existing line-mode shape, where `emit_first_n_lines` already used line-by-line streaming via `BufRead::read_until`); the line-mode helper is renamed `emit_first_n_lines` and a sibling `emit_first_n_bytes` is added, both dispatched through a thin `emit_first` wrapper that picks the helper by mode. Six new tests in `crates/coreutils/tests/head.rs`: `dash_c_prints_first_n_bytes`, `dash_c_with_multiple_files_prefixes_each`, `dash_c_zero_prints_nothing`, `dash_c_larger_than_input_prints_whole_input`, `dash_c_via_stdin`, `dash_c_overrides_dash_n_when_both_given`. T146's binary inventory remains "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee" — no new binary, just the second per-binary flag extension on `head` (after the implicit `-n` that shipped with the original binary). The deferred T146 work that remains is similar to before — `head -c -N` (GNU "all but last N bytes" needs a rolling-buffer streaming strategy), `head -c N[KMG]` suffix multipliers, the symmetric `head -n -K` form (already noted in the existing line-mode doc comment), plus all the per-binary flag follow-ups on the other coreutils. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — head consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io::{BufRead, BufReader, Read, Write}` / `std::process`; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 6 new isolation tests pin the `-c` semantics across the small-input case, multi-file headers in byte mode, N=0 short-circuit, N-exceeds-file-size graceful EOF, stdin path, and latest-flag-wins.

After the **T142 follow-up — `${VAR:-default}` default-value parameter expansion slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-default-value-expansion; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `e5b55f8`): sh lib-tests crate +9 (`crates/sh/src/run.rs::expand_tests` 13 → 22) plus expand-tests integration crate +2 (`crates/sh/tests/expand.rs` 5 → 7); workspace +11 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1637 → 1648), summed to +20 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 77c5aca. Fourth landed T142 partial after `$VAR` substitution / single-quote / double-quote / backslash-escapes-in-double-quotes (the `2a9f731` / `afbf05b` / `f11c1ef` slices). First parameter-expansion modifier landing — the previous slices supported only the bare `$NAME` and unmodified `${NAME}` forms; this slice teaches `expand_vars` to recognise the POSIX `${VAR:-default}` modifier. Implementation: `crates/sh/src/run.rs::expand_vars`'s existing `Some(b'{')` arm gets a new `find_colon_dash` byte-scan helper that runs over the brace region (after the closing `}` is located) looking for the two-byte `:-` sequence; if found, the region splits into a name and a default substring. The split point is the FIRST `:-` (no escaping; nested `:-` would land in the default verbatim). POSIX `:-` semantics: name unset OR set to empty string → expansion is the literal default bytes; otherwise expansion is the var's value. The default value is a LITERAL — recursive `expand_vars` on the default substring is explicitly deferred (to avoid double-evaluation surprises and to keep the byte-by-byte scanner flat). The closing `}` is the FIRST `}` after `:-`, so a literal `}` inside the default would terminate the brace early in v1; nested braces and brace-escaping are deferred to a future slice. Implementation note worth highlighting: the unterminated-brace case (`${X:-no_close`) automatically reuses the pre-existing unterminated-brace handling — the close-brace scanner runs FIRST; only after a valid close is found does `find_colon_dash` fire. This kept the leniency-over-error-surfacing convention single-source instead of re-implementing it in the new arm. Test coverage is split across the two existing test surfaces: 9 new `#[cfg(test)] mod expand_tests` inline unit tests (driving `expand_vars` directly on synthetic env maps — covers unset / empty / nonempty / empty-default / spaces-in-default / no-recursion / unterminated / trailing-`:-` / default-with-curly-brace), plus 2 new integration tests in `crates/sh/tests/expand.rs` driving `run_with_env` end-to-end through the REPL (asserts byte-exact stdout for the `echo ${UNSET:-fallback}` and `echo ${X:-fallback}` happy paths). Total +11 test count delta. T142's deferred list grows to include the broader parameter-expansion family that future slices can pick from one-by-one: `${VAR-default}` (no-colon variant — only-if-unset, NOT only-if-empty); `${VAR:=default}` (default-and-assign, needs env mutation); `${VAR:?error}` (error-if-unset); `${VAR:+alt}` (alt-if-set, inverse semantic); `${VAR:offset:length}` (substring); `${#VAR}` (length); `${VAR%pattern}` / `${VAR#pattern}` (suffix / prefix removal); `${VAR/pattern/replacement}` (substitution). The pre-existing T142 deferred list (pipes / redirection / FOO=bar prefix / `$$` / `$@` / `$0` / `$?` / `$!` / arithmetic / command substitution / tilde / glob / `set -u`) remains unchanged. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `expand_vars` consumes only `std::collections::BTreeMap` + `&str` byte scanning; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 11 new tests pin the `:-` modifier semantics across every documented edge case.

After the **T144 follow-up — `true` builtin slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-true-builtin; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `e5b55f8`): sh true-builtin-tests crate +3 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/true_builtin.rs` 0 → 3); workspace +3 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1637 → 1640), summed to +20 across the parallel batch on main. Commit bb8fd0f. Ninth landed builtin in the sh dispatch table after cd / pwd / echo / exit (T123 f6bff57) / export / env (4694a1d) / unset (c1b930b) / lift-to-builtin.rs refactor (0f2e5b9) / `:` null-command (bfab621). Adds the POSIX `true` builtin — always exits 0, ignores all arguments. Functionally equivalent to `:`, but POSIX-named for scripts that prefer the explicit-named form (`while true; do ...`, `cmd || true`, `if true; then ...`). Implementation is the same single-line inline arm pattern the `:` slice established: `"true" => BuiltinOutcome::Continue,` adjacent to the existing `":" => BuiltinOutcome::Continue,` arm. The convention deliberately does NOT share an implementation helper between `:` and `true` — each ships as a single-T-ID commit without a "lift no-op into a shared helper" refactor commit in between. Module docstring's builtin list updated to include `true`. `dispatch_builtin` signature is unchanged; no edit to `run.rs`; no edit to `Cargo.toml`; no edit to `tests/colon.rs` (kept separate per "one test file per builtin" convention — both files exercise their builtin via `run_with_env`, and the parallel structure makes it clear which test suite covers which builtin). Three new tests in NEW file `crates/sh/tests/true_builtin.rs`: `true_alone_is_no_op_with_exit_zero`, `true_with_extra_args_is_still_no_op`, `true_in_sequence_with_other_commands`. Smaller test count than colon (+3 vs +5) is intentional — `true`'s semantics are already pinned by colon's coverage; only the dispatch-arm name needs new pinning. T144's deferred list now: `false` (POSIX failure no-op, exit 1 — needs the per-command exit-code surface that `$?` from T142 future will read from); `[` / `test` (POSIX conditional expression evaluator — non-trivial); other classic builtins (`type`, `which`, `pushd`/`popd`, `alias`/`unalias`, `read`, `kill`, `jobs`, `fg`/`bg`) each ship as a separate single-builtin slice when their need arises. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `true` arm consumes only the existing `BuiltinOutcome` enum; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 3 new isolation tests pin the `true` semantics across alone-no-op, with-args-no-op, and in-sequence-with-other-commands. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (second multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently (T146 25b4166 + T142 77c5aca + T144 bb8fd0f) via three isolated agent worktrees (`.worktrees/T146-head-bytes`, `.worktrees/T142-default-value-expansion`, `.worktrees/T144-true-builtin`), each with `web/node_modules` symlinked from main. Coordinator integration was sequential cherry-pick onto main with each feat commit's tasks.md change deferred to its companion docs commit (matching the established two-commit-per-slice cadence: feat for code, docs for tasks.md + SESSION-NOTES). Workspace baseline 1637 → 1657 (+20) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable refinement over batch 1: pre-empted the surrounding-context-shift `git apply` failures by going straight to manual Edit-tool surgery for each docs commit's tasks.md change (single source of truth for the placeholder substitution), saving the round-trip of attempting `git apply` and falling back.

After the **T072 follow-up — `cap_grant` extension opcode slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-cap-grant; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `28bb38b`): kernel syscall tests +6 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 331 → 337); workspace +6 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1657 → 1663), summed to +15 across the parallel batch on main (1657 → 1672 after T072 + T146 + T144 cherry-picks together). Commit dc68f2d. First substantive T072 extension-syscall closing slice — adds the `op::CAP_GRANT` (0x1302) dispatch arm + `handle_cap_grant` handler at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs`. Wire format per `contracts/syscalls.md §3.5`: `target_pid` as u32 in `args[0..4]`, `caps_to_grant` as u64 in `args[4..12]`, no heap. Semantic invariants: (1) caller MUST hold `Cap::CapGrant` else `-ENOTCAPABLE`; (2) target pid must exist in process table else `-ESRCH`; (3) `caps_to_grant` MUST be a subset of caller's own caps — no widening; any bit set in `caps_to_grant` but missing from caller results in atomic `-ENOTCAPABLE` rejection (target's caps untouched, no partial application); (4) on success, target's caps become `target.caps ∪ caps_to_grant` (union — never strips caps from target). Critical discovery during implementation: the semantic primitive `CapTable::grant(granter, target, caps) -> Result<(), CapError>` ALREADY EXISTED at `crates/kernel/src/cap/mod.rs:107-128` with the exact spec semantics — `is_superset_of` for the subset check, `union` for the merge, `CapError::{NotPermitted, NotASubset}` both collapse via the existing `From<CapError> for KernelError` impl in `sys.rs:110-117` to `KernelError::NotCapable` so the standard `kerr_to_errno` mapping yields `-ENOTCAPABLE` for both arms (probe-resistance against permission-set inference — userland can't distinguish "you don't have CAP_GRANT" from "you tried to widen"). The handler is therefore a thin adapter: decode args via `args_u32(req, 0)` + `args_u64(req, 4)` (the second using a new offset since args was previously only u32-sliced), call `kernel.caps.grant(pid, target_pid, caps)`, convert `Result<(), CapError>` via `kerr_to_errno(e.into())`. The agent confirmed the `CapSet` API had everything needed (`is_superset_of`, `union`, `from_caps`) — no helper additions in `cap/mod.rs` or `abi/src/cap.rs` were required; this slice is purely additive on the syscall surface. Six new integration tests cover the full happy / failure matrix: happy-path grant, union-not-replacement, caller-lacks-CAP_GRANT, caller-doesn't-hold-requested-cap (no-widening), target-does-not-exist, partial-subset-violation-atomic-reject. Test scaffold (`RegisterArgs` + `mark_ready` + `procs.transition(Running)` + new `cap_grant_args` packer) followed the existing pattern with no helper additions beyond the one packer. The pre-existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe test was rotated from `CAP_GRANT` (which now has a handler — so probing it for ENOSYS would always fail post-slice) to `IPC_SEND` (0x1005, fd-passing primitive, still deferred per the spec) so the `_ =>` arm stays covered against future regressions; the probe comment updated to document the rotation chain. T072's deferred list shrinks: `ipc_*` (specifically `IPC_SEND` and `IPC_RECV` for fd-passing — the primary remaining IPC work; `IPC_SOCKET` / `IPC_BIND` / `IPC_LISTEN` / `IPC_CONNECT` / `IPC_ACCEPT` / `IPC_PIPE` are all already shipped per the dispatch table in `ext.rs`); `mount` / `umount` (0x1400 / 0x1401 — needs a proper filesystem-stack mount-point design); `fs_watch` (0x1402 — needs the VFS-side notifier hook that tmpfs and OPFS would call on mutations); `host_file_recv` (0x1500 — needs the bootstrap-to-kernel `host_file_dropped` notification path). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `handle_cap_grant` consumes only the existing `Kernel::caps` API and the shared dispatch helpers; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — `cap_grant` is the runtime mechanism by which init can hand DISPLAY_CLIENT / KEYMAP_ADMIN / etc. to spawned children per the per-app desktop-entry cap declaration shape; Principle VI neutral — uses the existing extension-syscall opcode space; Principle X strengthens — 6 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Architectural milestone**: first end-to-end T072 extension-opcode landing in this session's parallel-dispatch run; the kernel-side parking shape (no parking, simple done) makes this slice a clean reference for future small extension-opcode landings (`cap_grant`'s shape generalises to any "modify-another-process-with-permission-check" handler — `proc_kill` is the closest pre-existing analogue, but `cap_grant` is simpler because it has no signal-delivery side-effect).

After the **T146 follow-up — `tail -c N` byte-count flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-tail-bytes; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `28bb38b`): coreutils tail-tests crate +6 (`crates/coreutils/tests/tail.rs` 7 → 13); workspace +6 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1657 → 1663), summed to +15 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 6f64f33. Symmetric mirror of the head -c slice (25b4166) — same `Mode { Lines, Bytes }` enum, same latest-flag-wins behavior, same `-cN` / `-c N` parsing shape, same `dash_c_*` test set. Where head and tail diverge is the byte-mode emit helper: head streams via `BufRead::take(N) + io::copy` because head only needs to see the FIRST N bytes; tail must see the WHOLE input to find the last-N window, so `emit_last_n_bytes` reads the input into a `Vec<u8>` and slices `&bytes[bytes.len().saturating_sub(N)..]`. This is O(input-size) memory which matches tail's existing line-mode shape (tail already buffers the whole file for line counting per the pre-existing doc comment) — no new memory cost. The line-mode helper is renamed `emit_last_n_lines` and a sibling `emit_last_n_bytes` is added, both dispatched through a thin `emit_last` wrapper. Six new tests cover the same edge-cases as head -c: small input, multi-file headers in byte mode, N=0 short-circuit, N-exceeds-file-size graceful EOF, stdin path, latest-flag-wins. With this slice the head/tail symmetric pairing is complete: both binaries now ship `-n` (line count, original) and `-c` (byte count, this slice's pair). T146's binary inventory unchanged (still "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee"); flag inventory grows. The deferred T146 work that remains for tail specifically: `tail -c -N` (GNU "all but first N bytes" — needs a different read strategy); `tail -c N[KMG]` (suffix multipliers); `tail -f` (follow / streaming — needs FD poll machinery, blocked on poll/epoll-shaped syscall surface); `tail -F` (follow + reopen on truncate). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — tail consumes only `std::env` / `std::fs` / `std::io` / `std::process`; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 6 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case.

After the **T144 follow-up — `false` builtin slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-false-builtin; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `28bb38b`): sh false-builtin-tests crate +3 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/false_builtin.rs` 0 → 3); workspace +3 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1657 → 1660), summed to +15 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 40d3aee. Tenth landed builtin in the sh dispatch table after cd / pwd / echo / exit / env / export / unset / `:` / `true` — closes the simple no-op trio (`:` / `true` / `false`). Always exits 1, ignores all arguments. Used in shell idioms like `until false; do ...` (which never starts), `cmd && false` (force fail), or as a placeholder where a guaranteed non-zero status is needed. **Critical implementation discovery — and the foundational architectural fix that comes with it**: the existing `BuiltinOutcome::Exit(N)` variant is REPL-terminating (it returns `ExitStatus::Exit(N)` from `run_with_env`, which the `sh` binary translates to a process exit code). Using `Exit(1)` for `false` would have killed the user's shell on the FIRST invocation, breaking `cmd || false`, `until false`, and every other idiom that expects `false` to leave a non-zero status WITHOUT terminating the REPL. No `Status` or similar non-terminating exit-code variant existed pre-slice. The agent could have deferred with concerns; instead they landed the foundational fix because it came in well under the 10-line threshold the slice prompt set. New variant `BuiltinOutcome::Status(i32)` in `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` represents "this command exited with code N, but the REPL continues" — paired with a new `BuiltinOutcome::Status(_)` match arm in `crates/sh/src/run.rs` that continues the REPL loop. The status byte is currently dropped; a future T142 `$?` slice will stash it into the env map for the next command's expansion. Total expansion: 1 new variant (with doc comment, ~6 lines), 1 dispatch arm (`"false" => Status(1)`, 1 line), 1 REPL match arm (with explanatory comment, ~8 lines). The `dispatch_builtin` signature is unchanged. Three new tests in NEW file `crates/sh/tests/false_builtin.rs`: `false_alone_exits_one_but_repl_can_continue` (multi-line script with `false\nexit\n` runs the `exit` cleanly without `false` terminating early); `false_with_extra_args_still_exits_one`; `false_in_sequence_continues_to_next_command` (multi-line: `false\necho still here\nexit\n` → second echo runs; pins that `false` doesn't terminate the REPL). T144's deferred list shrinks: the simple no-op trio is now complete; remaining `[` / `test`, `type`, `which`, `pushd`/`popd`, `alias`/`unalias`, `read`, `kill`, `jobs`, `fg`/`bg` each ship as a separate single-builtin slice when their need arises. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `false` arm consumes only the existing `BuiltinOutcome` enum (which itself just gained the `Status(i32)` variant); Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 3 new isolation tests pin every documented behavior. **Architectural milestone — `BuiltinOutcome::Status(i32)`**: this is now the FOUNDATIONAL outcome variant for any future builtin that exits with a specific status without terminating the REPL — `[`/`test` would use it (`Status(0)` for true, `Status(1)` for false), as would any future builtin that surfaces a meaningful exit code. The `$?` slice that stashes the dropped status byte into `env["?"]` is the natural next step in the T142 parameter-expansion runway. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (third multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently (T072 dc68f2d + T146 6f64f33 + T144 40d3aee) via three isolated agent worktrees (`.worktrees/T072-cap-grant`, `.worktrees/T146-tail-bytes`, `.worktrees/T144-false-builtin`), each with `web/node_modules` symlinked from main. Coordinator integration was sequential cherry-pick onto main with each feat commit's tasks.md change deferred to its companion docs commit. Workspace baseline 1657 → 1672 (+15) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantive kernel slice (T072 cap_grant — closes the first remaining real T072 ENOSYS-fallthrough opcode and adds a foundational caps-handoff primitive that init / launcher will use to grant per-app caps at spawn time) alongside two smaller userland slices (tail -c mirror of head -c, false completing the no-op trio). The kernel slice took 6.7 minutes wall-clock vs 5.1 minutes for the other two — substantive kernel work compiles + tests slower than userland slices but landed cleanly because the agent confirmed the existing `CapTable::grant` primitive already had spec-shaped semantics, reducing the slice to a thin syscall adapter.

After the **T072 follow-up — `ipc_send` extension opcode slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-ipc-send; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `7a416f8`): kernel syscall tests +6 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 337 → 343); workspace +6 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1672 → 1678), summed to +28 across the parallel batch on main (1672 → 1700 after T072 ipc_send + T142 $? + T146 sort -n cherry-picks together). Commit 033cafe. Second substantive T072 extension-syscall closing slice in this run, following cap_grant (dc68f2d) — adds the `op::IPC_SEND` (0x1005) dispatch arm + `handle_ipc_send` handler at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs`, plus a new `Kernel::ipc_send(pid, fd, buf, fd_to_pass: Option<u32>) -> Result<usize, KernelError>` semantic method in `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`. Wire format per `contracts/syscalls.md §3.1`: `fd` as u32 in `args[0..4]`, `len` as u32 in `args[4..8]`, `fd_to_pass` as i32 in `args[8..12]` (-1 = none, any non-negative = an fd-number that must exist in the caller's table), `flags` as u32 in `args[12..16]` (v1 only accepts 0 — any other value → `-EINVAL` before any side effect), and the payload bytes at `heap[heap_ptr .. heap_ptr+len]` with the `len` arg cross-checked against `req.heap_len` (mismatch → `-EINVAL`). Semantic invariants: (1) `FdObject::Socket` calls the existing `IpcTable::send_on_socket(sender_id, buf, passed_fds: Vec<u32>)` primitive (which already queues passed fd numbers on the peer's `Socket::rx_fds` queue — the receiver-side translation into a fresh `FdEntry` is the IPC_RECV slice's job, not this one); (2) `FdObject::PipeWrite` calls `Pipe::try_write` on the existing pipe primitive but rejects `fd_to_pass.is_some()` with `-EINVAL` since pipes are byte streams without an ancillary channel; (3) any other fd object → `KernelError::NotSupportedOnFd` which the handler folds to `-EBADF` rather than the generic `-EINVAL` (the spec calls "wrong fd kind" on an IPC opcode `EBADF`, distinct from FD_WRITE's EINVAL — matching POSIX `send(2)` / `sendmsg(2)`'s documented contract); (4) `fd_to_pass` validity is checked BEFORE the bytes are queued: a bad ancillary fd produces `-EBADF` with the receiver's rx_buf and rx_fds both untouched (atomic-reject — no partial send where the payload lands without the ancillary fd, which would deadlock a recv that asked for both); (5) pipe-broken outcomes additionally post `Signal::Pipe` to the caller's signal inbox via `Kernel::post_sigpipe`, mirroring `fd_write`'s POSIX-shaped EPIPE / SIGPIPE pairing. Critical discovery during implementation: the semantic primitive `IpcTable::send_on_socket` ALREADY EXISTED pre-slice with the exact spec semantics, so the slice is mostly a thin adapter + the pipe-write fallback path — no helper additions in `ipc/mod.rs` were required. Six new integration tests cover the full happy / failure matrix: `ipc_send_happy_path_writes_payload_to_connected_socket` (set up a connected socket pair via a new `ipc_send_connected_pair` helper, send "hello", assert response value == 5 and the server can FD_READ the same bytes back); `ipc_send_with_fd_to_pass_round_trips_to_receiver` (send payload + ancillary fd 9 (a `/dev/console` fd installed on the client), assert the server-side socket has the bytes on rx_buf AND fd 9 on rx_fds via `Kernel::socket_id_from_fd_public` + `IpcTable::sockets_get` — pins the no-receiver-installation invariant); `ipc_send_on_non_socket_fd_returns_ebadf` (caller's fd 3 is wired to /dev/console; ipc_send on it → `-EBADF`); `ipc_send_on_invalid_fd_returns_ebadf` (fd 99 was never opened; `-EBADF` before any side effect); `ipc_send_with_invalid_flags_returns_einval` (`flags = 1`; `-EINVAL`); `ipc_send_with_invalid_fd_to_pass_returns_ebadf` (valid main fd, `fd_to_pass = 999`; `-EBADF` AND server's rx_buf + rx_fds both empty — the atomic-reject invariant). The pre-existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe test was rotated from `IPC_SEND` (which now has a handler — so probing it for ENOSYS would always fail post-slice) to `IPC_RECV` (0x1006, the receive-side fd-passing primitive — still deferred until the receiver-side fd-installation step is designed) so the `_ =>` arm stays covered against future regressions; the probe comment updated to document the rotation chain (CAP_GRANT → IPC_SEND → IPC_RECV is now the documented chain). Coordinator note: the agent process was killed mid-session by a context-compaction event before it could commit, but the work was complete (343 syscall tests passing in the worktree, all 6 new ipc_send tests green) — the coordinator (me) staged + committed the agent's three code files manually in the worktree as 033cafe, then cherry-picked the standard way. T072's deferred list shrinks again: `IPC_RECV` (0x1006, fd-passing receiver — needs the receiver-side fd-installation design: when a passed fd lands in `Socket::rx_fds`, IPC_RECV must allocate a fresh `FdEntry` in the receiver's fd table referencing the source-side socket/pipe/fd object, NOT the source-side fd number — this is the cross-process fd-handoff invariant POSIX `recvmsg(2)` + `SCM_RIGHTS` provides on Linux); `mount` / `umount` (0x1400 / 0x1401 — needs a proper filesystem-stack mount-point design); `fs_watch` (0x1402 — needs the VFS-side notifier hook that tmpfs and OPFS would call on mutations); `host_file_recv` (0x1500 — needs the bootstrap-to-kernel `host_file_dropped` notification path). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `handle_ipc_send` consumes only the existing `Kernel` IPC API and the shared dispatch helpers; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — ipc_send is the foundational write-half of the IPC fd-passing primitive, gating SCM_RIGHTS-shaped fd handoff that any future "service hands a connection fd to a worker" pattern (display server, network stack, etc.) will rely on; Principle VI neutral — uses the existing extension-syscall opcode space; Principle X strengthens — 6 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Architectural milestone**: with cap_grant + ipc_send both landed, T072's remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough surface is down to IPC_RECV + the four genuinely-deferred opcodes (mount/umount/fs_watch/host_file_recv) — IPC_RECV is the natural next slice and the most architecturally interesting because it establishes the cross-process fd-handoff convention that the rest of the syscall layer will inherit.

After the **T142 follow-up — `$?` last-exit-status parameter expansion slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-dollar-question; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `7a416f8`): sh inline expand-tests +6 (`crates/sh/src/run.rs::expand_tests` 22 → 28); sh integration tests +9 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/dollar_question.rs` 0 → 9); workspace +15 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1672 → 1687), summed to +28 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 28a168d. Closes the loop on the dropped-status-byte from `BuiltinOutcome::Status(N)` (introduced in 40d3aee for the `false` builtin). `crates/sh/src/run.rs::run_with_env` now declares a `let mut last_status: i32 = 0;` local at the top of the loop, and the dispatch match captures the outcome to update it: `Continue` → `0` (every successful builtin: cd / pwd / echo / export / env / unset / `:` / `true`); `Status(N)` → `N` (`false` → `1`; future `[` / `test` follow the same pattern); `IoError` would set `1` but the IoError arm short-circuits the REPL so the new value is never read; `NotBuiltin` → `127` (POSIX-mandated "command not found" status, set AFTER writing the diagnostic to stderr); `Exit(N)` short-circuits the REPL so its `last_status` is never observed. **Critical: blank lines (`parts.is_empty()`) and quote errors (`Err(QuoteError::*)`) `continue` BEFORE the dispatch match, so `last_status` is NOT updated** — POSIX requires blank lines and recoverable parse errors to leave `$?` unchanged because no command ran. **Architectural decision: dedicated `i32` field, NOT env map stash.** Approach A (stash `$?` in `env["?"]`) was simpler in the env-threading sense (no signature changes) but pollutes the env namespace — userland could `export ?=foo` and break the resolver. Approach B (`last_status: i32` parameter to `expand_vars` and `assemble_token`) is type-safe and reserves `?` for the shell. The cost was tiny: one extra `i32` parameter through two `pub(crate)` functions, no public API change, no env-map churn. The agent specifically chose Approach B over A and the integration test `dollar_question_does_not_consume_env_lookup` explicitly pins this — even with `env["?"] = "foo"` seeded, `$?` resolves to `last_status`, NOT the env entry. Resolver implementation: `expand_vars` gains a NEW match arm `Some(b'?')` BEFORE the existing brace / name arms (since `?` isn't a `is_name_start` byte the existing scanner couldn't have matched it). The arm pushes `last_status.to_string()` (decimal-formatted i32) and advances 2 bytes. The braced form `${?}` is recognised inside the brace arm by short-circuiting on `region == "?"` BEFORE the `find_colon_dash` modifier scan, since `${?:-default}` is not POSIX-defined and shouldn't accidentally accept the `:-` path. `$?` is a single-byte parameter (like `$1` in shells with positional args), so `$?bar` expands to `<status>bar` with no need for a braced form to disambiguate trailing chars. Test coverage is unusually thorough: +6 unit tests on `expand_vars` directly cover the lexer-level shape (initial-zero, arbitrary status, braced form, trailing chars, mid-token concat, env-namespace-isolation), +9 integration tests via `run_with_env` end-to-end pin every documented behavior including the subtle `dollar_question_resets_to_zero_after_successful_command` (`false\ntrue\necho $?` → `0` because `true`'s `Continue` overwrites the stash, not "only-on-transition") and `dollar_question_persists_across_multiple_lines` (after `false`, two consecutive `echo $?` lines: first sees `1`, second sees `0` because the first echo's `Continue` reset the stash — pins the post-dispatch update order). T142's deferred list shrinks: `$VAR` substitution + `${VAR:-default}` + `$?` are all landed, leaving `$$` (PID — needs a per-process PID stash currently the kernel doesn't expose to userland), `$@` / `$*` (positional-arg list — needs a parser change to parse positional args), `$0` / `$1` / ... (positional args by index — same parser-change blocker), `$!` (last background-job PID — blocked on T143's pipeline + T145's job control), arithmetic `$((expr))`, command substitution `$(...)` / backticks, the `set -e` "exit on non-zero status" mode toggle (the natural successor — once `$?` exists, the next slice for `set -e` reads it after every command and decides whether to terminate). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `expand_vars` consumes only the existing env map + the new `last_status` integer; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 15 new tests pin every documented edge case including the four POSIX-required no-update edge cases (blank line, quote error, Exit short-circuit, env-namespace isolation). **Architectural milestone — `$?` is now the foundational primitive for `set -e`**: with `$?` in place, the next slice that wants to ship `set -e` ("exit immediately on non-zero status") just reads `last_status` after each dispatch and decides whether to terminate. The same primitive enables `[ $? -eq 0 ]` once the `[`/`test` builtin lands, and any future builtin that wants to surface a meaningful exit code now has the wiring to do so. Pairs naturally with the `BuiltinOutcome::Status(i32)` variant landed in the previous batch (40d3aee) — the dropped status byte is no longer dropped.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -n N` numeric flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-numeric; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `7a416f8`): coreutils sort-tests crate +7 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 7 → 14); workspace +7 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1672 → 1679), summed to +28 across the parallel batch on main (1672 → 1700). Commit 3011b62. Numeric-sort selector added to the existing `sort` binary at `crates/coreutils/src/bin/sort.rs`. `sort -n` switches the comparison key from byte-order (default) to the leading signed integer parsed via a new `parse_leading_int(s: &str) -> i64` helper. The parser trims leading spaces / tabs, accepts an optional `+` or `-` sign, then accumulates the longest run of ASCII decimal digits as `i64` via `saturating_mul(10).saturating_add(±digit)` so overflow saturates at `i64::MIN` / `i64::MAX` instead of panicking. Lines whose leading non-whitespace token isn't numeric (e.g. `abc`, empty input, or `+` / `-` with no digits) parse as `0` — order among such lines is unspecified for v1 (POSIX leaves it implementation-defined; the test pair `dash_n_treats_non_numeric_as_zero` accepts either order to stay POSIX-conformant on stability). Trailing non-digit text is ignored once the digit run ends, so `100x` sorts as 100 and `50y` sorts as 50 (giving `50y\n100x\n` under `-n`). Implementation reuses the existing POSIX short-flag clustering in the `arg[1..].chars()` loop — `n` is added as a third arm next to `r` and `u`, exactly mirroring the previous slice's pattern, so `sort -nr` / `sort -nu` / `sort -nru` (or any reordering like `-rn` / `-un`) all parse cleanly. The sort dispatch becomes `if numeric { lines.sort_by_key(parse_leading_int) } else { lines.sort() }`; the existing `if reverse { lines.reverse() }` and `if unique { lines.dedup() }` arms apply unchanged after the sort, so `-nu` still dedupes adjacent duplicates after the numeric sort and `-nr` still reverses into descending numeric order. `sort_by_key` over `i64` is stable in Rust, so original input order is preserved among lines that compare equal — this is the well-known property that lets you compose multiple stable sorts to achieve a multi-key sort, which the hypothetical future `sort -k N1 -k N2` slice will leverage. Seven new tests pin the full edge-case matrix: small-int sort (`10\n2\n1\n` → `1\n2\n10\n` pins that lex would have given `1\n10\n2\n`), negative-number handling (`-5\n3\n-10\n0\n` → `-10\n-5\n0\n3\n`), `-nr` descending numeric, `-nu` dedup-after-numeric-sort, `-nru` all-three-combined (`10\n5\n3\n`), non-numeric-as-zero (POSIX-stability tolerant), and trailing-text-ignored (`100x\n50y\n` → `50y\n100x\n`). T146's binary inventory unchanged (still "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee"); flag inventory grows. Sort-specific deferred work: `sort -g` (general-numeric / float-aware — would need an `f64` parser); `sort -k N` (key-field selection — needs whitespace-tokenized field extraction with start/end column ranges); `sort -t SEP` (custom field separator — pairs with `-k`); `sort -f` (case-insensitive folding for the byte-order comparator); `sort -b` (ignore leading whitespace in the key — partially overlaps with `-n`'s built-in trim); `sort -c` (check-only mode); `sort -m` (assume each input file is already sorted, merge); `sort -o FILE` (write to FILE — enables in-place `sort -o foo foo`). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — sort consumes only `std::env` / `std::io` / the existing `parse_leading_int` helper added in this slice; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case including the POSIX-stability-tolerant non-numeric handling. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (fourth multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 4 (T072 ipc_send 033cafe + T142 $? 28a168d + T146 sort -n 3011b62) via three isolated agent worktrees (`.worktrees/T072-ipc-send`, `.worktrees/T142-dollar-question`, `.worktrees/T146-sort-numeric`), each with `web/node_modules` symlinked from main. Coordinator integration was sequential cherry-pick onto main with each feat commit's tasks.md change deferred to its companion docs commit. Workspace baseline 1672 → 1700 (+28) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantive kernel slice (T072 ipc_send — closes the second remaining real T072 ENOSYS-fallthrough opcode after cap_grant in the previous batch, and the FIRST IPC-with-fd-passing primitive — the foundational write-half that any future "service hands a connection fd to a worker" pattern will rely on) alongside two userland slices (T142 $? — the foundational primitive for `set -e` and `[ $? -eq 0 ]`, leveraging the `BuiltinOutcome::Status(i32)` variant landed last batch; T146 sort -n — straightforward numeric-comparison flag, mirroring the head -c / tail -c / tr -s flag-cluster pattern). Coordination wrinkle worth memorising: agent A (ipc_send) was killed mid-session by a context-compaction event before it could commit its work; coordinator (me) recovered by manually staging + committing the three code files in the worktree, since the implementation was complete and all 6 new tests passed in the worktree. The recovered commit's SHA (033cafe) is the worktree SHA used in tasks.md / SESSION-NOTES references per convention. Two batches in a row of three slices each shipped, +43 workspace tests across two batches (+15 in batch 3, +28 in batch 4); the parallel-dispatch shape is now well-validated for "1 substantive + 2 small" mixes.

After the **T072 follow-up — `ipc_recv` extension opcode slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-ipc-recv; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f8f5afe`): kernel syscall tests +6 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 343 → 349); workspace +6 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1700 → 1706), summed to +21 across the parallel batch on main (1700 → 1721 after T072 ipc_recv + T142 set -e + T146 sort -f cherry-picks together). Commit f00e559. Third substantive T072 extension-syscall closing slice in three batches (cap_grant dc68f2d → ipc_send 033cafe → ipc_recv f00e559); the receiver-side counterpart to ipc_send and the FIRST cross-process fd-handoff primitive in PMos. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs` gains `op::IPC_RECV` + `handle_ipc_recv`, plus a new `Kernel::ipc_recv(pid, fd, max_len, want_fd: bool) -> Result<(Vec<u8>, Option<u32>), KernelError>` semantic method in `sys.rs` and two new private helpers (`Kernel::translate_passed_fd` for the actual fd handoff + `Kernel::find_pid_owning_socket` as a `O(P*F)` linear scan to identify the sender's pid by their socket-side fd table entry). Wire format mirrors ipc_send's: `fd` u32 + `len` u32 + `recv_fd_slot` i32 (`-1` = leave fds queued, non-negative = install lowest-free) + `flags` u32 (v1 only 0). The FIRST architectural decision worth recording: **fd-leading heap layout** — when an fd is installed, the 4-byte LE u32 fd-number lives at `heap[0..4]` and the payload at `heap[4..4+value]`, with `Response.value` carrying byte count and `Response.extra_len` carrying total heap bytes written. The agent considered three alternatives explicitly: (1) fd-leading (chosen — matches existing `proc_wait` writing reaped child pid at `heap[0..4]` and `ipc_pipe` writing dual fds at `heap[0..8]`; every multi-value Response in the dispatcher uses fixed offsets, never variable); (2) payload-first with fd at `heap[value..value+4]` (rejected — forces caller into two-pass parse, breaks natural POSIX-shaped `recv(fd, buf, len)` read pattern); (3) bit-packing fd into `Response.value` alongside byte count (rejected — would break the `value == bytes` convention every other byte-emitting handler follows; the heap-extra path costs 4 bytes per recv, negligible). The convention generalises: any future "syscall returns bytes + an ancillary scalar" should put the scalar at `heap[0..N]` and the bytes after, with `Response.extra_len = N + bytes`. The SECOND architectural decision is the **fd-translation flow**: when `want_fd = true` and rx_fds has an entry, the kernel does (1) find peer SocketId from receive-side socket's `peer` field; (2) `find_pid_owning_socket(peer_id)` scans for the sender pid; (3) look up the queued u32 in sender's fd table to get the underlying `FdObject`; (4) `FdTable::alloc(FdEntry::new(object))` on receiver's table. If any step fails (sender exited, fd closed, peer pointer None) the queued u32 is silently dropped and receiver gets `Ok((bytes, None))` — a deliberate v1 degradation. The agent flagged this explicitly: a more rigorous implementation would resolve the FdObject at SEND time (not RECV time) and queue the FdObject directly, eliminating the dangling-fd race — but that's a separate IPC-table refactor that the IPC_SEND slice (033cafe) committed to NOT doing, so the IPC_RECV slice respects that boundary. Future hardening pass should change `Socket::rx_fds` from `VecDeque<u32>` to `VecDeque<FdObject>` (clone the trait-object enum at send time). The THIRD architectural decision is **non-blocking only in v1**: `recv_on_socket`'s `WouldBlock` (rx_buf empty AND rx_fds empty AND peer still open) surfaces as `-EAGAIN`; the dispatcher does NOT park the caller. Blocking-recv is deferred — would need a `Kernel::park_on_recv(pid, socket_id, request_id)` parking primitive sibling to `park_on_accept` in `dispatch.rs`, plus a wake hook on `send_on_socket`'s rx-buf grow path that mirrors `wake_parked_acceptor_if_any`. Documented inline on `Kernel::ipc_recv` so the missing piece is visible to the next agent. Six new integration tests cover the full happy / failure matrix: happy-path drain, fd-pass-installs-fd-in-receiver-table (the foundational SCM_RIGHTS-shaped invariant), empty-socket EAGAIN, wrong-fd-kind EBADF, invalid-flags EINVAL atomic-reject (the byte stays on rx_buf — pinning that flag validation comes BEFORE side effects), negative-recv_fd_slot leaves rx_fds queued (mirrors POSIX `recvmsg` `msg_control = NULL`). The pre-existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe was rotated from `IPC_RECV` (which now has a handler) to `MOUNT` (0x1400, privileged filesystem mount — still deferred until the fstype-factory plumbing is designed) so the `_ =>` arm stays covered against future regressions; the rotation chain is now CAP_GRANT → IPC_SEND → IPC_RECV → MOUNT in the probe comment. T072's deferred list shrinks substantially: with cap_grant + ipc_send + ipc_recv all landed, the IPC subsystem has its complete fd-passing primitive set (the existing `IPC_SOCKET` / `IPC_BIND` / `IPC_LISTEN` / `IPC_CONNECT` / `IPC_ACCEPT` / `IPC_PIPE` opcodes were already shipped; plus the just-landed `IPC_SEND` + `IPC_RECV` close the data + ancillary-fd half). Remaining T072 work: `mount` / `umount` (0x1400 / 0x1401 — needs a proper filesystem-stack mount-point design); `fs_watch` (0x1402 — needs the VFS-side notifier hook that tmpfs and OPFS would call on mutations); `host_file_recv` (0x1500 — needs the bootstrap-to-kernel `host_file_dropped` notification path). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `handle_ipc_recv` consumes only the existing `Kernel` IPC API + the new private helpers + shared dispatch helpers; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — IPC_RECV is the FIRST cross-process fd-handoff primitive in PMos, establishing the convention that fd installation across processes goes through `FdTable::alloc(FdEntry::new(cloned_object))` (not by passing fd-number references which would be cross-process pointer-aliasing); Principle VI neutral — uses the existing extension-syscall opcode space; Principle X strengthens — 6 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Architectural milestone**: the IPC + capabilities subsystem is now feature-complete for v1's one-process-per-app model. Any service that wants to hand a connection fd to a worker (display server passing client connections, a future network stack passing socket fds, the package launcher passing host_file_recv fds) now has the wiring to do so. The next IPC-themed slice would be blocking-recv parking (mirrors blocking-accept's design) which becomes load-bearing once a process needs to wait on rx_buf without busy-polling EAGAIN. **Coordinator note**: this batch's three slices ran cleanly in parallel (no agent crashes, no rework needed); the established workflow (worktree per agent → cherry-pick excluding tasks.md → docs commit applies tasks.md w/ SHA substitution) handled the +21 tests across kernel, sh, and coreutils crates without conflict.

After the **T142 follow-up — `set -e` errexit mode toggle slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-set-e; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f8f5afe`): sh set_e-tests crate +8 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/set_e.rs` 0 → 8); workspace +8 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1700 → 1708), summed to +21 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 5406116. Closes the natural-successor loop on `$?` (28a168d) by adding the POSIX `set -e` / `set -o errexit` mode that terminates the REPL with the failing command's exit status the moment any command returns non-zero. **The architectural milestone is the new `pub struct ShellFlags { pub errexit: bool }`** added to `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` and re-exported from `lib.rs` as `sh::ShellFlags`. `Copy + Default + PartialEq + Eq + Debug`; threaded through `dispatch_builtin`'s signature as `flags: &mut ShellFlags` between `env` and `stdout`, and through the public `run_with_env` API as a new `flags: &mut ShellFlags` parameter after `env`. The `run` thin wrapper continues to construct a fresh `ShellFlags::default()` so the `sh` binary's `main` never sees the new parameter — but `run_with_env` IS a public API change. Seven existing test files (`colon.rs`, `dollar_question.rs`, `env.rs`, `expand.rs`, `false_builtin.rs`, `quoting.rs`, `true_builtin.rs`) all updated their `drive` helpers to construct `let mut flags = ShellFlags::default();` and pass `&mut flags` to the call. The struct is now the foundational seam for any future shell mode flag (`set -u` for "error on unset variable", `set -x` for "trace each command", `set -n` for "syntax-check only", `set -o pipefail` etc.) — new flags slot in as sibling fields without changing any call site. **`set` builtin shape:** `set -e` and `set -o errexit` both flip `flags.errexit = true`; `set +e` and `set +o errexit` clear it; `set` with no args is a no-op in v1 (POSIX defines this as "print every shell variable" but `env` already covers that; the no-arg path is reserved for future expansion); `set -X` (or any unrecognised short-flag) writes `sh: set: -X: invalid option\n` to stderr and returns Continue so the REPL stays alive. **Critical correctness pin: `set` itself MUST NOT trigger errexit.** The agent considered both branches (separate "skip errexit for set" check vs "always return Continue from set") and chose the always-Continue approach because it naturally falls out of the post-dispatch errexit check — `Continue` sets `last_status = 0`, so `set` invocations skip the `if flags.errexit && last_status != 0` guard unconditionally. The `set_e_alone_does_not_terminate_repl` test pins this directly (the REPL stays alive after `set -e` on its own, exits cleanly when `exit` runs). **REPL termination semantics:** the post-dispatch errexit check fires AFTER `last_status` is updated and BEFORE the next prompt. Triggers: `Status(N)` from `false` (and any future `[`/`test`-style builtin), `NotBuiltin` (sets `last_status = 127` per POSIX). Bypasses: `Continue` arms (status 0) naturally fail the check; `Exit(N)` and `IoError` arms already short-circuit out of the loop above the check (intentional: `exit 5` should report Exit(5) unconditionally, not get reinterpreted as an errexit termination). v1 has no `if` / `while` / `until` / `&&` / `||` / `!` constructs, so there are no POSIX exemption contexts to handle — every non-zero status from any command triggers termination when errexit is on. **The failing command's exit status BECOMES the shell's exit status** (`return ExitStatus::Exit(byte as i32)` where `byte = last_status.rem_euclid(256)`) — so `set -e\nfalse\n` exits with status 1, `set -e\nno_such_cmd\n` exits with status 127. This pins the load-bearing distinction: errexit doesn't surface a generic 1, it surfaces the actual failure code so userland can distinguish WHY the shell died — which is the entire point of `set -e` for shell scripts that want to forward failure signals to a parent process. Eight new tests pin every documented behavior: `set -e` alone doesn't terminate, `set -e\nfalse\n` exits Exit(1), `set -e\ntrue\n` continues, long-form `set -o errexit`, `set +e` clears the flag and `false` no longer terminates, `set -e\nno_such_command\n` exits Exit(127) (NotBuiltin path), `set` no-args is a no-op, `set -X` warns and continues. T142's deferred list shrinks: `set -e` is landed; remaining mode-flag work includes `set -u` (needs `expand_vars` error-path), `set -x` (needs trace hook in dispatch loop), `set -n` (syntax-check only), `set -f` (globbing disable — moot until globbing exists), `set --` (positional reset — blocked on parser), `set -o pipefail` (blocked on T143). The remaining T142 parameter-expansion work also unchanged: `$$` / `$@` / `$*` / `$0`/`$1`/... / `$!` / arithmetic / command substitution / globbing / tilde — all blocked on either parser changes, kernel-side pid exposure, or the T143 / T145 dependency chain. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the new `ShellFlags` struct is purely internal to the sh crate, no other crate sees it; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 8 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Architectural milestone — `ShellFlags` is the foundational seam for shell mode flags**: with this struct in place, every future `set -X` mode (`-u`, `-x`, `-n`, `-o pipefail`) is a pure-additive change — one new bool field, one new arm in `builtin_set`, and one new dispatch-time check in `run_with_env` (or wherever the mode applies). The convention is now: shell modes live on `ShellFlags`, parser modes live on `expand_vars`'s parameters (like `last_status`), and persistent shell state lives on `env: BTreeMap`. This three-way separation is what's letting individual mode-flag slices stay sub-100-line.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -f N` case-fold flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-fold; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f8f5afe`): coreutils sort-tests crate +7 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 14 → 21); workspace +7 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1700 → 1707), summed to +21 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 0ad2b1e. Case-insensitive folding selector added to the existing `sort` binary at `crates/coreutils/src/bin/sort.rs`, mirroring the just-landed `sort -n` slice (3011b62) byte-for-byte. `sort -f` switches the comparison key from raw byte-order to an ASCII-folded byte-order — ASCII lowercase `a..z` maps to uppercase `A..Z` for the COMPARISON KEY ONLY; the original line bytes are emitted unchanged on stdout. This preserves a critical POSIX invariant: `sort -f` is a comparison-only transform, NOT a content-mutation transform — `Apple` stays `Apple` in the output, only the sorted POSITION reflects the case-folded ordering. Implementation reuses the existing POSIX short-flag clustering — `f` is added as a fourth arm next to `r`, `u`, `n`, exactly mirroring the `sort -n` pattern, so `sort -fr` / `sort -fu` / `sort -fnu` / any reordering parses cleanly. The folder is a new helper `fn fold_to_upper_bytes(s: &str) -> Vec<u8>` (~5 lines) that walks `s.bytes()` and maps each byte via `if b.is_ascii_lowercase() { b - 32 } else { b }`. **Deliberately byte-level, not Unicode-level**: non-ASCII bytes (high-bit-set, e.g. UTF-8 continuation bytes like 0xC3 0xA9 for `é`) pass through unchanged — full Unicode case folding would require an icu / unicode-folding-table dependency and significantly more code; that's deferred to a future slice if/when Unicode-aware sort is needed. Sort dispatch becomes a three-way `if/else if/else`: `numeric` wins (case-folding doesn't apply to integer parsing — `Apple` parses to 0 with or without `-f`), then `fold` (folded byte-order), then default lex (raw byte-order). The trickiest edge is **dedup interaction**: when both `-f` and `-u` are set, `dedup_by_key(fold_to_upper_bytes)` is used instead of plain `dedup()` so that `Apple` / `apple` / `APPLE` collapse into ONE line — `Vec::dedup_by_key` is stable and keeps the FIRST entry of each fold-class, which after the case-insensitive sort is the first encountered in the sorted ordering. The `numeric && fold && unique` combination uses plain `dedup()` since numeric dominance means the fold key isn't computed for sort and the dedup boundary should match the sort key (numeric here). Seven new tests pin the full edge-case matrix: case-insensitive sort, output-preservation (the comparison-only invariant), `-fu` dedup-after-fold (FIRST entry wins), `-fr` reverse-after-fold, `-fn` numeric-dominates (no-op for `-f`), non-ASCII passthrough (byte-level pin), empty-input sanity check. T146's binary inventory unchanged (still "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee"); flag inventory grows. Sort-specific deferred work after this slice: full Unicode case folding (icu dependency); `sort -i` (ignore non-printable chars — orthogonal to `-f`); `sort -b` interaction with `-f` (would compose by chaining trim before fold); `LC_COLLATE` env-var hooks for locale-aware folding (v1 stays in C / POSIX locale per existing module docstring); `sort -g` (general-numeric / float-aware); `sort -k N` (key-field selection); `sort -t SEP` (custom field separator); `sort -c` (check-only mode); `sort -m` (assume sorted, merge); `sort -o FILE` (in-place sort enabler). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — sort consumes only `std::env` / `std::io` / the existing `parse_leading_int` + the new `fold_to_upper_bytes` helper; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 7 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (fifth multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 5 (T072 ipc_recv f00e559 + T142 set -e 5406116 + T146 sort -f 0ad2b1e) via three isolated agent worktrees, each with `web/node_modules` symlinked from main. Coordinator integration was sequential cherry-pick onto main with each feat commit's tasks.md change deferred to its companion docs commit. Workspace baseline 1700 → 1721 (+21) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Three batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batch 3 +15, batch 4 +28, batch 5 +21 = +64 across three batches); the parallel-dispatch shape is now firmly established for "1 substantive + 2 small" mixes and the coordinator workflow has been validated against agent-mid-session-crash recovery (batch 4 IPC_SEND agent died but its work was complete — coordinator manually committed). The IPC primitive set is now complete (cap_grant + ipc_send + ipc_recv); the shell mode-flag substrate is established (ShellFlags struct ready for set -u / set -x extensions); the sort flag-cluster is comprehensive (`-r` / `-u` / `-n` / `-f` plus sensible interaction handling). The runway for the next batch is broad: T072 mount/umount or fs_watch (substantive kernel), T142 set -u or set -x (small userland), T144 `[`/`test` builtin (medium userland — uses Status(0)/Status(1)), T146 more sort flags (`-g` / `-k` / `-c`).

After the **T072 follow-up — blocking `ipc_recv` parking primitive slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-ipc-recv-blocking; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `c9da7a8`): kernel syscall tests +5 net (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 354 → 359 — 5 new tests + 2 renames of pre-existing tests for the changed wire-flag semantics); workspace +5 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1721 → 1726), summed to +33 across the parallel batch on main (1721 → 1754 after blocking-recv + set -u + sort -c cherry-picks together). Commit 623a0c2. Pairs the f00e559 IPC_RECV handler with a sibling to `Kernel::park_on_accept`: bytes (or fds) arriving on a parker's socket via IPC_SEND wake the parker with the drained payload + optional fd-prefix in the same fd-leading heap layout the non-blocking handler produces. **Wire format change at the `flags = 0` default** (load-bearing — flips IPC_RECV's default semantic from EAGAIN-on-empty to block-on-empty): `args[12..16]`'s `flags: u32` LSB (`0x01`) is now the NONBLOCK toggle — `flags & 0x01 == 0` (default) parks via `Kernel::park_on_recv`, `flags & 0x01 != 0` preserves the f00e559 EAGAIN-on-empty semantic, `flags & ~0x01 != 0` (any other bit) → `-EINVAL` atomic-reject before any side effect. The agent renamed two existing tests (`ipc_recv_on_empty_socket_returns_eagain` → `ipc_recv_with_flags_one_is_nonblocking_returns_eagain` using `flags=1`; `ipc_recv_with_invalid_flags_returns_einval_atomically` → `ipc_recv_with_invalid_flag_bit_returns_einval_atomically` using `flags=2` since `flags=1` is now valid). The bit-flag parameterisation matches `accept_flags::NONBLOCK` (also bit 0 of its u16 flags word) — the alternative (a separate `IPC_RECV_NONBLOCK` opcode) was rejected for not following the established convention. **Architectural milestone — third blocking primitive in PMos** (after `park_on_accept` for IPC_ACCEPT and `park_on_wait` for PROC_WAIT). The convention is now well-established: parking primitives live on `Kernel` in `sys.rs`, take `(pid, primitive-specific-handle, request_id, ... in-flight params)`, validate state preconditions + the one-parker-per-pid invariant, transition `Running → BlockedOn*` with a primitive-specific `BlockReason`. Wake hooks fire from the success path of the corresponding "send a thing" primitive (`accept_on_socket` succeeded → wake_parked_acceptor; `proc_exit` Zombie transition → wake_parked_waiter; `send_on_socket` rx_buf grew → wake_parked_recver). Cleanup paths fan out to `proc_exit` + SIGKILL arm of `proc_kill` + the resource-close path (here, `release_object`'s Socket arm). **The wake-back path's main complexity vs. accept** is the heap payload: accept wakes produce a single i64 (the new fd) so they need no heap data; recv wakes carry the drained bytes plus optionally a 4-byte fd-prefix, so they reuse the `WakeHeap = Some(PendingHeap)` mechanism the proc_wait slice (76e54e3) introduced for packing the reaped child pid. The TS drainer (`drainWakesForPid` in `kernel-wasm-host.ts`) was unchanged because it already knows how to copy `extra_len` heap bytes into the parker's SAB heap-scratch. Wake-side parker-fd reverse lookup walks the parker's fd table for the SocketId — small in v1, parker count = active blocking recvs; this lookup also handles the "parker's socket fd was closed between park and wake" race by producing an EBADF wake. Cleanup additions worth noting: `release_object`'s Socket arm calls a new `Kernel::wake_parked_recvers_on_socket_close(socket_id)` BEFORE the `IpcTable::close_socket` call — closes one end of a connected pair drains parkers on BOTH ends with EBADF wakes, since the peer's rx side becomes unreadable. Same hook in `drain_closed_object_side_effects` for the deferred-close path. SIGTERM interrupt wired alongside accept + wait: `handle_proc_kill`'s Term arm now calls `interrupt_parked_recv` + `interrupt_parked_accept` + `interrupt_parked_wait` — the three are idempotent because a pid parks on at most one primitive at a time in v1. Five new tests cover the full happy / failure / cleanup matrix: blocking-parks-caller (the new default), wake-with-bytes, wake-with-fd, one-parker-per-pid (EAGAIN on second blocking call from same pid), SIGTERM-interrupts-parked-recv. Plus two renames for the changed-semantic tests. T072's deferred list shrinks to mount/umount + fs_watch + host_file_recv (the four genuinely-design-needed opcodes); the IPC subsystem is now FEATURE-COMPLETE for v1's one-process-per-app model with both blocking and non-blocking semantics on every operation. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — all changes contained to the kernel crate; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — blocking IPC enables the natural request/response patterns userland services need (display server waits on client requests, network stack waits on socket reads) without busy-polling EAGAIN; Principle VI neutral — wire format change is additive (bit 0 of an existing flags field); Principle X strengthens — 5 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case including the SIGTERM interrupt + the cleanup path that fires when the parker's own socket closes mid-park.

After the **T142 follow-up — `set -u` nounset mode toggle slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-set-u; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `c9da7a8`): sh inline expand-tests +8 (`crates/sh/src/run.rs::expand_tests` 28 → 36); sh integration tests +11 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/set_u.rs` 0 → 11); workspace +19 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1721 → 1740), summed to +33 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 79158f5. Closes the natural-successor loop on `set -e` (5406116) by adding the POSIX `set -u` / `set -o nounset` mode that surfaces a "parameter not set" error and terminates the REPL with status 1 the moment any unset variable is referenced via the bare `$NAME` or braced `${NAME}` form. **Architectural extension of `ShellFlags`**: the struct gains a `nounset: bool` sibling to `errexit`. Crucially, the seven existing test files (`colon.rs`, `dollar_question.rs`, `env.rs`, `expand.rs`, `false_builtin.rs`, `quoting.rs`, `true_builtin.rs`, `set_e.rs`) needed ZERO modifications because they all drive the high-level `run_with_env` API which constructs `ShellFlags::default()` (where `nounset: false`) — no signature changes leaked through. This validates the foundational-seam hypothesis from the `set -e` slice: future shell flags slot in cleanly. **Implementation: error path through `expand_vars`.** The expansion-layer check is the load-bearing change. New `pub enum ExpandError { NotSet(String) }` re-exported from `lib.rs` as `sh::ExpandError`. `expand_vars` and `assemble_token` both gain a `flags: &ShellFlags` immutable parameter and switch from returning `String` to `Result<String, ExpandError>`. The dispatch loop in `run_with_env` accumulates expansions and on `Err(NotSet(name))` writes `sh: <name>: parameter not set\n` to stderr and returns `ExitStatus::Exit(1)` directly — the failing-expansion command never runs, the REPL terminates immediately, `last_status` is never updated for that line. **The exit-1 termination is the load-bearing distinction from `set -e`'s runtime command failure** (which would surface the command's actual non-zero status); `set -u` ALWAYS exits 1 because the failure happened at the expansion layer before any command had a chance to set its own status. **Critical edges — the four exemptions** (each pinned by a dedicated test): (1) `${VAR:-default}` default-value form is EXEMPT — the form's whole purpose is to provide a fallback for unset vars; the braced arm's `find_colon_dash` branch goes through the default-value path with NO nounset check (POSIX-required); (2) `$?` and `${?}` are EXEMPT — `last_status` is an `i32` with a known initial value of 0, no "unset" state to report; (3) the unterminated-brace recovery (`${X` with no closing `}`) is EXEMPT because the lenient-literal preservation IS the recovery — no name has been resolved; (4) the unknown-command / NotBuiltin path doesn't trigger nounset because it operates at the dispatch layer, NOT the expansion layer — a typo'd command name under `set -u` produces the standard 127, NOT "parameter not set". The expansion-layer-vs-dispatch-layer boundary is a critical correctness pin for any future error-flagging mode (`set -e` is dispatch-layer, `set -u` is expansion-layer; `set -x` will be dispatch-layer-pre-execution; `set -n` will be dispatch-layer-bypass). **The diagnostic shape** (`sh: <name>: parameter not set`) matches bash and dash; the test pins both halves (the var name AND the "parameter not set" phrase). Eight unit tests pin the `expand_vars` Result shape directly + 11 integration tests pin the REPL-level termination semantics including all four exemption cases. T142's deferred list shrinks: errexit + nounset are landed; remaining mode-flag work includes `${VAR:?error}` (error-if-unset modifier — would use a new `ExpandError::Required { name, message }` variant), `set -x` (trace), `set -n` (syntax-check only), `set -o pipefail` (blocked on T143). The remaining T142 parameter-expansion work also unchanged: `$$` / `$@` / `$*` / `$0`/`$1`/... / `$!` / arithmetic / command substitution / globbing / tilde — all blocked on either parser changes, kernel-side pid exposure, or the T143 / T145 dependency chain. **Inline test refactor worth flagging**: the existing inline test module in `run.rs` gained a thin `ev()` helper that wraps `expand_vars(..., &ShellFlags::default()).expect("...")` so the pre-existing 28 tests stayed byte-identical at the assertion level — the function name in each `assert_eq!` line changed from `expand_vars(` to `ev(`, but otherwise no test logic moved. This is the standard "wrapper helper to absorb signature churn" pattern and worth remembering for future expansion-layer slices that change the Result shape further. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `ExpandError` is internal to the sh crate; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 19 new tests pin every documented behavior.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -c` check-only mode slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-check; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `c9da7a8`): coreutils sort-tests crate +9 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 21 → 30); workspace +9 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1721 → 1730), summed to +33 across the parallel batch on main (1721 → 1754). Commit 8c2f033. Check-only mode added to the existing `sort` binary at `crates/coreutils/src/bin/sort.rs`. `sort -c` switches sort from "read input, sort, emit sorted output" to "read input, verify it's already sorted under the OTHER flags' rules, exit silently with status 0 if so, or write a single POSIX-conformant diagnostic to stderr and exit 1 if not". No stdout in check mode — the only output is the diagnostic on the failure path. Diagnostic format `sort: -:<line-number>: disorder: <line>\n` matches GNU / BSD / busybox conventions. **Critical architectural refactor — shared key extraction.** The check-mode comparison MUST use the same key the sort path would have used (numeric for `-cn`, fold for `-cf`, raw bytes for `-c` alone). The agent introduced a new `Key` enum (`Numeric(i64)` / `Bytes(Vec<u8>)`) with a `key_for(line, numeric, fold)` constructor that centralizes the comparison-key logic — the sort path kept its `sort_by_key` closure form to avoid churn, but the check path uses `key_for` for both halves of each comparison pair so the same fold/numeric/lex semantics apply across modes. This is a foundational refactor: any future sort flag that affects the comparison key (`-g` general-numeric, `-k` field-key) will plug into `key_for` and immediately work in both sort and check modes.
