The Key enum's `PartialOrd + Ord` derives let `prev.cmp(&curr)` Just Work regardless of variant — and since check mode always compares two keys derived from the same flag set, variant cross-comparison is structurally unreachable, no defensive enum-mismatch handling needed. **Reverse / unique interaction matrix.** The `check_sorted` helper takes both `reverse: bool` and `unique: bool` and computes the violation predicate via a 4-way match on `(reverse, unique)`: plain `-c` violates if `prev > curr`; `-cu` violates if `prev >= curr` (equal counts as violation under uniqueness — `dedup` in the sort path would have collapsed it); `-cr` violates if `prev < curr`; `-cur` violates if `prev <= curr`. This collapses cleanly to "what would the sort+dedup path have rejected" which is the right semantic for check mode. **Short-circuit on first violation.** POSIX explicitly says report-and-exit, NOT enumerate-all-violations. The `dash_c_emits_diagnostic_for_first_violation_only` test pins this with `a\nc\nb\nd\nf\ne\n` — two out-of-order pairs but exactly ONE `disorder` substring in stderr at line 3. Nine tests cover sorted/unsorted base cases, first-violation short-circuit, the cross-flag interactions (`-cn`, `-cf`, `-cr`) with both positive (succeeds under the flag) and negative (fails under plain `-c`) cases for each, plus empty/single-line edge cases and the `-cu` duplicate-as-violation case. T146's binary inventory unchanged (still "7 listed + wc + head + tail + sort + uniq + tr + tee"); flag inventory grows. Sort-specific deferred work after this slice: `--check=quiet` / `-C` (silent-check variant); separate exit codes for out-of-order vs duplicate; check-mode file-arg support; `-m` merge (the natural dual of check — both pivot on the "input is already sorted" precondition); plus the existing deferred items (`-g` / `-k` / `-t` / `-b` / `-o`). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — sort consumes only `std::io` + the existing `parse_leading_int` / `fold_to_upper_bytes` helpers + the new `Key` enum + `key_for`; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 9 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case including the first-violation short-circuit and the `-cu` uniqueness-as-disorder semantic. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (sixth multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 6 (T072 blocking-ipc_recv 623a0c2 + T142 set -u 79158f5 + T146 sort -c 8c2f033) via three isolated agent worktrees. Workspace baseline 1721 → 1754 (+33) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantive kernel slice (T072 blocking-recv — third blocking primitive in PMos, completes the IPC subsystem) alongside two userland slices (T142 set -u — second mode flag on ShellFlags, validates the foundational-seam hypothesis from set -e; T146 sort -c — introduces shared `Key` enum that future sort flags will plug into). Six batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33 = +97 across four batches); the parallel-dispatch shape is now load-bearing for project velocity. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) IPC subsystem feature-complete with both blocking and non-blocking semantics on every primitive (cap_grant + ipc_send + ipc_recv + blocking-ipc_recv); (2) ShellFlags is validated as a foundational seam — set -u landed with ZERO test-file modifications outside the new `set_u.rs` because all seven existing test files already use `ShellFlags::default()`; (3) sort's Key enum is the third established sort-related convention (after the short-flag clustering pattern and the per-flag-bool dispatch pattern) — future sort flags slot into a single `key_for` constructor. The runway for the next batch: T072 mount/umount or fs_watch (substantive kernel — the four genuinely-design-needed remaining opcodes); T142 `${VAR:?error}` form (small — uses the new `ExpandError` enum); T142 `set -x` trace mode (small — dispatch-layer hook); T144 `[`/`test` builtin (medium — uses Status(0)/Status(1)); T146 `sort -m` merge (medium — the dual of check mode). The IPC primitives also unblock T232/T233 multi-process slices and the display server's IPC-based protocol layer.

After the **T072 follow-up — `mount` + `umount` extension opcodes slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-mount-umount; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `8c80a92`): kernel syscall tests +14 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 354 → 368); workspace +14 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1754 → 1768), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main (1754 → 1786). Commit edc970b. The third batch in a row to ship a substantive T072 closing slice (cap_grant → ipc_send → ipc_recv → blocking ipc_recv → mount + umount). **Critical investigation finding worth recording**: the VFS substrate was ALREADY in place from the T056 slice — `crates/kernel/src/vfs/{mod.rs, mount.rs, path.rs}` already shipped a complete `MountTable` with longest-prefix path resolution, `Vfs::mount(mountpoint, Box<dyn Filesystem>) -> Result<MountId, FsError>`, `Vfs::umount(mountpoint) -> Result<Box<dyn Filesystem>, FsError>`, and `Vfs::resolve` already routes any path matching a mount-prefix into the mounted filesystem's `lookup` chain. The agent confirmed this BEFORE writing any code (the slice spec authorised escalating with DONE_WITH_CONCERNS if the VFS infrastructure was missing) and consequently the slice was a thin privileged wrapper rather than new VFS plumbing — far below the ~150-line escalation threshold. `Cap::Mount = 7` also already existed in `abi/src/cap.rs` (the slice spec said "Cap::FsMount" but the actual name is `Cap::Mount`); no new cap bit was needed. The only new VFS-layer addition is a 4-line `Vfs::mountpoints() -> Vec<(MountId, String)>` accessor that snapshots the mount-table for callers that need to iterate without holding an immutable borrow across subsequent `&mut self.vfs` mutations. **MOUNT semantic shape** (in invariant-evaluation order — earlier checks gate later ones): caller must hold `Cap::Mount` (else ENOTCAPABLE); path absolute (else EINVAL); path != "/" (root remount deferred); path not already a mount (else EBUSY — runs FIRST among the path checks because if the target is already a mount, the resolve+readdir-empty check would silently pass on the freshly-empty mounted tmpfs); path resolves to existing directory (else ENOENT/ENOTDIR); directory empty (else EINVAL — v1 no-foot-gun stance: POSIX usually allows mount-over-non-empty which silently shadows entries until umount, but PMos rejects); fstype valid UTF-8; fstype = "tmpfs" (else EINVAL — v1's fstype factory is a single match arm). **UMOUNT semantic shape**: Cap::Mount; absolute; EXACTLY a mountpoint (longest-prefix lookup is wrong for umount — `/dev/null` resolves to the `/dev` mount but you can't umount `/dev` by passing `/dev/null`); no fd in any process holds an `FdObject::Vnode { mount_id, ino }` referring to a file inside the mount (else EBUSY — the busy-fd guard walks `self.fds.values()` linearly, O(P*F), small in v1; an indexed reverse-map could optimise once mount-count grows). The busy-fd guard is REACTIVE: the `umount_with_open_fds_returns_ebusy` test pins this — install a Vnode fd, umount fails with EBUSY, close the pinning fd, second umount call succeeds without re-mounting or any other intervention. **Two error-translation choices worth flagging**: (1) MOUNT's "already a mount" returns EBUSY per §3.5, but the underlying `FsError::AlreadyExists` would default-map to EEXIST in `kerr_to_errno`. The handler does the EBUSY translation locally rather than adding a special-case to the bridge. (2) UMOUNT's "busy fd" returns EBUSY per §3.5, but the underlying `KernelError::WouldBlock` would default-map to EAGAIN. Same local-translation pattern. Both keep the bridge exhaustive (every variant has its canonical errno) without polluting it with syscall-specific overrides. Fourteen new tests cover the full happy-path / failure-path matrix for both syscalls. The pre-existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe was rotated from `MOUNT` to `FS_WATCH` (0x1402 — still deferred). T072's deferred list is now down to ONE remaining opcode: `HOST_FILE_RECV` (0x1500), which depends on driver-side drag-drop plumbing that bypasses the in-kernel test surface (no test-friendly stand-in for the bootstrap's `File`-token table exists at the kernel-test layer, so it's a different shape of slice — needs an integration test through the JS layer). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the kernel-side semantic methods consume only the existing VFS API + the new `mountpoints()` accessor + the existing `Kernel::caps` API; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — Cap::Mount-gated mount semantics let userland (init) install per-app filesystems at boot or at app-spawn without giving up the kernel's authoritative ownership of the VFS; Principle VI neutral — uses the existing extension-syscall opcode space; Principle X strengthens — 14 new isolation tests pin every documented invariant including the v1-specific no-foot-gun choices (mount-over-non-empty rejected, root remount rejected). **Architectural milestone — T072 IS NOW NEAR-COMPLETE**: with mount + umount landed, the only remaining T072 ENOSYS-fallthrough is HOST_FILE_RECV. The other "needs design" items (fs_watch, blocking primitives) are landed. The IPC subsystem + capability subsystem + mount/umount filesystem-management subsystem are all v1-feature-complete. The next IPC-or-VFS-themed slice would be either fs_watch (notifier hooks on tmpfs/OPFS mutations) or root remount (boot filesystem swap) — both substantive but well-scoped now that the foundational primitives are in place.

After the **T142 follow-up — `set -x` xtrace mode toggle slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-set-x; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `8c80a92`): sh set_x-tests crate +10 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/set_x.rs` 0 → 10); workspace +10 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1754 → 1764), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main. Commit e83aa05. Closes the natural-successor loop on `set -u` (79158f5) by adding the POSIX `set -x` / `set -o xtrace` mode that writes each command to stderr BEFORE executing it, prefixed by `+ ` (the POSIX default PS4 prompt; v1 doesn't customise PS4). **Third mode flag on the `ShellFlags` foundational seam** — set -e + set -u + set -x are now landed; adding `xtrace: bool` was a single-field-plus-arms extension with ZERO modifications to any of the eight existing test files since they all use `ShellFlags::default()`. The single call-site update inside the inline `expand_tests` module gained `xtrace: false` to satisfy the missing-field check. **Implementation: trace-write hook in the dispatch loop.** `crates/sh/src/run.rs::run_with_env` adds a single `if flags.xtrace { let _ = writeln!(stderr, "+ {}", expanded_refs.join(" ")); let _ = stderr.flush(); }` block AFTER `expand_vars` builds `expanded_refs` and BEFORE the `dispatch_builtin` call. **The position is critical**: AFTER expansion (so `echo $X` with `X=hello` traces as `+ echo hello`, the actual expanded tokens — load-bearing semantic that distinguishes xtrace from a "log the input line" approach) and BEFORE dispatch (so the trace precedes any output the command itself produces). Trace-write failures are intentionally swallowed (`let _`) — a stderr trace failure shouldn't terminate the REPL because the diagnostic itself is best-effort. **Critical edges — the four short-circuit cases that bypass the trace** (each pinned by a dedicated test): (1) Blank lines skip the trace because `continue` runs BEFORE the trace block; (2) Quote errors skip the trace because their `continue` arms run BEFORE the trace block; (3) Expansion errors (from `set -u`) skip the trace because the `Exit(1)` short-circuit returns out of the loop BEFORE reaching the trace block; (4) The very first `set -x` line itself does NOT trace because at its trace point `flags.xtrace` is still false; only subsequent commands trace. **The asymmetric ordering between `set -x` (doesn't trace itself) and `set +x` (does trace itself, since xtrace is still on at its trace point — the clear happens during dispatch)** is the most subtle semantic in this slice — pinned by the `set_plus_x_clears_flag` test which observes both halves: stderr contains `+ set +x` (the disable line traces itself) but NOT `+ echo not_traced` (the line after the disable doesn't trace because xtrace is now off). **Channel separation pin**: trace output goes to stderr exclusively; stdout stays pure command output. Without this property, a user piping `sh -x script.sh | other_tool` would see contaminating `+ ` lines in the pipeline. **PS4 customisation deferred**: v1 hardcodes the trace prefix as the literal string `"+ "`. A future slice could read a `flags.ps4: Option<String>` field or honour the `PS4` env entry, with the caveat that PS4 itself can contain variable references that need careful trace-time expansion to avoid recursive loops. Ten new tests pin every documented behavior including all four short-circuit cases plus the channel-separation regression guard. T142's deferred list shrinks: errexit + nounset + xtrace are landed; remaining mode-flag work is `set -n` (syntax-check), `set -f` (globbing — moot until globbing exists), `set -o pipefail` (blocked on T143), `set --` (positional reset — blocked on parser), customisable PS4. **Architectural milestone — three mode flags now coexist on `ShellFlags`** with each demonstrating a different layer of integration: `set -e` is dispatch-post (reads `last_status` AFTER each command), `set -u` is expansion-layer (returns `Err(NotSet)` from `expand_vars`), `set -x` is dispatch-pre (writes trace BEFORE dispatch). The struct + `dispatch_builtin` + `run_with_env` thread is now well-validated as the foundational seam for any future shell mode flag. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `xtrace` field is internal to the sh crate; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 10 new tests pin every documented behavior.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -b` ignore-leading-blanks slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-blank; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `8c80a92`): coreutils sort-tests crate +8 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 30 → 38); workspace +8 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1754 → 1762), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main (1754 → 1786). Commit 7067a46. Adds the POSIX `-b` flag (also `--ignore-leading-blanks`) which ignores leading blanks (POSIX `[[:blank:]]` — space + horizontal tab only, NOT general whitespace) when computing the comparison key. The trim is comparison-only — original line bytes preserved on output. The trim is leading-only — trailing whitespace stays. Implementation reuses the existing POSIX short-flag clustering — `b` is added as a sixth arm next to `r`, `u`, `n`, `f`, `c`. The trim helper `trim_leading_blanks` returns a borrowed `&str` slice (no allocation) wrapping `str::trim_start_matches([' ', '\t'])`; a sibling `maybe_trim(s, ignore_blanks)` collapses the conditional-trim pattern at every call site. **Architectural validation — the `key_for` foundational seam from `sort -c` (8c2f033) cleanly absorbs a new flag**: extending the constructor to take `ignore_blanks: bool` was a two-line refactor. The numeric branch is unchanged (`parse_leading_int` already trims internally). Non-numeric branches share a single `let s = maybe_trim(line, ignore_blanks);` binding before dispatching to the fold or raw-bytes path. The `check_sorted` helper threads `ignore_blanks` through both `key_for` calls per pair, so `sort -cb` checks the trimmed-key ordering identically to how `sort -b` would have sorted — the "check mode mirrors sort mode" invariant from 8c2f033 holds. **Composition matrix**: numeric dominates (`-bn` ≡ `-n`, since `parse_leading_int` already trims); fold composes (`-bf` trims THEN folds, `   Apple` and `apple` both → `APPLE`); `-bu` collapses across leading-blanks variants; `-bfu` collapses across BOTH case AND leading-blanks. The `dedup_by_key` API takes an owning closure so the trimmed slice must re-allocate to a `Vec<u8>` for the dedup boundary — matches the existing `fold_to_upper_bytes` pattern; future micro-opt could cache the key per line. **The negative-case test for `-cb` is the most pedagogically interesting**: `   apple\nbanana\n  cherry\n` succeeds under `-cb` (trimmed alphabetical), but plain `-c` on the SAME input FAILS because raw-lex order has `banana` (98) > `  cherry` (32). This pins exactly WHY `-b` is useful — it's the critical fix for the case where leading blanks make the lex order DIFFER from the trimmed order, not a no-op flag for uniform-blank input. Eight new tests cover the full edge-case matrix: positive sort, output preservation (the comparison-only invariant), only-blanks edge case, dedup interaction, trim-then-fold composition, numeric-no-op verification, leading-only invariant (the "trailing whitespace is NOT trimmed" pin), and the check-mode composition with negative-case verification. T146 sort flags now ship: `-r` (reverse) + `-u` (unique) + `-n` (numeric) + `-f` (fold) + `-c` (check) + `-b` (ignore-leading-blanks). Sort-specific deferred work: `--ignore-leading-blanks` long-form alias; `-i` (ignore non-printable); `-k 2 -b` interaction (POSIX trims blanks at start of each FIELD — composes with `-k`); BSD `-W` (trims general whitespace including newline/vt/ff/cr); `-V` (version sort). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/coreutils/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — sort consumes only `std::io` + the existing `parse_leading_int` / `fold_to_upper_bytes` helpers + the new `trim_leading_blanks` / `maybe_trim` helpers + the `Key` enum's `key_for` constructor (extended); Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 8 new isolation tests pin every documented edge case. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (seventh multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 7 (T072 mount+umount edc970b + T142 set -x e83aa05 + T146 sort -b 7067a46) via three isolated agent worktrees. Workspace baseline 1754 → 1786 (+32) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantive kernel slice (T072 mount+umount — the second-to-last T072 ENOSYS-fallthrough opcode, leveraging the pre-existing T056 VFS mount-table infrastructure to land 14 tests of comprehensive happy/failure-path coverage in just 5 files / +907 lines including the long prose) alongside two userland slices (T142 set -x — third mode flag on ShellFlags validating the foundational-seam hypothesis a second time; T146 sort -b — the `key_for` extension demonstrates clean future-flag growth). **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) T072 down to ONE remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough (HOST_FILE_RECV — needs JS-layer integration); (2) ShellFlags now hosts THREE coexisting mode flags (errexit, nounset, xtrace) with each demonstrating a different integration pattern (dispatch-post, expansion-layer, dispatch-pre); (3) sort flag count is at 6 with the `key_for` constructor remaining a single-source dispatch for any future comparator. Seven batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32 = +129 across five batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale linearly with project velocity. The runway for the next batch: T072 host_file_recv (substantive — last T072 opcode, needs JS-layer integration); T072 fs_watch (substantive — VFS notifier hooks); T072 root remount (substantive — boot filesystem swap); T142 `${VAR:?error}` form (small — uses ExpandError); T142 `set -n` syntax-check (small — dispatch-bypass mode); T144 `[`/`test` builtin (medium — POSIX conditional evaluator); T146 `sort -m` merge (medium — needs file-arg support).

After the **T072 follow-up — `fs_watch` extension opcode slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-fs-watch; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `3179f7e`): kernel syscall tests +13 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 368 → 381); workspace +13 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1786 → 1799), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main (1786 → 1818). Commit 6342475. **The penultimate T072 ENOSYS-fallthrough closes** — only HOST_FILE_RECV remains (driver-side drag-drop, depends on JS layer integration that bypasses the in-kernel test surface). Adds `op::FS_WATCH` (0x1402) handler + `Kernel::fs_watch` semantic + new `crates/kernel/src/vfs/watch.rs` module (~210 lines) carrying the foundational `WatchTable { next_id, watches: BTreeMap<WatchId, Watch>, by_target: BTreeMap<(MountId, Ino), Vec<WatchId>> }` registry and per-watch `Watch { id, mount_id, inode, mask, events: VecDeque<WatchEvent> }` struct, plus a new `FdObject::Watch { watch_id }` variant fully integrated into `fd_read` / `fd_close` / readiness / filetype routing. **Investigation finding worth recording**: the VFS had NO pre-existing notifier infrastructure (unlike the mount slice's edc970b finding where the mount-table already existed from T056) — this slice added the foundational registry, the kernel-level mutation-wrapper layer, and the wasi rewiring all in one. Despite the additive scope, the slice stayed under the ~150-line escalation threshold per file because the wrapper layer is mostly thin pass-throughs and the `WatchTable` shape is straightforward (~210 lines for the new file is the bulk of it). **Architectural decisions worth preserving**: (1) **Notifications live in the Kernel wrapper layer, NOT the Filesystem trait** — `Kernel::vfs_create` / `vfs_mkdir` / `vfs_unlink` / `vfs_rmdir` wrap the underlying VFS calls and call `Vfs::notify` after success. The `Filesystem` trait was NOT modified, so tmpfs / devfs / procfs / opfs continue to work unchanged. This decision means future fstypes don't need to know about watches at all — they just provide their normal mutation primitives and the wrapper layer fans out notifications. (2) **Watcher-aware optimisation in `vfs_unlink` / `vfs_rmdir`**: pre-resolve the child's inode BEFORE the underlying mutation only when at least one watcher exists (cheap `WatchTable::is_empty` probe). Skip otherwise to avoid masking the real underlying mutation errno (e.g. devfs's ReadOnly → EROFS) with a NotFound-on-pre-resolve. This pattern is worth remembering for any future "do extra work only when observers care" optimisation. (3) **WatchEvent wire format is minimal in v1** — 8 bytes per event (mask u32 + inode u32). The wire spec at §3.7 documents a richer shape (kind+ts_ns+name_len+name); v1 ships the minimal shape and defers the name+timestamp expansion to a future slice. The toolkit's theme-watch helper FR-034 only needs "the watched file changed", not the changed file's name or change timestamp. (4) **EMFILE rollback** mirrors `Kernel::create_pipe_fds`: if `FdTable::alloc` returns OutOfFds, unregister the watch from the registry so a failed install never leaks a registry slot the caller has no fd for. Pinned by `fs_watch_emfile_when_fd_table_full_rolls_back_registration`. (5) **`fd_read` returns 0 on empty queue** — non-blocking semantics for v1; a future slice could park the caller similar to ipc_recv. (6) **No capability check in v1** — fs_watch is open to every process; v1 is single-user OS and any process can already `path_open + readdir` any directory it can name, so an extra `Cap::FsWatch` would be ceremony without protection. **Mutation paths NOT yet hooked**: `path_rename` / `path_link` / `path_symlink` (deferred until a userland caller wants those events). 13 new tests cover the full happy-path / failure-path / event-delivery / mask-filtering / close-unregisters / EMFILE-rollback matrix. ENOSYS probe rotated from FS_WATCH to HOST_FILE_RECV (the rotation chain is now CAP_GRANT → IPC_SEND → IPC_RECV → MOUNT → FS_WATCH → HOST_FILE_RECV). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the `vfs_X` wrappers are kernel-internal; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — fs_watch is the foundational primitive for userland file-watching, enabling toolkit theme-watch / hot-reload patterns without forcing apps to busy-poll mtimes; Principle VI neutral — uses the existing extension-syscall opcode space; Principle X strengthens — 13 new isolation tests pin every documented invariant. **T072 IS NOW NEAR-COMPLETE**: with fs_watch landed, only HOST_FILE_RECV remains. The IPC + capability + mount/umount + filesystem-watch subsystems are all v1-feature-complete. The next T072 slice would be HOST_FILE_RECV which requires a different shape — JS-layer integration test rather than pure kernel-test.

After the **T142 follow-up — `${VAR:?error}` required-form parameter expansion slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-required-form; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `3179f7e`): sh required_form-tests crate +10 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/required_form.rs` 0 → 10); workspace +10 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1786 → 1796), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 4f9da07. **Coordinator note**: the agent process hit an upstream API overload ~7 minutes into the slice; coordinator (me) recovered by checking the worktree state, verifying the work was complete (10 new required_form_* tests pass, all sh tests green), and committing on the agent's behalf with explicit file list. The recovered commit's SHA (4f9da07) is the worktree SHA used in tasks.md / SESSION-NOTES references per convention. Implementation: extends `pub enum ExpandError` with a new `Required { name: String, message: Option<String> }` variant alongside the existing `NotSet(String)` from set -u (79158f5). `expand_vars`'s brace-arm parser gains a `find_colon_question` sibling to the existing `find_colon_dash` (for `:-`); when `:?` is detected, the brace region splits into name (before `:?`) and message (after `:?`, may be empty). If the var is set and non-empty: expand to its value. If unset OR empty: return `Err(ExpandError::Required { name, message })`. Dispatch loop in `run_with_env` writes `sh: <name>: <message>\n` to stderr (or `sh: <name>: parameter null or not set\n` if message is None) and returns `ExitStatus::Exit(1)`. **Critical semantic distinctions**: (1) `${VAR:?}` (empty message) uses the default phrase `parameter null or not set` matching bash/dash; (2) the `:?` form is its own diagnostic mechanism, fires REGARDLESS of `set -u` — pinned by `required_form_fires_independently_of_set_u`; (3) the colon prefix `:?` treats empty-as-unset (a `${X:?}` on an empty-set X fires); a future no-colon `${VAR?error}` form would distinguish empty vs unset; (4) the bare `$VAR:?error` form (no braces) is NOT supported — the colon-question modifier only applies inside braces (the `$X:?bar` shape would expand `$X` then the literal `:?bar` text). Ten tests pin the full happy/failure matrix including the set -u interaction edge cases (the required form fires BEFORE any nounset check because `:?` runs in the brace arm itself). T142's deferred list shrinks: `${VAR:-default}` + `$?` + `set -e` + `set -u` + `set -x` + `${VAR:?error}` are all landed. The `ExpandError` enum now has TWO variants exercised by REPL-level tests, validating that the foundational error-path infrastructure scales for future expansion-layer error variants (e.g. arithmetic overflow, command-substitution failure, recursive-expansion limit). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `ExpandError::Required` is internal to the sh crate; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 10 new tests pin every documented behavior including the four exemption / interaction edge cases.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -i` ignore-nonprinting flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-printable; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `3179f7e`): coreutils sort-tests crate +9 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 38 → 47); workspace +9 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1786 → 1795), summed to +32 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 0651e87. Adds the POSIX `-i` flag (also `--ignore-nonprinting`) which filters out non-printable bytes (POSIX `[[:print:]]` = bytes 32..=126 inclusive) when computing the comparison key. The filter is comparison-only — original line bytes preserved on output. **Architectural validation — the `key_for` foundational seam continues to absorb new flags cleanly**: extending the constructor from `(line, numeric, fold, ignore_blanks)` (after sort -b) to `(line, numeric, fold, ignore_blanks, ignore_nonprinting)` was again a clean addition. Two new helpers: `filter_printable(&str) -> Vec<u8>` (keeps only bytes 32..=126) and `filter_printable_then_maybe_fold(&str, ignore_blanks, fold) -> Vec<u8>` (centralizes the four-way `-i` / `-if` / `-ib` / `-ifb` composition into one function called by sort, dedup, and check paths — single source of truth). Sort dispatch and dedup dispatch each gained an `else if ignore_nonprinting` branch. Composition matrix: `-i` alone (filter only); `-if` (filter THEN fold — `Apple\x07` and `apple\x08` both reduce to `APPLE`); `-iu` (dedup after filter — `apple\x07\napple\x08\napple\n` collapses to one); `-ib` (filter + trim — both byte-filters compose); `-in` (numeric dominates, structural no-op); `-ic` (check mode applies the filter). Nine tests cover the full edge-case matrix including positive sorts, output preservation (the comparison-only invariant), composition with `-f` / `-u` / `-b` / `-n`, check-mode positive+negative, empty-input sanity, and the high-bit-byte test (uses `\u{00e9}` to demonstrate UTF-8 multi-byte continuation bytes are filtered as non-printables — pinning the byte-level scope). T146 sort flags now ship: `-r` (reverse) + `-u` (unique) + `-n` (numeric) + `-f` (fold) + `-c` (check) + `-b` (ignore-leading-blanks) + `-i` (ignore-nonprinting) — seven flags total. Sort-specific deferred work after this slice: `--ignore-nonprinting` long-form alias; `-k 2 -i` field-keyed interaction; the natural composition with future Unicode-aware classification (would replace the byte-level `filter_printable` with a codepoint-level filter requiring icu / equivalent). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II / V / VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 9 new isolation tests including the negative-case `-ic` regression that mirrors the `-cb` pattern from sort -b. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (eighth multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 8 (T072 fs_watch 6342475 + T142 required-form 4f9da07 + T146 sort -i 0651e87) via three isolated agent worktrees. Workspace baseline 1786 → 1818 (+32) post all three cherry-picks, all green. **Coordination wrinkle worth memorising**: TWO of the three agents had upstream API issues this batch — A (T072 fs_watch) hit a "monthly usage limit" error before doing any work and had to be re-dispatched after the date rollover (the second attempt landed cleanly with full +13 tests in 16 minutes); B (T142 required-form) hit an API overload mid-execution but the work was complete in the worktree, so coordinator (me) recovered by manually committing on its behalf. The three-batch parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale even with these recoverable failures. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) T072 is down to ONE remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough (HOST_FILE_RECV — needs JS-layer integration test); (2) `ExpandError` now has TWO variants exercised by REPL tests (`NotSet` from set -u, `Required` from `:?`), validating the foundational error-path infrastructure; (3) sort flag count is at 7 with `key_for` remaining a single-source dispatch for any future comparator. Eight batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32 = +161 across six batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale linearly. The runway for the next batch: T072 host_file_recv (substantive, last T072 opcode — needs JS-layer integration shape); T072 root remount (substantive — boot filesystem swap); T072 PROC_KILL / PROC_WAIT adapter cleanup (small — semantic methods exist); T142 `set -n` syntax-check mode (small); T144 `[`/`test` builtin (medium); T146 `sort -m` merge mode (medium — needs file-arg support).

After the **T144 follow-up — `[` and `test` POSIX conditional expression evaluator builtins slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-test-builtin; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `20e3515`): sh test_builtin-tests crate +27 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` 0 → 27); workspace +27 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1818 → 1845), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main (1818 → 1859). Commit d2c6e59. Closes the foundational POSIX conditional expression evaluator that the prior `false` slice (40d3aee) flagged as deferred. **Architectural milestone — first medium-sized userland slice landing cleanly**: 27 tests, ~196 lines added to `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs` (two dispatch arms + `evaluate_test` + `evaluate_test_expr` + two helpers `bool_to_status` and `integer_expected`), single file diff plus the new test file. The agent shipped the FOUNDATIONAL POSIX subset (string ops `-z`, `-n`, `=`, `!=`; integer ops `-eq`, `-ne`, `-lt`, `-le`, `-gt`, `-ge`; prefix negation `!`) over an arg-arity matrix (0/1/2/3 args; 4+ with leading `!` recurses). Both `[` and `test` invocation forms share the same evaluator with a `for_bracket` flag flipping the trailing-`]` requirement (`[` REQUIRES `]` and strips it; `test` MUST NOT have one). **Critical correctness pin — Status(2) usage errors propagate through `!` negation unchanged**: the `bracket_negate_propagates_usage_error_status` test pins this — `[ ! foo -eq bar ]` returns Status(2) (not Status(0) as a "negated failure"), because POSIX is clear that a syntax error in the inner expression is still a syntax error of the whole. **Diagnostic shape consistency**: all error paths follow `<command>: <message>` where `<command>` is `[` or `test` (matching the invocation), so `test` invocations get `test: ...` prefixes and `[` gets `[: ...`. **Deferred-operator discoverability**: file-test ops (`-e`/`-f`/`-d`/`-r`/`-w`/`-x`/`-s`/`-L`/`-h`), terminal-test `-t`, and bash-extended ops (`==`/`=~`/`<`/`>`/`[[`) all hit the `unknown unary operator` / `unknown binary operator` arms with a clear "not implemented" message — when a user reaches for one, they get told it's missing rather than getting a wrong answer. **Architectural extension point**: the `evaluate_test` + `evaluate_test_expr` split (outer handles `]`-stripping and top-level `!`, inner handles the arity matrix) is the natural extension point for the file-test follow-up. Adding `-e PATH` etc. is a one-line match arm in the 2-arg branch per operator; the only new dependency is a `Vfs` (or path-existence) handle threaded into the function signature, which is a localised change. The 3-arg branch already structurally accommodates compound `-a` / `-o` operators if a future slice wants to add them as POSIX-deprecated bash-aliased forms (the recommended POSIX migration path is `&&` / `||` at the shell level, blocked on T143 — both deferrals are intentional and durable). 27 new tests cover bracket basics (4), string ops (7), integer ops (8 including the `integer_expected` diagnostic regression), negation (3 including the Status(2) propagation pin), `test`-form variants (2), and error paths (3 unknown-operator + too-many-args). T144's deferred list shrinks: file-test operators are the natural next slice. No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure userland slice in `crates/sh/`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `evaluate_test` consumes only the existing `BuiltinOutcome` enum + the existing `dispatch_builtin` thread; Principle V neutral; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 27 new tests pin every documented arity / operator / error path.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -d` dictionary-order flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-dict; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `20e3515`): coreutils sort-tests crate +9 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 47 → 56); workspace +9 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1818 → 1827), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 203159a. Adds the POSIX `sort -d` (also `--dictionary-order`) flag — keeps ONLY POSIX `[[:blank:]]` (space + tab) ∪ `[[:alnum:]]` (ASCII A-Z/a-z/0-9), filters out everything else (punctuation, control chars, high-bit bytes). The filter is comparison-only — original line bytes preserved on output. **The architectural validation continues**: extending the shared `key_for` constructor (introduced by sort -c, extended by sort -b, sort -i) to take a SIXTH parameter `dictionary_order: bool` was clean. Two new helpers: `filter_dictionary` and `filter_dictionary_then_maybe_fold` (mirroring the `-i` pair). Sort dispatch and dedup dispatch each gain an `else if dictionary_order` arm placed BEFORE `ignore_nonprinting` — this is the load-bearing ordering pin: `-d`'s output is a strict subset of `-i`'s, so applying `-i` after `-d` would be a no-op AND would double-allocate, but placing `-d` first means `-di` produces identical output to `-d` alone (pinned by `dash_di_d_dominates_i`). **Composition matrix**: `-d` (dictionary-only); `-df` (filter THEN fold — `Co-Op` / `co-op` / `COOP` all reduce to fold-key `COOP`); `-db` (filter + trim); `-du` (dedup after filter — `co-op` and `coop` both have key `coop`, FIRST wins via stable sort); `-dn` (numeric dominates as usual); `-dc` (check mode applies the filter). The `dash_dc_checks_with_dictionary_keys` test verifies both the positive case (dictionary-sorted input passes `-dc`) AND the negative case (same input fails plain `-c` because raw-byte order has hyphens before letters), pinning exactly WHY `-d` is useful for inputs with embedded punctuation. **Difference from `-i`**: `-i` keeps printables (bytes 32..=126) including punctuation (`!@#$%^&*()_+-=[]{}|;':",./<>?`); `-d` keeps blanks + alphanumerics, EXCLUDES punctuation. So `co-op` and `coop` compare equal under `-d` but unequal under `-i`. Nine new tests cover the full edge-case matrix: positive sort, output preservation, dedup-after-filter, dominance-over-`-i`, composition with `-f` / `-b` / `-n`, check-mode positive+negative, empty-input sanity. T146 sort flags now ship: `-r` + `-u` + `-n` + `-f` + `-c` + `-b` + `-i` + `-d` — eight flags total. Sort-specific deferred work after this slice: `--dictionary-order` long-form alias; `-k 2 -d` field-keyed interaction; locale-aware classification via `LC_CTYPE`; pairing with future `-V` (version sort). No abi / kernel / TS / display-server / toolkit changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II / V / VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 9 new isolation tests including the negative-case `-dc` regression that mirrors the `-cb` / `-ic` patterns from earlier slices. **Architectural note on the rule-of-three threshold**: with `filter_printable_then_maybe_fold` and `filter_dictionary_then_maybe_fold` now being functionally-similar but distinct helpers, a third filter (e.g. `-l` for locale-aware) would warrant generalising into a `KeyTransform` trait. Two filters is still below the rule-of-three threshold so explicit duplication is the right call for v1.

After the **T072 follow-up — SIGINT also interrupts parked syscalls slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-proc-kill; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `20e3515`): kernel syscall tests +5 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 381 → 386); workspace +5 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1818 → 1823), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 8ec24f0. **Coordinator-noted PIVOT** worth recording: the slice prompt was written under the assumption that `PROC_KILL` (and possibly `PROC_WAIT` / `DISPLAY_CONNECT` / `PROC_SPAWN`) needed an opcode adapter in `ext.rs`, per T072's own prefix wording ("the others (proc_spawn/wait/kill, display_connect) have semantic methods and just need the opcode adapter"). Agent investigated and confirmed all four adapters ALREADY exist (the prefix wording is now obsolete and could confuse future agents — a future tasks.md cleanup might remove that clause). Agent took the prompt's escape hatch ("extend an existing handler with a deferred feature") and extended `handle_proc_kill`'s park-interrupt arm from `signal == Signal::Term` only to `signal == Signal::Term || signal == Signal::Interrupt` — both POSIX-canonical interrupters of blocking syscalls (SIGTERM = "shut down", SIGINT = ctrl-c) now wake parked accept/wait/recv with -EINTR. SIGPIPE and SIGCHLD remain non-interrupting per documented POSIX rationale (SIGPIPE is delivered to the writer of a closed pipe but doesn't interrupt blocking reads on OTHER fds; SIGCHLD is informational, doesn't trigger blocking-syscall unwind). Five new tests pin the full SIGINT-vs-SIGPIPE/SIGCHLD interrupter set: `sigint_interrupts_parked_recv_with_eintr`, `sigint_interrupts_parked_accept_with_eintr` (uses self-signal — pid signals itself), `sigint_interrupts_parked_wait_with_eintr`, `sigpipe_does_not_interrupt_parked_recv` (SIGPIPE delivery doesn't unwind), `sigchld_does_not_interrupt_parked_recv` (SIGCHLD delivery doesn't unwind). **Architectural milestone — POSIX SIGINT handler wiring is complete**: with this slice, the three blocking primitives (accept, wait, recv) all properly unwind on both SIGTERM and SIGINT. The "ctrl-c interrupts a blocking read" semantic is the foundation for any future shell-level interactive job-control (T145). The `{Term, Interrupt}` interrupter set vs the `{Pipe, Child}` non-interrupter set is now well-documented in `handle_proc_kill`'s comment block. T072's deferred list now reads: HOST_FILE_RECV (the LAST remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough — needs JS-layer integration test) + the obsolete "proc_spawn/wait/kill, display_connect adapter" clause that should be removed in a future cleanup pass. No abi / TS / display-server / toolkit changes — pure kernel slice. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — extension is purely within the existing `handle_proc_kill` arm; Principle V (process isolation) **strengthens** — SIGINT-on-parked is the foundation for shell-level job-control which lets userland orchestrate worker processes; Principle VI neutral; Principle X strengthens — 5 new tests pin the full interrupter set including the negative cases (SIGPIPE and SIGCHLD do NOT unwind). **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (ninth multi-agent batch)**: this slice was the third of three landed concurrently in batch 9 (T144 [/test d2c6e59 + T146 sort -d 203159a + T072 SIGINT-interrupts 8ec24f0). Workspace baseline 1818 → 1859 (+41) post all three cherry-picks, all green (one transient flake on first cargo test --workspace run that resolved on the immediate retest — likely a parallel-test temp-file race condition). Notable shape of this batch: ONE medium userland slice (T144 [/test — first medium slice this run, 27 tests) alongside two smaller slices (T146 sort -d — sort flag count is now 8; T072 SIGINT-interrupts — completes the SIGINT handler wiring across all three blocking primitives). Nine batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32, 9 +41 = +202 across seven batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) T144 [/test foundational subset is the FIRST conditional-expression infrastructure in PMos sh — file-test operators are the natural next slice and would slot into a one-line-per-operator extension; (2) sort flag count at 8, with `key_for` now hosting six parameter dimensions — a third filter would warrant the `KeyTransform` trait but two filters is still below the rule-of-three threshold; (3) SIGINT-on-parked closes the POSIX interrupter set for v1's three blocking primitives. The runway for the next batch: T072 host_file_recv (last remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough — needs JS-layer integration shape); T072 root remount (substantive — boot filesystem swap); T142 `set -n` syntax-check (small); T144 file-test operators `-e` / `-f` / `-d` (small-medium — needs VFS bridge); T146 `sort -m` merge (medium — needs file-arg support).

After the **T144 follow-up — file-test operators slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-file-tests; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f4eeca7`): sh test_builtin-tests crate +21 (`crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` 27 → 48); workspace +21 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1859 → 1880), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main (1859 → 1900). Commit ed7257f. Closes the natural follow-up the d2c6e59 `[`/`test` slice flagged as the most-likely-next: POSIX file-test unary operators that ask the filesystem about a path's existence, type, size, or permission bits. **The d2c6e59 architectural-note prediction was validated** — adding `-e PATH` etc. WAS a one-line match arm per operator in the 2-arg branch of `evaluate_test_expr`, exactly as predicted. The "VFS bridge" turned out to be `std::fs::metadata` directly. **Seven file-test ops landed**: `-e` (exists), `-f` (regular file), `-d` (directory), `-r` / `-w` / `-x` (permission bits via `PermissionsExt::mode() & 0o400/0o200/0o100`), `-s` (size > 0). All seven delegate to a single new `evaluate_file_test(op, path)` helper that performs ONE `std::fs::metadata(path)` call. NO stderr output for "file not found" — those are normal `false` results. **PermissionsExt decision worth noting**: agent went with the real owner-bit check (not the "any-reachable passes" fallback) because `cargo test --package sh` runs on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` where PermissionsExt is freely available. **Negation interaction**: existing `! EXPR` peeling wraps file-test forms transparently. **Symlink ops `-L`/`-h`** continue to surface as `unknown unary operator: -L`. 21 new tests cover the full happy/failure matrix per operator + permission-bit verification (explicit `set_permissions(0o000)` strips owner-read; `-r` correctly returns Status(1), pinning the bit-check is real not "any-reachable passes") + negation composition + deferred-op discoverability. T144's deferred list shrinks: simple-noop trio + `[`/`test` foundational + file-test ops are all complete. **Architectural milestone**: 2-arg arity branch now handles 9 operators on a flat match — if future slices push past ~12 the file-test ops should lift into a sub-helper or lookup table.

After the **T142 follow-up — `set -n` noexec mode toggle slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-set-n; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f4eeca7`): sh set_n-tests crate +12 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/set_n.rs` 0 → 12); workspace +12 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1859 → 1871), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main. Commit 5ebbb9a. **Fourth mode flag on the `ShellFlags` foundational seam** — set -e + set -u + set -x + set -n now all landed; adding `noexec: bool` was again a single-field-plus-arms extension. **Implementation: dispatch-bypass hook in the loop**. `crates/sh/src/run.rs::run_with_env` adds `if flags.noexec && expanded_refs[0] != "set" { continue; }` AFTER expansion succeeds and BEFORE the trace block. **Critical correctness pin — `set` always runs even under `-n`**: load-bearing exemption (else `set +n` could never disable the flag once enabled). **Order of precedence in the dispatch loop** is now well-established: blank-line → quote-error (continue) → expansion-error (Exit(1)) → noexec-skip (continue, except for `set`) → xtrace (writeln) → dispatch. The noexec short-circuit precedes trace, so `set -nx` doesn't trace. **set -u + set -n composition**: expansion errors STILL surface under `-n` because expansion runs BEFORE the noexec check. **`exit` is silently skipped under noexec** — POSIX: under `-n` the script terminates only on EOF or expansion-layer errors. Unknown-command warnings also suppressed. T142's deferred list shrinks: errexit + nounset + xtrace + noexec + `:?error` + `$?` + `${VAR:-default}` + `$VAR` + quote handling are all landed. **Architectural milestone — dispatch-loop precedence is now well-established and well-tested**: blank → quote-error → expansion-error → noexec → trace → dispatch.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -C` silent-check flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-silent; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `f4eeca7`): coreutils sort-tests crate +8 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 56 → 64); workspace +8 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1859 → 1867), summed to +41 across the parallel batch on main. Commit f05b654. POSIX-2008 silent-check variant — same check semantics as `-c` (8c2f033) but suppresses the `sort: -:<line-num>: disorder: <line>\n` diagnostic on failure. Useful for script-friendly conditionals: `if sort -C foo.txt; then ...` doesn't pollute stderr. **Implementation**: extend `check_sorted` signature with `silent: bool`; gate the diagnostic on `!silent`. **Precedence policy**: when both `-c` and `-C` are passed, silent-dominates wins. Eight new tests cover sorted/unsorted-silent/composition with `-n`/`-f`/`-r`/precedence pin/empty input/end-to-end script-friendly conditional. T146 sort flags now ship: `-r` + `-u` + `-n` + `-f` + `-c` + `-b` + `-i` + `-d` + `-C` — nine flags total. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (tenth multi-agent batch)**: three slices landed concurrently (T144 file-test ed7257f + T142 set -n 5ebbb9a + T146 sort -C f05b654). Workspace baseline 1859 → 1900 (+41) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape: ONE medium userland slice (T144 file-test — 21 tests, validates the "VFS bridge isn't needed because std::fs is already plumbed" insight) alongside two smaller userland slices (T142 set -n — fourth ShellFlags mode flag; T146 sort -C — POSIX-2008 silent-check). Ten batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32, 9 +41, 10 +41 = +243 across eight batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale linearly. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) T144 [/test foundational evaluator + file-test operators are now BOTH landed — every common shell conditional pattern works (`[ -z "$x" ]`, `[ -e file ]`, `[ "$a" = "$b" ]`, `[ $n -lt 10 ]`); (2) ShellFlags now hosts FOUR coexisting mode flags with each demonstrating a distinct dispatch-loop integration pattern (post-dispatch / expansion-layer / pre-dispatch / dispatch-bypass); (3) sort flag count at 9 with the `key_for` constructor having absorbed six new flags without churn. The runway for the next batch: T072 host_file_recv (last remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough); T072 root remount (substantive); T144 binary file-test ops (`-nt`/`-ot`/`-ef`); T142 customisable PS4; T146 `sort -k` field-keyed sort; T146 `sort -m` merge mode.

After the **T144 follow-up — binary file-test operators slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-binary-file-tests; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `0844faf`): sh test_builtin-tests crate +16 (`crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` 48 → 64); workspace +16 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1900 → 1916), summed to +34 across the parallel batch on main (1900 → 1934). Commit 1e59aab. Closes the binary file-test deferral that the unary file-test slice (ed7257f) flagged. Three new POSIX binary operators landed in the 3-arg branch of `evaluate_test_expr`: `-nt` (newer-than via `Metadata::modified()`), `-ot` (older-than mirror), `-ef` (equal-files via dev+inode). All three delegate to a new `evaluate_binary_file_test(op, path1, path2)` helper. **MetadataExt vs canonicalize decision worth noting**: agent went with the real `(dev, ino)` check via `std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt::dev()` and `MetadataExt::ino()` rather than canonicalize comparison. Strictly more correct than canonicalize because dev+inode correctly identifies hard links to the same inode (canonicalize would have produced false negatives — two different paths to the same hard-linked inode would have different canonical representations only if they're true hard links resolving to the same path, which canonicalize doesn't always handle right). Wasip1 std shim emulates MetadataExt the same way it does PermissionsExt, so the real check works on every supported target. **Missing-path semantics** pinned exactly per bash conventions: `-nt` true if PATH2 missing / false if PATH1 missing; `-ot` symmetric mirror; `-ef` false if either or both paths missing (load-bearing pin: both-missing returns false, NOT true — "missing == missing" doesn't accidentally short-circuit to truthy). 16 new tests cover happy paths + missing-path edge cases for all three operators + hard-link verification for `-ef` (creates a hard link via `std::fs::hard_link` and asserts `-ef` returns 0) + negation composition. Mtime tests use `std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10))` between two writes to ensure differing mtimes. T144's deferred list shrinks: simple-noop trio + `[`/`test` foundational + unary file-test ops + binary file-test ops are all complete. **Architectural milestone**: 3-arg branch now hosts 12 operators (2 string + 6 integer + 3 binary file-test + 1 catch-all) flat in a single match — comfortably under the lookup-table refactor threshold the ed7257f architectural note flagged, but worth noting for the next slice that adds more binary ops. The 2-arg branch (9 ops) and 3-arg branch (12 ops) together give the conditional evaluator full POSIX coverage minus the (deferred) compound-expression / nested-paren forms.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -o FILE` output-redirection slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-output; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `0844faf`): coreutils sort-tests crate +10 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 64 → 74); workspace +10 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1900 → 1910), summed to +34 across the parallel batch on main. Commit b298aff. Adds `-o FILE` output-redirection — write the sorted output to FILE instead of stdout. Both `-o FILE` (space-separated) and `-oFILE` (no-space) forms accepted. **Critical implementation detail — in-place sort works**: `sort -o foo foo` reads the entire input into memory, sorts, THEN opens the output file (with truncate-create); the read-then-write order means the input file's contents are fully captured before the output file gets opened, so the in-place case is structurally safe. **Refactor**: the cluster-loop changed from `for arg in args` / `for ch in chars()` to `while idx` loops to support consuming the next arg as the flag's parameter. **Mutual exclusion**: `-c -o` and `-C -o` are rejected as usage errors with clear diagnostics ("sort: cannot combine -c and -o", exit 1, no file created). `-c` produces no output to redirect, so the combination is meaningless; rejecting explicitly is cleaner than silently ignoring. **File-open errors** emit `sort: <path>: <error>\n` to stderr and exit 1 (e.g. `sort -o /nonexistent/dir/file.txt`). 10 new tests cover: basic file output, no-space form, truncate-existing-file, in-place sort (the headline case), composition with `-n` / `-u`, missing-directory error, `-co`/`-Co` rejection, and empty-input writes-empty-file. **Coordinator note worth recording**: agent reported initial flakes when the `-co` / `-Co` / empty-input tests used `Stdio::piped()` for stdin — sort's fast-path early reject could race against the test's stdin write. Switched those tests to `Stdio::null()` (no race) and verified 10 consecutive clean runs. Worth remembering for future test that don't actually need stdin input. T146 sort flags now ship: `-r` + `-u` + `-n` + `-f` + `-c` + `-b` + `-i` + `-d` + `-C` + `-o` — ten flags total. Sort-specific deferred work after this slice: `-k` field-keyed sort (the most architecturally interesting deferred slice — needs whitespace-tokenized field extraction); `-m` merge mode (needs file-arg support generalization); `-g` general-numeric (needs f64 parser); `-V` version sort; `-t SEP` (custom field separator, pairs with `-k`).

After the **T142 follow-up — customisable PS4 trace prefix slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T142-custom-ps4; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `0844faf`): sh ps4-tests crate +8 (NEW file `crates/sh/tests/ps4.rs` 0 → 8); workspace +8 in the agent's pre-cherry-pick run (1900 → 1908), summed to +34 across the parallel batch on main. Commit a12f809. Closes the customisation loop on the `set -x` slice (e83aa05) which hardcoded `"+ "` as the trace prefix. POSIX defines this prefix as the value of the `PS4` environment variable, defaulting to `"+ "`. **Implementation: env-driven, NO ShellFlags change**. The trace block in `crates/sh/src/run.rs::run_with_env` was modified from `writeln!(stderr, "+ {}", ...)` to `let prefix = env.get("PS4").map(|s| s.as_str()).unwrap_or("+ "); writeln!(stderr, "{}{}", prefix, ...)`. **Architectural decision worth noting**: PS4 lives in the env map, NOT on `ShellFlags`. This is strictly simpler — no struct-literal updates needed in any of the inline `expand_tests` cases — and matches POSIX exactly (PS4 IS an env var per POSIX), and integrates cleanly with the existing `export` builtin (users set PS4 via `export PS4=...` which the export builtin already handles). **Critical pins** (each by a dedicated test): PS4 read at trace-time NOT cached at trace-enable time (`ps4_change_takes_effect_for_next_command` mid-script `export PS4=` propagates to the next command's trace); empty PS4 produces a bare command with NO prefix (`export PS4=` ≠ unset PS4 — the env get returns Some("") not None, so the empty string is used verbatim, not the default fallback); PS4 is verbatim NO recursive expansion in v1 (`export PS4='$X '; export X=hello` traces literal `$X` not `hello` — pinned by `ps4_does_not_recursively_expand`); special chars pass through as-is (`[trace] ` works). The deferred recursive-expansion semantic has a recursion-guard concern (PS4="$PS4 " would loop) that this slice avoids by NOT expanding; a future slice that adds expansion is forced to update the test rather than blindly enabling recursion. T142's deferred list shrinks: `set -e` + `set -u` + `set -x` + `set -n` + customisable PS4 + `${VAR:?error}` + `$?` + `${VAR:-default}` + `$VAR` + quote handling are all landed. Remaining mode-flag work: `set -f` (globbing — moot until globbing exists), `set -o pipefail` (blocked on T143), `set --` (positional reset — blocked on parser). **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (eleventh multi-agent batch)**: three slices landed concurrently (T144 binary file-test 1e59aab + T146 sort -o b298aff + T142 PS4 a12f809). Workspace baseline 1900 → 1934 (+34) post all three cherry-picks, all green (one transient parallel-test flake on first cargo test --workspace run that resolved on the immediate retest). Notable shape of this batch: ONE medium userland slice (T144 binary file-test — 16 tests, completes the 3-arg branch's POSIX file-test set) alongside two smaller userland slices (T146 sort -o — tenth sort flag, completes the file-output story; T142 PS4 — env-driven approach validates that not every customisation needs a ShellFlags field). Eleven batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32, 9 +41, 10 +41, 11 +34 = +277 across nine batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) T144 [/test now has FULL POSIX file-test coverage (unary `-e`/`-f`/`-d`/`-r`/`-w`/`-x`/`-s` + binary `-nt`/`-ot`/`-ef`); (2) sort flag count at 10 with the cluster-loop refactored to support flag-with-parameter (`-o FILE`); (3) PS4 customisation validates env-driven trace-time customisation as an alternative to ShellFlags fields. The runway for the next batch: T072 host_file_recv (last remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough); T072 root remount; T146 `sort -k` field-keyed sort (the most architecturally interesting deferred sort slice); T146 `sort -V` version sort; T146 `sort -m` merge mode.

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -k FIELD` field-keyed sort slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-key; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `92094cf`): coreutils sort-tests crate +15 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 74 → 89); workspace 1934 → 1949. Commit 0928c07. Adds `-k N` field selector to `crates/coreutils/src/bin/sort.rs` — selects the Nth (1-indexed) whitespace-tokenized field of each line as the COMPARISON KEY while keeping the FULL ORIGINAL LINE in the output, mirroring the comparison-only invariant pattern already established by `-b`/`-f`/`-i`/`-d`. The line is whitespace-tokenized via `str::split_whitespace` (POSIX `[[:blank:]]` runs collapse to a single separator; `split_whitespace` semantics implicitly trim leading/trailing whitespace, so `-b` on a `-k`-keyed comparison is a structural no-op for the FIELD itself and only meaningful for the trim helper composition order assertion). Both `-k N` (space-separated, two argv slots) AND `-kN` (no-space, glued one-slot) forms accepted, with the parser arm sitting next to the existing `-o FILE` parameter-shape that established the cluster-loop refactor in the prior `-o` slice. **Critical refactor — `key_for` constructor extension AGAIN**: the shared `key_for` constructor extended in the prior file-test, dictionary, and `-i` slices is extended once more to take a SEVENTH `key_field: Option<usize>` parameter (None = whole-line key, Some(N) = the Nth whitespace-token of the line, or empty string if missing — the empty-string fallback puts unkeyed lines first under stable sort, matching POSIX behaviour for missing fields). The `check_sorted` helper signature gains the same parameter and threads it through both `key_for` calls per pair, so `sort -k 2 -c` checks the field-keyed ordering identically to how `sort -k 2` would have ordered the same input — the "check mode mirrors sort mode" invariant continues to hold. The new `select_field(line, field) -> &str` free function is a one-liner (`line.split_whitespace().nth(field - 1).unwrap_or("")`) sitting alongside `filter_printable`, `filter_dictionary`, `fold_to_upper_bytes`, `trim_leading_blanks`. Composition order is `select_field → trim → filter (printable / dictionary) → fold`, so `-k 2 -n` sorts numerically by field 2, `-k 2 -f` case-folds the field key, `-k 2 -r` reverses the field-key sort, `-k 2 -u` dedupes by field key, `-k 2 -c` / `-C` checks by field key, `-k 2 -d` applies the dictionary filter to the field, `-k 2 -i` applies the printable filter to the field. Argument parsing extends the short-flag clustering loop to handle `-k` specially as a value-consuming flag (like `-o`); validation rejects `-k 0` (zero invalid since fields are 1-indexed), `-k -1` (negative), `-k foo` (non-integer), `-k` with no following argv value (placeholder `<missing>`), and `-k ""` (empty string) — all five write `sort: invalid field specification: <value>\n` to stderr and exit 1. The five invalid-arg tests use `Stdio::null()` directly rather than the existing `run_check` helper because `-k` validation fails BEFORE stdin is read; `run_check`'s `Stdio::piped() + write_all` races the early-exit child and panics with `BrokenPipe` (same shape that `dash_co_combination_returns_usage_error` already uses for the same reason in the prior `-o` slice). +15 integration tests in `crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs`: `dash_k_selects_field_n_for_sort` (input `"alpha 3\nbravo 1\ncharlie 2\n"` with `-k 2` → `"bravo 1\ncharlie 2\nalpha 3\n"`, pins basic field-keyed sort + the full-line-output invariant); `dash_k_glued_form_works` (`-k2` no-space gives identical output to `-k 2` space-separated, pins the parameter-shape parity with `-o FILE`); `dash_k_zero_is_invalid_field` (`-k 0` → `sort: invalid field specification: 0` / exit 1); `dash_k_negative_is_invalid_field` (`-k -1` rejected); `dash_k_non_integer_is_invalid_field` (`-k foo` rejected); `dash_k_with_no_value_is_invalid_field` (`-k` at end of argv → placeholder `<missing>` in diagnostic); `dash_k_empty_string_is_invalid_field` (additional case beyond the spec list — `-k ""` rejected, ensuring no-arg-but-explicit-empty path is also covered); `dash_k_missing_field_uses_empty_key` (line with fewer tokens than N gets empty-string key, sorts first under stable sort); `dash_k_preserves_full_line_in_output` (output is the full line, not just the field — pins comparison-only invariant); `dash_kn_combines_field_and_numeric` (`-k 2 -n` sorts numerically by field 2: `"x 100\ny 9\nz 50\n"` → `"y 9\nz 50\nx 100\n"`, pins numeric × field composition); `dash_kr_combines_field_and_reverse` (reversed field-key sort); `dash_ku_dedupes_by_field_key` (`-k 2 -u` collapses duplicates of the field key, keeps first); `dash_kc_checks_with_field_keys` (negative half: plain `-c` on the SAME input FAILS because raw lex disagrees with field-key order; `-k 2 -c` PASSES — pins exactly WHY `-k` is useful); `dash_kf_combines_field_and_fold` (case-fold the field key); `dash_kd_combines_field_and_dictionary` (dictionary filter on the field). Workspace tests 1934 → 1949 (+15 from this slice alone). **Explicitly DEFERRED** (out of slice scope, future `-k` follow-ups): `-k N,M` start-end range form (sort by the slice from field N through field M); `-k N.C` start-field-plus-character-offset form; the full `-k F[.C][OPTS][,F[.C][OPTS]]` POSIX notation; per-key sort options like `-k 2n,3` (numeric on field 2 only, lex on field 3 — needs per-key flag overrides); `-t SEP` custom field separator (currently hardcoded to whitespace); `--key=` long form alias. **Constitution check** PASS: Principle II (strict layering) — sort is a self-contained coreutils binary, no kernel/display/toolkit dependency added; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — slice uses no extension syscalls; Principle X (testability at every layer) — +15 isolation tests with scratch-dir cleanup pattern. The `key_for` parameter count is now 7 — the slice prose's earlier "lift into a builder once parameter count crosses ~5" suggestion is now objectively overdue; flagged as the natural next architectural slice on this binary. The runway for the next batch: T146 `sort -V` version sort (semver-aware comparator, would slot in alongside `Numeric`/`Bytes` in the `Key` enum); T146 `sort -m` merge mode (assumes pre-sorted inputs and merges them — substantively different from sort-and-output); T146 `key_for` builder pattern refactor (lift the 7-param positional constructor into a `KeyFor::default().with_field(N).with_numeric().build()` shape); T072 host_file_recv (last remaining ENOSYS-fallthrough); T072 root remount.

After the **T072 follow-up — SIGUSR1/SIGUSR2 user-defined signals slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-sigusr; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `92094cf`): kernel syscall-tests crate +7 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` 386 → 393); workspace 1934 → 1941. Commit 6a53fa9. `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs` gains two new `Signal` enum variants — `Signal::User1 = 10` (POSIX `SIGUSR1`) and `Signal::User2 = 12` (POSIX `SIGUSR2`) — sitting alongside the existing `Kill = 9 / Term = 15 / Interrupt = 2 / Pipe = 13 / Child = 17` set with the same `#[repr(u16)]` POSIX-aligned numeric mapping that lets userland code hard-code the signum constants without a translation table. Both new variants are catchable (`is_catchable()` returns true for both — only `SIGKILL` is non-catchable in v1) and route through the existing `Kernel::proc_kill` inbox-delivery path: the `Signal::Term | Interrupt | Pipe | Child` arm in `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs::Kernel::proc_kill` was extended to `Signal::Term | Interrupt | Pipe | Child | User1 | User2`, so a `proc_kill(target, User1)` call posts the variant to `target.signal_inbox` exactly the same way SIGTERM does — userland reads them off `FdObject::SignalChannel`'s fd 3 as 2-byte LE pairs alongside every other catchable signal. Wire-format mapping in both `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs::handle_proc_kill` (the `PROC_KILL` opcode) and `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs::handle_proc_raise` (the WASI `proc_raise` opcode for self-signal) gained `10 => Signal::User1, 12 => Signal::User2` arms; the proc_raise sibling extension is for symmetry — without it, a process self-raising SIGUSR1 would unexpectedly EINVAL even though a peer's `proc_kill(self, User1)` would succeed (POSIX `raise(SIGUSR1)` is universally defined and userland would assume it works wherever `kill` does). **Critical behavioural choice — SIGUSR1/2 do NOT interrupt parked syscalls in v1**: `handle_proc_kill`'s post-`Kernel::proc_kill` park-interrupt arm continues to fire `interrupt_parked_accept` / `interrupt_parked_wait` / `interrupt_parked_recv` ONLY for `Signal::Term || Signal::Interrupt`; User1 and User2 fall through to the inbox-only path that SIGCHLD and SIGPIPE already take. The handler-arm comment block was rewritten to spell out the `{Term, Interrupt}` interrupter set vs the `{Pipe, Child, User1, User2}` non-interrupter set with the POSIX rationale: POSIX gates user-signal interruption of blocking syscalls behind `SA_RESTART` (or its inverse) which v1 doesn't model, so the safe default is "deliver to inbox, don't disturb the parked syscall." A future slice can flip this conditionally once a use case appears (e.g. a daemon that wants SIGUSR1 to trigger a config reload mid-blocking-recv) — adding `User1` to the interrupt set is a one-line change. **Default disposition** in v1 is "deliver to inbox; nothing else" — POSIX defaults SIGUSR1/2 to terminate, but the v1 substrate doesn't auto-terminate on inbox-delivered signals (no signal-handler dispatch in v1; userland that ignores the inbox simply never observes the signal). +7 integration tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: `sigusr1_lands_on_inbox_via_proc_kill` (PROC_KILL signum 10 against a child; status 0; pending_signals == 1; inbox drains `[Signal::User1]`; child stays Ready), `sigusr2_lands_on_inbox_via_proc_kill` (mirror for signum 12 / `Signal::User2`), `sigusr1_does_not_interrupt_parked_recv` (parker A parks on ipc_recv, init delivers SIGUSR1, parker stays BlockedOnIpc, no EINTR wake on A's req_id, inbox holds `[Signal::User1]` — both halves of the conservative-delivery contract fire), `sigusr2_does_not_interrupt_parked_recv` (mirror for signum 12), `sigusr1_to_unknown_pid_returns_esrch` (PROC_KILL signum 10 against pid 9999 → -ESRCH; the signum doesn't change which errno arm fires), `sigusr1_does_not_interrupt_parked_accept` (display server parks on ipc_accept, self-delivers SIGUSR1, listener's `parked_acceptor` slot still populated, ds still BlockedOnIpc, no EINTR wake, inbox holds `[Signal::User1]`), `sigusr1_and_sigusr2_are_distinct_in_inbox` (deliver one of each to the same target; pending_signals == 2; inbox drains `[Signal::User1, Signal::User2]` — the SignalInbox coalesces SAME-variant deliveries but never different variants). The non-interrupter tests use `find` on `pending_wakes_snapshot` (matching the existing SIGPIPE/SIGCHLD pattern) rather than asserting `wakes.is_empty()` since the dispatcher may queue multiple wake entries per tick. Two existing `proc::signal::tests` units were also extended (not new tests, just additional assertions): `is_catchable_distinguishes_kill_from_other_signals` gained `assert!(Signal::User1.is_catchable()); assert!(Signal::User2.is_catchable());` lines and `number_matches_posix_values` gained `assert_eq!(Signal::User1.number(), 10); assert_eq!(Signal::User2.number(), 12);` lines. No probe rotation: `HOST_FILE_RECV` remains the canonical ext-range ENOSYS probe target (this slice extends existing handlers rather than landing a new opcode). **Explicitly DEFERRED**: auto-terminate-on-default-disposition for SIGUSR1/2 (would need a default-handler registry — out of scope until any inbox-delivered signal gets default-terminate behaviour); real-time signals (`SIGRTMIN..SIGRTMAX` — POSIX queueable signals with separate ordering + payload semantics; the existing coalescing `SignalInbox` would need a queue-per-signum sibling); `sigaction` to register custom handlers (needs userspace dispatch infrastructure on the wasm32-wasip1 side); `sigprocmask` / signal blocking (needs per-process mask state on `Process`); future opt-in "interrupt-parked on USR1/USR2" mode (would extend the interrupter set conditionally per-process, behind a new field on `Process` set via a future `sigaction`-shaped opcode). **Constitution check** PASS: Principle V (process isolation) — signal delivery still routes through Kernel-mediated proc_kill; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — extends existing PROC_KILL + proc_raise without adding opcodes; Principle X (testability at every layer) — +7 isolation tests with the established conservative-delivery test shape. T072's signal-related deferred list now reads: PROC_KILL with all five legacy signums (Kill/Term/Interrupt/Pipe/Child) + User1/User2 — done; SIGINT-interrupts-park — done (8ec24f0); SIGUSR1/2 added to inbox-only delivery — done (this slice). Remaining T072 work after this batch: host_file_recv (last ENOSYS-fallthrough); root remount.

After the **T144 follow-up — `read` builtin slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-read; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `92094cf`): sh test_builtin-tests crate +10 (`crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` 64 → 74); workspace 1934 → 1944. Commit f615adf. Closes the `read` deferral that the bb8fd0f / 40d3aee follow-up lists flagged as a "ships when its need arises" classic builtin — the canonical POSIX input primitive that pulls a single line from stdin into a named env var, used in every shell script that prompts for or pipes in user input. `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs::dispatch_builtin` gains a new `"read" => builtin_read(&tokens[1..], env, stdin, stderr)` arm; the helper sits adjacent to the other env-mutating builtins (`export` / `unset`) and reads from the SAME `BufRead` source the REPL pulls command lines from, so a `read VAR` line that arrives via piped stdin sees the next available line — exactly matching bash / dash semantics where `read` and the REPL share one input stream. **Critical signature change — `dispatch_builtin` now takes `stdin: &mut R: BufRead` between `flags` and `stdout`** (the natural slot — input precedes output, mirroring the standard POSIX file-descriptor ordering 0/1/2). Pre-slice signature was `dispatch_builtin<W: Write, E: Write>(tokens, cwd, env, flags, stdout, stderr) -> BuiltinOutcome`; post-slice is `dispatch_builtin<R: BufRead, W: Write, E: Write>(tokens, cwd, env, flags, stdin, stdout, stderr) -> BuiltinOutcome`. The single call site in `crates/sh/src/run.rs::run_with_env` was updated to pass `&mut stdin` (the REPL's own line-source, already a `BufRead` parameter from the function signature). No shims, no `Option<R>` wrappers, no per-builtin gating — every dispatched builtin gets the stdin handle, even though only `read` consumes from it (the other twelve builtins ignore the parameter entirely). Threading the parameter unconditionally lets a future builtin (e.g. a hypothetical `eval`-from-stdin shape, or a `wait`-for-input form) plug in without revisiting the dispatch signature. The `pub(crate)` visibility means no external caller signatures break — only the in-crate `run_with_env` needed the one-line update. `builtin_read` semantics: for the happy path (`read VAR` against an available line), the helper calls `BufRead::read_line(&mut buf)` to pull one line WITH its trailing newline, then strips one trailing `\n` followed by one trailing `\r` (defensive CRLF stripping for copy-pasted input from Windows terminals). Internal whitespace is preserved verbatim — `read X` against `"  foo  bar  \n"` assigns `X="  foo  bar  "` (no trim, no split, no expansion). The post-strip text (which may be the empty string for a bare-newline line) is inserted into the env map via `BTreeMap::insert`, which OVERWRITES any existing entry under the same name. **Status outcomes**: `Status(0)` on a successful read; `Status(1)` on EOF (`read_line` returns `Ok(0)` because stdin is exhausted) — POSIX-canonical EOF return that scripts can use in `until ! read X; do ...` loops; `Status(1)` on any underlying I/O error from the read path, with NO stderr write (POSIX-aligned silent-failure behavior — `read` writes diagnostics ONLY for usage errors, never for read-failure paths). Usage errors produce `Status(2)` and a stderr diagnostic: `read` with NO args writes `sh: read: missing variable name\n`; `read ""` (empty-string var name) writes `sh: read: : not a valid identifier\n` — the latter mirrors the existing `export` empty-name diagnostic shape verbatim, keeping the user-facing error vocabulary consistent across the env-mutating builtin family. EOF and I/O-error paths produce NO env mutation (any pre-existing entry under any of the named vars is left untouched) — pinned by the `read_returns_one_after_last_line` test that runs three sequential `read X` calls against `"a\nb\n"` and asserts `X == "b"` after the third (EOF) read. Variable-name validation: any nonempty string is accepted as a name in v1 (no POSIX identifier-charset check — matches the existing `export` lenient policy). Multi-VAR form (`read A B C` against `"x y z\n"`): v1 simplification — the entire line goes to the FIRST var (`A="x y z"`) and every remaining var is set to the empty string (`B=""`, `C=""`). Full POSIX IFS-splitting (split on `IFS` whitespace, give the first N-1 tokens to vars 1..N-1 and the joined remainder to the last var) is explicitly DEFERRED — scripts that use the multi-var form for "consume the line and discard the rest" semantic already work because the first var gets the right value; scripts that use it for true field-splitting will get a degraded-but-not-broken behavior until the IFS slice lands. The deferral is pinned by the `read_multi_var_first_gets_line_rest_empty` test, which is the future IFS slice's regression target — when real splitting lands, the test will be UPDATED rather than deleted. +10 integration tests in `crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs`: happy path (`read_assigns_line_to_var_returns_zero`); whitespace preservation (`read_strips_trailing_newline_only`); CRLF handling (`read_strips_crlf`); EOF semantics (`read_returns_one_on_eof` — uses `set -e` to surface the Status(1) as the REPL exit code; pins the no-env-mutation rule, `read_returns_one_after_last_line` — three sequential reads against `"a\nb\n"`, third hits EOF, X holds `b`); usage errors (`read_no_args_is_usage_error`, `read_empty_name_is_invalid_identifier`); v1 simplifications (`read_multi_var_first_gets_line_rest_empty` — pins the no-IFS-split semantic); env-map shape (`read_overwrites_existing_var` — pre-seeded `X=old` becomes `X=new`, pins BTreeMap insert-overwrites-on-collision); unicode (`read_handles_unicode_line` — multibyte utf-8 round-trips cleanly because the strip-trailing-newline matchers are single-byte ASCII).
