The EOF tests deliberately omit a final command line so the REPL hits EOF naturally after the third read returns Status(1) — pins the shared-stdin contract. **Explicitly DEFERRED**: all `read` flags — `-r` (raw mode, no backslash escape — the most-requested follow-up since modern POSIX scripts conventionally use `read -r line`), `-n N` (read at most N chars), `-N N` (read EXACTLY N chars), `-t SEC` (timeout — needs `Atomics.wait` integration on the wasip1 substrate), `-p PROMPT`, `-s` (silent / no echo — needs terminal-mode toggling, blocks on terminal-control infrastructure), `-d DELIM` (custom line delimiter), `-a ARRAY` (blocks on array type); each `-X` arg in v1 is currently treated as a regular var name (a bash-style "unknown flag rejected" path would need a flag-parser layer that doesn't exist yet — keeping the v1 path lenient avoids a confusing error for users typing the canonical `read -r line` who would otherwise get `not a valid identifier` for `-r`); IFS-based field splitting for multi-VAR reads (the natural follow-up); POSIX identifier-charset validation (matches existing `export` lenient policy); trailing backslash handling for line continuation; heredoc / herestring interaction (those are tokenizer-level concerns that would land in T143's redirection slice). **Constitution check** PASS: Principle II (strict layering) — sh is a self-contained userland crate, no kernel/display/toolkit dependency added; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — slice uses no syscalls (the BufRead is a process-internal abstraction over already-opened stdin); Principle X (testability at every layer) — +10 isolation tests covering happy path, EOF, errors, multi-VAR, encoding round-trip. **Architectural note**: the `read` slice introduces the FIRST builtin that consumes from stdin — every prior builtin only wrote to stdout / stderr or mutated env / cwd / flags. The dispatch-signature change (adding `stdin: &mut R: BufRead`) is the load-bearing infrastructure that any future input-consuming builtin can build on without revisiting the signature; the ordering choice (input → output → error mirrors the POSIX FD 0/1/2 convention) gives the signature a stable, recognisable shape. The v1 simplification on multi-VAR (whole line to first var, empty string to rest) is a deliberate degradation rather than a panic / error — it preserves the right SHAPE (assignments happen, env mutates, status is 0) so scripts that don't depend on field-splitting work right away. The lenient-flag-handling decision (treat `-X` as a regular var name) is the same shape as the lenient-identifier decision in `export`. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (twelfth multi-agent batch)**: three slices landed concurrently (T146 sort -k 0928c07 + T072 SIGUSR1/2 6a53fa9 + T144 read f615adf). Workspace baseline 1934 → 1976 (+42) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantive userland slice (T146 sort -k — the most architecturally interesting deferred sort flag, refactors `key_for` constructor to 7 parameters and is the natural trigger for the deferred builder-pattern refactor) alongside ONE kernel slice (T072 SIGUSR1/2 — adds POSIX user-defined signals 10/12 with the conservative non-interrupter delivery policy) and ONE small userland slice (T144 read — the canonical POSIX input primitive, introduces the first builtin that consumes from stdin and threads a BufRead through dispatch_builtin). Twelve batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32, 9 +41, 10 +41, 11 +34, 12 +42 = +319 across ten batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale with no integration-time conflicts on this batch (the T144 tasks.md patch needed manual Edit-tool surgery because surrounding T146 prose grew between agent fork and integration, but the recovery was clean). **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) sort `-k` flag count at 11 — the cluster-loop's flag-with-parameter pattern proven on `-o` and reused for `-k`; the `key_for` constructor's 7-param positional shape is now objectively overdue for the builder refactor; (2) SIGUSR1/2 give userland the POSIX-canonical "out-of-band signaling" primitive that daemons use for config reload / state dump / etc., even without the future SA_RESTART-shaped interrupt opt-in; (3) `read` introduces stdin-consuming builtin infrastructure — any future input-driven builtin now plugs in without revisiting `dispatch_builtin`'s signature. The runway for the next batch: T146 `sort -V` version sort (semver-aware comparator); T146 `key_for` builder pattern refactor (7-param positional → builder); T072 host_file_recv (last ENOSYS-fallthrough); T072 root remount; T144 `read -r` raw-mode flag (the natural next read-flag slice); T144 `IFS`-based field splitting for `read` multi-VAR.

After the **T144 follow-up — `read -r` raw-mode flag slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T144-read-raw; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `79b13da`): sh test_builtin-tests crate +9 (`crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` 74 → 83); workspace 1966 → 1975. Commit 2ab327f. Closes the `-r` deferral that the f615adf `read` slice flagged as "the most-requested first follow-up since modern POSIX scripts conventionally use `read -r line`" — adds the POSIX `-r` flag that disables backslash interpretation, used in the canonical line-iteration idiom `while IFS= read -r line; do ... done`. Without `-r`, POSIX `read` interprets a TRAILING backslash at the end of an input line as a LINE-CONTINUATION marker — the backslash and following newline are stripped, and a SECOND line is read and concatenated to the first. With `-r`, ALL backslashes are LITERAL — no continuation, no escape interpretation, just `\n` / `\r` strip then assign verbatim. `crates/sh/src/builtin.rs::builtin_read` gains a single-flag parse step at the top: if `args[0] == "-r"` (the standalone, exact-match form), set `raw: bool = true` and slice the flag off the front; otherwise `raw = false` and `args` passes through unchanged. The v1 simplification keeps the parse single-flag-at-a-time — combined / clustered short flags like `-rs` or `-rt 5` are NOT recognised, and `-rX` (with any non-empty char after the `-r`) is treated as a regular var name (NOT a `-r` flag with a `-X` cluster), preserving the f615adf "lenient flag handling" decision for unknown flags. Only the EXACT standalone `-r` form is recognised. The continuation-handling logic when `raw == false`: after stripping trailing `\n` / `\r` from the just-read line, count the trailing backslashes from the END of the post-strip text using `bytes().rev().take_while(|b| *b == b'\\').count()`. An ODD count means the LAST backslash stands alone (continuation marker — strip ONE trailing backslash and read another line, appending); an EVEN count means every backslash is paired (the second-to-last escapes the last, so the last is a literal backslash, no continuation). Multiple consecutive continuations are supported because the continuation step is a `loop` that re-evaluates the trailing-count after each appended line — `"a\\\nb\\\nc\n"` produces `"abc"` because the loop runs three times. When `raw == true`, the trailing-count check is skipped entirely — the post-strip text is appended verbatim and the loop breaks immediately, so `read -r X` against `"foo\\\n"` yields `X=foo\\` (single backslash preserved). **EOF mid-continuation behavior** (the v1 simplification the slice prompt called out as POSIX-ambiguous): if the FIRST line ends with a continuation backslash but EOF arrives before the SECOND line, the loop's `read_line` returns `Ok(0)` on the second iteration. The implementation tracks `any_line_read: bool` to distinguish "EOF on first call" from "EOF after at least one successful line"; the FIRST-call EOF returns `Status(1)` (canonical EOF — no input read at all, matches the pre-slice behavior), but the MID-CONTINUATION EOF returns `Status(0)` with whatever was accumulated so far (the slice picks the more useful semantic for scripts that want to capture what they got). Bash returns `Status(1)` for "unfinished" reads, but the v1 choice (Status(0) with partial value) is documented + pinned by the `read_default_eof_during_continuation_returns_partial_value` test, which becomes the regression target if a future slice ever wants to switch to bash-strict semantics. The `-r` flag works alongside the multi-VAR shape: `read -r A B C` consumes the first arg slot for the flag, then `A B C` are the var names; the v1 multi-VAR simplification still applies (whole accumulated line to `A`, empty string to `B` and `C`). The `read -r` with no var names case (`read -r\n`) flows through the `names.is_empty()` check after the flag is stripped, producing the same `sh: read: missing variable name\n` diagnostic as bare `read`. +9 integration tests in `crates/sh/tests/test_builtin.rs` in a new `// ---------- read -r flag ----------` block: `-r` literal-backslash cases (`read_r_treats_trailing_backslash_as_literal` — canonical raw-mode pin; `read_r_passes_through_double_backslash` — both backslashes preserved through the raw path which is a different code path than the default-mode even-count path; `read_r_preserves_backslash_in_middle_of_line` — mid-line backslashes pass through verbatim); default-mode continuation cases (`read_default_treats_trailing_backslash_as_continuation` — canonical line-continuation pin; `read_default_handles_multiple_continuations` — pins the loop-runs-multiple-times semantic; `read_default_double_backslash_is_not_continuation` — pins the EVEN-count rule); multi-VAR + flag interaction (`read_r_with_multi_var_works` — pins that the flag consumes the first arg slot but the multi-VAR v1 simplification still runs on the rest); usage error (`read_r_with_no_var_names_is_usage_error` — pins that the flag-strip happens BEFORE the empty-args check); EOF semantics (`read_default_eof_during_continuation_returns_partial_value` — pins the v1 "Status(0) with partial value" choice over the bash-style "Status(1) with no value"). **Explicitly DEFERRED**: the `--` end-of-flags separator (would need a tiny extension to the flag-walk loop, blocked on whether the v1 shell ever wants the `--` convention generally); combined / clustered short flags like `-rs` or `-rt 5` (the v1 path stays single-flag-at-a-time); the remaining `read` flags `-n N` / `-N N` / `-t SEC` / `-p PROMPT` / `-s` / `-d DELIM` / `-a ARRAY` (each ships independently when its need arises — `-p PROMPT` is likely the next-most-requested for interactive scripts); backslash-as-IFS-delimiter-escape (waits on the IFS slice — the only non-end-of-line backslash semantic that POSIX `read` cares about, and v1 doesn't have IFS-splitting yet); backslash-as-prompt-escape with `-p` (waits on the `-p` slice). **Constitution check** PASS: Principle II (strict layering) — sh is a self-contained userland crate, no kernel/display/toolkit dependency added; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — slice uses no syscalls; Principle X (testability at every layer) — +9 isolation tests covering raw / default × continuation / no-continuation × multi-VAR / single-VAR cells. **Architectural note**: the `-r` slice introduces the FIRST flag-bearing builtin in the v1 shell — every prior builtin either took zero flags (`pwd`, `:`, `true`, `false`) or treated arg-parse heuristically by content (`set -e` / `set +e` parsed by the SET-TOKEN convention, `export NAME=VALUE` parsed by the `=` presence, `[` / `test` parsed by the arity matrix). The single-flag parse pattern (`if args[0] == "-r" { (true, &args[1..]) } else { (false, args) }`) is the smallest-possible flag-parser shape — no enum, no bitfield, no shared parser layer; each future flag will add one more line of the same shape. When the count crosses ~3 flags this should be lifted into a tiny flag-walk loop or an enum-based parser. The accumulator-loop refactor (replacing the single-`read_line` match with an outer `loop { read_line; check_continuation; break-or-continue }`) is the load-bearing structural change: every future `read` flag that affects line acquisition (`-n N` would constrain the read length, `-N N` would constrain it strictly, `-t SEC` would wrap the read in a timeout, `-d DELIM` would replace the `\n` strip with a custom delimiter, `-p PROMPT` would write to stderr before the loop) now has a clean place to plug in without revisiting the `Ok(0)` / `Ok(_)` / `Err(_)` arms. The slice deliberately does NOT plumb `-r` through `crates/sh/src/run.rs` — `-r` is purely a `builtin_read`-internal change; future flags that need REPL-level state (e.g. `-p PROMPT` writing to stderr) will pass through stderr (already in the signature) and won't need run.rs changes either. Next runway for T144 follow-ups: `-p PROMPT` (prompt before reading — minor wrapper around the existing stderr handle); `-n N` (read at most N chars — needs a Read-not-BufRead path); `-t SEC` (timeout — blocks on Atomics.wait integration); IFS-based field splitting for multi-VAR (the test `read_multi_var_first_gets_line_rest_empty` is the future regression target).

After the **T146 follow-up — `sort -V` version sort slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T146-sort-version; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `ab372d8`): coreutils sort-tests crate +13 (`crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs` 89 → 102); workspace 1966 → 1979. Commit 442ca5d. Adds the eighth sort comparator flag (after `-r` / `-u` / `-n` / `-f` / `-c` / `-b` / `-i` / `-d` / `-C` / `-o` / `-k`) — `-V` enables version sort, the canonical fix for the `file1, file10, file100, file2` lexicographic mis-ordering of files-named-with-embedded-numbers. With `-V`, `file2` correctly sorts BEFORE `file10` because the embedded digit run `2` is parsed and compared NUMERICALLY against `10` (2 < 10) rather than byte-order. The algorithm walks both compared strings simultaneously with two cursors `i` / `j`, classifying each position as either a digit run (a maximal `[0-9]+` substring) or a non-digit run (single byte at a time for the v1 simplification). When BOTH cursors land on digit runs, the runs are extracted, parsed to `u64` via a new `parse_u64_run` helper, and compared by value first; if values are equal, the run with FEWER characters wins. When the two cursors land on different run TYPES, the v1 simplification compares byte-order on the first differing byte (matches GNU output for plain ASCII inputs without needing the full GNU algorithm with its file-extension special case and `~` Debian-version handling). Numeric overflow: `parse_u64_run` returns `None` when the digit run exceeds 20 chars (u64 max is exactly 20 digits) OR when the `checked_mul` / `checked_add` arithmetic would overflow; on `None` from EITHER side the comparison falls back to byte-order lex on that pair of runs (no panic, safe semantic for pathological version strings — pinned by `dash_V_overflow_falls_back_to_lex`). **Implementation routes through a NEW `Key::Version(String)` variant** rather than reusing `Key::Bytes(Vec<u8>)` because the version comparator is fundamentally NOT a key-derivation function (you can't precompute a sortable Vec<u8> that mimics version-compare order without effectively serialising the full algorithm into the bytes); instead the variant carries the raw (optionally case-folded) string and the manual `Ord for Key` impl dispatches `(Key::Version, Key::Version)` to a dedicated `version_compare(&str, &str)` helper. The `Ord` impl is hand-written rather than derived because the Numeric / Bytes / Version variants are now mutually-incomparable in any meaningful way — the original derive over Numeric / Bytes worked because a single sort pass picks ONE variant for every line; adding Version as a third variant required preserving that invariant explicitly while still supplying a total ordering for any Key pair (variant tag order: Numeric < Bytes < Version, which never matters in practice). The `key_for` constructor and `check_sorted` helper both gain an EIGHTH positional `version_sort: bool` parameter (mirroring how `-d` and `-i` were threaded through in earlier slices) — yes the seven-then-eight-parameter shape is overdue for a builder/options-struct refactor (flagged in the previous `-k` slice prose) but this slice intentionally keeps to the established pattern; the refactor is its own future scoped slice. Dispatch precedence in `key_for`: `numeric` is checked FIRST (so `-Vn` and `-nV` both produce `-n` numeric sort, pinned by `dash_Vn_numeric_dominates`), THEN `version_sort`, THEN `dictionary_order`, THEN `ignore_nonprinting`, THEN the lex/fold/blanks fallback — the version-after-numeric, version-before-filters order matters because version_compare consumes the RAW string (digits + dots + dashes + underscores) as a single indivisible unit, so running the dictionary-filter (which strips dots and dashes) BEFORE version_compare would destroy the very segment delimiters the version comparator relies on. `-V` composes with `-r` (reverse), `-u` (dedupe by version-key — two strings that compare equal under version sort collapse), `-c` / `-C` (check input is in version order — `dash_V_combines_with_check` is the load-bearing test that VERIFIES `-Vc` PASSES on `file1\nfile2\nfile10\n` while plain `-c` on the same input FAILS, pinning that `-V` is doing real work that lex check cannot do), `-k N` (sort by version comparator on the Nth field), and `-f` (case-fold then version-compare — digit runs unaffected by ASCII fold so the numeric path is identical). **Tiebreak rule for equal-value digit runs with different leading-zero counts**: SHORTER representation sorts FIRST — so `1` < `01` < `001` (pinned explicitly by `dash_V_leading_zero_tiebreak`); the spec authorised picking either rule and pinning it, so v1 picks the simpler "shorter wins" rule (opposite to GNU which puts longer rep first); a future slice can swap the `len.cmp` direction if user feedback wants GNU parity. Two new helper functions land alongside `version_compare`: `digit_run_end(bytes, start)` walks bytes forward while `is_ascii_digit()` holds and returns the exclusive end index, and `parse_u64_run(run)` parses a `&[u8]` of pure ASCII digits to `Option<u64>` returning `None` on length>20 or overflow. A third helper `maybe_fold_for_version(line, fold)` returns either the original line as `String` or an upper-folded `String` (since `Key::Version` carries an owned `String` payload, not `&str` — `sort_by_key` lifetime constraints require an owned key). +13 integration tests in `crates/coreutils/tests/sort.rs`: `dash_V_sorts_versions_naturally` (canonical `file1, file10, file2` case), `dash_V_handles_multi_segment_versions` (semver-style `1.0.10 / 1.0.2 / 1.0.1`), `dash_V_compares_digit_runs_numerically`, `dash_V_leading_zero_tiebreak` (pins shorter-rep-first), `dash_V_pure_text_falls_back_to_lex`, `dash_V_pure_digits_compares_numerically`, `dash_V_combines_with_reverse`, `dash_V_combines_with_unique`, `dash_V_combines_with_check` (load-bearing — pins the `-Vc` PASSES vs `-c` FAILS distinction), `dash_V_with_field_key`, `dash_Vn_numeric_dominates` (pins dispatch order in `key_for`), `dash_V_overflow_falls_back_to_lex` (25-char digit runs sort lex with no panic), `dash_V_combines_with_fold`. Workspace tests 1966 → 1979 (+13 from this slice alone). **Explicitly DEFERRED**: the full GNU `-V` algorithm including the special handling for file extensions (e.g. `foo.tar.gz` vs `foo.tar.bz2` — GNU treats `.gz` and `.bz2` as suffix metadata sorted after the rest); locale-aware version sort (GNU honours `LC_COLLATE` for the non-digit run lex compare; v1 stays C/POSIX locale = byte-order); the `--version-sort` long-form alias; the `~` (tilde) Debian-versioning special case where `~` sorts BEFORE everything (so `0.9~rc1` < `0.9` < `1.0`); BigInt / arbitrary-precision digit-run support (overflow falls back to lex per spec above, no extension to `u128` in v1); the overdue builder-pattern refactor of `key_for` / `check_sorted` (this slice keeps the established positional-parameter shape; the refactor is its own scoped slice with explicit before/after API delta and a dedicated PR-shape test plan). **Constitution check** PASS: Principle II (strict layering) — sort is a self-contained coreutils binary; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — no extension syscalls used; Principle X (testability at every layer) — +13 isolation tests covering basic / multi-segment / leading-zero tiebreak / pure-text fallback / overflow fallback / composition with every other comparator flag. **Architectural note**: the `Key::Version(String)` variant is the FIRST variant in the `Key` enum to require a hand-written `Ord` impl rather than `#[derive(Ord)]` — the previous Numeric / Bytes pair was derive-friendly because both variants compared by their inner payload; adding Version forced the issue that variant TAG ORDER never matters for the actual sort runtime (every line in a single sort uses the same variant) but Rust's `derive(Ord)` would have produced wrong cross-variant orderings if it ever did matter. The hand-written impl makes the cross-variant behavior explicit (Numeric < Bytes < Version, but practically irrelevant) and gives a clean dispatch site for future variants (e.g. a hypothetical `Key::General(f64)` for `-g` general-numeric sort). The `version_compare` algorithm is intentionally simpler than GNU's full implementation — the file-extension special case and `~` handling are deferred but the deferred-list documents the exact mechanism each would need (suffix metadata extraction; `~`-as-special-byte in the non-digit comparison) so the future slice has a clear path forward. The 8-parameter `key_for` is now objectively past the threshold where a builder-pattern refactor would be valuable; flagged TWICE in successive slices (the `-k` slice prose first, this slice's prose again) — the next slice that touches `key_for` should consider whether to do the refactor first or the new-flag work first; the slice prompt's framing of "refactor is its own scoped slice" is the right call. **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (thirteenth multi-agent batch)**: three slices landed concurrently (T146 sort -k 0928c07 + T072 SIGUSR1/2 6a53fa9 + T144 read f615adf in batch 12 — wait, those were batch 13; batch 14 is this batch with T072 host_file_recv pending + T144 read -r 2ab327f + T146 sort -V 442ca5d). Workspace baseline 1966 → 1979 (+13) post the T146 cherry-pick; T144 read -r added another +9 in the same batch; T072 host_file_recv still pending. The runway for the next batch: T146 `key_for` builder-pattern refactor (overdue, flagged twice in a row); T146 `sort -m` merge mode; T146 `sort -t SEP` custom field separator; T146 `sort -g` general-numeric (would add `Key::General(f64)` variant); T144 `read -p PROMPT` flag; T144 IFS-based field splitting for multi-VAR; remaining T072 deferred items (root remount; host_file_dropped TS-side bootstrap wiring once host_file_recv lands).

After the **T072 follow-up — `host_file_recv` opcode + token table slice** (parallel dispatch agent in worktree-T072-host-file-recv; coordinator-cherry-picked onto local main HEAD `777af15`): kernel syscall-tests crate +10 (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`) + `crates/kernel/src/host_file.rs` unit tests +3 (NEW file 0 → 3); workspace 1966 → 1979. Commit a0a478f. Lands the LAST T072-deferred extension opcode `HOST_FILE_RECV (0x1500)` — the userland-facing entry point for receiving host-OS files dropped or uploaded into the browser tab via the bootstrap layer, completing the ext-range opcode coverage. **Wire format implemented per `contracts/syscalls.md §3.6` verbatim**: `host_file_recv(token: u32) -> fd`. Args layout: `args[0..4]` = `token: u32`, no heap; response `value` = freshly-allocated fd-number. Atomic-reject errors: `-EBADF` on unknown OR already-consumed token (per spec: "Exactly one `host_file_recv` call is permitted per token; a second call ... returns `EBADF`. ... any other caller [unknown token] receives `EBADF`" — both arms collapse to the same EBADF return for v1, since the v1 substrate has no per-pid token visibility model that would distinguish "token not yours" from "token doesn't exist"); `-EMFILE` on full fd table with rollback (the token gets PUT BACK in the pending table so userland retry after closing some fds is non-destructive); `-ESRCH` on unknown caller pid. **No capability check** — per spec §3.6 ("no capability is required"); the effective access control is EBADF-on-unknown-token, not a Cap::HostFileRecv check. **Implementation routes through a NEW `crates/kernel/src/host_file.rs` module** (~150 lines) carrying a `HostFile` struct (`name: String`, `bytes: Vec<u8>`, `mime: Option<String>`) and a `HostFileFd` streaming-state struct (`bytes: Arc<Vec<u8>>`, `position: usize`); chosen over inline-in-sys.rs since the slice scope marked it as optional and keeping the host-file domain types in their own module follows the existing `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs` precedent for domain-scoped sub-modules. **Kernel state additions** in `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: `Kernel.host_files: BTreeMap<u32, HostFile>` is the kernel-wide pending-token table (the bootstrap-side `host_file_dropped` notification path WILL populate this once the TS-side wiring lands in a future slice — that path is OUT OF SCOPE for this slice, but the receive end is fully wired); `Kernel.host_file_fds: BTreeMap<(Pid, Fd), HostFileFd>` is the per-fd streaming state populated when a successful `host_file_recv` consumes a token; `Kernel.next_host_file_token: u32` is the monotonic token allocator for the future-slice bootstrap-side path (test-only access via `Kernel::host_file_dropped(name, bytes, mime) -> u32` which the integration tests use to seed the pending table without going through the TS-side notification path). **FdObject routing**: a new `FdObject::HostFile` variant in `crates/kernel/src/fd/mod.rs` directs `fd_read` / `fd_close` / `fd_filestat_get` / `fd_seek` to the per-fd HostFileFd state. `fd_read` streams from `bytes[position..]` advancing `position`, returns 0 at EOF (POSIX-canonical end-of-file); `fd_close` removes the (pid, fd) entry from `host_file_fds` (the underlying `HostFile` was MOVED out of `host_files` at recv time, so close drops the last reference and frees the bytes — the analog of "releases the browser-side `File` reference" in the spec, once the TS-side cleanup hook lands); `fd_filestat_get` returns `(REGULAR_FILE, dev=0, ino=token, size=bytes.len(), nlink=1, atim=mtim=ctim=0)` matching the spec's "returns the host file's size in `st_size` and zero for all timestamps". `fd_seek` supports SEEK_SET / SEEK_CUR / SEEK_END for random-access reads of host files (e.g. an image viewer that reads PNG headers then jumps to specific offsets). **EMFILE rollback**: if the fd allocation fails after the token has been removed from `host_files`, the token is RE-INSERTED so userland can retry the recv after closing fds; this is structurally important because losing the token to a transient EMFILE would surface as a permanent "host file disappeared" condition with no recovery path. The rollback is the single-line `self.host_files.insert(token, host_file);` in the EMFILE arm, paired with an `assert_eq!(self.host_files.contains_key(&token), false)` test that pins the post-rollback re-presence. **Probe rotation**: the existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` test was REWRITTEN in place (no test count change for that test) to probe a synthetic unallocated extension-range opcode `0x1008` — the gap between `IPC_PIPE = 0x1007` and `PROC_SPAWN = 0x1100`. HOST_FILE_RECV was the LAST real-but-unimplemented opcode; every opcode in `abi::ext::FIRST..LAST_EXCL` now has a handler. The synthetic probe target preserves the "ENOSYS-fallthrough must keep working" invariant for the dispatch loop's fallback arm, mirroring the WASI probe's rotation to `0x0005` when `FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS` landed. +10 integration tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (in a new `// ---------- host_file_recv ----------` block): `host_file_recv_dequeues_dropped_file` (kernel.host_file_dropped seeds; userland host_file_recv returns fd; fd_read streams the bytes), `host_file_recv_returns_ebadf_on_unknown_token`, `host_file_recv_returns_ebadf_on_already_consumed_token` (second call with the same token), `host_file_recv_preserves_drop_order` (multiple drops, recv returns each by token), `host_file_recv_per_token_isolated` (different tokens for different files; one recv doesn't consume the other), `host_file_recv_eof_after_full_read`, `host_file_recv_emfile_on_full_fd_table_rolls_back_token` (load-bearing — verifies the token is re-presented in `host_files` after EMFILE), `host_file_recv_filestat_returns_size_and_zero_times`, `host_file_recv_seek_random_access`, `host_file_recv_dispatches_via_ext_opcode` (full wire-path test through dispatch_ext with opcode 0x1500). +3 unit tests in `crates/kernel/src/host_file.rs` (the new module's tests): `host_file_fd_streams_bytes_in_chunks`, `host_file_fd_returns_zero_at_eof`, `host_file_fd_seek_set_cur_end_work`. Workspace tests 1966 → 1979 (+13 from this slice alone — 10 integration + 3 unit; the synthetic-probe rewrite of `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` is in-place, no count change). **Explicitly DEFERRED**: TS-side bootstrap drag-drop wiring (`web/src/bootstrap.ts` → `host_file_dropped` postMessage with the kernel-side `Kernel::host_file_dropped` already wired but currently only test-callable); chunked streaming for huge files (current implementation reads the entire `bytes: Vec<u8>` into kernel memory; a future slice could swap to a streaming `Read` adapter over the browser's `File.stream()` Web API); blocking `host_file_recv` (park-on-empty-queue — the v1 path is non-blocking with EBADF-on-no-token); `fd_pread` (positional read without seek — would be a one-line addition to FdObject::HostFile routing); ENOENT-on-expired-token arm (current spec collapses unknown / consumed / never-existed into EBADF; a future strict-mode slice could distinguish via a separate "tombstone" table); extra metadata (mime type is captured in HostFile but not yet exposed via fd_filestat_get — would need an extension to the filestat structure or a new opcode); cross-tab-refresh token GC (when a tab refreshes, the kernel restarts but the bootstrap might re-issue tokens; a future slice would need a token-namespace-per-session scheme). **Constitution check** PASS: Principle II (strict layering) — host_file is a kernel-side domain module; FdObject routing keeps the fd abstraction uniform across syscall types; Principle V (process isolation) — host files are kernel-managed; userland only sees opaque tokens (which the future TS-side wiring will pass via the trusted bootstrap channel) and fds; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) — opcode 0x1500 lands per spec §3.6 with the EXACT wire format documented; Principle X (testability at every layer) — +10 integration + 3 unit tests with the load-bearing EMFILE-rollback assertion, plus the synthetic-opcode probe rotation that keeps the ENOSYS-fallthrough invariant testable. **Architectural note**: the `host_file_recv` slice closes the LAST ENOSYS-fallthrough on the extension-syscall range — every documented ext-opcode in the contract now has a real handler. The dispatch_ext fallback arm is now exclusively for "future slice opcodes that haven't shipped yet" (the 0x1008 synthetic probe target proves the fallback still works). The spec-driven choice to use a TOKEN-and-EBADF model (rather than the slice-prompt-suggested per-pid queue with EAGAIN) was the right call: token-based wire shape is simpler (4 bytes args, 4 bytes return), atomically-reject semantics avoid the "what does an empty queue mean" ambiguity, and the "exactly one recv per token" constraint maps cleanly onto the browser's File reference model where each drop produces a single File object that can be read once and closed. The new `crates/kernel/src/host_file.rs` module sits cleanly alongside `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs` as a second domain-scoped sub-module — establishes the precedent that any future complex-state kernel feature gets its own module rather than growing `sys.rs`. The EMFILE-rollback pattern (re-insert the token if fd allocation fails) is the kernel's first explicit transactional pattern; future slices that allocate multiple kernel resources atomically should consider adopting the same shape (try-reserve-everything-or-rollback). **Parallel-dispatch coordination note (fourteenth multi-agent batch)**: three slices landed concurrently (T072 host_file_recv a0a478f + T144 read -r 2ab327f + T146 sort -V 442ca5d). Workspace baseline 1966 → 2001 (+35) post all three cherry-picks, all green. Notable shape of this batch: ONE substantial kernel slice (T072 host_file_recv — closes the LAST ENOSYS-fallthrough, lands a new domain module + FdObject variant + 4 new state-bearing fields on Kernel, follows the spec rather than the slice prompt's queue-design suggestion since the spec was authoritative), ONE small sh slice (T144 read -r — first flag-bearing builtin in v1, validates the single-flag parse pattern), ONE medium coreutils slice (T146 sort -V — eighth sort comparator, introduces the first hand-written `Ord` impl for `Key` enum, flagged the overdue `key_for` builder refactor TWICE in a row). Fourteen batches in a row of three slices each shipped (batches 3 +15, 4 +28, 5 +21, 6 +33, 7 +32, 8 +32, 9 +41, 10 +41, 11 +34, 12 +42, 13 +42, 14 +35 = +396 across twelve batches); the parallel-dispatch shape continues to scale. **Architectural milestones in this batch**: (1) ext-range opcode coverage is now COMPLETE — every documented opcode in the contract has a real handler, with the ENOSYS-fallthrough now exclusively reserved for "future slice opcodes"; (2) sort comparator count at 8 (numeric/version/dictionary/printable/fold/blanks/lex/whatever-`-c`-and-`-o`-add) with `key_for`'s 8-parameter shape now objectively past the refactor threshold; (3) `read` builtin's first flag (`-r`) validates the smallest-possible flag-parser shape that future flags can reuse without a shared parser layer until count > 3. The runway for the next batch: T146 `key_for` BUILDER REFACTOR (now urgent — flagged in three successive slice prose paragraphs); T146 `sort -m` merge mode; T146 `sort -t SEP` custom field separator; T146 `sort -g` general-numeric (would add `Key::General(f64)` variant alongside Numeric / Bytes / Version); T144 `read -p PROMPT` flag (next-most-requested read flag); T144 IFS-based field splitting for multi-VAR; T072 root remount; T072 TS-side bootstrap wiring for host_file_dropped (would be the first slice that touches the JS layer in this stretch).
